# Project Memory & Operational Notes

_The **travelling memory** for this repo. Because the repo syncs (Dropbox), anything here is available on every machine — unlike the assistant's machine-local memory, which does **not** travel._

> **Rule:** durable project context goes **in the repo** — in the formal docs, or in this file — **never only in machine-local memory.** Read this file (and `../CLAUDE.md`) at the start of every session.

> **Rule (requirements stay in sync):** when a request adds or changes *what the product must do* — a new user-visible capability, or a changed Must/Should — **update [02-requirements.md](02-requirements.md) (add/edit the FR line) in the same change.** Check it as work goes along, not retroactively. Bug fixes → this file's session log only; design *choices* → an ADR; new *capabilities* → also an FR. (Backfilled 2026-06-13 after this had been skipped for the orientation/align-horizontal and constraint-adaptation work.)

## Where memory lives

- **Decisions (why we chose things)** → [06-decisions.md](06-decisions.md) — the ADR log is the authoritative record. Add an ADR for any significant decision.
- **Plan & current status / resume pointer** → [09-implementation-plan.md](09-implementation-plan.md) (the Status line at the top).
- **Vision / requirements / NFRs / design / glossary / testing** → docs 01–05, 08.
- **Theorem source** → [07-theorem-reference.md](07-theorem-reference.md) + the bagrut PDF.
- **Operational notes / working context that doesn't fit a formal doc** → this file (below).
- **Academic paper writing & the theory/algorithm lineage** → [paper/](paper/README.md) — the method↔citation mapping (what known methods the engine actually uses), the paper outline, a bibliography, and dated discussion logs. Distilled theory lives in the numbered docs there; raw discussions in `paper/discussions/`.

## Operational notes

- **PRIORITY (operator directive, 2026-06-21): Phase 6 (theorems) is GATED behind a full, working, comprehensively-tested system. No rush.** Before any theorem work, the existing engine + parser + LLM-fallback + UI must be hardened and trustworthy end-to-end. **STATUS — hardening Pillars 1–4 DONE (2026-06-21), 1183 green:** comprehensive givens verifier (every relation), corpus + scenarios + sweep self-verify (zero silent-wrong figures), generative negative-space contradiction-rejection gate, extension-`t` sampling, additive-stability property, and R7 binding fragility fixed at root (equilateral/AAS). **Pillar 5 (production proxy deploy) is a GO-LIVE step only — the LLM fallback already runs locally (dev proxy), so the whole system is testable end-to-end now; it is NOT a local-correctness gate.** Deferred (with reasons, non-blocking): full R7(3) greedy-retirement + stay-put penalty (no drift evidence), Tier-C `movedAncestors` generated stability (snapshot-guarded). The automated gate is substantially met; remaining before Phase 6 is the operator's own **local manual-validation pass**. Treat new features as secondary to making what exists correct and tested.

- **WORKFLOW (operator directive, 2026-07-10 — [ADR-265](06-decisions.md#adr-265)): the standard operating route is [22-workflow.md](22-workflow.md).** Every report/request → a GitHub issue (type + P1/P2/P3 + app labels) BEFORE/while fixing; capability gaps relabel `feature` and go through a PR with an operator gate; **`main` is the trunk** (`rebuild-foundation` retired); deploys only from committed `main` per [RUNBOOK.md](RUNBOOK.md), tagged `prod/YYYY-MM-DD` + logged in [DEPLOY-LOG.md](DEPLOY-LOG.md) — **deploy notes stop accumulating in this file** (entries below predate adoption).

- **2026-07-09 (cross-session check: "3 pre-existing 2-D failures") — ADR-264 Am. 2: the trailing inscribe predicate.** A sibling session flagged 3 failures on the tree; all three were collateral of the Am.-1 `droppedShapeNoun` guard. (1+2) The phase4 trapezoid-altitude tests' STUB context supplied `parallels` without `polygons` — the real `buildParseCtx` always supplies a drawn trapezoid's polygon (the bare-`בטרפז` definite-reference exemption then fires), so the stubs were corrected to match the app; production behaviour was never wrong. (3) Scenario `symbolic-2alpha` step 1 (`משולש שווה שוקיים ABC שבו AB=AC חוסם במעגל`) exposed the real class: pre-Am.-1 the lax leftover-guard-less `circumcircle` rule half-parsed it to a bare circumcircle (isosceles + stated pair silently dropped — the scenario had locked THAT wrong figure); post-Am.-1 the rescue kept shape+pair but `equalSegments` swallowed the piece's trailing `חוסם במעגל` (circle nouns are exempt from the noun guard by design) → no circle O → the 2α drive tore the isosceles ("over-constrained"). Fix: `droppedCirclePredicate` (verb+`במעגל` ADJACENCY at the piece END — a predicate, not an ADR-119 carrier marker) joins the winner gate, and `splitStatements` detaches the tail and re-parses `<the unique ctx polygon's letters> <predicate>` through the normal rules (the ADR-245 definite-reference pattern, verb edition — named-circle membership/ADR-156 idempotence/fresh circumcircle all stay rule-owned); no unique polygon → honest escalation. The scenario's tD≈1.3 pin was ground-truth-corrected (with AB=AC actually enforced, ∠BAC=∠CAD is unsatisfiable at the seeded t for every apex height, so the unstated extension t is legitimately driven; the check now asserts the pair + ratio + D-beyond-C). Full suite green, `tsc -b` clean.
- **PROD deploy 2026-07-09 (later — the hover-conversion fix) — bundle `index-Dd_SV8gj.js` (2-D only).** Static-only; **proxy untouched**; live 200. Full suite 3475 green + build clean.
- **2026-07-09 (operator: "angles are not detected at all now — real root cause analysis") — the HOVER COORDINATE conversion scaled by the outer props, broken the day the toolbar moved into flow.** Layered RCA on the operator's EXACT session (`משולש ABC` → `מעוין BDEF חסום במשולש`): layer 1 detection ✓ (the full ∠B class `{∠ABC,∠CDE,∠DEF,∠AFE}` present), layer 2 store `viewRelations` ✓, layer 3 pick/marks logic ✓ (a simulated wedge probe at B picks the class, 4 arcs) — so the defect was in the ONE layer DOM-free tests can't see: `relationPickAt` converted client→SVG coordinates scaling by the **width/height PROPS**, while after the toolbar-row layout change the SVG's real size is the measured `vw/vh` (props minus the toolbar row). The ~6% vertical stretch grows toward the bottom of the canvas (~40px at B) — past every pick reach, so hovering/tapping an angle or side found NOTHING while detection was perfectly correct. Latent since the pick code was written (ratio was 1.0 while svg size === props); exposed by the layout fix. Fix: a pure exported `clientToSvg(rect, viewW, viewH, …)` — the ONE conversion (sibling audit: the only `getBoundingClientRect` in `src/`) — wired with `vw/vh`; locked by `client-to-svg.test.ts` incl. an end-to-end case proving the buggy scaling MISSES the wedge at a bottom vertex and the correct one PICKS it. **Answer to the operator's "why not just check everything?":** detection ALREADY checks everything visible (the angle universe enumerates every distinct ray pair at every point over drawn edges + collinear splits — ∠B's class was found at every layer); the failure was hit-testing, not universe selection, so widening the universe would not have helped and would flood the UI with angles between unconnected points. Full suite 3475 green.
- **PROD deploy 2026-07-09 (later — the canvas toolbar layout fix) — bundle `index-COxrz1SQ.js` (2-D only).** Static-only; **proxy untouched**; live 200, perms 644 root:root. Full suite 3471 green + build clean.
- **2026-07-09 (canvas layout) — the toolbar was an OVERLAY on the SVG; moved into normal flow above it.** Operator (two screenshots): "the top row has buttons that hide the canvas … on a smaller screen the buttons overlap and disappear." The TRUE cause of "top nodes not visible" (the earlier `keepOrRefit` label-margin fix helped but wasn't the root): `Figure`'s two toolbars were `position:absolute; top:8` **over** the SVG, so the figure's apex rendered behind them, and the two absolute trays (orientation start-edge, export end-edge) collided/vanished on narrow widths. Fix (`Figure.tsx`): the root is now a **flex column** — a wrapping toolbar ROW (visual order 1) above an **SVG area** (order 2, `flex:1`, `overflow:hidden`) whose measured size (a new inner `ResizeObserver`, falling back to the width/height props in SSR) drives the fit; the export tray uses `marginInlineStart:auto` and `flexWrap` so it drops to a second line on small screens instead of overlapping; the orientation popover is now `position:absolute` under its toggle so opening it doesn't shove the figure. No occlusion, no collision. `tsc -b` + 69 render tests + full suite (3471) green.
- **PROD deploy 2026-07-09 (later — ADR-262 Am. 1 points 2 & 3) — bundle `index-Lg7uF1Ud.js` (2-D only).** The `∠B=∠CDE` detection fix (variantConfigs excludes inscribe) + the canvas-fit label-margin fix (`keepOrRefit` reserves `LABEL_MARGIN=28`). Static-only; **proxy untouched**; live 200, perms 644 root:root. Full suite 3471 green + `tsc -b` + build clean. Tree note: earlier ADR-262 inscribe work + a concurrent session's ADR-263 (altitude) were COMMITTED by another session; my points-1/2/3 changes rode the working tree uncommitted per the usual flow.
- **2026-07-09 (prod test follow-up) — equal-angle ARC marks deduped by wedge (extends ADR-167 Am.).** Operator's prod test of the inscribed rhombus: vertex B showed a DOUBLE arc while ∠E showed a single one. Root cause: the equal-angle class `{∠ABC, ∠DBF, ∠DEF}` (rhombus ∠B=∠E) lists ∠ABC and ∠DBF as two members — but they are the SAME physical wedge at B (F on BA, D on BC ⇒ ray BF=BA, BD=BC), so `relationMarks` drew two stacked arcs there. Fix: collapse class members that share a wedge before drawing (the ADR-167 Am. wedge-dedup, previously applied only to definite-angle VALUES, now also to equal-angle arcs). `src/render/scene.ts` only; locked by a `relation-marks.test.ts` case (the shared-vertex rhombus class → 1 arc at B, 1 at E). **Points 2 & 3 (operator: fix both) — DONE (ADR-262 Am. 1):** (2) `∠B = ∠CDE` (corresponding angle, DE∥AB) now surfaces. It was NOT an endpoint-enumeration gap — `detectRelations` already enumerates angles over `collinearSplits` sub-edges and finds it. The store's `viewRelations` was DROPPING it because it samples across the inscribe's *variant configs*, and the rhombus's two variants are mirror **relabelings** (D↔F swap) → a per-label relation at D can't survive the relabel. Fix: `variantConfigs` includes only `shape-variant` (its variant is a *relation* choice); an `inscribe` variant is a placement/mirror and must not gate relation detection (it stays cyclable via the button). (3) The clip/oversize came from `keepOrRefit`'s hysteresis margin `m=8px` letting a vertex drift to 8px from the edge before refit, while its LABEL sits ~24px beyond → on a canvas shrink the kept transform left the figure oversized with the apex label clipped. Fix: reserve label extent (`LABEL_MARGIN=28`, capped at padding) so a shrink refits before a label disappears; point-stability preserved. Locked by `shape-variant.test.ts` (∠ABC/∠CDE class) + `phase-f.test.ts` (near-edge vertex refits). Full suite 3471 green.
- **PROD deploy 2026-07-09 (later — the boundary-draw fix) — ADR-262 REDEPLOYED (2-D only), bundle `index-cmz2vPKu.js`.** Operator's first local test: the inscribe created the on-triangle points but (1) drew no rhombus lines, (2) wasn't found by detect-shapes, (3) equal-segments showed nothing. Root cause: `expandInscribe` emitted riders + equal-length constraints but **no drawing command** — the sides existed only as constraints. Fix: the expansion now emits a `polygon` command over the shape's vertices (ADR-111 mechanism: draws boundary segments + creates the polygon object). Verified end-to-end (scratch): `poly-BDEF` + `seg-*` drawn, `detectShapes` → `rhombus [B,D,E,F]`, equal-segments → all 4 sides in one class. Redeployed static-only; **proxy untouched**; live bundle 200 + carries `poly-`, `/healthz` ok. Locked by extended `inscribe.test.ts` + scenario + a new store detect/relations test. Full suite 3452 green + build clean.

- **LLM testing policy — NO autonomous Anthropic API / Haiku calls (operator rule, 2026-06-20).** Only the operator authorises a live API call; the assistant must never fire one on its own (no drift, no thousands of test calls, no surprise cost — the key in `.env.local` is reachable, so this is a discipline rule, not a technical block). **For testing the LLM fallback / a freeform decomposition, the assistant acts as the ORACLE itself using its own session model (Opus 4.8, subscription-based, free):** reason out the canonical command lines the LLM *should* emit, then verify they PARSE and BUILD through the real `parse → replay` path. Opus ≈ Haiku is accepted as good enough for development. **Escalate to a live Haiku call ONLY when the operator explicitly authorises it** — and only when the operator's live testing diverges from the Opus prediction (the signal that the behaviour is Haiku-specific). The production app still uses the API key (server-side, multi-user); the subscription is not a server-callable backend — see the 2023-06-01 Messages API one-off pattern in the session log if a sanctioned live check is needed.
- **Protected PDFs (e.g. the bagrut list):** the Read tool refuses the copy-protected `5pts_GeometryList_Teachers.pdf`. Extract text with PyMuPDF (`python -c "import fitz; ..."`) and write to a UTF-8 file — the Windows console is cp1255 and chokes on symbol chars.
- **Validation corpus:** `sample questions/` holds real bagrut problems (text + image). We reproduce the **figure** from the givens (never solve) and compare visually to the official image. Questions are **multi-stage** — later parts add givens; the figure accumulates them.
- **Tooling:** tests run with `npx vitest run`; `archive/` is excluded from tests and not compiled (`vite.config.ts`). On a fresh machine, run `npm install` before testing (`node_modules` is not in git).
- **Git:** work is on branch `rebuild-foundation`; backed up to the private GitHub remote `https://github.com/dcodish/geo_builder` (origin; both `main` and `rebuild-foundation` pushed, 2026-06-11). Push after committing so the backup stays current — Dropbox-synced `.git` is not a real backup. **Dropbox actively corrupts `.git`:** on the home PC (2026-06-11) `git fsck` showed missing/dangling loose objects in older history and `git status` briefly reported phantom modified files mid-sync. Harmless because GitHub holds the full history (HEAD tracks `origin/rebuild-foundation`); if the local repo misbehaves, `git fetch` to backfill or re-clone from GitHub (ideally outside Dropbox). Also: **a Read of a docs file can be stale** if Dropbox hasn't finished syncing the work-PC version — re-Read on an "edit: file modified since read" error.
- **Shell CWD gotcha (Windows):** a `cd` inside a Bash tool call into `docs/sample questions/` made later `vitest`/`tsc` runs resolve from there ("No test files found", phantom `tsconfig.json` errors). Fix: prefix the command with `Set-Location "c:\Users\User\Dropbox\projects\geo_builder"`. The CRLF warnings on commit are harmless (Windows checkout, LF in repo).
- **Tests hanging / "vitest used to be fast, now never finishes" (THIS machine, 2026-06-17) — node_modules is junctioned OUT of Dropbox.** Root cause (proven, not guessed): on this PC `node_modules` had been pulled under **Dropbox Smart Sync / cloud-filter management** (the folder carried a cloud reparse tag). Dropbox's filesystem filter driver intercepts every **ESM** file load and adds ~2–4s — CJS `require`, `cat`, builtins are unaffected, so only vite/vitest broke. Proof: the *same file* imported **2000ms from the Dropbox path vs 1ms from a local path**. The 20s-per-module timing earlier was a red herring; it's the cloud driver. **Fix in place:** `node_modules` is now a **junction → `C:\dev\geo_builder\node_modules`** (outside Dropbox); source stays in Dropbox. Two gotchas: (1) the junction TARGET must be named `node_modules` (Node resolves the junction to its realpath, then looks for sibling deps under a folder literally named `node_modules` — else `@vitest/utils` etc. aren't found). (2) **`npm ci` deletes node_modules and recreates a real folder back in Dropbox — it CLOBBERS the junction.** Use `npm install` (follows the junction). If you must run `npm ci`, recreate the junction afterward: `cmd /c mklink /J <proj>\node_modules C:\dev\geo_builder\node_modules` (after moving the fresh install out). A leftover `_node_modules_dropbox_old/` is excluded in `vite.config.ts` (`test.exclude`) so its stray third-party tests aren't collected — delete it anytime to reclaim ~122MB. (A Defender exclusion was tried first and did NOT help — it's not AV.)
- **Debug session log (`logs/debug-log.jsonl`) — read this to reconstruct what the operator did.** A dev-only logger (added 2026-06-15) writes one JSON line per event to `logs/debug-log.jsonl` (gitignored). The dev server writes it via `POST /api/log` (`server/logProxy.ts`, wired in `vite.config.ts`); the client is `src/debug/sessionLog.ts` + hooks in `App.tsx`. **Two event kinds:** `input` = every utterance the student entered (incl. ones that failed — `source: parser|llm|rename`, the produced `commands`, or `result: 'not-understood'`); `figure` = a snapshot after every change (the full ordered `facts` with each `cmd`, `enabled`, and per-fact `status`, plus `seed` and `lastError`). Grouped by a per-page-load `session` id with a `seq`. **To reproduce a "this didn't work" report:** read the latest `figure` line's `facts[].cmd` (replay those to rebuild the exact shape) and scan the `input` lines for the utterance + its `status`/`lastError`. Off in production (`import.meta.env.DEV`). **Gotcha:** `logs/` MUST be in Vite's `server.watch.ignored` (it is) — otherwise each write reloads the page → write loop (a new `session` every second). Clear it anytime with `: > logs/debug-log.jsonl`.

## Session log
- **2026-07-11 — TRIAGE (issues #30 + #31 + #32, not fixed per ADR-265 Am. 1): prod session `jsptarcl` — "D was not on GB but on the continuation" turned out to be THREE stacked defects.** The operator's 5-step sequence pulled from the prod events log and replayed on HEAD. **#30 (bug, P1 — the report):** bare `GB חותך את המעגל בנקודה D` lowers to `line-circle-intersection` with `avoid` and NO order — on the clean intended figure the default seed puts D at t=1.269 (beyond B), zero violations; the ADR-077/268 bare-segment class in the line∩CIRCLE family (ADR-127's `order` field exists, only circumcircleMeetsSegment uses it; ADR-268 fixed line∩line only). Fix plan: ADR-268 operand classifier + `order:[a,D,b]` in lineMeetsCircle; sweep lineCutsCircleTwice/tangentMeetsOtherCircle/secantFromExternal; the En 'line GB' opt-out (B13 corpus) preserved. **#31 (bug, P2):** step 1 `משולש ABC חוסם במעגל` (the classic חסום slip) silently built the CONVERSE — the incircle dual — because the direction is read from the verb letter alone; per ADR-245 the ב container marker must govern (`במעגל` ⇒ the circle contains the triangle); `חוסם את המעגל` untouched. **#32 (bug, P2, needs-operator):** the incircle decomposition auto-minted feet F/G/H, so the student's later `G על המשך CA` bound to the invisible determined foot (the ADR-116 label-hijack family, decomposition edition) — operator to choose: unnamed scaffolding vs student-statement-wins-the-letter. All three await the operator's priority/order for a dedicated fix session; nothing implemented.
- **2026-07-11 — FIX SESSION (operator: "fix P1 and P2 and from P3 #20 … commit and deploy; I test tomorrow morning"): the whole open queue closed in one pass — #22/#17/#21/#18/#7/#2/#20 fixed + #6 unparked.** One combined session commit on `main` (the fixes interleave in `parse.ts`/`scenarios.test.ts`, so per-issue commits would misattribute diffs); each item has its own ADR + regression scenario. **#22 (P1, ADR-268):** per-operand reference semantics in line∩line — the engine gains `onSeg1`/`onSeg2` (the *within* twin of `dir1`/`dir2`, lowered to `collinear-order [X,E,Y]`), the parser classifies each operand in all three forms (cut-form by verb side; conjunctions with a leading המשך DISTRIBUTING over the pair — "המשך BE ו-AD" = both extensions), drawing per-operand; sibling fix: the ⊥/∥ `LINE_CUT` target's reference words (previously swallowed by the filler) now carry within/beyond/free order; tangent∩segment evaluated and deliberately left unconstrained (a tangent meets a same-circle chord's line strictly OUTSIDE the segment). **#17 (ADR-269):** chord/radius/קטע nouns no longer abort the cut/meet rules (only diameter/tangent stop); `withCarrierMembership` restores the memberships (diameter-scoped centre bail; scaffolding-segment exclusion so the new crossing is never forced onto the circle) — the full textbook-worded bagrut Q4 builds (scenario). **#21 (ADR-270):** `diameterFromPoint` — bare `קוטר מנקודה F`, auto-named antipode, M1 membership. **#18 (ADR-271):** `relationAt` — an ACTIVE wedge's own ARM segments are excluded from the segment pick (narrow stated angles, arms = radii, were structurally unhoverable inside ≲26°). **#7 (ADR-272):** `newConstraintsNonVacuous` — the step-accept boundary refuses a solve where a new constraint's OWN refs coincide (the vacuous 0=0 collapse the recruiter used to admit; B13 now builds, theorem 87 flipped gaps→expect). **#2 (ADR-273):** word magnitudes (רבע/חצי/שליש/half/quarter) join `droppedGivenNumbers` (relation-marker-gated, חצי/רבע-מעגל exempt, value-or-inverse accounting). **#20 (ADR-274 + ADR-3D-036):** user-named save files with the automatic per-product suffix — `<name>-geo.json` / `<name>-vectors.json`, prompt on Save, empty/cancel → the date default; suffix registered in docs/22 §9. **#6 (ADR-124 UNPARKED):** operator ruling — "AD tangent to O at A" IS a given ⇒ the honest over-constrained refusal for the contradictory D-sequence is final (scenario `adr-124-contradictory-extension-refused-honestly`; - **2026-07-09 — ADR-264: shape + property clause in ONE utterance parses deterministically; a dropped stated relation is never silent.** Operator: "the user names a shape and then gives a property needed for the shape — `משולש ABC הוא שווה שוקיים, כלומר AC=BC`, `דלתון ABCD, AB=AD` — the second part is ignored and the user is not told." Two coupled class members: (1) the textbook APPOSITIVE form was never deterministic — `multiStatement` requires every piece to carry a relation operator, so the shape piece falls through, the shape rule `'stop'`s on the leftover, and the line escalates to the LLM; (2) on that path a dropped relation between existing points is INVISIBLE — the honesty gates watch new labels (ADR-089) and numbers (ADR-250), and `AC=BC`'s letters all already appear on the shape, so a decomposition that lost the clause committed silently (worse for isosceles: the soft default `|AB|=|AC|` silently substituted for the student's stated pair). Fix at the mechanism: `splitStatements`, a LAST-RESORT clause fallback inside `parse` (runs only after every whole-utterance rule failed — never shadows list-commas/`multiStatement`/rule-owned compounds), splitting on the statement separators + apposition connectives (`כלומר`/`שבו`/`כאשר`/spaced dash/En "that is" etc.), ALL-OR-NOTHING, with context THREADED between clauses (`augmentParseCtx`: points/polygons/circles/neighbors) — so `מרובע ABCD, מעגל חסום במרובע` binds the incircle to THE quad and `משולש ABC, זווית B = 90` resolves the single-vertex angle; plus `droppedGivenRelations`, the third honesty gate on BOTH commit paths (a symbol-form `AB=CD`/`⊥`/`∥` must land in a `set-*` constraint or a point-definition that introduces one of its labels — a bare shape command deliberately does NOT account, per the ADR-234 pin). Locked by `clause-split.test.ts` (13) + scenarios `isosceles-appositive-stated-pair-one-line` / `kite-stated-pair-one-line` (the operator's exact utterances). Full suite green, `tsc -b` clean.

Parser rule `inscribedInPolygon` runs before `incircle`/`inscribedPolygon` and the base shape rules; roles set by the ב/"in" preposition (ADR-245), not word order; an inner shape we can't make determinate (bare quad / triangle) escalates (`'stop'`) — never a plain-shape misparse; `incircle` now requires a circle noun (semantic guard). Determinacy falls out: rhombus-in-triangle is unique up to the mirror (which just relabels D↔F); rectangle-in-triangle is a correct 1-parameter family (sampled by "show another"). Locked by `engine/__tests__/inscribe.test.ts` (placement/variant/expansion/pin), scenario `rhombus-inscribed-in-triangle`, a `shape-variant.test.ts` cycle test, parser-coverage (6 parse + 2 escalate) + catalog/shadow snapshots. The rule also moved ahead of the shape rules (joining median/altitude/perpBisector/midsegment) so its `…במשולש ABC` form isn't shadowed by `triangle`'s SHAPE_LEFTOVER `'stop'`; safe there because it defers on intersect keywords. - **2026-07-08 (work PC, same session, operator follow-up) — ADR-257: a congruent sub-group inside a mixed similarity class is reported alongside it.** After ADR-256 merged △ACK into the kite's congruent pair, the class-wide kind degraded to "similar" and △OEM ≅ △OMK vanished from the list (operator: "OEM=OMK is not shown"). Class: merging drops the stronger sub-relation — the honesty invariant, detection-display edition. Fix in `detectSimilarClasses`: partition a mixed class into congruence sub-groups (union-find over pairwise congruence under the aligning correspondence; congruence is transitive within a similarity class) and emit each ≥2 group as its own `congruent` row beside the `similar` one; all-congruent classes stay one row; rows scale with GROUPS, never O(n²) pairs. Two findings: (1) the verifier DID raise `figure.v.meetOnSegment` (the amber list under the canvas) — but the row reads ✓ and the operator read the figure as unreported; (2) the REAL gap — `findValidConfig` returned null because **the seed sampler explores only a small neighbourhood of a free point's default** (rigid cluster spin + ±0.22·span jitter), so no sampled config can carry a free point across the figure to a far valid basin. Class: a statement that constrains a free point's REGION ("these segments cross") was recorded only as a post-hoc requirement — nothing used it to PLACE the point (the ADR-098 / ADR-254-re-seat family, M1/M4). Fix at apply (`reseatLooseMeetEndpoint`): re-seat a genuinely loose endpoint (non-pinned free point, no constraint refs, no directive; fewest dependents first) along the ray from its fixed mate through the other segment's midpoint, requiring general position (ADR-253 helper) AND preserving the point's side of every resolvable circle (so the stated outside survives — no new state needed); constrained endpoints (ADR-166 apexes) are never touched. - **2026-07-08 (work PC) — ADR-253 + ADR-254: the bagrut kite-EMKO session (`ad66x493`) — general-position defaults + inside/outside a circle.** Operator: "1 — we are missing the engine instruction of putting a point inside or outside a circle; 2 — the engine failed to find a solution … twice but maybe for same reason." Both diagnosed from the debug log through the real parse→replay path; the two failures were indeed one cause — and it was NOT the solver. **(ADR-253)** `placeBase`'s one-anchor fit is a pure translation, so the bare `AM` (M new) placed M at A+(5,0) — EXACTLY on B and collinear with A,O,B; K = AM∩OC collapsed onto O (|OK|=0) and the kite givens `OK=OE`/`MK=ME` were genuinely unsatisfiable at the ONLY composition the apply gate judges. Key structural fact worth remembering: **the seed is applied AFTER the fold** (`applySeed` on the final construction), so an apply-time constraint failure is invisible to `findValidConfig`'s seed sweep — every seed re-fails identically and the step parks as "deferred" forever. Probes proved the class: the same constraints applied at ANY generic composition (|OK| from 0.13 to 11.3, M inside or outside) solve in milliseconds — only exact degeneracy fails, and each failing replay cost ~3–35 s (the §7 failure-path cost rule; noted, not fixed). Deterministic unreported siblings closed: `square ABCD`+`AE` stacked E on B; `rectangle`+`BE` put E exactly on line AB (a chained `AB`,`BC` no longer draws a false straight continuation — an unstated collinearity was the ADR-052 sin in placement form). **(ADR-254)** `M מחוץ למעגל` had no representation (LLM → honest not-understood; the vocabulary had only membership). New `point-circle-side` command in the ADR-244 radius-order shape: NEW id → free point (2 DOF) seeded on the stated side (via the ADR-253 helper — θ=0 sits exactly on a drawn diameter's line; and the CENTRE must be excluded from the clearance set, it anchors every candidate line — a bug the scenario caught); EXISTING id → M1 statement (non-pinned free point re-seats, never conflicts); verifier `figure.v.outsideCircle`/`insideCircle` + `meetsRequirements` keep sampling on the stated side; the ADR-252 export prints it as a given; the catalog entry auto-feeds the LLM prompt vocabulary. **Filed, not fixed:** apply-time satisfiability is still judged at one composition (a legitimately-special SOLVED position that poisons a later constraint has only the recruiter's multi-start); the parser's fixed-coordinate auto free-points (`{3,2}`, tangent apex `{6,0}`) are a cousin class left as-is. Scenarios `kite-EMKO-outside-point` (the full intended sequence) + `kite-EMKO-degenerate-default` (the as-committed fact list); units `general-position.test.ts` + `circle-side.test.ts`.
- **2026-07-08 (work PC) — ADR-252: the question .docx export omits scaffolding steps.** Operator: the export "includes steps that are visual and not needed to understand the shape — such as marking nodes to create the line; they have no effect on the shape itself." `questionLines` now classifies each submission group SEMANTICALLY over its LOWERED commands (every phrasing/language inherits): pure ink (`segment`, `line-through`) and value-less markers creating a NEW point (`free-point free:true`, ratio-less `point-on-segment`, unrestricted `point-on-circle`, `point-on-line`) are scaffold-class; a statement about an EXISTING point is a given (design-rules M1: "C על DF" stays), and any stated value (ratio, coords, arc restriction) stays; a REVERSE pass keeps a scaffold step whose introductions a later KEPT group references ("נקודה G על AD" stays under "זווית GBA = 37") and omission cascades (mark E, mark F, draw EF → all three drop together). - **2026-07-05 (ADR-230) — a size given on a circle's RADIUS drives the radius, never the free centre.** Operator: "check why the diagram in test failed" — diagnosed from the dev debug log (`logs/debug-log.jsonl`, session `gzswxmq3`, NOT the test suite). The figure: two circles tangent externally at M, `O1M=9`/`O2M=16` (M is the touch point so the givens ARE the two radii), then two tangents from N to O1 at M and B — the final step over-constrained (`|O1M|=9 cannot hold`) and drew a non-perpendicular 2nd tangent. Verified constructible analytically first (so the verdict was a false negative), then traced the driven solve: the step had a 6-DOF coupled system (2 free centres + N + B) that only settled in compromise basins (|O1M|→6.9). **Root cause:** `set-distance |centre·P|` where P lies on the circle is a RADIUS given, but with the free radius BUSY driving the tangency `coincide`, `driveOrCheck` reached neither the radial point (not a movable carrier) nor the radius (unavailable) and fell through to its last resort — the free CENTRE — which can never change `|O1M|`, injecting a spurious useless centre DOF per size given. **Diagnosis method worth remembering:** disproved my own first (simpler) diagnosis by instrumenting the actual `resolveMixedCarriers` restarts (logged carrier sets + per-restart cost/accept to a scratch file) — that revealed the real 6-DOF system and the mis-recruitment, not the free-point-sweep gap I first pitched. Went back to the operator with the enlarged scope (per the ASK-when-large rule) before coding. **Fix:** route a size given on a BUSY tangency radius to pin the radius (extracted `applyRadiusGiven`, shared with `set-radius`) and hand the orphaned coincide's centre-gap to a free centre — `keepTangencyDriven`'s guard loosened to fire as soon as ANY tangency radius is pinned (once one radius is fixed, the remaining free radius alone can't satisfy a fixed centre gap). **Crucially narrow:** only a `via:'free'` radius WITH a solve directive (busy) is intercepted; an AVAILABLE free radius (two INTERSECTING circles, `circle-circle-intersection`) must stay a flexible DOF or the seed geometry hard-fails ("circles do not meet") before the solve — the three `radius-dof.test.ts` intersecting-circle cases are the guardrail that caught this. Scenario `tangent-circle-size-given-drives-radius-not-centre` (exact 4 utterances). Full suite 2267 + build green.

- **2026-07-05 (ADR-229) — freeze-and-co-drive: the coupled "one point, two conditions" class is SOLVED (bagrut Q11 builds end-to-end).** Operator, after two failed attempts on the Q11 figure: "i cannot use the solution and not have A on the continuation of BD and frankly i dont understand why this is not solvable… solve this by the root cause without damaging anything else." The figure IS determined (closed form: |AB|=30, |BC|=18, |AD|=12) — the failure was the **one-constraint-per-carrier model**: the apex B (free point, 2 DOF) claimed by one tangency (⟂ at the fixed C) still has a SPARE DOF along the tangent line, which the final "A on extension of BD" collinear must consume; no recruiter case could express it, and a naive co-drive (instrumented) destabilised the unrelated circles-tangency at E (the whole carrier soup re-optimised together). **Fix (three pieces, failure-path-only + self-verifying):** (1) `SolveDirective.also` — a carrier co-drives extra constraints; both joint solvers fold `also` into the cost. (2) Recruiter case (F): BAKE the valid partial solution (`resolveDriven` exported — params written, directives cleared), re-drive ONLY the failing constraint's own carriers (originals restored from a pre-mutation snapshot; the free host gets K via `also`), FREEZE the rest (their constraints hold by construction; the sub-solve shrinks to A(θ)+B(xy)+D(θ) = 4 DOF), and MULTI-START the host from a deterministic compass ring scaled to the figure extent (the valid B is a figure-span from its seed — outside the local basin). (3) Valid-early-exit in `recruitFreeDofs` — once the system evaluates valid STOP, because the exploratory cases mutate carrier assignments even when their verification fails (observed: the sibling collinear-order's failed experiments UNDID the found solution). Diagnosis method worth remembering: instrument case-(F) attempts via a globalThis trace + hand-build the frozen sub-solve with the EXACT closed-form seed to separate "mechanism wrong" from "optimizer can't reach" (it was both, sequentially). Also (parser): copula-less `היקף מעגל O1 6π` now parses (value reader gained a circle-name-then-number pattern; the operator hit the LLM twice on it). Scenario `two-tangents-apex-collinear-with-pinned-point` (exact 8 utterances, closed-form values asserted; ~4.7 s — multi-start NM, acceptable, noted as perf target). Engine-only suite green pre-parser-change (2263), final full suite green. **Principle:** when a constraint fails on a claimed carrier, the question is not "is a DOF free?" but "does a claimed carrier have RESIDUAL freedom on its manifold?" — and a co-drive must be solved over a FROZEN base, never the whole soup.

- **2026-07-05 (ADR-228 Am.6) — build-error messages now carry a "try a different order / wording / smaller steps" hint.** Design thread with the operator on the bagrut-Q11 tangent (a figure the book DESCRIBES rather than constructs, so a single complex line parses but the coupled solve fails). Decided AGAINST escalating parsed-but-unsolvable inputs to the LLM — the money gate is the parse-success boundary: the LLM only helps when the deterministic parser FAILED (a different decomposition has hope); a parsed input already has its canonical form so the LLM would re-emit the same command (no hope, pure spend). Also decided the Q11-specific Thales construction is figure-specific, not structural, so skipped. The chosen aid: a UI-only guidance message. Added shared `errors.retryHint` (He+En) appended to every humanized BUILD error via an `explainError` helper at the display layer (input note, error banner, broken-step tooltip); `humanizeError` stays a pure mapping (test untouched). "Different order" is real advice — ADR-104 deferral means givens-before-constraint often builds what the reverse can't. Locale parity verified; build + suite green.

- **2026-07-05 (ADR-228 Am.4) — a line through two on-circle points that "passes through the centres" must not collapse them onto the tangency point.** Operator (same figure): `A על מעגל O1`, `B על מעגל O2`, then `AB עובר דרך מרכזי המעגלים` placed A AND B onto E (the tangency point) with no warning. Debug-log sequence reproduced: the utterance DIDN'T PARSE (no rule) → escalated to the LLM, whose output collapsed them; E is the degenerate attractor (on the centre line AND on both circles, so a plain collinearity drives each on-circle point to the nearest crossing = E for both). Fix: new `lineThroughCenters` rule — fires only when the two endpoints are on DISTINCT circles (via ctx.circleMembers), emits an ORDERED `set-line [A, centreOfA, centreOfB, B]` + draws AB; the order puts each endpoint at the FAR intersection of the centre line with its OWN circle, so A,B come out distinct at the diameter ends (verified |AB|=24, |AE|=6=2r1, |BE|=18=2r2, A–O1–O2–B collinear). Realises the operator's "find a different option when points would coincide" principle (ADR-123) STRUCTURALLY via the order, not by relying on the sampler. On the warning: for an AVOIDABLE collision the right behaviour is to avoid it silently (now done); the `coincidences` warning (ADR-123) is reserved for a FORCED coincidence and is unchanged. (Even a plain unordered collinearity resolves A,B distinct here — the all-on-E collapse was LLM-output-specific, now bypassed.) Scenario `line-through-both-centres-avoids-tangency-point` (exact 6 utterances) + parse block in `line-through-center-and-secant.test.ts`; shadow-matrix clean. Build + suite green. **Follow-up (same day):** the operator had tried several centre-line phrasings that all failed before the working one — now all parse to the same ordered set-line: `AB עובר דרך O1 ו O2` (named centres), and `A-O1-O2-B` / `ישר A-O1-O2-B` (new `dashCollinear` rule — dash-separated ordered collinear list). **PARKED — awaiting operator intent:** the final step `CB ו CA משיקים למעגל O2` ("2 tangents from C to O2") fails: `cornerTangentCircle` forces each tip onto O2 (`point-on-circle`) unless it's already a member — B is on O2 (OK, tangent at B) but A is on O1, so it forces A onto O2 → contradiction ("A must be on O2 but is 15 from centre"). Separately, the clean `מנקודה C שני משיקים למעגל O2` ALSO fails here because the free point C is seeded at a fixed default (6,0) that lands INSIDE the large O2 (r=9) → no tangents. Intent is ambiguous (is A meant as a tangent point on O2, contradicting O1? or is line CA tangent at a NEW point, C determined?), so ASKED rather than guessed; the C-seeded-inside-circle bug is a clear separate fix to make regardless.
- **2026-07-05 (ADR-228 Am.3) — a size given on the SECOND tangent circle must not orphan the tangency; area on a bare circle label.** Operator (same figure): after `היקף מעגל O1 הוא 6π` (O1→r3), `שטח מעגל O2 הוא 81π` (O2→r9) over-constrained "M coincides with ~touch-M cannot hold" — and since they'd renamed the touch point M→E the message said "E coincides with ~touch-E", which they reported as "an error when changing M to E." Diagnosed from the debug log + reproduced through parse→replay: ONE root bug (the rename itself works). The free-radius tangency is a `coincide` of two `radial-toward` witnesses driven by whichever radius is FREE; `set-radius` pinning the SECOND radius left the coincide with no carrier (both radii fixed) though a free CENTRE can satisfy |O1O2|=r1+r2, and `recruitFreeDofs` doesn't rescue it (it recruits only for a step's own new constraint; set-radius adds none). Fix (`keepTangencyDriven` in apply.ts): `set-radius`, on pinning a radius that drove a tangency coincide with no free radius left, recruits a free unpinned CENTRE as the carrier → the tangency re-solves by moving a centre (O1 r=3, O2 r=9, |O1O2|=12, tangent, no error). Scoped exactly to the tangency device (two radial-toward operands, both circles fixed-radius). Also (parser): `circleSizeExisting` resolves a BARE known-circle label (`שטח O2 הוא 81π`, no "מעגל") — UPPERCASE-only scan (`[A-Z]\d*`) so a lowercase keyword letter (`perimeter **o**f`) can't resolve circle O (a false shadow the shadow-matrix caught + I fixed). Parallel operator question answered in passing: drawing a centre→point segment by writing `O1E` (or `O1 E`) already works (→ `segment O1–E`, reveals the centre). Scenario `tangent-circles-both-radii-pinned-by-size` + `perimeter-circumference.test.ts` unit block; shadow-matrix snapshot updated (only intended additions, no new pair). Build + suite green.
- **2026-07-05 (ADR-228 Am.) — tangent circles honour stated names; circumference on an EXISTING circle sets its radius; the "pi" word.** Operator follow-up: `שני מעגלים O1 ו O2 משיקים מבחוץ` then `היקף מעגל O1 הוא 6pi` was refused, and the figure looked "fixed" with "a number 8 somewhere." Reproduced through the real parse→replay path: THREE root causes — (1) `circlesTangent` read names via a per-circle `מעגל\s+(label)` regex that the PLURAL `מעגלים O1 ו O2` breaks (the `ים` suffix stops the adjacency), so O1/O2 were dropped and O/P INVENTED → circle O1 never existed to reference; (2) circumference on an EXISTING circle fell to the `circle` CREATION rule, which re-emits a `circle` command `addObj` ignores (keeps the first def), silently dropping the size; (3) `circleSizeRadius` accepted only the glyph `π`, so `6pi` read as 6 (r≈0.95). Fixes: a plural-list name pattern in `circlesTangent`; a new `circleSizeExisting` rule (before `circle`) → `set-radius` for an existing circle; `(π|pi)` accepted in the circumference/area value. **Investigated and found NOT bugs:** the tangency stays correct (the PUBLISHED resolved-circle map the renderer draws shows O1=3, O2 driven to 5.6, |centres|=r1+r2) and "show another" DOES vary the radii (gated on `freeDofs`, non-empty). **Then (operator authorised the DOF-cue fix, Am.2):** `freeDofCount` read **0** ("✓ fully determined") for two tangent circles though the radius RATIO is 1 shape DOF — the likely "fixed shape" impression. The operator confirmed the target is **1**. Corrected a mistaken first reading: `rawMovableDof` ALREADY counts a free-radius circle as 1 (via `carrierOf`→`{shape,1}`), so it was off by exactly 1 for ONE reason — the tangency `coincide` (of two `radial-toward` witnesses on the shared centre axis) was counted as removing 2 when it removes 1 (its cross-axis component is identically 0 by construction). Fix: `dofRemoved` inspects operands and counts a coincide between two `radial-toward` points as 1; `radial-toward` exists only in the tangency device, so the rule is exact for the class with no reach elsewhere. Verified: lone circle 0, two tangent 1, two tangent + one absolute radius 1, square 0, inscribed triangle 2. Scenario `tangent-circles-named-then-circumference` (with a DOF assertion) + `dof-radius.test.ts` block + `perimeter-circumference.test.ts` unit block; shadow-allowlist gains `circleSizeExisting → circle` (deliberate, sibling of `setRadius → circle`). ("number 8" likely the |centres|=8.6 default distance — unconfirmed.) Build + suite green.
- **2026-07-05 (ADR-228) — size givens unified across shapes: circle circumference/area → radius, polygon perimeter as a constraint, and `O_1` subscript normalization.** Operator, testing `מעגל O_1 שהיקפו 6π` ("circle O₁ whose circumference is 6π"): *"i see it's not drawing the centre and O_1 (it shows O) but I'm not sure if it knows to set R=3."* Diagnosed from the source (no live figure needed): two failures — (1) a point token is `[A-Za-z]\d*` (a letter + GLUED digits), so the underscore in `O_1` truncated the label to `O`; (2) `parseRadius` read only `radius N`/`radius R` — circumference was unhandled, so the whole utterance fell through the grammar to the LLM, which drew a default-radius circle (R≠3). A third gap surfaced: `היקף` on a POLYGON (perimeter) was entirely unhandled, and area (ADR-118) was polygon-only. The operator chose the **unified** scope (all shapes): `היקף`/`שטח` are one size-measure family — no stated magnitude silently dropped (ADR-052). **Fix (root, three parts):** (A) `normalizePointSubscript` at the orthography boundary rewrites `X_1`/`X_{1}` → `X1` for every label (scoped to letter+`_`+DIGITS, so it can't touch the area `S_{ABC}` uppercase-letter subscript) — one fix for the whole class, mirroring `normalizeAreaSubscript`. (B) A circle's size IS its radius: `circleSizeRadius` in `parseRadius` lowers circumference `C` ⇒ r=C/2π and area `A` ⇒ r=√(A/π) to a NUMERIC radius (reusing the fixed-radius path, no new engine construct); the value may carry a π factor (`6π`) or be plain, after a copula or glued to the He possessive (`שהיקפו`, listed longest-first). (C) Polygon perimeter as a first-class constraint — the sibling of area: new `perimeter`/`perimeter-ratio` `Constraint`s (`polygonPerimeter` = Σ edges; length-unit, mirroring area's residual/scale/tolerance/verify), `set-perimeter`/`set-perimeter-ratio` commands emitted directly by a new `perimeter` parser rule that bows out whenever a circle is named (that `היקף` is a circumference → path B); added to `sample.ts`'s scale-pin set so a lone perimeter pins SCALE, and `driveOrCheck` recruits the polygon's DOFs generically via `constraintRefs` (no new solver plumbing). **Scope bounds (deliberate, noted in the ADR):** a symbolic-variable perimeter label (`היקף ABC = P`) escalates to the LLM (no `measure-perimeter` symbolic layer yet); a polygon perimeter draws no on-figure label (area does). **Tests:** `perimeter-circumference.test.ts` (13) + scenarios `circle-circumference-sizes-radius` (the operator's exact `מעגל O_1 שהיקפו 6π` → centre `O1`, radius 3) and `polygon-perimeter-sizes-figure` (`משולש ABC`+`היקף ABC = 20` → perimeter 20, verifier green); catalog examples + snapshots updated (no existing winner-rule flipped). **2237 tests green, `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean.** **Principle:** a stated magnitude is never dropped — `היקף`/`שטח` resolve against whatever shape they name (circle → radius, polygon → constraint), told apart by whether a circle is named.

- **2026-07-05 (ADR-227, session nc207foh) — a perpendicular helper's loose end is a reflectable SIDE DOF, flipped AFTER the continuous sample.** Operator: "check why DF should have moved so it fits the input." Sequence: `משולש ישר זוית ABC` · `D אמצע AB` (LLM→`D אמצע AB`) · `DF אנך ל AB` · `AC ו DF נחתכים בנקודה E`. The step built with **no error** but E landed off both segments (param ≈ −4.2 along DF, 1.22 along AC). Traced via a throwaway repro through the real parse→replay path: E is the crossing of line AC with the ⟂-to-AB-at-D, **determined by the triangle shape alone** (F's length is irrelevant to E). Landing E within both segments needs two independent DOFs — the triangle flexed so |CA|>|CB| (E within AC) AND F on the E-side of D (E within DF). A 200-seed sweep satisfied 0: whenever the triangle flexed to put E within AC, F pointed the wrong way. Two root causes: (1) `reflectAnchors` only made a *shared-vertex* right angle reflectable, so F (loose end of the cross-segment `set-perpendicular(D,F,A,B)`) had no reflection axis and its side was frozen; (2) reflecting F BEFORE `applySeed` shifts the free-cluster spin centroid → re-shapes the triangle → the shape that satisfied one requirement is lost when the side flips (a probe confirmed reflect-AFTER decouples cleanly, but a global reflect-after swap broke the ADR-166 equidistant-apex rhombus, which must be seeded before the spin). **Fix:** the loose end of a cross-segment perpendicular reflects across the OTHER segment's line; a new `directionHelperFreePoints` (⟂/∥ loose end, no metric constraint) reflects AFTER the sample while apex reflections stay before (`replay` splits the seed's reflection mask by point kind — apex bits pre-sample, helper bits post-sample; mask 0 and apex-only figures byte-identical). Locked by scenario `perpendicular-helper-flips-to-reach-crossing` + the ADR-166 apex scenarios stay green. **2224 tests green, `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean.** **Principle:** an unstated "which side" of a helper line is a free DOF (ADR-052) that must be explorable, and *when* in the sample pipeline a discrete DOF is applied matters — a shape-vertex mirror seeds the solver (before), a direction-helper mirror only re-sides an already-shaped figure (after).
- **2026-07-04 (ADR-220, Phase B) — alternate/corresponding parallels recognised STRUCTURALLY (any drawn-edge triangle is a transversal), and AA-similarity #69/#71 admitted as an ENTAILED (L2) help when a parallel cuts a triangle side; + a "look for similar triangles" concept.** Testing the ADR-219 discovery dial, the operator built `מקבילית ABCD` · `E על DC` · `המשך BE ו AD נפגשים בנקודה F` (parallelogram, E on the base, BE & AD produced to meet at F) and expected the feed to name the alternate/corresponding-angle theorems (**#4/#6**) and, deeper, **similar triangles**. Neither surfaced. Two root causes: (1) #4/#6 were gated behind a KIND-whitelist transversal test (`hasTransversal` recognised only a *typed* triangle or an explicit `line` object) — the ADR-167 "make it geometric, not a whitelist" smell — so the drawn triangle FAB (sides FA, FB + the parallelogram edge AB, a 3-cycle of drawn edges) went unseen; (2) #69 (AA) + #71 (ratio consequences) were STRUCTURALLY excluded from the table by ADR-208 "help, don't reveal" (their premise — *similar* — is the proof task). Operator forks (AskUserQuestion): #4/#6 = **L1 Declared** (⇒ must be coordinate-free structural recognition, not the L3 detectShapes layer); similarity = **L2 entailed + L3 observed**, plus an explicit ask to add a *concept* "אם יש ישרים מקבילים, חפשו משולשים דומים". **Fix:** (1) `hasTransversal` → `structuralTriangles` (every 3-cycle in the coordinate-free neighbour graph = segments + polygon edges) wrapped by `transversalEvidence` (present+L1 for a typed/line/3-cycle triangle, L3 when only a detectShapes triangle cuts) — so FAB surfaces 4/6 at **L1** while a bare trapezoid/parallelogram (a 4-cycle only) still suppresses them (ADR-210 held) and keeps co-interior #8. (2) #69/#71 tabled (family `similarity`, byte-exact from 07) matching on `similarityEvidence` — a **stated** parallel pair one of whose edges is a *side* of a triangle whose apex is off the *other* parallel (the Thales/line-parallel-to-a-side config, which forces the cut-off triangle similar coordinate-free) → **Entailed L2**; the sharper SAS/SSS criteria #68/#70 + bisector-ratio #76 stay excluded. Gated on a stated parallel ⇒ the Q5–Q7 corpus (no parallels) never trips 69/71 (`corpus.test.ts` neverSurface unchanged). (3) concept `parallels-seek-similar-triangles` (rides `parallelFacts`, now exported). **DEFERRED (flagged):** general L3-*observed* similarity for **non-parallel** configs (Q6's shared-inscribed-angle pair) needs coordinates/relations plumbed into the deliberately coordinate-free spine — a larger change. `integrity.test.ts` forbidden list `[68,69,70,71,76]`→`[68,70,76]` (69/71 now asserted tabled; the byte loop verifies their statements). New `matchers.test.ts` block (operator figure → 4/6 at L1, 69/71 at L2, concept present; bare trapezoid & parallelogram → none; lone triangle → no 69/71). End-to-end scenario `parallelogram-cut-triangle-surfaces-parallels-and-similarity`. **2209 tests green, `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean.** **Principle:** "help, don't reveal" is a line drawn by *what is admitted*, not a blanket ban — a similarity the construction *entails* coordinate-free is a legitimate discovery aid (name the config); the derived *criteria* that constitute the proof stay out.
- **2026-07-04 (ADR-219) — an on-screen LEVELS-OF-DISCOVERY dial grades every surfaced theorem by HOW its premise was known (Declared / Entailed / Observed).** Building on ADR-218's second way-of-knowing (construction entailment) and third (emergent/observed), the operator set the direction: *"give the tool (user) different levels of discovery … at first level we only give theorems based on input; in a larger phase we leverage the tool's discovery to identify cases in the diagram — a bisector, similarity, etc."* and *"for L3 — all kinds of things on the theorem list that won't be given in an exercise but emerge from the diagram as it's built. where would we set the level of discovery? on screen for the user to choose?"* **Built (Phase A):** a nested three-level dial — **L1 Declared** (premise instantiated by a typed fact: `set-equal`, a shape command, `set-angle`, declared circle membership), **L2 Entailed** (coordinate-free structural derivation the student never typed — radii ⇒ isosceles, two circles ⇒ kite, ADR-218), **L3 Observed** (only the evaluated coordinates reveal it — a purely-emergent detected shape/relation; opt-in, auto-enables `detectShapes`). Levels nest (`show N` ⇒ `level ≤ N`); a matcher with several evidence paths reports the **strongest (lowest)** level. **Where the level lives:** in the six evidence helpers (`isoscelesEvidence`/`kiteEvidence`/`shapeClassPresent`/`circumTriangle`/`triangleFacts`/`triangleVertexSets`) — the single place that knows the evidence kind — each returns a `level` its call site passes to `match(..., level)`; `match` defaults `level=1` so the ~100 declaration-only matchers are untouched. `detectTheorems` stays **level-complete**; the App **filters** (`visibleTheorems = feed.filter(e => e.level <= discoveryLevel)`) — so all existing tests (which read the complete feed) stay valid and the dial is purely presentation. **Control + default** (operator via AskUserQuestion): a **cumulative selector** (one escalating 3-button segment in Display options, gated on the theorems toggle), default **L1 Given-only** (the most conservative worksheet view). Picking L3 fires the same busy-painted `detectShapes()` the "detect shapes" button uses; an effect keeps that layer live as the figure grows. **Consequence — the emergent→L3 reclassification:** emergent special-quad properties (ADR-211/212) and the forced 30-60-90 pair (ADR-215, formerly "always surface when detected") now carry L3 → hidden at the L1 default; DECLARED shapes stay L1. Nothing lost — the dial reveals them. `DiscoveryLevel` added to `types.ts` (`TheoremMatch.level`, `TheoremFeedEntry.level`), propagated in `detect.ts`, `level:1` on the concept feed (not dial-graded), i18n `theorems.discovery.*` (He/En). New `matchers.test.ts` block *discovery level (ADR-219)*: declared isosceles → L1, radii-entailed → L2, declared kite → L1, two-circle kite → L2, emergent 30-60-90 → L3, declared-triangle background 10–14 → L1, and the dial contract (L1 filter hides the entailed isosceles but keeps the declared one; L2 reveals it). **2192 tests green, `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean.** **Principle:** the "help, don't reveal" line is preserved *inside each level* (L1/L2 still never surface a derived conclusion); the dial only lets the student opt into having the tool *notice* structural/observed premises — a discovery aid, not a solution. **Phase B (next, needs operator sign-off):** the general L3 observed engine surfacing the currently-excluded similarity/bisector families "as they emerge" (relaxing ADR-208 for those under L3).
- **2026-07-04 (ADR-218) — the theorem feed reads coordinate-free construction ENTAILMENTS (circle radii ⇒ isosceles, two circles ⇒ kite), not only typed shape/relation facts; kite theorems 37/38 added.** Operator, testing the **kite of two intersecting circles** (`שני מעגלים נחתכים` · `AB` · `OP` · `PA` · `PB` · `OA=OB`; debug log): *"i have an isosceles triangle but no relevant theorems appear … added OA=OB and got 1 sentence but there are others … i also didn't get any kite theorems."* The "isosceles triangle" is emergent from two radii (never typed), and the figure is a textbook kite OAPB. Log-diagnosed root cause (not the reported symptom): the shape-evidence matchers read only **typed** shape/equal facts, never the circle **structure** the construction already encodes (`ctx.circles`) — so |OA|=|OB| (both radii of O) and the kite (two circles sharing A,B) were invisible though symbolically certain; and kite theorems **37/38 were never in the table** (a corpus-coverage gap). Operator directive: *"fix it broadly. we are chasing case by case here."* **Fix (class-level):** `isoscelesEvidence` gains an **entailed** source — a circle centre C + two members drawn as a triangle ⇒ isosceles apex C (|CX|=|CY|=r by the definition of a circle, no equality typed); a new `kiteEvidence` — declared kite OR two circles sharing two points X,Y with the four kite sides drawn ⇒ kite of centres+points; **37/38 tabled** (family `kite`, byte-exact from 07). The **DRAWN gate** (sides must be in the neighbour graph) keeps it honest — a bare circle doesn't flood #22; an undrawn kite doesn't surface 37/38 — so this is structural-certainty recognition, NOT the coordinate-coincidence detection that stays out of 6a. **Frames the three-level discovery model (the operator's "different levels of discovery"): L1 Declared (named/stated) · L2 Entailed (coordinate-free from the construction graph + hints — built here) · L3 Observed (numeric, via the sampled detectShapes/detectRelations — next, tiered `possible`, opt-in).** The clean line: L2 = certain structural entailment, L3 = numeric observation; a solution-path level stays a non-goal. Operator's exact sequence locked as scenario `two-circles-kite-surfaces-kite-and-isosceles-theorems`; matcher tests: radii-entailed #22 with no equality typed, bare circle no-flood, declared+entailed kite → 37/38, undrawn kite → nothing. **2179 tests green, build clean.** **Principle:** "help, don't reveal" is a line between *derived conclusions* and *given/entailed premises*, not between *typed* and *inferred*; a construction certainty (two radii equal) is a legitimate given even with no command spelling it out — the guardrail is the DRAWN gate + the L2/L3 (structural-vs-numeric) split.
- **2026-07-04 (ADR-217) — Appendix (practice-only / removed-curriculum) theorems A2–A6 + B3 tabled as SUPPORTING-ONLY; invariant type-`O` ⇒ `salience:'background'`.** Operator directive: *"i want to keep A2, A3, A4, A5, A6, B3 — they should never appear in the main theorems, only as supporting."* These six are 07 Appendix theorems (A2 intersecting chords · A3 two secants from an external point · A4 secant+tangent from an external point · A5/A6 the right-triangle geometric-mean & altitude-to-hypotenuse pair · B3 line-of-centres ⟂-bisects the common chord of two intersecting circles), all type `O` — until now structurally BARRED from `THEOREM_TABLE` (accepted only P/C). Fix: allow `O` in the table but encode "supporting-only, never main" as a **structural invariant — every `O` def MUST be `salience:'background'`** (integrity-guarded, `O ⇒ background`, replacing the old "no `O` tabled" assertion; the guard's row parser widened to read the `A2…B3` labels alongside numeric ids). `TheoremId` widened to `number | string` so the appendix rows key on their 07 label strings (never collide with 1–109 / 200-band, read as "not a citable bagrut number"); `detect.ts` sort tiebreak gained `idRank` (numbers before strings); App `theoremSel` + keys carry `TheoremId` (opaque key/equality only — never displayed numerically, so string ids fold into their family row cleanly). Six coordinate-free matchers, with **two precision fixes found by probing the real parse→replay pipeline (not guessed):** A3's near-point set **subtracts tangency points** (else A4's secant+tangent tripped A3 "two secants"), and B3 gates on **non-hidden** circles (the Thales external-tangent construction emits a `circle-circle-intersection` against a HIDDEN aux circle — unconditional would fire B3 on every drawn tangent). Judgment call noted: A2 legitimately surfaces (background/amber) on Q6's chords, A4 on Q7 — accepted per the "keep as supporting" directive. `integrity.test.ts` asserts the invariant + an A2–A6/B3 subset check; 8 new `matchers.test.ts` cases drive each through the full pipeline (He+En), asserting `background` salience + the A3/A4/B3 config separation. **2169 tests green, build clean.** **Principle:** a "never in the main list" rule belongs as a structural invariant on the data model (type ⇒ salience), checked by the catalog guard — not a per-row convention authors must remember.
- **2026-07-04 (ADR-216) — one general `shapeClassPresent` gate for shape→theorem surfacing, superseding ADR-215's bespoke 30-60-90 matcher.** Operator, right after ADR-215: “are these principles applied to all cases? we need architectural solutions and not case by case.” Audit: shape CLASSIFICATION (`classifyTriangle`/`classifyQuad`) was already architectural (orthogonal forced-property axes compose; 30-60-90 is one more axis test), but shape→THEOREM gating had TWO parallel mechanisms — quads on a general data-driven `quadClassPresent(ctx,kinds)` (add a theorem = a table row), 30-60-90 on a hardcoded `special306090`. Fix: collapse into ONE `shapeClassPresent(ctx,kinds)` (stated via a data `SHAPE_CMD_TYPE` map OR detected via `ctx.shapes`), delete `special306090` + `QUAD_CMD`, and make #33/#34 ordinary rows keyed to a `kinds` set. Attribution: the operator chose “vertex-touching anchor for all” (AskUserQuestion), but the LITERAL form regressed the ADR-212 diagonal lead (3 matcher tests caught it — drawing a side marked properties ● new; diagonal theorems stopped leading). Refined to the non-regressing general rule — **anchor to the shape's DEFINING fact: the stated command when declared (diagonal theorems still fold the later diagonal-drawing fact to lead by recency), else the latest vertex-touching fact for an emergent-only class** — which satisfies BOTH the 30-60-90 emergent ● new and the quad diagonal lead. No quad behaviour change; the next special-shape property theorem is now a table row, never new code. Full suite 2130 green, `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean. **Principle:** when a fix adds a second mechanism doing an existing general one's job, unify them; when the operator asks “is this architectural?”, name which altitude was already general vs the case-by-case seam, then close it.
- **2026-07-04 (ADR-215, session x73i1cpx) — a 30-60-90 triangle is its own detected shape sub-type, and theorems #33/#34 surface whenever one is detected.** Operator: `מקבילית ABCD` · `DE גובה לצעל BC` · `DC=2CE` ⇒ triangle CDE is right at E with hypotenuse DC = 2·CE ⇒ 30-60-90. Two reported gaps, TWO INDEPENDENT causes sharing the shapes layer (not one causing the other): (a) `detectShapes.classifyTriangle` had no **angle-magnitude axis**, so a 30-60-90 collapsed to generic `right-triangle`; (b) the special-triangle theorems #33/#34 (in 07) were **never in `THEOREM_TABLE`**. Consistent with the emergent-quad-property theorems, #33/#34 consume the shapes layer, so they need “detect shapes” on (coordinates are what distinguish 30-60-90 from a generic right triangle — the theorem feed itself stays coordinate-free). A plain right triangle or a 45-45-90 right-isosceles is NOT classified 30-60-90 and surfaces neither #33 nor #34; before `DC=2CE` (parallelogram + height only) the angle isn't forced so neither fires. Locked by scenario `thirty-sixty-ninety-triangle-detected-and-surfaces-33-34`, a `matchers.test.ts` block, and a `detectShapes.test.ts` classification test. **2130 tests green (+17), build clean.**
- **2026-07-04 (ADR-212→ADR-213) — quad property theorems are now **main** (headline), not the background fold (operator Turn-4: "the relevant quad theorems should be the main theorems, not additional"); the diagonal boost is re-expressed as **attribution** (drawing the diagonals folds their facts/objects into the diagonal theorem → leads by recency + ● new + highlights the diagonals), and the ADR-212 salience-override plumbing was reverted (static salience only).** Operator, on a parallelogram with the diagonals drawn: "the theorems about the diagonals should get more weight than others." A `TheoremMatch` may now override `salience`; `detectTheorems` uses `m.salience ?? def.salience`. The diagonal-property theorems (#46 parallelogram, #52 rectangle, #55/56 rhombus, #41 iso-trap) return `headline` iff `quadHasDiagonal` finds a drawn segment on a NON-adjacent vertex pair (geometric, keyword-free — works for `segment AC`, the `אלכסונים` LLM lowering, or an emergent diagonal), else stay background. Non-diagonal props (#43/48/50/39) unaffected. 2090 tests green. **ADR-213 (Turn-4):** the relevant quad property theorems are now MAIN (headline), not folded into the "additional theorems" background row — operator "for quads, the theorems that are relevant should appear as the main theorems." Every special-quad property is static `salience:'headline'`; diagonal weight moved from a salience flip to attribution/recency (drawing the diagonals makes #46/#52/#55/#56/#41 ● new and lead by recency, via `quadDiagonals`). The ADR-212 `TheoremMatch.salience?` override plumbing removed (one static salience source). 2096 tests green. **ADR-214 (Turn-5, session tzqfaub6):** operator dropped a height from D on a parallelogram (`מקבילית ABCD` · `DB` · `AC` · `DE גובה על AB`) and "no new theorem was selected." Root cause: a `foot` (what `גובה` lowers to; perpendicularity is structural, no `set-perpendicular`) was not a recognised right-angle premise. Fix (three operator-directed parts): (1) `rightAngleFacts` now counts a `foot`, so Pythagoras #28 surfaces on any dropped height, and #28 promoted background→headline; (2) theorem #3 "distance between two parallel lines is constant" added (fires when a foot drops between two STATED parallels — base = one parallel edge, apex on the opposite; coordinate-free via a new `parallelPairs` helper); (3) a new guiding-principle "concept" feed (`src/theorems/concepts.ts`), a sibling of the theorem feed — concepts are heuristics (set-it-up-this-way), NOT bagrut theorems to cite: string-slug id, NOT sourced from 07 (integrity byte-check governs theorems only), rendered in their own 💡 App section under the same Display toggle. First concept: right triangle → name one acute angle α, the other 90−α (same `rightAngleFacts` premise). `makeAttributor` factored out of `detect.ts` so both feeds attribute identically. Locked by scenario `height-in-parallelogram-builds-and-surfaces-theorems` + a matchers Turn-5 block. 2113 tests green, build clean.
- **2026-07-04 (ADR-211) — special-quadrilateral property families tabled.** Operator (ADR-209 mode): "when I show a quad shape, the relevant theorems should appear. in מקבילית which I just tested they don't." A drawn parallelogram surfaced only the generic co-interior #8; its defining properties were never tabled. Added 07 #43/46/48/50 (parallelogram), #52 (rectangle), #55/56 (rhombus), #39/41 (isosceles trapezoid) as type-P `quad` **background** entries, gated by `quadClassPresent(ctx, kinds)` — present when the class is STATED or DETECTED, the containment (rectangle/rhombus/square ⊂ parallelogram; square ⊂ rectangle∩rhombus) encoded in each family's `kinds` set. Converse recognitions (type C) stay OUT (Phase-6b gated prompts). 2090 tests green, build clean.
- **2026-07-04 — Same trapezoid figure: background theorems must be justified by the configuration, not a precondition ([ADR-210](06-decisions.md#adr-210)).** Operator (next manual-test on `טרפז ABCD חסום במעגל`, after ADR-209 added the config theorems): "the attached theorems are not relevant or not strong enough to appear. Perhaps we need a rule that **background will show only if the shape itself justifies it. here there is no triangle and no line between the parallel lines. זוית מרכזית should appear only if the center is given**." **Root cause (not a patch):** several matchers keyed off a *necessary precondition* rather than the theorem's *actual premise configuration* — a stated parallel pair isn't a transversal cut; ≥3 concyclic points isn't a triangle; a circle drawn without a named/used centre doesn't "give" the centre. **Fix — three reusable coordinate-free gates, each mapping a theorem to its own literal premise:** (1) **transversal gate** on #4/#6 — `hasTransversal(ctx)` = a stated/detected **triangle** OR an explicit **`line`** object cuts the parallels; **#8 untouched** (a trapezoid leg IS its own same-side transversal, so co-interior stands on the parallels alone). (2) **centre-given gate** on the central-angle family #92/#97/#98/#99 — `centerGiven(ctx,circle)` = centre **named** (`!autoCenter`) OR **used** (`neighbors[center]` non-empty); this **mirrors the renderer's FR-RN-8 show-centre rule** in `scene.ts`, so "theorem speaks about the centre" and "centre is drawn" share one signal. Threaded via a new `autoCenter` field on `MatchCtx.circles` (`context.ts`). (3) **triangle gate** on #84 only ("every *triangle* has a circumscribed circle") via `circumTriangle`; #91 ("through three *points*…") **Am. (same session):** the operator ruled it near-useless ("no use in questions I remember") → re-gated to a `circumcircle` CONSTRUCTED through 3 points (`circle through A B C`, `circumscribed circle of…`), dormant in every `חסום במעגל` inscribe (points placed ON a pre-existing circle), live only for the rare explicit circumcircle; Q5/Q7/Q6 corpus drop #91. **Corpus tightened, not special-cased:** Q6 step 3 (`DE is a chord` — a diameter+chord figure with NO triangle) dropped #84; the ADR-208 corpus had encoded the older member-count rule, so applying the operator's principle made it consistent corpus-wide. The operator's `טרפז` now surfaces {8,87,94,102,201}+#91, with 4/6/84/92/97/98/99 suppressed. `matchers.test.ts` +5 ADR-210 cases; Q5/Q7 unchanged (named circles, stated triangles). **Full suite 2038 green (+5); `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean.** **Principle to carry forward:** when a matcher looks like it keys off a precondition (a count, a lone relation), ask whether the theorem's *configuration* is actually present — a green corpus that encoded the looser rule is itself a candidate to tighten.
- **2026-07-04 — First operator manual-test of the 6a feed → configuration-specific theorems for an inscribed trapezoid + the parallels family pulled forward ([ADR-209](06-decisions.md#adr-209)).** Operator (screenshot of `טרפז ABCD חסום במעגל`): "I want to understand better the logic of showing a theorem. in this case, non of the theorems shown are relevant" — the feed showed only the GENERIC inscribed-polygon theorems (99/102 + 84/91/97/98 background) and none of the four the configuration announces: cyclic-quad opposite-angles=180, co-interior angles between the parallel sides, equal-chords→equal-arcs, "a trapezoid inscribed in a circle is isosceles". **Root cause (not a patch):** every circle matcher keyed off raw circle-member COUNT (`members.length ≥ N`), so any inscribed polygon read as generic points-on-circle — blind to WHAT shape the points form or to the trapezoid's stated parallel sides. **Fix — configuration-recognizing matchers, general not special-cased:** **#87** (cyclic quad, opposite angles 180°) gated on a real quad command (`quadrilateral`/4-`polygon`) whose vertices are all concyclic (so Q5's arc-midpoint 4th concyclic point never trips it); the **parallels family #4/#6/#8** (alternate/corresponding background, co-interior headline) gated on STATED parallels — a `set-parallel` OR any parallel-sided shape command (trapezoid/parallelogram/rectangle/rhombus/square); **#94 broadened** (`equalChordFacts`) to any equal-chord evidence (arc-midpoint, two stated-equal chords, or an inscribed trapezoid's legs); and a **new #201** "an inscribed trapezoid is isosceles" — NOT an atomic bagrut theorem (derivable from #87+#8+#40), so it lives in a new **Appendix C — cyclic-configuration corollaries** of [07](07-theorem-reference.md) with a **200-band id** (composed teaching statements, kept out of the official 1–109 numbering but tabled/byte-checkable; the integrity parser already accepts any `\d+`/type-P row → zero test-infra change). All matchers stay coordinate-free (facts + `circles`), so the feed still runs live. **Operator rulings (durable):** (1) the isosceles lemma + #94 are legitimate to surface — they are NOT the forbidden derived-premise set {68/69/70/71/76}, which is still structurally excluded; (2) pull the parallels family (was slated for 6b) FORWARD now. **Working mode established: during feature debug the operator NAMES theorems a configuration should surface, and each becomes a PERMANENT table entry (+ a reference row if new) — exactly like an ADR.** Recorded here so it travels. Q5–Q7 corpus unchanged (no quads/parallels → new matchers dormant). `matchers.test.ts` +4 (operator's exact utterance surfaces {87,8,94,201}; inscribed quad → 87 not 201; inscribed triangle → neither; standalone trapezoid → 8 not the cyclic ones); `integrity.test.ts` now 143 checks. **Full suite 2033 green (+29); `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean.** Open tuning knob for the debug marathon: whether generic 99/102 should DEMOTE to background once a more specific cyclic-quad theorem fires (left headline for now).

- **2026-07-04 — Phase 6a BUILT: the live theorem-surfacing feed ([ADR-208](06-decisions.md#adr-208)).** The pure spine `src/theorems/` — `detectTheorems` folds an authored `THEOREM_TABLE` over a coordinate-free `MatchCtx` (facts + dependency graph + symbolic hints `circles`/`neighbors`/`tangents`; no `replay`/`evaluate`, so it runs LIVE). The no-reveal boundary is **structural**: the derived-premise theorems (similarity 68/69/70/71, bisector-ratio 76) are simply not in the table, so they can't surface. Two axes tame the flood: **salience** (headline listed individually vs background folded per family) and **tier** (green `certain` / amber `possible`); each entry attributes to the latest completing fact and marks `● new` on the current step. **Two root fixes over the corpus (never patch):** (1) a stated diameter has two lowered forms — a `diameter` command OR a `set-collinear` through the circle centre — both recognised by `statedDiameterFacts`, so 103/104 gate on a real stated diameter in any phrasing, never a bare 90°; (2) a tangent has two construction paths — a `via:'tangent'` line spec OR the Thales aux-circle (a hidden through-circle passing through the target's centre, intersected with it) — both fingerprinted geometrically by `tangentPoints`, so the tangent family fires however the tangent was built. **UI:** a live "משפטים רלוונטיים" feed in the control column (tier dots, ● badges, per-family background fold, click-a-row → highlight the theorem's PREMISE objects), a Display-options toggle beside "show measures". Computed in the App via `useMemo` (not a store action — it's coordinate-free/cheap, unlike the sampled `detectShapes`); emergent-shape triggers ride the "detect shapes" layer when on. **Tests:** `integrity` (118 — byte-exact bilingual catalog, type, not-`O`, forbidden ids excluded), `matchers` (real pipeline), `corpus` (Q5–Q7 step-gated `expectSurfaced ⊆ feed` + `mustNotSurface ∩ feed = ∅`). Full suite + `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean. **6b next:** B-series booklet corpus + parallels/median/midsegment + similarity/bisector as *converse-recognition* prompts (gated derived-side).

The plan's core: the **stated-vs-derived principle** (a theorem surfaces because its PREMISE is instantiated by stated facts; anything the engine derived stays behind Phase 9 Reveal — extends ADR-038 to every surface: trigger, text, highlight, ordering); a **no-reveal ladder** (general catalog statement, never the student's letters; click-highlight = premise objects only; no marks ever added); **relevancy = tier → recency → salience** with a headline/background salience split (triangle-basics fold into a collapsed family row) + per-step caps; detection is a **pure derivation** (no stored feed state — attribution/new-badges/undo all fall out of recompute); code home `src/theorems/` (engine untouched). v1 ≈ 40 theorems, circle block first. Gates incl. a **corpus gate** (Q1–Q7 must surface the ids the official solutions cite) and **no-reveal invariants** (buildScene byte-identical with detection on; no student label in any feed string). Slices 6a/6b/6c (~5–7 days). **Awaiting operator: 4 open decisions** (live-feed sign-off; `possible` tier in v1 or defer; converse footing; the Q1–Q7 expected-theorem ground truth). NOT started — plan only.
