- **2026-07-02 — A ∥/⟂ constraint on a zero-length operand ('BB') froze the solver; now rejected up front ([ADR-202](06-decisions.md#adr-202)). COMMITTED (26efa93) + PUSHED.** Operator (manual test, dev server): "take a look at the latest test — something there freezes and doesn't reach a solution." **Reconstructed from `logs/debug-log.jsonl` session `wetjqgsj`:** two free-radius circles → `C על מעגל P` → `CA משיק למעגל O בנקודה A` (valid tangent → `set-perpendicular(O,A,C,A)`) → `CA` → `D על מעגל O` → **`BB משיק למעגל P בנקודה B`**. The last tangent is named by the DEGENERATE line "BB" (one point repeated), so the parser emitted `set-perpendicular(a:P,b:B,c:B,d:B)` — the operand B→B is a zero-length vector, so the ⟂ residual is NaN. **Isolated with a throwaway repro:** a SINGLE `replay` with just that degenerate ⟂ took **4.4 s** (base without it = 9-19 ms) and ended in a bogus `over-constrained: OA ⟂ CA cannot hold` (the VALID earlier tangent, dragged in as a co-driver); the B-redefinition alone was 19 ms → the ⟂ is the culprit. `applyStep` evaluates `next` then `recruitFreeDofs` re-evaluates, both chasing the NaN over every free DOF (2 free radii + on-circle θs) → the churn; the app's config-search loop (`firstSatisfyingSeed`/`findValidConfig`) runs that 4.4 s replay many times → UI FREEZE. **Root fix (ADR-202, `src/engine/step.ts`):** a cheap `degenerateConstraintError(cmd)` guard beside `circlesTangentError` — a `set-perpendicular`/`set-parallel` whose operand has identical endpoint ids (`a===b` or `c===d`) is rejected BEFORE any evaluate, keeping the prior figure, with a clear message (`⟂ needs two distinct points on each side — "BB" is a single point, not a segment`). 4.4 s → ~15 ms. Fixes the CLASS at the earliest boundary (any source: parser typo, LLM, `AA ⟂ BC`), never rejects a well-formed ∥/⟂ (the valid `OA⟂CA` of the same session passes). Scoped to the operand-segment direction constraints; a sibling `set-angle` degenerate-arm case is noted in the ADR but not pre-emptively added (no repro, ADR-052 anti-speculation). The general 'any slow single replay freezes the config-search loop' is the separate Phase E / STO-2 wall-clock-deadline item — this removes the pathological input that triggered it. Locked by scenario `degenerate-tangent-line-fails-fast-no-freeze` (the operator's exact 6-step sequence) + unit test `src/engine/__tests__/degenerate-constraint.test.ts` (⟂ `c===d`/`a===b`, ∥ zero operand rejected <500 ms; well-formed ⟂ still accepted). **Full suite 1795 green (+2); `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean.**

- **2026-07-02 — Hardening plan Phase D complete (engine anti-pattern: kind-whitelist duplication). COMMITTED (26efa93) + PUSHED.** Operator asked to do Phase D (the two ENG items) after being told plainly it's preventive plumbing, not user-visible payoff. **D1 / [ADR-200](06-decisions.md#adr-200):** `evaluate`'s coupled-solve cycle detector (`resolveDriven`/`dependsOn`) walked the object graph with `PT_FIELDS`, a hand-scraped string list of point-id field names that silently dropped `to`/`toward`/`line`/`line1,2`/`circle1,2` edges and never chased line/circle specs — so a solved-on-segment point coupled to another THROUGH such an edge went undetected → topological deadlock → false "unresolved dependencies" instead of numeric routing. Replaced with a single **exhaustive** `objectParents(o)` in `types.ts` (a `switch` over every GeoObject kind + a `never` guard, so a new kind is a compile error). Decided AGAINST the plan's literal "reuse `pointParents`" — `pointParents` is a point-only DOF-walk policy that deliberately stops at on-circle/line carriers (returns []), so it too would miss the line∩circle case; a new complete walker is correct and leaves `pointParents` (and the recruiter) byte-identical. **Reproduced first** (repo rule): a coupled E⇄P figure through a `perp-offset`'s `to:E` edge errors under the restored `PT_FIELDS` and resolves after the fix — locked in `src/engine/__tests__/object-parents.test.ts`. **D2 / [ADR-201](06-decisions.md#adr-201):** a solver-driven `via:'free'` radius is baked into `positions` but the stored circle keeps its seed, so the renderer reverse-engineered the true radius via `pointOnCircleId` — a six-kind on-circle whitelist (the strongest "renderer is a pure consumer" violation; already bit once, ADR-144). `evaluate` ALREADY returns a resolved `circles` map with the true radius; threaded it through `Derived.circles` → `<Figure circles>` → `buildScene(opts.circles)`, took the radius from the published value (centre still from view-oriented positions — radius is orientation-invariant), and **deleted `pointOnCircleId`**. Direct `buildScene` callers that don't thread the map fall back to the object's declared radius (correct for length/through; the two ADR-144 free-radius render tests now supply the map, the intended path). New store-contract test: `replay(...).circles.get('circle-O').r` = solved 9 while the object keeps seed 5. Both fixes are the same kind-whitelist anti-pattern the point-kind `Record` (ADR-043) was built to prevent; no behaviour change for existing figures. Plan checklist D1/D2 = ✅. **Full suite 1790 green (+3: object-parents ×2, radius-dof store-contract ×1); `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean.** Remaining hardening: Phase E (store robustness) + Phase F (renderer/UX) — the user-visible payoff phases.

- **2026-07-02 — A base-less midsegment ("EG קטע אמצעים" with E on a side) is a 2-variant construct, cyclable ([ADR-199](06-decisions.md#adr-199)). COMMITTED (d877ddf) + PUSHED + DEPLOYED (static dist/ → httpdocs/geo-builder/, proxy untouched; live bundle index-Da6GjzLH.js, 200).** Operator: "EG was not drawn correctly. In this case G should have been placed on either side CB or AB with ability to alternate between them." **Traced to session `n6zuhw65`:** `משולש ABC` → `E על AC` → `EG קטע אמצעים` (NO parallel base). The log's `input` line shows it parsed to a bare `segment E-G` — the `midsegment` rule required a base (named triangle, or the ADR-196 figure-resolved base) and bailed, so the utterance fell to the plain-segment rule and G was left undefined. **Interpretation (confirmed with the operator — a midsegment joins two MIDPOINTS):** E is the midpoint of AC, G the midpoint of one of the two OTHER sides — AB (⇒ EG ∥ CB) or CB (⇒ EG ∥ AB) — and WHICH side is genuinely unstated (ADR-052), so it must be a cyclable alternative, not a fixed default. **Root fix (ADR-199):** a `midsegmentBaseless` parser branch resolves which endpoint (E) already rides a triangle side from a NEW `ctx.onSegment` hint (`E → [A,C]`, derived in `buildParseCtx` from the figure's `on-segment` objects — same category as `neighbors`/`parallels`), resolves the third vertex R via `ctx.neighbors` (unique common neighbour of the side's endpoints), requires the other endpoint (G) fresh, and emits a `shape-variant` of a NEW `midsegment` shape `[P,Q,R,E,G]`. `expandShapeVariant` is refactored to DISPATCH by shape (kite/isosceles path byte-identical) → `expandMidsegment` emits `set-equal(P,E,E,Q)` (pins E to the midpoint, driving the free on-segment point per ADR-033) + `midpoint(G,…)` on PR (variant 0) / QR (variant 1) + `segment(E,G)`. `VARIANT_COUNT.midsegment = 2`, so the store's `cycleVariant` / "show another configuration" and `variantConfigs` sampling treat it exactly like a kite axis / isosceles apex — no per-construct wiring. Chose the variant mechanism over defaulting G to one side (that would silently assert an unstated given — the ADR-052 cardinal sin) or a bare segment. **Design check with operator:** asked whether declaring the midsegment should PIN E (placed as a free point) to the exact midpoint — confirmed yes (it's definitional). Verified end-to-end through parse→replay AND the store `cycleVariant` path (V0 G on AB, cycle → V1 G on CB; E always the midpoint; equal halves AE=CE, AG=BG surface). ADR numbered 199 because a concurrent session took 197/198 (right-isosceles-triangle badge + emergent-quad straight-vertex gate). Locked by scenario `baseless-midsegment-places-G-on-a-side-and-alternates` + doc index. **Full suite 1787 green (+1 scenario); `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean.**

- **2026-07-02 — A midsegment named AFTER the triangle resolves its triangle from the figure ([ADR-196](06-decisions.md#adr-196)). COMMITTED (d877ddf) + PUSHED + DEPLOYED (with ADR-199, same dist/ deploy).** Operator: "Is קטע אמצעים supported? … it should be supported. When I select show equal sides and angles, no equal side and no equal angles are shown." **Traced to session `z5dkmbla` in `logs/debug-log.jsonl`:** `משולש ABC` then, in a LATER step, `GE קטע אמצעים מקביל ל AB`. The log's `input` line shows it parsed to `[segment G-E, segment A-B, set-parallel]` — a **plain parallel segment, not the midsegment macro** (no midpoints). **Verified the engine's relations layer is NOT at fault:** built the correct midsegment (`triangle` + two `midpoint`s + `segment`) directly and `detectRelations` surfaces AG=CG, BE=CE and the corresponding equal angles — via BOTH `detectRelations` and the store's `viewRelations`/`detectRelationsAcross` path. So the symptom ("no equal sides shown") is a downstream consequence of the utterance never building as a midsegment. **Root cause:** the `midsegment` rule required the triangle NAMED in the SAME utterance (`/(?:triangle|משולש)\s*ABC/`); the student named the triangle in an earlier step (the app's PRIMARY incremental flow — CLAUDE.md "building the figure up one step at a time"), so `triM` was null, the rule bailed, and `parallelConstraint` (which runs later but now the only matcher) claimed it → a free parallel segment with no equal halves. **Fix (root, [ADR-196](06-decisions.md#adr-196), `src/parser/parse.ts`):** `midsegment` restructured to require only the base match; the triangle is either named in-utterance OR resolved from the figure via `ctx.neighbors` — the apex is the unique vertex adjacent to BOTH base endpoints (0 or ≥2 common ⇒ fall through, no guess). Same context-inference channel `altitude` (`ctx.parallels`) and the single-vertex angle already use, so it's consistent, not a special-case. `GE קטע אמצעים מקביל ל AB` (triangle drawn earlier) now decomposes to `midpoint(C,A)=G`, `midpoint(C,B)=E`, `segment GE`, and the equal halves + corresponding angles surface in the relations layer. Locked by scenario `incremental-midsegment-resolves-triangle-from-figure` (the operator's exact 2-step sequence; asserts AG=CG, BE=CE, GE∥AB, and the relations detected) + doc index. Parser/named-shapes suites unchanged (the in-utterance named form still parses). **Full suite 1782 green (+1 scenario); `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean.**

- **2026-07-02 — Sibling-bug audit of ADR-193/194 → the givens verifier is now the backstop for on-segment membership ([ADR-195](06-decisions.md#adr-195)). COMMITTED (8d59e55, concurrent session) + PUSHED; now DEPLOYED live via the ADR-196–199 dist/ build (d877ddf).** Operator asked to "not just fix the root cause but scan to see if similar areas would have similar bugs so we fix them as well." Ran two read-only engine audits (best-effort-accept class + carrier-domain-escape class). **Findings:** (a) ADR-193's `evaluate` re-check already GENERALISES — it re-verifies every driven-carrier kind, so the other best-effort-discarding spots (`resolveFreeDriven`, `resolveMixedCarriers` fallback, single-carrier no-root) are all caught by it; no new fix needed. (b) other `setCarrierVals` carriers (on-circle θ, rotated angle) are PERIODIC — any value is valid, not an escape; perp-offset/scaled-offset one-sided clamps are possible over-restriction (no repro) → left untouched, no speculative patch. (c) **the real gap: `verify.ts` checked on-CIRCLE membership but had NO on-SEGMENT membership check** — a point declared "on AB" that landed off it (t∉[0,1]) by any path was silently green (the exact oversight that let ADR-194 through). (d) adding that check surfaced a latent MODELLING bug: the external-secant rule created the outside point E at `t:1.3` but **omitted `extension:true`** — E was a "plain on-segment point" at t=1.3, contradicting "E on AB" vs "E on the *extension* of AB" (the operator's own distinction, raised mid-session). **Fixes (root, [ADR-195](06-decisions.md#adr-195)):** (1) `checkGivens` re-derives `point-on-segment` membership — plain → collinear + t∈[0,1], extension → beyond b (new i18n `figure.v.onSegment`); the general net for "a point slid off its object," independent of solver path. (2) An OPERATIVE-DEFINITION guard: only a point's LAST defining command is checked, so a point redefined by a later command that OUTPUTS it ("E על המשך AC" then "E על מעגל P") is verified against its real definition, not the stale on-segment one; a constraint that merely references the point (ADR-194 drift) still counts as checkable. (3) The secant rule now emits E with `extension:true`. **Regression question answered:** git confirms `evaluate.ts` was last committed 2026-06-25 — both ADR-193/194 solver bugs PRE-EXISTED (latent); this session's ADR-192 parser fix (making `DF:FC=1:N` parse deterministically) is what made those figures REACHABLE and surfaced them. The verifier check initially flagged 5 existing scenarios: 4 were the secant modelling bug (fixed by extension:true), 1 (`redefine-existing-point-onto-circle`) was the superseded-command false positive (fixed by the operative-definition guard) — none were real geometry regressions. Locked by `verify.test.ts` "on-segment membership" block (5 cases). **Full suite 1776 green; `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean.**

- **2026-07-02 — A plain on-segment point stays WITHIN its segment (t∈[0,1]); the joint solver can't slide it onto the extension ([ADR-194](06-decisions.md#adr-194)). COMMITTED (8d59e55, concurrent session) + PUSHED; now DEPLOYED live via the ADR-196–199 dist/ build (d877ddf).** Operator manual test (session `wdrfq1wf`, sibling of the ADR-193 figure): square ABCD, E on AB, F on DC with `DF:FC=1:10`, then `EF ∥ BC`. Reported "E on AB is violated as well as F on DC." **Reconstructed through parse→replay:** the parallel IS satisfiable in-segment (E slides on AB to F's x, both at t=1/11), but E and F came back at **t=−1/9 — OUTSIDE their segments, on the extension beyond A and D** (green, `violations:[]`). **Root cause:** the distance-based ratio `|DF|=0.1·|FC|` has TWO roots — internal t=1/11 AND external t=−1/9 (both are |DF|:|FC|=1:10) — and the multi-carrier joint solver (`resolveMixedCarriers`) landed on the external one because `setCarrierVals` HARD-clamped an EXTENSION on-segment point (t≥1.02, ADR-105) but wrote a PLAIN on-segment point's t **unclamped** (comment even said so), so the unbounded search slid a point declared "on the segment" off onto its line. The single-carrier 1-D path was already safe (`drivenRoots` bounded to [0,1]) — only the joint path leaked. **Fix (root, [ADR-194](06-decisions.md#adr-194), one line in `setCarrierVals`):** a plain on-segment point lives between its endpoints by definition — clamp `t` to [0,1] (mirroring the extension clamp). `setCarrierVals` feeds both the cost landscape and the final write, so out-of-segment placements score at the clamped boundary (internal-root constraint unsatisfied) and the solver moves the figure's OTHER DOFs, landing t=1/11; E,F now inside their segments with `DF:FC=1:10` + `EF ∥ BC` both holding. Completes the ADR-105 pair (extension pinned OUTSIDE, plain pinned INSIDE). General for the class (any dual-root distance/ratio/equal driving an on-segment carrier through the joint solver). Locked by scenario [`on-segment-point-stays-within-its-segment`](../src/__tests__/scenarios.test.ts) + doc index. **Full suite 1776 green (was 1775; +1 scenario), no regressions from the clamp; `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean.**

- **2026-07-02 — `evaluate` now verifies DRIVEN constraints: an unsatisfiable driven system is over-constrained, not a silently-accepted best-effort ([ADR-193](06-decisions.md#adr-193)). COMMITTED (8d59e55, concurrent session) + PUSHED; now DEPLOYED live via the ADR-196–199 dist/ build (d877ddf).** Operator manual test (session `vpt763yn`): square ABCD → `F על CD` → `DF:FC=1:4` → `E על AB` → `FE אנך ל BC`. Reported "2 major issues: (1) F moved and should not, its location is set by the ratio; (2) the line drawn was parallel and not perpendicular as asked." **Reconstructed through parse→replay:** the parse was correct (`set-ratio(D,F,F,C,k=0.25)`, `set-perpendicular(F,E,B,C)`). The ratio ALONE places F correctly at t=0.8 (F=(1,5)). But `FE ⟂ BC` is **geometrically impossible** — F on the top edge (y=5), E on the bottom edge (y=0), BC vertical ⇒ `FE·BC` is a constant −25 with zero gradient. **Root cause (one defect, both symptoms):** the ⟂ drives E and the ratio drives F, both `on-segment-solved`; neither is a *check* constraint (only concyclic/length-radius are pushed as checks). `resolveMixedCarriers` can't satisfy the impossible ⟂, and its fallback `return solveFor(carriers,false).result` **discards `ok=false`**, returning a best-effort placement where the regulariser pulled F,E to the seed midpoints (F=(2.5,5): ratio broken; FE vertical ≡ ∥ BC). `setCarrierVals` clears the solve directives, so the driven ratio/⟂ are neither drivers nor checks, and `evaluateCore`'s final loop (which checks only `c.constraints`) returns **`ok:true`** on a figure violating both — only the givens-verifier caught it, as amber, after F had moved. **Fix (root, [ADR-193](06-decisions.md#adr-193), `src/engine/evaluate.ts`):** `evaluate` re-verifies every driven constraint (`drivenConstraintsOf` gathers each carrier's `.constraint`/`.solve.constraint` from the original construction) at the resolved positions with the same `isSatisfied` gate; an unsatisfiable driven system → `ok:false` (`over-constrained: … cannot hold`). So the ⟂ hard-fails, the store keeps the prior figure (F stays at t=0.8), and ADR-191 group-atomicity drops the FE scaffolding. The message names the whole CONFLICT SET (all driven constraints failing `isSatisfied`, joined by "and" — the tiny λ=1e-3 regulariser means a *satisfiable* driven constraint still solves to ~0, so only the real conflict fails the gate; residual-ranking is biased since a ratio's relative residual is unbounded while ∥/⟂ caps at 1) → `|DF| = 0.25·|FC| and FE ⟂ BC cannot hold`. General fix at the evaluate boundary (covers `resolveMixedCarriers`/`resolveFreeDriven`/single-carrier alike), consistent with `solutionAccepted` (same gate) so a solve it accepted passes unchanged. Locked by scenario [`impossible-perp-does-not-clobber-ratio-pinned-point`](../src/__tests__/scenarios.test.ts) (F stays at ratio t=0.8, over-constrained names the ⟂, no seg-EF, square intact, no verifier violation) + doc index. **Full suite 1774 green (was 1773; +1 scenario), no regressions from the core solver-acceptance change; `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean.**

- **2026-07-02 — A segment ratio `DF:FC = 1:2` is a proportion, never a silent equality ([ADR-192](06-decisions.md#adr-192)). COMMITTED (8d59e55, concurrent session) + PUSHED; now DEPLOYED live via the ADR-196–199 dist/ build (d877ddf).** Operator: "do we have a plan to address `DF:FC=1:2`? this is currently silently failing." **Traced to session `o2m8f0w8` in `logs/debug-log.jsonl`:** square `ריבוע ABCD` → `E על AB` → `F על CD` → `DF:FC=1:2`. The log's `input` line showed `source:"llm"`, `built:["DF = FC/2"]` — so the keyword-free colon ratio had **no deterministic rule** (`dividesInRatio` only matches keyworded `divides`/`מחלק`/`ratio`/`יחס`), escalated to the LLM, which correctly returned `DF = FC/2` — but that then hit `equalSegments`, whose regex matched the `DF = FC` **prefix** and silently dropped the `/2`, emitting `set-equal(D,F,F,C)` = a plain `|DF| = |FC|`. Green status, no error, WRONG geometry (1:1 where the student stated 1:2). The ADR-024/026 silent-half-parse class, in a form no existing guard covered (the divided RHS). **Two root fixes at the parser boundary (whole class, not the one input): (A)** `ratioConstraint` now reads a trailing divisor `= FC/2` → `set-ratio(k = n/(m·d))` (here ½), and a new `SEG_DIV_RHS` guard makes `equalSegments` bail on any `= CD/d` RHS (defence-in-depth vs. rule reorder, mirroring `SEG_RATIO_LHS`); **(B)** a new `segmentRatioColon` rule parses the bare `seg:seg = p:q` form deterministically → `set-ratio(k = p/q)`, offline, so it never escalates. Both forms now emit the identical `set-ratio(D,F,F,C, k=½)` driving F's free DOF on CD. Locked by scenario [`segment-ratio-colon-drives-division-point`](../src/__tests__/scenarios.test.ts) (the operator's exact 4-step sequence, `|FC|/|DF| ≈ 2`) + doc index. Backward-compatible (no trailing divisor ⇒ d=1; existing ratio/equal/segmentRatio tests unchanged). **1464 tests green (parser+scenario+engine slice; +1 scenario), `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean.**

- **2026-07-02 — An utterance is ATOMIC: a hard-failed group drops its scaffolding ([ADR-191](06-decisions.md#adr-191)). COMMITTED (8d59e55, concurrent session) + PUSHED; now DEPLOYED live via the ADR-196–199 dist/ build (d877ddf).** Operator: "check the latest test — it should have failed to create the EF line and indeed it gave a message but still drew the line, it should not." **Traced to session `8twmmb5r` in `logs/debug-log.jsonl`:** square `ABCD` → `E על צלע AB ב- 40%` → `F על DC` → `EF ו BC מאונכים`. The ⟂ is geometrically impossible (EF spans the square bottom→top, so it can never be perpendicular to the vertical BC). The parser lowers that utterance to a GROUP `[segment EF, segment BC, set-perpendicular(E,F,B,C)]` (FR-IN-7 auto-draws the ⟂ pair), and `App.submit` committed it via the ADR-104 deferrable-constraint path. Reproduced in-repo through parse→replay: `set-perpendicular` hard-failed with "cannot place F on segment DC so that EF ⟂ BC" (pending=false — a genuine contradiction), but the two scaffolding `segment` facts had status `ok` and **seg-EF was still in the construction.** **Root cause: `replay` folded each fact in a group INDEPENDENTLY** — a group's facts weren't atomic, so a hard-failed constraint didn't stop its group-mate scaffolding segments from committing. Not a keyword/one-input issue; structural, affecting ANY multi-command utterance (⟂/∥/=, inscribed-shape macros) on a hard failure. **Fix (root, [ADR-191](06-decisions.md#adr-191)):** the build loop is wrapped in an inner `runBuild(forced)`; after the in-order build + ADR-104 deferral + pending classification, any group with BOTH a hard-failed fact AND a succeeded one is POISONED and the figure is **rebuilt** with the whole group blocked (each member carries the group's genuine error). Rebuild, not object-deletion, so a segment SHARED with an earlier shape (seg-BC of the square) is still drawn by that shape (a naive delete would remove the square edge). Gated on `!pending`, so the ADR-104 deferral case (a constraint that resolves once later givens arrive keeps its scaffolding) is untouched. Verified: seg-EF no longer drawn, seg-BC + square intact, the "can't place F" message still shown, `violations: []`. Locked by scenario [`impossible-perpendicular-drops-its-segment`](../src/__tests__/scenarios.test.ts) (the operator's exact 4-step sequence) + doc index. The ADR-104 `pending-vs-contradiction` scenarios still pass (the carve-out). **1773 tests green (was 1772; +1 scenario), `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean.**

- **2026-07-02 — Created [docs/paper/](paper/README.md) for academic writing + theory (no engine change).** Operator wanted a home to "save and manage the academic paper writing and theory behind the development." Set up `docs/paper/` — a `README` (purpose + conventions: discuss → capture in `discussions/` → distill into numbered theory docs; claims grounded in `file:line`; refs verified before citing), `01-methods-and-mathematical-lineage.md` (the session's core output — every optimization/algorithmic method the engine actually uses, mapped to its citeable name + code location, the GCS/DOF-analysis/rigidity framing, what's genuinely novel vs. borrowed, ready-to-use Methods prose), `references.md`, `outline.md` (evolving paper skeleton), and `discussions/2026-07-02-optimization-methods.md`. Seeded from the operator's question *"are there known optimization methods used, or is the code invented?"* — answer: **not invented; a composition of well-established methods** (bisection root-find, derivative-free nonlinear least squares via Nelder–Mead + coordinate-descent + Gauss–Seidel, Tikhonov regularization → stability, closed-form analytic geometry; framed as Geometric Constraint Solving with Kramer DOF analysis + Laman rigidity). Grounded by reading `geometry.ts`/`solve.ts`/`evaluate.ts`/`sample.ts`. Also earlier this session: the talk deck [docs/presentation/geo-builder-algorithms.html](presentation/geo-builder-algorithms.html) (pipeline, DOF dependency-graph, algorithm listings, verification stack). Indexed from [docs/README.md](README.md) + the "Where memory lives" list above. **Docs-only; no code, no tests affected.**

Read the actual code to ground the verdicts. **Findings:** **N4** (busy indicators) and **N5** (error messages) are ALREADY implemented (distinct in-flight states in `App.tsx`; `humanizeError.ts` covers 15 error shapes He/En) → strike off. **N3** (synonyms) is already per-rule and centralizing collides with ADR-077 → not quick. Section-3 threads (R7, ADR-073, ADR-049, coord-campaign slices) are big/gated/proxy-dependent by nature. **Genuine quick wins, ranked:** (1) ★ **N6 dev step-through panel** — the operator's flagged top pick, and the replay backend already exists (`replaySession` in `src/validation/replaySession.ts`), so it's just a dev-only paste-and-step UI over the existing submit path; zero prod risk, high testing leverage; (2) **N1 first step** — mine `logs/debug-log.jsonl` for "error but image looks OK" cases + decide whether to escalate/amber on verifier violations (not only `not-handled`); (3) **ADR-052 DOF-honesty audit** — confirm nothing in `rawMovableDof` is missing from samplable `freeDofs` (esp. a circle's free centre, ADR-103). Captured as the prioritized index in `docs/14-backlog.md` (also linked from `docs/README.md`) so it isn't buried here. **Not yet started — parked for the operator to pick up.**

- **2026-07-01 — A trapezoid's height drops to the OPPOSITE PARALLEL BASE ([ADR-169](06-decisions.md#adr-169)). COMMITTED + PUSHED + DEPLOYED (commit `43ae0a3`).** _Client-only change (parser + engine helper); static `dist/` → `httpdocs/geo-builder/`, proxy untouched. Live verified: `index.html` → new bundle `index-kwz3JBqQ.js` (HTTP 200), proxy `/healthz` still `ok`._ Operator (this session): "take a look at the latest tests. I have גובה בטרפז which I think we should have covered. It works in a triangle." **Traced to session `sub2ys2a` in `logs/debug-log.jsonl`:** `טרפז ABCD ישר זווית` → `CE גובה בטרפז` escalated to the LLM and returned **not-understood**, while the same student then cleared and confirmed `ABC משולש` → `CD גובה` works (`foot(D, from C, a A, b B)` + `segment CD`). Reproduced in-repo through the real `parse → replay` path. **Root cause: the `altitude` rule resolves the "opposite side" with triangle-only logic** — an explicit "to BC", else a named triangle, else the two-other-points fallback, else the apex's *unique enclosing triangle* read off `ctx.neighbors` (two neighbours of the apex that are themselves joined). In trapezoid ABCD (AB ∥ DC) the apex C's neighbours are **B and D — a diagonal, not an edge** — so no triangle closes and the rule returns `null` → LLM → not-understood. The rule had no notion of a height in a quadrilateral. **Fix (root, not a keyword patch):** a height/altitude drops to the **opposite parallel base**. Added a pure engine helper `parallelEdgePairs(construction, positions)` (drawn segments + polygon edges → vertex-disjoint parallel pairs, e.g. a trapezoid's bases `[['A','B'],['D','C']]`) and a new `ParseContext.parallels` hint (the same category of figure-derived hint the parser already uses — `neighbors`, `circleMembers`), threaded in App's `parseCtx` + tests' `ctxOf`. In `altitude`, when no side is stated, `oppositeParallelBase(apex, ctx.parallels)` runs BEFORE the triangle inference: **exactly one** parallel pair whose *other* edge carries the apex ⇒ that partner is the base (C on DC ∥ AB → foot on AB, CE ⟂ AB); **zero** (a triangle — its `parallels` are empty, so the branch never interferes — or a leg-only apex) or **several** (a parallelogram's two parallel pairs → two valid heights) ⇒ null, so it defers per ADR-052. Rejected the special-case alternative (map "trapezoid + height" to "foot on the 1st–2nd vertices") because it would assume canonical ABCD labelling — an unstated given ADR-052 forbids — and wouldn't generalise. Locked by scenario `altitude-in-trapezoid-drops-to-opposite-base` (the operator's exact 2-step sequence — E on AB, CE ⟂ AB, all steps verified) + `phase4.test.ts` unit cases (named `CE גובה בטרפז` → foot on AB not the leg AD; unnamed `גובה מ-A בטרפז` → auto-foot on DC; a parallelogram's two pairs → not-handled/defer). Triangle altitudes byte-identical (empty `parallels`). **1681 tests green, tsc + build clean. Not committed/deployed yet.**

- **2026-07-01 — Admin dashboard: one PRODUCTION `submit` per user submission ([ADR-168](06-decisions.md#adr-168)). COMMITTED + PUSHED + DEPLOYED (commit `43ae0a3`).** _Client-only logging change; rode out with ADR-169 in the same `dist/`-only deploy (proxy untouched). Note: takes effect once the `/api/log` sink is live (ADR-146 deploy still pending); until then the cleaner event stream is simply what the SPA would send._ Operator, with a screenshot of the usage dashboard's "recent activity" table: a construction they'd entered themselves (`F ו E נמצאות על הצלע AB של מקבילית ABCD`) appears as TWO rows with the same 04:58 timestamp — one tagged `נותח (LLM)` (llm-built), one `חלקי / נשמט` (weak). "I know it worked; I assume the first is the engine sending to the LLM and the other the LLM succeeding, but only the LLM success should appear." **Traced the full path:** the admin dashboard (`server/admin.ts`) aggregates `events.jsonl`, written in PROD by `server/eventLog.ts` from the SPA's `logDebug` (`src/debug/sessionLog.ts`), which emits one `submit` per `kind:'input'` event. In `App.tsx.submit`, every submission logs exactly ONE `kind:'input'` and returns — EXCEPT the escalation path: when the grammar parses only partially (dropped a new label) or builds nothing, it logs a `weak` step and then **falls through** to the LLM, which logs the FINAL outcome. Both are `kind:'input'`, so PROD emitted a `submit` for both → two dashboard rows. The debug log confirms the pattern (a real utterance logs `parser … weak:*` then `llm …` ~2 s apart). **Root cause: `logDebug({kind:'input'})` conflates the DEV debug-trace step with the PROD analytics submit; the intermediate weak step is a diagnostic, not a user action.** Fix (client-logging only, no engine/parser/render touch): the two `weak` `logDebug` calls are tagged `intermediate:true`; a new pure `analyticsSubmit(event)` returns the lean submit payload or `null`, dropping non-`input` kinds AND intermediate steps; `logDebug`'s PROD branch uses it. DEV is untouched (returns before the PROD branch), so the full reconstruction trace still records the weak step. Chose the emission-layer fix over a dashboard-side dedup so `events.jsonl` only ever holds real submissions and every stat is correct with no per-consumer dedup. **Historical note (told to operator):** pre-fix rows in the deployed `events.jsonl` keep their duplicates — the fix prevents new ones; the dashboard's `rel=` release filter separates pre-/post-fix data. Locked by `src/debug/__tests__/sessionLog.test.ts` (5 cases: figure→none, final parser/LLM→one submit, intermediate weak→none, weak→LLM pair→exactly one). **1677 tests green, tsc + build clean. Not committed/deployed yet.**

- **2026-07-01 — The implicit-edge universe is GEOMETRIC, not a whitelist of point kinds ([ADR-167](06-decisions.md#adr-167)). COMMITTED + PUSHED + DEPLOYED (commit `6bef74c`).** _Client-only change (engine + renderer + i18n); static `dist/` → `httpdocs/geo-builder/`, proxy untouched. Live verified: `index.html` → new bundle `index-DUKQ-1pk.js` (200), proxy `/healthz` still `ok`._ Operator, with a screenshot (rectangle `ABCD`, equilateral triangles `BCE`/`DAF` inward, `EC ו DF נפגשים בנקודה G`, `H = חיתוך BE ו-AF`): "we keep detecting shapes/lines shown on the diagram but never defined by the user — the rhombus is detected, CDG's segments aren't highlighted right, ABH isn't detected. I want a **deep review** of the root cause since we keep playing around it. My view: if two nodes exist and there's a line between them (not necessarily defined by the nodes), include them in both cases." **Deep review, grounded by rebuilding the exact figure + instrumenting:** (a) DETECTION actually WORKS on HEAD for this figure — `detectShapes` finds ABH, CDG (isosceles) AND the EGFH rhombus, and all equal segs/angles are found (ADR-166's whitelist entry happens to cover this crossing kind); the reproducible symptom is the **highlight** — App's `shapeHighlight` matches only stored `segment` objects and `Figure` lights strictly by object id, so a sub-segment like `G–C` (a slice of `EC`, no object) is unhighlightable → rhombus highlights 0 segments, ABH/CDG light only their one rectangle-side edge. (b) The operator's "we keep patching" instinct is CORRECT and the ADR log proves it: ADR-162 built the implicit-edge universe as `pointNeighbors + onHostEdges + visibleLineEdges` where `onHostEdges` is a hand-maintained list of point KINDS allowed to split their host; ADR-166 had to ADD another entry (`onSeg` intersection) — the operator's own note: "the node-definition issue, **again**." **Root cause: the edge universe was derived from how a point was CONSTRUCTED, not from the geometry on screen.** Operator chose the **full geometric edge model** and steered it **engine-only (no renderer/build changes)**. Built ([ADR-167](06-decisions.md#adr-167)): `onHostEdges` deleted, replaced by `collinearSplits(c, samples)` — for every drawn segment/polygon-edge, any point COLLINEAR on it in every sample AND within its span in some valid config splits it, regardless of construction kind (sub-segments become first-class edges); `figureEdges` now takes `samples` and feeds the equal-segment universe, the equal-angle adjacency, and the emergent-cycle graph from one set. **Key subtlety (a first attempt REGRESSED the ADR-166 rhombus):** requiring within-span in EVERY sample is wrong — the sampler visits mirror configs where a segment-meet crossing sits past an endpoint (`t` ∈ [0.74, 1.62] across converged samples), so `collinearAll && withinSome` is the correct criterion (collinearity is forced; within-span is not). The topological edge set stays generous; the "forced in every sample" classifier still decides real shapes. The reproducible **highlight** gap (sub-segments unhighlightable) is genuinely renderer-side and was left as a parked follow-up per the engine-only steer. Locked by `collinear-splits.test.ts` (kind-independence + coincidence/endpoint negatives) + scenario `emergent-shapes-through-crossings` (the operator's exact 5-utterance figure — ABH, CDG, EGFH all detected + the equal-segment classes). **1667 tests green, tsc + build clean.** **Then (same session, renderer — operator: "i do want to have the shapes highlight" + "what to do with the show-similar-length case when the diagram becomes cluttered… the user will misread it? ideas?"):** ([ADR-167](06-decisions.md#adr-167) Am.) **(1) sub-segment highlight** — App's `shapeHighlight` now emits the shape boundary as point-PAIRS (`shapeHighlightEdges`), and `Figure` gained a `highlightEdges` prop that strokes each pair between its endpoints' oriented positions, so an edge that is only part of a longer drawn segment (`G–C` ⊂ `EC`, no object) lights up (the "CDG not highlighted" fix); `transform.ts` gained `toWorld`. **(2) hover-to-focus for "view relations"** (the clutter answer — operator picked "focus mode" and asked "can it happen when the student hovers the diagram itself, not a chip?" → yes, and it's the better interaction): the relations layer is now HOVER-DRIVEN — resting figure clean, hovering a side/angle on the CANVAS reveals ONLY its equality class. `relationAt(relations, positions, world, segReach, vertReach)` picks the class under the cursor (a segment by perpendicular distance; an angle by pointing INTO its wedge near the vertex), `relationsForPick` narrows the RelationsResult to that class, and a hovered length class also gets accent strokes on its members (reuses the highlightEdges overlay). Cursor→world via `transform.toWorld` + undoing the pan/zoom `<g>` (SVG viewBox is 1:1 with px). A one-line hint ("hover a side or angle to see what's equal") for discoverability. Pure pickers unit-tested in `relation-marks.test.ts`; the pointer wiring in `Figure`; engine untouched. _Open: the hover FEEL (coordinate accuracy, reach thresholds 10px seg / 44px angle) wants a visual check in the running app — logic is unit-tested + typechecks + builds, but pointer interaction isn't covered headlessly. Angle-hover uses a simple in-wedge + near-vertex heuristic; may want tuning._ **(3) corner value drawn once** — operator on the working hover feature: "the 60 appears twice." At a rhombus 60° corner the angle is forced under two IDs (`∠AFD` and `∠GFH`, since `H`∈`FA` and `G`∈`FD`), so the cross-sample angle universe enumerates two angles for one wedge → two "60°" labels. Fix (render-only): `relationMarks` keys a definite-angle value by its WEDGE (vertex + the two ray DIRECTIONS from the drawn positions) instead of point IDs, deduping the label; the equal-arc suppression is wedge-based too. Correct for the DISPLAYED config (the artifact comes from mirror/diverged samples where the collinearity breaks). Locked by a `relation-marks.test.ts` case. **1672 tests green, tsc + build clean.**
- **2026-06-30 — Two segments meet ON the segments, not the continuation + detect the emergent EGFH rhombus ([ADR-166](06-decisions.md#adr-166)). COMMITTED + PUSHED + DEPLOYED (commit `1d42eb4`).** Operator (bagrut Q9, session `doykqc2m`): "check my latest test and tell me why it could not create the textbook image. basically, when we say that 2 segments intersect, it needs to be on the segments and not the continuation of them." Diagnosed from the log + a repro: rectangle `ABCD` with equilateral triangles `AED`/`BCF` on the two vertical sides, then `AE ו BF נפגשים בנקודה G` / `DE ו CF נפגשים בנקודה H`. The figure built clean but was the **mirror** of the textbook — the equilateral apexes `E`,`F` were sampled pointing **OUTWARD** (E≈(−3,1.8), F≈(9,1.7), outside the rectangle), so segments `AE`,`BF` diverge and `line-line-intersection` (which computes the INFINITE-line crossing) put `G` on the **backward continuation** (param ≈ −1.02 on both). A 200-seed scan found a valid inward config at only **4 seeds (~2%)**, first at seed 62 — beyond the 40-seed auto-resolver, so it was unreachable. **Operator chose the full root fix.** TWO coupled root causes: (1) a plain `line-line-intersection` had NO requirement that the crossing lie within both segments; (2) the apex side is an unstated DISCRETE DOF (ADR-052) the continuous sampler could only flip by luck. Built: parser flags a plain meet `onSeg` (utterance has no `המשך`/extension and no `הישר`/`line`/`ray` — so an explicitly-extended figure like `המשך CA ו BD` is untouched); the verifier raises amber `figure.v.meetOnSegment` (loose tol) + `meetsRequirements` requires within (strict tol); and an **apex REFLECTION DOF** encoded in the seed's high bits — `reflectableFreePoints`/`reflectAnchors`/`withReflectMask` (engine, structural) + `applyReflections` (store; evaluates once to learn the anchor line, mirrors each masked apex's solved position across it, re-seeds the solver to the mirror basin). `firstSatisfyingSeed`/`findValidConfig` search the **targeted** mask (mirror exactly the failing apexes) first, then subsets × a small seed band; the store add-path also re-resolves on a within-failure. seed `< REFLECT_STRIDE` (2²⁰) ⇒ mask 0 ⇒ byte-for-byte unchanged, so every existing seed/test is untouched. The fix flips E,F inward and lands G,H within the segments (verifier clean). _Perf: the resolve takes ~7 s on this coupled figure (behind the thinking spinner) — acceptable, not great; a leaner per-replay would help but the figure is heavy regardless._ _Note: `reflectableFreePoints` also surfaces the rectangle's base vertices A,B (referenced in the triangles' equal constraints) so the chosen mask was 15 (reflect all four) — harmless, gated by the full `meetsRequirements`._ Locked by scenario `segment-meet-lands-on-segments` (the operator's exact 5-step sequence). 6 `phase4.test.ts` exact-command assertions updated for the new `onSeg` field. **Then (operator follow-up) — the emergent `EGFH` rhombus is now DETECTED (ADR-166 Am.):** "there is a מעוין that the shape detector didn't detect, probably because of the node-definition issue again." Three coupled causes fixed: (1) **node definition** — `onHostEdges` now splits an `onSeg` `line-line-intersection`'s operand segments (`G` ↔ `A,E,B,F`), so the `EGFH` cycle exists in the implicit-edge universe (the ADR-162 class, new node kind); (2) **wrong sampled config** — `viewRelations`/`detectShapes` replayed at seed 0 (the requirement-violating OUTWARD figure); both now use `firstSatisfyingSeed` (0 for any figure without a segment-meet/extension, so unchanged elsewhere); (3) **diverged samples** — the equilateral-apex solver diverges at ~2/16 seeds (coords ~1e8, `evaluate` still `ok`), poisoning the "forced in every sample" test → a shared `convergedSamples` outlier filter (drop bbox-diagonal > 50× median) in both detection layers. `detectShapes` now reports `rhombus:EGFH`; the scenario `check` asserts it. **1663 tests green, tsc + build clean.**
- **2026-06-30 — Right-triangle angle reseat + single-vertex angle + trapezoid-morph amber ([ADR-163](06-decisions.md#adr-163)/[164](06-decisions.md#adr-164)/[165](06-decisions.md#adr-165)). COMMITTED + PUSHED + DEPLOYED (commit `dc8516e`).** Operator: "`ABC משולש ישר זוית` forces C as the right angle; then `angle B = 90` refuses — wrong, and the workaround is renaming/reordering. Easy fix?" → **ADR-163:** the right-angle vertex is an UNSTATED default (ADR-052) that yields to an explicit angle — a `replay` pre-scan reorders the right-triangle ids so the explicitly-90° vertex is the structural right angle (same shape as the ADR-114 soft equal-pair; works either entry order). Operator then reported two follow-ups on the fix: (1) the triangle drew a "weird image" — TWO right-angle knees, because `angleMarkFor` read the un-reseated `ids[2]` (knee stranded at C while ∠B is the real 90°); fixed by reseating the MARK too (single knee at B, dedups with the set-angle mark). (2) a right trapezoid + `∠ABC = 90` silently became a RECTANGLE. **Operator chose allow-but-flag** (the figure is valid; ADR-157 only guards re-declaring a shape WORD) → **ADR-165:** the givens verifier raises `figure.v.trapezoidMorph` when a declared trapezoid's both opposite-side pairs become parallel (scoped to the trapezoid family; a general "specialise X→Y" detector deliberately deferred). Also **ADR-164** (asked during the fix): a single-vertex angle `∠B = 90` resolves its arms from the figure when the vertex has exactly two edges; with more, a NEW `ambiguous-angle` ParseResult variant asks for three letters (no LLM escalation). Locked by scenarios `right-triangle-explicit-angle-reseats-right-vertex` (+ a one-knee assertion), `single-vertex-angle-on-triangle-vertex`, `trapezoid-constraint-morph-flags-amber` (`expectViolations`), and `parser/__tests__/single-vertex-angle.test.ts`. **1662 tests green, tsc + build clean.** _Deployed same commit as the parked shape-badge work (ADR-161/162) + the right-trapezoid construct — static-only; proxy untouched; live verified (new bundle 200, Apache→proxy 400-on-bad-body, no live LLM call)._
- **2026-06-30 — Right trapezoid `טרפז ישר זווית` construct (closes the book's shape-vocabulary gap).** Operator: "we don't have טרפז ישר זוית — take a look at the geo book and see what else we might be missing." Fetched the book index (`themathbible.com/bagrut/5-yehidot/571/geometry`) and diffed its full shape list against the catalog: **the right trapezoid was the ONLY constructible-shape gap.** Everything else the book lists that we lack is **theorem-layer** material (central/inscribed angles, Thales' theorems, tangent/interior/exterior angle theorems) — the intentionally-gated Phase 6. The right trapezoid was *half-built already*: present in `detectShapes.ts` (the FR-SH shapes layer could already RECOGNIZE one), `shapes/shapeCatalog.ts` (book slug `trapez-yashar-zavit`), and both i18n files — but with **no parser rule to TYPE it**. Added the missing piece as an [ADR-110](06-decisions.md#adr-110)-style macro: `rightTrapezoid` = `trapezoid` (AB∥DC) + `set-perpendicular(AD⟂AB)` (forces the two right angles at A and D; non-implicit, so it draws the right-angle mark) — the constraint solver does the work, no new engine construct. **Ordering subtlety (the one trap):** `rightTriangle`'s guard matches a bare `ישר זווית`, so it would otherwise mis-grab "טרפז ישר זווית ABCD" as a 3-vertex right triangle — `rightTrapezoid` is registered before BOTH `trapezoid` and `rightTriangle` (mutually exclusive with `isoscelesTrapezoid`, which requires שווה שוקיים). Catalog entry + parser-coverage rows (He/En, plene/defective spelling, label-first) + a detectShapes classification test (builds via parse→replay, asserts `right-trapezoid` and NOT plain `trapezoid`/`isosceles-trapezoid` — proving the typed figure round-trips through the shapes layer). **1654 tests green, build clean.** _**COMMITTED + PUSHED + DEPLOYED 2026-06-30** (commit `dc8516e`)._
- **2026-06-30 — Detect IMPLICIT geometry ([ADR-162](06-decisions.md#adr-162), follow-up to ADR-161).** Operator tested the new shape badges (debug log session `177a8cfc`): `טרפז ABCD חסום במעגל` · `E על AB` · `ED מקביל ל BC` → "there is also a parallelogram which was not detected — why." Diagnosed by replaying the logged facts: the parallelogram **EBCD** (sides `EB`=part of `AB`, `BC`, `CD`, `DE`) has **no `polygon` object**, and the detector classified only declared polygons. The operator reframed it as the real root cause, general across all three layers: **detection only considered DECLARED objects, but a student sees implicit geometry** — their key second example: `AB` a diameter through centre `O` makes radii `OA`/`OB` (the two halves of `AB`), which "view relations / equal sides" never marked because the equal-SEGMENT universe was drawn-segments+polygon-edges only. **Root fix (operator chose the unifying fix + conservative emergent rule):** a shared implicit-edge universe `figureEdges(c)` = `pointNeighbors` + `onHostEdges` (on-segment/midpoint/foot ↔ host endpoints — yields `OA`/`OB`, `EB`) + `visibleLineEdges` — the SAME set the equal-ANGLE pass already used — now feeds (a) the equal-SEGMENT pass (`detectRelations`) and (b) a new emergent triangle/quad **cycle detector** in `detectShapes` (classify each cycle, keep only named special types, require simple-in-every-sample, 16-vertex perf cap). **This MODIFIES existing "view relations" behaviour** (more implicit equalities surface) — explicitly the operator's ask; flagged + approved via plan. Verified: EBCD parallelogram now detected (plus the genuine isosceles △ADE, since EBCD ∥-gram ⇒ DE=BC and iso-trapezoid ⇒ AD=BC ⇒ AD=DE), and `OA=OB` radii detected. Locked by `relations.test.ts` (diameter OA/OB class), `detectShapes.test.ts` (emergent parallelogram + a conservative no-generic-triangle negative), scenario `emergent-parallelogram-between-segments`. **1653 tests green, build clean.** _**COMMITTED + PUSHED + DEPLOYED 2026-06-30** (commit `dc8516e`, bundled with the ADR-163/164/165 angle work; static `dist/` → `httpdocs/geo-builder/`, proxy untouched; live bundle `index-BEwaRlEm.js` 200, Apache→proxy chain verified 400-on-bad-body, no live LLM call fired)._ The angle layer already used this universe, so angles were already covered; if the operator finds a specific failing angle case, revisit. **Then (same day) — badge interaction (ADR-161 FR-SH-3 amendment, UNCOMMITTED local work on top of the deployed bundle):** operator asked whether a chip should highlight the shape on the diagram (expected) vs. just link to the book (higher value). Resolved as BOTH: **hover a badge → highlight** the shape on the canvas (vertices + drawn edges among them, reusing the existing `highlight: Set<Id>`), **click → inline card** (name + definition + book link) rendered *below the badges*, replacing the figure-hiding full-screen Modal so the highlighted figure stays visible. UI-only, additive (App.tsx + i18n `shapes.close`); tsc + build clean. _**COMMITTED + PUSHED + DEPLOYED 2026-06-30** (commit `5cbc9ea`; static `dist/` → `httpdocs/geo-builder/`, proxy untouched; live `index.html` → bundle `index-D5mUHOHO.js`, page + asset 200, proxy `/healthz` ok)._
- **2026-06-30 — Shape-detection badges (FR-SH, [ADR-161](06-decisions.md#adr-161)).** Operator: "start working locally on the feature that shows the shapes cards." Clarified (it had been discussed before): as the figure builds, detect which **named shapes** it contains (kite/rhombus/isosceles-triangle/…) and surface each as a **badge**; clicking a badge opens a popup that **links to that shape's page in the geometry book** on the same site. Distinct from the theorem layer (Phase 6) and "view relations" — a lighter "what shapes are here, where do I read about them." Decisions taken with the operator: **on-press** (a "detect shapes" button like "view relations" — opt-in, keeps the pedagogy boundary since an *emergent* shape is a clue), and the popup's core is the **book link** (the operator pointed me at `themathbible.com/bagrut/5-yehidot/571/geometry`; I fetched the index and mapped all 13 shape→slug). Operator constraint honoured: **strictly additive — no change to existing behaviour without sign-off.** Built: `src/engine/detectShapes.ts` (samples across free DOFs like the relations layer — a self-contained sampler so `relations.ts` is untouched — and classifies each polygon/circle by properties FORCED across all samples → emergent shapes caught, coincidental ones rejected); `src/shapes/shapeCatalog.ts` (the versioned ShapeType→book-slug map); a `shapes` store slice mirroring `relations` (facts-keyed cache, auto-clears, excluded from undo); a button + badges row + Modal popup in `App.tsx`; a `shapes` i18n group (names + one-line defs + UI, He/En). Locked by `src/engine/__tests__/detectShapes.test.ts` (classification + emergent-kite + the negatives: free quad ≠ named quad, rhombus ≠ square) and `src/store/__tests__/shapes-slice.test.ts`. **1645 tests green, build clean.** _**COMMITTED + PUSHED + DEPLOYED 2026-06-30** (commit `dc8516e`; see the ADR-162 entry above for deploy details)._ **Out of scope (later):** theorem detail in the popup (Phase 6), n>4 polygons (no book page yet), theorem→book links (FR-REF-1 theorem surface).
- **2026-06-29 — Triage tooling (Option A) → production batch 2, DEPLOYED ([ADR-152..155](06-decisions.md#adr-152)).** Operator asked to improve the usage dashboard with a drill-down into not-understood / unexpected-result commands. Built **Option A** instead of a dashboard view: a **headless session-replay harness** (`src/validation/replaySession.ts` + `sessionsFromLog.ts`) that re-runs real `events.jsonl` sessions through the live parse→replay pipeline and classifies each step (ok / ok-amber / empty-collision / coverage-gap), plus a self-contained **HTML triage report** (`triageReport.ts` + operator `triageVerdicts.ts`, env-gated dump/html runners). Key insight surfaced: the dashboard's flat recent-list **interleaves concurrent sessions**, which is why "basic square not understood" looked inexplicable — it was a different user, and the failures were mostly **collisions with existing points** (re-declaring ABCD), not parser bugs. The triage verdicts gate what a fix-loop may touch (in-scope vs out-of-scope/noise), answering the operator's "how do we avoid fixing what isn't broken". Then the operator picked the in-scope items + two features to ship: **(ADR-152)** `name-center` reveals an existing circle's hidden centre — the **deferred ADR-148 #2**, now done (flips `autoCenter` off without clobbering the radius; `circleCenter` keyword-order-independent); **(ADR-153)** Thales `זווית היקפית נשענת על הקוטר` on an existing circle; **(ADR-154)** **generic incircle** — any polygon, flexing a quad/trapezoid to tangential (the original `חסום בטרפז` ask), all touch points equidistant from the incentre; **(ADR-155)** altitude-from-a-point infers the opposite side from the apex's unique triangle via `ctx.neighbors`. Each locked by a `production-feedback.test.ts` block + an end-to-end scenario; catalog updated. **1580 tests green, build clean. DEPLOYED** (static `dist/` → `httpdocs/geo-builder/`; client-only changes, proxy untouched — bundle verified live, SPA/JS 200, proxy healthy). Parallel session shipped the error-message UX (#4) + incircle tangency-points (ADR-151) in the same window. _(Still open: the dashboard drill-down/session-grouping itself — Option A is the diagnostic path; the dashboard UX is a separate follow-up.)_
- **2026-06-29 — FIRST production-traffic analysis → a safe parser batch ([ADR-148](06-decisions.md#adr-148)).** Operator: "look at the server usage — what text do users enter that the engine doesn't recognize." Pulled the live `events.jsonl` (ADR-146 analytics) over SSH: **57 unique visitors / 286 real submissions, one ~9-hour window, 100% Hebrew** (a class got the link). **31% of submits escalated to the LLM, ~19% failed outright.** Methodology note worth keeping: the dashboard's `weak` + `not-understood` buckets **double-count** — a parser-recognised-but-build-failed submit logs `parser weak:error` AND retries the LLM (`built-nothing`), so the same action appears twice; collapsing the pairs per-session is required to see the true funnel (61.5% parsed, 18.2% build-failed-in-context, 13.3% LLM-rescued, 6.6% total-fail). The genuine grammar gaps were almost all *phrasing* misses on constructs the engine ALREADY supports. Operator directive: **fix Tier 0–3, no major changes, don't break anything.** Shipped six small/additive fixes (a new rule only fires on currently-`not-handled` input, so it can't regress a working parse), each verified with real context + an anti-hijack check: **#1** definite chord `ה?מיתר` + descriptor tolerance (biggest cluster, 16×); **#5** perpendicular noun `אנך`; **#3** `AB קוטר במעגל` defines a circle from a new diameter; **#6** perpendicular/altitude FROM a point (`אנך מD ל AB`, `גובה מנקודה D`); **#7** new `dividesInRatio` (`G מחלקת את DC ביחס 1:2`); **#8** new `radiusSegment` (`OB רדיוס`). **Deferred #2** (`O מרכז המעגל` — name an existing circle's hidden centre): no engine command names a centre (rename is a STORE op outside the construction pipeline), so it's a "major change" the directive excluded — parked for a dedicated session. Locked by `production-feedback.test.ts` (27 assertions incl. anti-hijack guards) + end-to-end scenario `bagrut-chord-diameter-perp-session` (the dominant real student session — `violations: []`). **1512 tests green, tsc + build clean.** **NOT yet deployed** — code is ready; deploy per `deploy/README.md` (build → scp `dist/` → restart `geo-proxy`) when the operator says go. _(Separate, larger UX item still open: re-typing an existing shape reads as "couldn't understand" — 52× in the log — needs a no-op-on-redefine in the App.submit flow; not in this batch.)_

- **2026-06-28 — two future-feature ideas captured (no code).** Operator wanted to record two ideas so they aren't lost. Documented as deferred requirements only. (1) **Save/load a figure to a file** — [FR-HS-10](02-requirements.md) + US-14, scheduled into Phase 8. Design hook: the store's ordered fact list is the single source of truth and the figure is derived by `replay`, so a save = serialize the enabled/ordered facts (utterances + lowered commands + branch indices) + a small versioned header; load = replay them. Positions are never stored (recomputed on load) → compact, diffable, engine-change-robust; a loaded figure is indistinguishable from a typed one. Distinct from FR-HS-4 (auto local persistence) and FR-HS-6 (share-via-link). (2) **Deep-link detected shapes/theorems to the geometry book** on the same site (`themathbible.com`) — new [FR-REF-1](02-requirements.md) (Textbook references), deferred follow-on to Phase 6. Keys off the theorem-detection layer + the shared bagrut numbering; opt-in/quiet, points to definitions/properties (learning material, not the figure's answer). **Open item:** needs a stable shape/theorem → book URL(+anchor) map, which depends on the book exposing addressable pages. No tests / no engine change.

- **2026-06-28 — relations-layer: value-supersedes-arc + foot's 90° ([ADR-145](06-decisions.md#adr-145)).** Operator (production figure, AB diameter + DE∥BC + EF⊥AB): "∠DEC and ∠ECB show twice as equal" and "∠EFA is not shown as 90 — maybe the shape wasn't defined somehow?". Both real, both root-caused: (1) `relationMarks` drew an equal-angle ARC *and* a definite VALUE label on the same angle (∠DEC=∠ECB AND each =18.4°) — fixed by making a shown value supersede its arc (floating equal angles, e.g. isosceles base angles, still arc). (2) the relations angle universe is built from the neighbour graph, and a point ON a segment (F on AB, or any perpendicular FOOT) is only connected to its OFF-line neighbour — never to the host endpoints — so no angle at F was enumerated and the foot's 90° was invisible; fixed by `onHostEdges` (an on-segment/foot point contributes angle-graph edges to its host's endpoints, dedup-safe at the endpoints). The operator's "the shape wasn't defined somehow" was exactly right — F's host-line connection was missing. Confirmed offline on parseable equivalents (equilateral double-marked; altitude foot's square missing), both fixed; isosceles still arcs its floating base angles. Locked by `relation-marks.test.ts`. **Bundled the "show equal sides & angles" button rename in the same deploy.** **1450 tests green, build clean.**

- **2026-06-28 — two TANGENT circles carry FREE radii ([ADR-144](06-decisions.md#adr-144)).** Operator "take a look at the test I did. 2 tangent circles. why is 8 set there? is point M not fixed (when I did OM=4, M moved)? why did I get an error? seems some radius is fixed at 8?" — debug-log session `23vqi9u8` (built "שני מעגלים משיקים מבחוץ", then `OM=`/`OP=`). **First answered the diagnosis**: the "8" is not a radius — it is `|OP|`, the centre distance an external tangency forces to `r1+r2 = 5+3`; M is the touch point, a derived 0-DOF point at `|OM|=r1=5` (so it can't honour `OM=4`); the error was honest *given* the build. **The root bug (ADR-052 violation):** the figure was built with both radii **pinned** at 5/3 — the unnamed phrasing had no deterministic rule, so it escalated to the LLM, which emitted fixed-radius circles + `circles-tangent`. Pinned radii ⇒ `|OP|` rigid at 8, so the student's own `OP=4` was "over-constrained". **Root fix (operator chose "full fix"):** (1) `circlesTangent` now materialises the two circles with **free** radii (distinct seeds, `ifAbsent` so a *stated* radius is preserved) + handles the unnamed plural ("שני מעגלים…"/"two circles…") + an auto-named touch point — no LLM; (2) the engine builds external tangency as `coincide(M, hidden ~-witness)` with M=`radial-toward(c1→c2)`, witness=`radial-toward(c2→c1)` (residual `||OP|−(r1+r2)|`, forces external + reads both free radii), both radii marked **permanent drivers** (touch points carry no DOF, and `recruitFreeDofs` only widens a step's *own* new constraint — so a build-time check would never re-flex); (3) recruiter learns `radial-toward` (`circlesOfPoint`/`pointParents`); (4) verifier checks `|OP|=r1±r2`. Now `OP=4` resizes the radii (sum 4), `OM=4` sets r1=4, both clean; stated radii still honoured (|OP|=8); 20 "show another" seeds all stay tangent while r1 varies 2.2→7.6. Tests that asserted the old fixed behaviour were updated to pass ctx (mirrors the real app, where existing circles aren't re-created). Locked by scenario `two-tangent-circles-then-size-given-flexes-radii`. **1446 tests green, build clean.** **Amendment 1 (renderer, [ADR-144](06-decisions.md#adr-144) Am.1):** the operator then showed (screenshot, fresh-port 5180, debug log confirming `src=parser` + correct free-radius commands) that the figure STILL drew wrong — circle O at its SEED radius 5 with M floating *inside* at 3. The engine `positions` were correct all along (|OM|=3); the bug was in the RENDERER: `scene.ts`'s `pointOnCircleId` (which recovers a free circle's drawn radius from a point on it) didn't list `radial-toward`, and for tangent circles the touch point + witness are the ONLY on-circle points, both `radial-toward` — so it fell back to the seed radius. Diagnosis path mattered: the debug log proved the PARSE was right (ruling out cache/HMR after several false leads), then replaying the EXACT logged commands showed positions correct but `pointOnCircleId` missing the kind. One-line fix (add `radial-toward`); **lesson: a free-radius render needs a SCENE-level test (`SceneCircle.r`), not just a positions test** — every prior check (scenario, units) passed because they asserted positions, never the drawn radius. Locked by a scene test in `render/__tests__/lines-circles.test.tsx`. **1448 tests green, build clean.**

- **2026-06-28 — tangent-chord misparse fixed ([ADR-143](06-decisions.md#adr-143)).** Operator "the latest test isn't working" — debug-log session `vk346px4` (two intersecting circles O,P; tangent chord CB; then `המיתר AD במעגל P משיק למעגל O בנקודה A`). The figure built with every step **green ✓ and zero errors**, but the last step silently **dropped point D and the chord AD** and asserted a contradictory mutual tangency between the two (intersecting!) circles — a classic "no-error ≠ correct". Root cause: `circlesTangent` fires on "two `מעגל` tokens + `משיק`", but `במעגל P` here is the chord's *host*, not a second tangent circle. Fix: a new `tangentChord` parser rule (host circle before the tangent keyword, tangency circle + touch point after → both endpoints on the host circle + radius⟂chord + the segment) ordered before `circlesTangent`, plus a `chord`/`מיתר` guard on `circlesTangent`. Operator confirmed intended geometry (chord AD of circle P, tangent at A) via AskUserQuestion before building. Verified: D on circle P (|PD|=|PA|), ∠OAD=90°, verifier clean. Locked by scenario `chord-tangent-to-other-circle-at-endpoint`. **1445 tests green, build clean.**

- **2026-06-28 — Ambiguous-named-shape design ([ADR-138](06-decisions.md#adr-138)) + item #2's recruiter/conversion fixes ([ADR-139](06-decisions.md#adr-139)/[ADR-140](06-decisions.md#adr-140)); a parser+solver remainder PARKED.** The two "open items" the operator asked to confirm were both **proven real** first: (#1) the relations-layer false positives — an independent engine-free variety trace from the drawn config found ∠A(B,D) ranging 0.5°–114° across valid configs while both equalities hold, so the "definite angles" ARE false (see the confirmed 2026-06-27 entry below); (#2) the kite-tangent over-constraint reproduced exactly. **The kite design question** ("how to pick which sides are equal for `דלתון`/isosceles") was reviewed by a multi-agent panel and decided as **[ADR-138](06-decisions.md#adr-138)**: the equal-pair is a **discrete, sampler-visible VARIANT choice** (not a frozen soft default), unifying the kite (2 variants) AND the isosceles triangle (3 variants, migrating off ADR-114) in one release; item #2 is explicitly carved OUT (it's a solver problem, not a default-pair one). Then **Stage A of the dev plan** (item #2) was worked and turned out to be **four layered defects**: **(a)** the parser drops `נקודות B C D על מעגל שמרכזו O` membership (parses to just `circle O` — C never on the circle); **(b)** [ADR-139, FIXED] the recruiter's case (B)/(C) recruited a DECOY free DOF (apex A) that pre-empted the self-verifying redundancy cases (D)/(E) — fixed by verify-then-fall-through (a recruit only earns its `continue` when it actually makes the system valid); **(c)** [ADR-140, FIXED] `point-on-circle` on a constraint-DRIVEN vertex dropped its `solve`, so the conversion rolled back and the point never reached the circle — fixed by preserving the `solve`; **(d)** [PARKED] even with (b)+(c), the fully-membership figure (C,D both on-circle, both driving kite equalities, + 2 tangencies) does NOT converge in the joint solver — **0 of 24 seeds** (a valid 1-DOF figure; the recurring coupled-constraint convergence gap). **Diagnosis discipline:** a recruiter trace nailed (b) (the ADR-138 first guess of an `ancestors` under-recruit was WRONG — corrected in the ADR); a seed-sweep proved (d) is not a seed/auto-resolve matter. **Key consequence:** the operator's EXACT input now builds (over-constraint gone, B,D genuine tangents, |AB|=|AD|) but **only because (a) leaves C off-circle**, thinning the coupling enough to converge — so fixing (a) would EXPOSE (d). **Operator decision: lock (b)+(c) [both non-regressing, general — they fix the redundant-carrier + driven-point-on-circle classes broadly], park (a)+(d) as a COUPLED limitation, and proceed to Stage B (the kite VARIANT mechanism — the headline).** Locked by `engine/__tests__/driven-point-on-circle.test.ts` (ADR-140) + scenario `kite-tangents-redundant-equality-not-over-constrained` (ADR-139+140, asserts the over-constraint is gone + real tangencies; C-off-circle documented as the parked (a)/(d) limit). **Then Stage B — [ADR-138](06-decisions.md#adr-138) — B1 BUILT (2026-06-28, committed 89c05a3 was Stage A; B1 is the NEXT commit):** a `shape-variant` SymbolicCommand (`src/engine/shapeVariants.ts`) the kite/isosceles macros emit; `replay` expands it to the base shape + the variant-selected equal pair, with an explicit equality PINNING the matching variant (subsumes the ADR-114 isosceles soft default — now migrated); a `cycleVariant` store action + the "show another configuration" button cycles the pair (kite 2 axes, isosceles 3 apexes). seed-0/variant-0 = identical geometry → **zero regression (1437 tests green, tsc clean)**. Locked by `store/__tests__/shape-variant.test.ts` + the unchanged `named-shapes.test.ts`/parser-coverage. _UI-button cycling is wired (tsc-clean, mirrors the cycleAlt path) but the actual on-canvas click wants a quick manual check._ **Stage B2 DONE (2026-06-28):** `detectRelations` generalised to `detectRelationsAcross(constructions[])` (pool samples across the variant configs; `constructions[0]` = the shared universe) + `viewRelations` builds the variant alternatives (`variantConfigs`, bounded by the SUM of variant counts). A lone kite now reports **no forced equal-pair** (verified); non-variant figures byte-identical; `freeDofCount` unchanged (a discrete variant = 0 continuous DOF; a kite already reads 2). Locked by `relations.test.ts` (single-config forced vs across-variants not) + `shape-variant.test.ts` (viewRelations). **So Stage B (ADR-138) is functionally COMPLETE** — variant data model + cycle + explicit-pin + per-shape scoping + isosceles migration + relations coherence. **1441 tests green, build clean.** **Stage C DONE (2026-06-28, [ADR-141](06-decisions.md#adr-141)):** equality-recruited parametric carriers are now SAMPLED — `applySeed` perturbs a driven on-circle θ / on-segment t (about its current value, keeping the `solve`) under two gates: the figure is under-determined (`freeDofCount>0`) AND the carrier's constraint is OVER-recruited (`carrierCount > dofRemoved`); a small in-basin jitter so a fully-consumed `|AB|=|AC|` snaps back instead of flipping to its mirror root. The `DF=DB` figure now reports **0** forced angles (was 11); determined figures (square/equilateral) still report theirs (no false negative). **Finding:** the `dof-radius` "determined" example (`inscribed AB=AC` + `∠BAC=2∠CAD`) was itself UNDER-determined (freeDofCount 1; ∠BAC ranges 39°–67°) — the old test relied on this very frozen-carrier bug; corrected to a square, and the operator's original "5 DOF but every view is the same" was this same bug (now root-fixed, not symptom-fixed). Locked by scenario `equality-recruitment-not-forced`. **So all three open threads from this session — kite design (Stage B), item #2 (recruiter/conversion, ADR-139/140), and item #1 (Stage C, ADR-141) — are CLOSED.** **★ NEXT (resume here): the PARKED solver thread** — the kite-tangent (d) convergence (0/24 seeds on the full-membership figure) + parser (a) "B,C,D on circle O" membership-drop, a coupled pair to fix together; then **Phase 6 (theorems)**, the gated milestone. _Then a live manual-testing session (server on :5174) banked UX fixes: the relations layer draws a forced **90° as a right-angle SQUARE** (knee), not a "90°" number; the **givens-verifier messages are now Hebrew** (i18n key+params → `t()`, `figure.v.*`); and **[ADR-142](06-decisions.md#adr-142)** — a SHARED-ENDPOINT extension's side is inferred from the circle, so a reversed "המשך BD" (typo for DB) no longer cascades into a wrong figure (the bagrut-Q4 two-circles+tangent-secant figure; the operator's exact typo'd input now builds clean). Also confirmed (operator): a kite/isosceles correctly shows NO forced equal-pair in "view relations" (the ADR-138 free-choice behaviour — working as intended). **1444 tests green.** UI variant-cycling still wants a final on-canvas eyeball but the operator tested the figures live successfully._

- **2026-06-27 — ⚠️ OPEN / PARKED: suspected relations-layer FALSE POSITIVES from EQUALITY over-recruitment (a deeper sibling of [ADR-136](06-decisions.md#adr-136) Am.2).** *CONFIRMED TRUE 2026-06-28 — the definite angles ARE false: an engine-free variety trace from the drawn config (and a 4000-point random multistart) found ∠A(B,D) ranging 0.5°–114° across valid configs while `DE=EF` and `DF=DB` both hold. So this is a real bug; the fix direction below (perturb the equality-recruited carrier while keeping its `solve`) stands. Now tracked as **Stage C** of the [ADR-138](06-decisions.md#adr-138) dev plan.* **Symptom:** on the operator's tangent/secant figure, AFTER adding two equality givens the "view relations" layer prints MANY definite angle values, and it's unproven they're all genuinely forced. **Exact reproduction** (parse→replay, all steps `ok`): `משולש ABC חסום במעגל` · `המשיק בנקודה D והמשך AB נפגשים בנקודה E` · `AD` · `DB` · `נקודה F על AB` · `DE=EF` · `DF` · then **`DF=DB`**. BEFORE `DF=DB`: `freeDofCount`=3, `freeDofs`=[O,circle-O,A,B,C,D], `detectRelations().definiteAngles` = **[] (correct — ADR-136 Am.2 holds)**. AFTER `DF=DB`: `freeDofCount`=**2** but `freeDofs`=**[O, C]** only — A,B,D (on-circle) and F (on-segment) are all marked `solve ← "equal"` and frozen out of sampling, so `detectRelations` samples just O,C and reports **11 definite angles** (A(B,D)=19.4, B(A,D)=66.5, D(A,B)=94.2, …). **The smell (same ADR-052 class):** `freeDofCount` reads 2 but only **1** shape DOF (C) is actually sampled — 1 DOF is hidden among the frozen equality-recruited carriers. Two equalities (`DE=EF`,`DF=DB`) removed 2 DOF but RECRUITED 6 carriers (A,B,D,F,O,circle) and marked them all `solve`; the N−M residual DOF (N recruited > M removed) are frozen, so any angle that depends on that residual freedom looks "definite" when it isn't. **Why ADR-136 Am.2 does NOT cover it:** that fix only un-froze ORDER-only solves (0 DOF removed). An EQUALITY solve removes 1 DOF and IS partially consuming — the problem is *over-recruitment* (more carriers marked than DOF removed), not the order/region case. **Likely direction (unverified):** mirror how `applySeed` already perturbs a constraint-driven FREE-POINT (sample.ts line ~89: "one scalar constraint under-determines a 2-DOF point, so it keeps residual freedom; re-solving from a perturbed start gives a different valid config") — i.e. ALSO perturb an equality-driven on-circle/on-segment carrier while KEEPING its `solve`, letting `evaluate` re-solve. A fully-consumed single carrier just snaps back (harmless); an over-recruited one yields a genuinely different config (correct sampling). RISK: this changes sampling for ALL constraint-driven parametric carriers — must re-run the full suite (esp. relations + alt-config + dof tests) and re-confirm no determined figure starts wandering. **CAVEAT — not proven a bug yet:** a quick perturb-and-resolve probe was inconclusive (the regulariser pulled back to the same config), so it's possible the 2 equalities + tangent geometry genuinely pin A,B,D up to similarity+C. **First step at work:** prove it true/false — find (or fail to find) a SECOND valid configuration of the AFTER figure where ∠A(B,D) differs while `DE=EF` and `DF=DB` still hold (e.g. solve from many independent random starts, NOT a local re-solve). If a different valid config exists, the definite angles are false → fix per the direction above; if not, the layer is correct and only the DOF-cue/`freeDofs` mismatch (counts 2, samples 1) needs reconciling. **NOTE:** the operator did NOT complain about this AFTER state (they accepted "new angle values were found"); their original complaint was the BEFORE state, which IS fixed (ADR-136 Am.2). This is a proactive flag, lower priority than a reported bug.

- **2026-06-27 — "AB is a diameter" of an EXISTING circle is a CONSTRAINT, not a re-creation ([ADR-137](06-decisions.md#adr-137)).** Operator session `ylea4zal`: "but I added `AB קוטר במעגל P` and it failed" (`'B' is already defined → built-nothing`). **Diagnosed from the log:** the figure's circle really was P (a circumcircle through A,B,D, centre P a derived circumcentre), and A,B already lay on it — so `circleOnDiameter` deferred (named-circle-exists guard) and the `diameter` rule fired. Its `diameter` command re-creates A as a fresh on-circle point and B as "the antipode of A", redefining the already-existing A,B → the ADR-009 redefinition guard rejected it. The `diameter` command only builds a *new* diameter; it had no path for "the existing chord AB *is* a diameter." **Root fix (the established pattern, not a patch):** a declaration against an existing circle/points is a CONSTRAINT (cf. ADR-080/092/099/115). When the `diameter` rule's endpoints already exist, emit **`set-collinear [A, centre, B]`** — the centre is equidistant from A,B (both on the circle), so collinearity forces it to their midpoint ⇒ AB passes through the centre ⇒ a diameter (Thales: the far vertex then subtends 90°). `set-collinear`→`addCollinear` adapts to the representation with **no dependency cycle**: numeric `driveOrCheck` for a derived circumcentre (flex the free points until the centre lands on AB), antipode conversion for an independent centre with on-circle endpoints. Scoped: only when both endpoints exist AND a circle resolves — "A,B exist, no circle yet" still defines a fresh circle on AB (ADR-092), and "diameter DE in circle O" with NEW D,E still builds the diameter. Verified on BOTH representations: ∠ACB=90 in every config, centre at the midpoint of AB, no error/violation, figure keeps its remaining freedom. _Note: current HEAD's inscribe auto-names the circle O via on-circle points (not the session's circumcircle-P) — a representation drift, so the scenario references O; the literal circumcircle-P case is a unit test._ Locked by scenario `diameter-on-existing-chord-is-a-constraint` + a unit test in `circle-on-diameter.test.ts`; corrected a pre-fix parser test that asserted the buggy `diameter`-command output. **1430 tests green, build clean.**

- **2026-06-27 — Relations layer false positives RETURNED via the ADR-135 order constraint ([ADR-136](06-decisions.md#adr-136) Am. 2).** Operator (manual test, screenshot): an inscribed triangle ADB + "the tangent at D and the extension of AB meet at E" showed every angle as a definite number (51.8°, 65°, …) in the "view relations"/DOF cue while the figure has **2 free DOF** — "the shape is not defined, so this should not have happened." **Diagnosed by reproducing the exact two utterances (`משולש ADB חסום במעגל`, `המשיק בנקודה D והמשך AB נפגשים בנקודה E`) through parse→replay and dumping the construction:** all three on-circle vertices A,D,B (+ centre O + free-radius circle) carried a `solve` directive whose constraint was `collinear-order [A,B,E]` — the ADR-135 order field on the tangent∩extension `line-intersection`. The relations layer samples via `applySeed`, but the "samplable" predicates (`isFreeOnCircle`/`isFreeOnSegment`/`isSamplableExtension`/`isFreeOnLine`/shape-carrier + `freeDofs`) all required `solve === undefined`, so the recruited vertices were **frozen** → all 16 samples identical → every angle "definitive". The exact **ADR-052 smell**: `freeDofCount` read **2** (an order constraint removes 0 DOF) but `freeDofs` read only `[O]`. ADR-136 (same day) had un-frozen the *lone* inscribed triangle, but its regression test never exercised the order-recruited combination, so the second step re-froze it. **Root fix (not a patch):** an **order/region** constraint (`angle-order`/`length-order`/`collinear-order`/`angle-acuteness`) pins a carrier into a *region*, not to a point — it does **not consume** the DOF. Added `isOrderOnlySolve`/`notConsumed` to `sample.ts`; every samplable predicate now accepts `solve === undefined` OR an order-only `solve`. `applySeed` perturbs the carrier while keeping the `solve`, so `evaluate` re-enforces the order from the perturbed seed (an in-region perturbation has 0 order residual). Verified: the triangle's ∠ADB now ranges ~35–86° across seeds, E stays beyond B (t>1) in every config, `definiteAngles` → `[]`. General over the whole class (any order constraint recruiting an otherwise-free shape DOF) and restores "show another configuration" variety for those figures. Locked by `relations.test.ts` ("an order-recruited inscribed triangle is still samplable") + scenario `order-only-solve-stays-samplable` (operator's exact utterances). **1428 tests green, build clean.**

- **2026-06-26 — PARKED BUG: symmetric tangent-kite falsely "over-constrained" (`|AB|=|AD| cannot hold`).** Operator session `5anuc529`, "cannot place E". **Two layers, diagnosed by replaying the exact utterances through the live parser + a hand-built corrected stream:** (1) the *logged* crash (a tangent constraint against a phantom centre **P** + dropped B/C/D membership → `Cannot read 'x' of undefined` in `residual` when placing E) was a **stale dev server** — on HEAD the parser handles `AB ו AD משיקים למעגל` correctly (resolves the one circle O, emits `point-on-circle B/D` + `set-perpendicular(O,…)`), so a server restart clears the crash. (2) The **real remaining bug**: the figure `דלתון ABCD` + `נקודות B C D על מעגל שמרכזו O` + `AB ו AD משיקים למעגל` then breaks at the tangent step with **`over-constrained: |AB| = |AD| cannot hold`**, and that error state covers every later step incl.
