E (E *does* get a valid antipode position, but the figure stays red). **It's a FALSE over-constraint:** two tangents from A make `AB=AD` automatically, so the kite's apex equality is *redundant*, not contradictory — but `דלתון` (ADR-110 macro) asserts a **hard** `set-equal(AB=AD)`, and the coupled "B,D on circle + OB⊥AB + OD⊥AD" solve can't drive its residual to 0, so it declares it impossible. **DOF check confirms it's well-posed:** 7 shape DOF, 7 constraints but one (`AB=AD`) is dependent → it should resolve. **Likely root fix:** make the kite's apex equality SOFT/yielding when those vertices are otherwise pinned (mirror [ADR-114](06-decisions.md#adr-114) isosceles soft default), or detect a redundant equality, so a tangent/on-circle construction that implies the equality isn't double-counted. **Capture the operator's exact 7-utterance sequence as a regression scenario when fixed.** Class: *a hard equality geometrically redundant with a tangent/on-circle construction is falsely reported over-constrained* — likely affects other named-shape macros (equilateral/iso-trapezoid) under similar pinning. Deferred to finish the GUI/UX phases first (operator directive).

- **2026-06-26 — Production LLM-proxy deploy, Phase A (code) DONE ([ADR-128](06-decisions.md#adr-128)).** Operator: "let's start the deployment." Decisions taken: live at **`themathbible.com/geo-builder/`** (subpath under `httpdocs`, not a subdomain) + the proxy runs as a **Node systemd service on the existing server** (like the Flask apps). The `/api/parse` proxy existed ONLY as a Vite dev plugin — no production entry point. Built: `server/parseHandler.ts` (one shared handler — method/key/rate-limit/body-cap/dynamic-SDK — used by BOTH the dev plugin and prod, so they're byte-identical); `server/standalone.ts` (bare-`http`, loopback `127.0.0.1:8788`, `/healthz`, matches any path ending `/api/parse`); `server/build.mjs` → `npm run build:proxy` bundles the server **+ the Anthropic SDK** into one self-contained `dist-server/proxy.mjs` (~504 KB, no `npm install` on the box). `vite.config.ts` `base:'/geo-builder/'` (build only; dev stays root) + the client fetch is now base-relative (`${import.meta.env.BASE_URL}api/parse`). Per-IP rate-limit reads **`X-Forwarded-For`** (behind the proxy every socket is 127.0.0.1, which would collapse the limiter to one global bucket). Deploy artifacts in **`deploy/`**: `geo-proxy.service`, `nginx-geo-builder.conf` (Plesk "Additional nginx directives"), `README.md` (full Phase B/C runbook). Smoke-tested the running bundle with NO key: `/healthz`→200, `POST /api/parse`→503 `no-api-key` (never fires a live call), prefix form matched, `GET`→405. **1387 tests green, build clean.**

  **DEPLOYED same day (Phases B+C) — LIVE at `https://themathbible.com/geo-builder/`.** Key server fact for next time: **nginx is OFF on this box** (failed since 2026-04) — it serves over **HTTPS via Apache**, and the real reverse-proxies live in **`/var/www/vhosts/system/themathbible.com/conf/vhost_ssl.conf`** (`/hw/`→8000, `/akinator`→5000, `/bagrut`→3000, `/akinator2`→5001). So the proxy is two `ProxyPass`/`ProxyPassReverse` lines appended there (backup → `apache2ctl -t` → `systemctl reload apache2`); the `vhost_nginx.conf` attempt was a no-op and was reverted byte-identical. Layout: `geo-proxy.service` (User=www-data, `127.0.0.1:8788`) running `/var/www/geo-proxy/proxy.mjs`; key at `/var/www/geo-proxy/geo-proxy.env` (root:root 600); static SPA at `httpdocs/geo-builder/` (root:root, Apache default-served). Node v20.20.2, Ubuntu 22.04, Plesk. Public verify (no live call): static 200, assets 200, `…/api/parse` empty→400 `empty`, existing sites unaffected. **Live LLM path CONFIRMED in-browser** (`תצייר לי ריבוע עם שני אלכסונים` → escalated → correct figure), so the full Apache→Node→Haiku→re-parse→build chain works in prod. Only follow-up: set the [Console](https://console.anthropic.com/settings/billing) spend cap. **Persistence caveat:** a Plesk reconfigure could drop the direct `vhost_ssl.conf` edits (static keeps working; LLM fallback degrades gracefully) — re-append from `deploy/apache-geo-builder.conf` or add via the Plesk GUI. The whole long-parked "production proxy deploy" thread is now CLOSED.

- **2026-06-25 — "The circumcircle cuts CE at D" keeps D ON the segment in every configuration ([ADR-127](06-decisions.md#adr-127)).** Operator (circle O, two tangents from A at B,C, ∠CAB=90, chords CE/BE, then "המעגל שחוסם את משולש ABC חותך את CE בנקודה D"): "all worked well but the last view violates the rule that D is on CE." **Diagnosed from the log + a cross-seed reconstruction:** `circumcircleMeetsSegment` built D as a `line-circle-intersection` on the INFINITE line through C,E (avoid the shared vertex) with NO order constraint — so seed 0 happened to land D on the chord (t≈0.14) but "show another configuration" re-sampled the chord endpoints and slid D onto the extension (t up to **4.3**). **Two-step fix (the first try is instructive):** appending `set-line [C,D,E]` FAILED at apply — `set-line`'s `addCollinear` searches its points for a free on-circle carrier to drive and mis-picked the chord endpoint **C**, attempting the second-intersection conversion on it → "unresolved dependencies for E, D, line-CE, line-CD" (a cycle; D is ALREADY collinear, being a point on the line, so it never needed addCollinear). **Root fix:** a new optional `order` field on `line-circle-intersection` → apply pushes a lone `collinear-order [C,D,E]` constraint (no addCollinear, no carrier re-marking); its residual is folded into the joint minimisation, so the figure flexes the free DOFs to keep D between C and E in EVERY config. Verified: t ∈ [0.12, 0.88] across seeds 0–8 (was −2.1…+4.3), collinear residual 0, no error/violation. Locked by scenario `circumcircle-cuts-segment-d-on-side`. **1381 tests green, build clean.** _Note: the operator's actual run had a typo "חותרך" → LLM (un-ordered decomposition); the deterministic rule the fix lives in fires on the correct spelling. A future catalog/LLM-prompt note could teach the `order` field to the fallback._

- **2026-06-25 — "Two tangents from a point" no longer needs the touch points named ([ADR-126](06-decisions.md#adr-126)).** Operator (session `gd0kkj`): "why in the last test I cannot get `מנקודה A יוצאים שני משיקים למעגל O` to work, and in a previous question this worked nice?" **Diagnosed from the log:** the step (seq 98) went `src=llm result=built-nothing` — the deterministic parser didn't handle it. Root cause: `tangentsFromExternal` REQUIRED a named touch-point pair (`const abM = s.match(…"X and Y"…); if (!abM) return null`), so the natural phrasing that doesn't name the touch points bailed the rule → LLM → nothing. The operator's "previous question that worked" had NAMED them ("…בנקודות B ו C"). **Fix (root):** when the touch points aren't named, auto-name two fresh ones (dodging the figure's labels) and build the same Thales construction (touch points = circle O ∩ the circle on diameter O-E); a named pair is still honoured. The rule was already gated (tangent + plural + "from <point>" + a resolvable circle), so reaching the touch-point step means it really is "two tangents from a point" — building is correct, failing was the bug. Verified end-to-end: `circle O radius 5` → `מנקודה A יוצאים שני משיקים למעגל O` builds two touch points on O + the two tangent segments, no LLM. Locked by scenario `two-tangents-from-point-unnamed-touch`. **1381 tests green, build clean.**

- **2026-06-25 — A subject-named incentre is honoured + a hidden circle is truly invisible ([ADR-125](06-decisions.md#adr-125)).** Operator manual-testing session `djvbb7`, three reports on the SAME figure: (1) `M מרכז המעגל החסום במשולש BDC` put the centre at **O** not M; (2) "hide circle" only made it dashed; (3) the BD/circle tangency point showed **G** and flipped to **F** when hidden. **Diagnosed from the log:** #1 and #3 are ONE root cause — the `incircle` rule named the incentre with `circleCenter` (catches only "circle M"/"centred at M", a label AFTER the centre word), so the **subject** form "M [is the] centre of …" (label BEFORE the word) left M **unreferenced** → the dropped-label gate (ADR-089) escalated to the LLM → the LLM built a **second complete incircle** (centre O, foot G, circle-O) stacked on the parser's (centre I, foot F, circle-I): wrong centre + two duplicated feet that flip when one is hidden. **Fix (root):** `incenterLabel(s)` captures the subject-named incentre ("M מרכז…" / "M is the [in]centre…" / trailing "incentre M"), guarded against grabbing a triangle vertex; the rule names the incentre + `circle-<M>` and only auto-centres when un-named. M honoured → nothing dropped → no escalation → ONE incircle. **#2 (hide):** operator chose "truly invisible + clickable" — the circle group already has an always-present transparent hit-ring, so a hidden circle now draws **nothing** (was a faint dashed ghost) while staying clickable to un-hide; the tangency foot stays a normal hideable point (the "different letter appears" was just the duplicate underneath, now gone). Locked by scenario `named-incenter-of-incircle` (the operator's exact `מלבן DCBA` / `BD` / `M מרכז…`). **1380 tests green, build clean.**

- **2026-06-25 (work PC) — `extend-onto-circle` onto an EXISTING target is a CONSTRAINT, never a silent no-op ([ADR-124](06-decisions.md#adr-124)); + a concurrent-session reconciliation.** Operator: "check why C-A-D was not respected in the last test i did." **Diagnosed from the log (session `v7veg7sc`):** the figure was two circles O,P meeting at A,B; `המיתר AD במעגל P משיק למעגל O בנקודה A` (LLM → D as a free on-circle point of P + tangency `OA⟂AD`), then `המשך CA חותך את מעגל P בנקודה D`. The extension step was a **complete no-op** — D already existed, and `extend-onto-circle` re-creates its target with `addObj`, which **keeps the first definition** (the deterministic-id idempotency rule), while the shared-endpoint path it took (A is an O∩P crossing → on circle P) pushes **no constraints**. Replaying the operator's exact facts: C-A-D collinearity residual **0.38** (not remotely straight). **Fix (root cause):** when the target already exists, the extension becomes a **constraint** on it — `collinear` + `collinear-order` (beyond the 2nd letter); a free on-circle target of that circle gets the order-aware far-crossing `solve` attached (the same mechanism the fresh case uses), else the constraints flex the figure. New targets unchanged. Verified on a buildable variant: D lands on line CA, beyond A (collinear residual 0, proj 1.955). Locked by a unit test in `extend-onto-circle.test.ts`. **Deeper finding (the operator's *exact* figure is contradictory):** proved by tangent-chord-angle algebra AND an independent 15,000-sample brute-force sweep (zero engine code) that "AD tangent to O at A" + "CB tangent to P at B" make **C-A-D collinear impossible** for any non-degenerate circle pair (only the degenerate coincident-circles case). So after the fix the step reports `over-constrained: OA⟂AD cannot hold` **honestly** instead of hiding it behind a clean-looking but wrong figure. Likely "AD tangent" is the problem's *conclusion*, not a construction given. **Concurrent-session note:** the operator ran two sessions at once; the OTHER session committed my working-tree fix **verbatim** as `8b098fa` ("ADR-124 doc entry to follow") and the pre-existing dirty tree (ADR-119–123) as `8efc246`. Revalidated: `git show 8b098fa` is byte-for-byte my edit and touches only `apply.ts`; `8efc246` does not touch `apply.ts`; build clean, **full suite green (1378)**. **PARKED (operator "let me look first"):** the end-to-end scenario for the operator's *exact* sequence is **held** pending their check of the bagrut source — whether to (a) keep the honest over-constrained error or (b) model it with the tangency dropped (then C-A-D builds). The engine behaviour is already locked by the unit test.

- **2026-06-25 (work PC) — Area-ratio on a kite: parse fix + a FORCED coincidence is allowed with a notice ([ADR-121](06-decisions.md#adr-121) Am. + [ADR-123](06-decisions.md#adr-123)).** Operator session `id4dn4a2`: building a kite inscribed in a circle (`דלתון חסום במעגל` + manual `AB=AD`/`CB=CD`, `E על DC`, `BE⊥DC`, `N = חיתוך BE ו-AC`), the area-ratio given failed **two ways** — `S_{ACD}=4S_{NCE}` was `not-handled`, and `שטח משולש NCE = רבע שטח משולש ACD` applied but errored "O and N would be at the same point". **Parse fix (ADR-121 Am.):** a coefficient glued to the area marker (`4S_{NCE}`) broke the word-boundary before `S` in BOTH the ref regex and `normalizeAreaSubscript`; `(?<![A-Za-z])S` (not `\bS`) fixes it (the spaced `4 S_{NCE}` always worked). **The apply failure — a long, instructive investigation (the operator's question "is ACD similar to NCE / why wouldn't it work" drove it):** (1) △NCE~△ACD is STRUCTURAL — a cyclic kite is a *right kite* (∠ADC=90°), BE⊥DC gives ∠NEC=90°, shared ∠C ⇒ similar in every config (engine angle-triples match <0.1°); (2) so the area ratio ¼ ⟺ the linear ratio CN/CA=½; (3) ∠ADC=90° ⇒ by Thales AC is a DIAMETER ⇒ O is the midpoint of AC (`|O−mid(CA)|=0`) ⇒ CN/CA=½ places **N exactly on O**; (4) **the solver was never broken** — driving CN/CA to 0.3/0.4/0.6/0.7 all converge; ONLY the ½ (N=O) value failed, and only because the coincidence guard hard-rejected it. **Two abandoned approaches first (recorded so they're not re-tried):** a sensitivity-filtered recruiter (scale-normalised residual to drop gauge DOFs) — reverted, the joint re-solve still failed; and the similarity→side-ratio reduction — turned out **unnecessary** once the real blocker (the guard) was found. **Fix (ADR-123, operator's rule verbatim: "you never put 2 nodes in the same position as a default unless the shape dictates it; if it happens, a notice says they converge — part of the building experimentation"):** `evaluateCore` no longer hard-fails a non-intended coincidence — it COLLECTS the pairs into `coincidences` on the OK result; `Derived`/the app surface an info notice ("X and Y have landed at the same point"). DEFAULT collisions stay avoided upstream (sampler seeds apart; `chooseComposition` prefers a coincidence-free placement and errors only when BOTH composition sides stack — `phase5-composition` stays green). Justified here because **O was never user-defined** (auto/hidden centre). Result: the operator's exact sequence builds, `area(NCE)=¼area(ACD)` holds, N converges on O with a notice, no error. Locked by scenario `area-ratio-converges-points-allowed`. **1375 tests green, build clean.** **Then (same session) — the freeze ("engine in a loop, page freezes"):** allowing the coincidence unmasked an inconsistency — the figure-validity checks still treated N=O as invalid. `meetsRequirements` required `pointsDistinct`, which N=O fails → `resolveAfterCommit`→`autoResolve`→`findValidConfig` looped ~136 replays (~5 s each) hunting a distinct-points config that CAN'T exist (the area ratio forces N=O) → effective hang. Fix: `pointsDistinct(c, pos, allowed)` skips the figure's `coincidences` (a forced coincidence is not an avoidable near-collision); `meetsRequirements`/`resample` pass `fig.coincidences`. Now `meetsRequirements` is true → autoResolve early-returns, no loop. Locked by `src/store/__tests__/coincidence.test.ts`. **1378 tests green, build clean.** _Perf follow-up (NOT the freeze; flagged to operator): a single `replay` of this coupled figure is ~1.5 s because the area-ratio over-recruits DOFs (incl. the gauge centre/radius) into an expensive multi-start solve — bounded/usable now, but a leaner recruitment or the similarity→side-ratio reduction would speed it up; "show another" is correspondingly slow on this one figure._

- **2026-06-25 (work PC) — Swap two labels (A ↔ B) + a token-aware utterance relabel ([ADR-122](06-decisions.md#adr-122)).** Operator (chord figure, diameter AB + parallel chords CD/AF): "CD was set so C is on one side and D on the other and pressing new options gave no other presentation that changes it (no reason C must be on that side)" + "I tried to rename the nodes manually so C and D change sides — this caused an error." **Diagnosed from the log (session `opu22he8`):** Issue 1 — swapping which endpoint is C vs D is a **relabeling**, not a distinct shape, so "show another" correctly never offers it (it DID vary the chord across seeds 1–5; the C-vs-D assignment is a label, fixed until swapped). Issue 2 — two root causes: (a) **no swap op**, and `rename(C→D)` returns `target-taken` (deliberate anti-merge guard), so the natural swap is blocked → the "error"; (b) the temp-label workaround hit a **substring-corruption bug** — the utterance relabel `f.utterance.split(F).join(T)` rewrote the `C` inside `C1`, turning `CC1`→`DD1` (confirmed directly from the log: `"CC1".split('C').join('D')==="DD1"`; commands stayed correct — they're id-based — so only the displayed text desynced). **Operator chose UX = the SEGMENT (line) menu + a typed command** (corrected from my initial "node menu"). **Built:** `swap(a,b)` store action (3-step `\0`-sentinel exchange across commands/utterances/hidden/segStyle/hiddenCircles, one undo; verified C and D visibly switch sides since the on-circle sampler is order-keyed); `relabelUtterance` token-aware (`from+'(?!\\d)'`) replacing the substring relabel in rename + merge (the bug fix); a "Swap endpoints" button on the segment menu (`SceneSegment` now carries `aId`/`bId`); `parseSwap` ("swap C and D"/"swap C ↔ D"/"החלף בין C ל-D" — בין marks a swap so the plain "החלף E ב-G" rename is untouched), intercepted in App before parseRename; `swap` REMOVED from `parseRename` (it used to live there → "swap C and D" became rename(C,D) → the same target-taken error); the rename "taken" note now points at swap. Issue 1 left as-is (show-another explores shapes, not labelings — swap is the right tool). Locked by `src/store/__tests__/swap.test.ts` (operator figure: C↔D exchange positions + undo + the `C1` non-corruption regression) + `src/parser/__tests__/swap-parse.test.ts`. **1371 tests green, build clean.** _Note: swap is a store op (not a parse command), so its regression lives as a store test, not a scenarios.test.ts entry (the scenario harness is parse→replay only)._

- **2026-06-25 (work PC) — Area subscript notation `S_{ABC}` / `S_ABC` + an `S_{}` toolbar button ([ADR-121](06-decisions.md#adr-121)).** Operator: "how would a student enter areas? should (can?) we have an `S_{}` format?". Confirmed against the live parser what works today (verbose `שטח המשולש ABC הוא 13` / `area of triangle ABC = 13`; glued compact `SABC = 13`; label `נסמן…ב-S`; ratios) and what didn't: the textbook subscript `S_{ABC} = 13` / `S_ABC = 13` were `not-handled` (the area rule's compact matcher needs the vertices glued straight onto `S`). Operator chose scope = **input + toolbar button** (not canvas subscript rendering). **Fix:** a `normalizeAreaSubscript` pass in `parse` (after `normalizeGreek`) rewrites `S_{ABC}` → `SABC` and `S_ABC` → `SABC`, scoped to `S_` + **3–4 uppercase vertex labels** so it can only touch area references (a 2-label `S_{AB}` or a `S_1` is left alone) and every downstream area path (absolute/ratio/label) inherits it; plus an `S_{}` button in the symbol palette (`insertSymbol` gained an optional `caret` offset so the template inserts and the caret drops between the braces). The glued `SABC` + verbose forms are unchanged; the empty `S_{}` template no-ops as `not-handled` until vertices are typed. Catalog area examples switched to the `S_{ABC}` form (coverage guard verifies bilingual parse) and note all three input shapes + the button. Locked by `src/parser/__tests__/area-subscript.test.ts` (incl. safe-scoping: `S_{AB}` rejected, `AB = 6` untouched). **1355 tests green, build clean.** _Deferred (the scope the operator did NOT pick): rendering the on-canvas area label as a true subscript $S_{ABC}$ — would reuse the `O1`→`O₁` mechanism._

- **2026-06-25 (work PC) — The `△` triangle glyph as a parser keyword + toolbar button ([ADR-120](06-decisions.md#adr-120)); congruence/similarity confirmed already-supported.** Operator question during manual testing: "do we support congruent and similar triangles? if a user writes `משולש ABC חופף למשולש GHT` what happens? … we are missing the symbol of a triangle in the input symbols." **Investigated against the real parser/engine:** congruence (`חופף`/congruent/`≅` → SSS, reshapes the 2nd triangle to match the 1st) and similarity (`דומה`/similar/`~` → AA) are **already fully supported** ([ADR-032](06-decisions.md#adr-032)) — `משולש ABC חופף למשולש GHT` builds both triangles and GHT comes out congruent to ABC (verified `|GH|=|AB|, |HT|=|BC|, |TG|=|CA|`); `△ABC ≅ △GHT` already worked too (the glyph passes through the label-triple regex). The palette already had `≅` and `~`. **Two real gaps, both fixed:** (1) the textbook triangle glyph `△` was missing from the symbol palette; (2) `△ABC` alone was `not-handled` (the `△` wasn't a triangle keyword — inconsistent with `∠ABC`). Fix: `△`/`▲` added to the palette (`App.tsx`) + to the base `triangle` rule keyword (`/triangle|משולש|[△▲]/`, so `△ABC` builds a triangle) + to the `twoTriangles` strip. Scoped to the bare triangle (qualified shapes still need their word); relation rules run first, so `△ABC ≅ △DEF` is unaffected. Catalog congruence/similarity examples switched to the `△ABC ≅ △DEF` form (the coverage guard now verifies `△` parses bilingually) and the triangle entry notes the shorthand. Locked by scenario `congruent-triangles-word-form` (operator's exact `חופף` phrasing, build-level SSS). **1346 tests green, build clean.** _The separate ADR-119 word-equality gap ("X ו Y שווים") was raised in the same exchange and the operator declared it NON-actionable: students spell equality out as `AB=BC`._

- **2026-06-25 (work PC) — A chord's endpoints lie ON the circle in ANY phrasing ([ADR-119](06-decisions.md#adr-119)).** Operator manual testing (session `sflkyd0r`): "do we have the construct of מיתר/מיתרים? the output suggests not — but maybe I wrote a too-complex text." Diagnosed from the debug log: after `AB קוטר במעגל` (circle O + diameter AB, an LLM step), `CD ו AF מיתרים המקבילים זה לזה` ("CD and AF are chords parallel to each other") parsed via `parser` to `segment CD`, `segment AF`, `set-parallel(C,D,A,F)` — the `מיתר`/chord **membership was silently dropped**, so C,D,A,F were free points joined by parallel segments, NOT on circle O. **The standalone `chord` construct DOES exist and works** (single chord, both endpoints on the circle, named or implicit circle, He+En — confirmed by probing the real parser); the bug is that it doesn't survive a relation. **Root cause (code-traced, not symptom):** `parse` is **first-match-wins**; `parallelConstraint` (which understands only plain segments) runs BEFORE the `chord` rule and claims the whole utterance, and even the `chord` rule handles only ONE chord (exactly 2 labels, no relation). Same for `⟂` chords — a whole class ("a chord used inside any relation loses its circle"). Operator's call: "this rule sounds like a must — why would we even consider not having it?" → built the general fix, not a per-relation special case. **Fix:** a centralised post-pass `withChordMembership(commands, s, ctx)` in `parse` (before `withImplicitCircles`): when the utterance is chord-flavoured AND a circle resolves (`resolveCenter` — named or the single circle), assert `point-on-circle` for every **`segment` endpoint** in the result (segments are the universal carrier the relational rules already emit). Safeguards: idempotent (the standalone chord rule's own membership is deduped); a circle **centre** is excluded (so "radius OE" keeps O off the circle, only E goes on); a chord's **midpoint** is never a segment endpoint, so `C אמצע מיתר AB` puts A,B not C on the circle. Verified end-to-end: the operator's exact sequence lands C,D,F at the radius distance from O with CD∥AF, verifier green; `⟂` chords and the singular `המיתר CD מקביל למיתר AF` behave the same; single-chord / midpoint / radius-operand / no-circle cases unchanged. Locked by scenario `parallel-chords-keep-circle-membership`. **1345 tests green, build clean.** _Surfaced a SEPARATE gap (not fixed, awaiting operator greenlight): the **word-form equality** "X ו Y שווים" / "equal chords" isn't recognised as an equality at all — only the symbolic `AB = CD` is (`equalSegments`) — so "AB ו CD מיתרים שווים" still falls to the single-chord rule and drops the 2nd chord + the equality. A missing word-equality construct, broader than chords._

- **2026-06-24 — "Isosceles" picks a SOFT default equal-pair that yields to an explicit one ([ADR-114](06-decisions.md#adr-114)).** Operator (manual session `yi4p8150`): "when we say `משולש ABC שווה שוקיים` you fix `AB=AC`, which is not always the case and not what the user said — it just says 2 sides must be equal. I wrote `AB=BC`, which doesn't mean the triangle is שווה צלעות." Confirmed from the log: the isosceles macro (ADR-110) emitted a HARD `set-equal(AB=AC)` ("apex = first vertex"); the student's own `AB=BC` then stacked `|AB|=|AC|` + `|AB|=|BC|` → all three equal → **equilateral** (every step `ok`, so silently wrong — not an error). The assumed pair is exactly the unstated magnitude [ADR-052](06-decisions.md#adr-052) forbids pinning. **Fix:** the macro tags its default equality `soft` (a new optional flag on `set-equal`); `replay` pre-scans and **drops a soft equality whose 3 vertices wholly contain another enabled explicit (non-soft) `set-equal`**, so the student's stated pair becomes the isosceles pair (the default steps aside, status `ok`, NOT pushed to the verifier's givens). Position-independent. With no explicit pair the soft default stands, so a lone `משולש שווה שוקיים ABC` still draws isosceles (`|AB|=|AC|`). The engine ignores the flag — conflict resolution is a pure fact-orchestration concern, lives where the ADR-104 deferral already lives. Scoped to the isosceles TRIANGLE (the one genuinely ambiguous pair); `equilateral`/`kite`/`isosceles trapezoid` stay hard (pairs unambiguous or fixed by labelling). Verified: `משולש שווה שוקיים ABC` + `AB=BC` → `|AB|=|BC|`, `|AC|` distinct (not equilateral); lone isosceles still `|AB|=|AC|`. Locked by scenario `isosceles-explicit-pair-overrides-default` (the operator's exact sequence + the two medians + their intersection). **1324 tests green, build clean.** _Follow-up (not built): with no pair stated, "show another" could cycle WHICH pair is equal (3 isosceles configs) — a fuller ADR-052 treatment._

- **2026-06-24 — A constraint now flexes a shape DOF reached *through* a parametric point ([ADR-113](06-decisions.md#adr-113)).** Operator (manual session `oew743rq`, debug log): a rhombus figure — `ABCD מעוין`, `E אמצע AB`, `K` = diagonal intersection, `G על המשך BD` (G on the extension of diagonal BD, beyond D), then `GE⊥AB` — failed *"over-constrained: GE ⟂ AB cannot hold."* Operator: "I think this worked before our last fix… in any case we must fix root cause." **Method (the disciplined one):** reproduced the exact logged command sequence via `parse→replay`; worked the geometry by hand (AB horizontal, E at its midpoint ⇒ `GE⊥AB` forces G directly above E; on line BD that crossing sits beyond D only when ∠A < 60°; at exactly 60° the unique solution is the degenerate **G=D**). So the figure IS constructible but needs the **rhombus angle to flex** jointly with G's extension param — a 1-parameter family, NOT an over-constraint. **Tested the "worked before" claim properly:** `git bisect` (in-place checkout — the node_modules junction + untracked repro survive) across 5 commits 06-21→06-24 (all fail) and back to **2026-06-17 (b9968b8)** where the same figure failed with the more honest *"D and G would be at the same point"* — i.e. it **never worked**; the engine only ever drove G onto D. Reported that honestly rather than accept the regression framing. **Root cause (code-traced):** `ancestors(…, 'drivable')` — the reachable-free-DOF walker used by `recruitFreeDofs` on the failure path — **stopped at a free 1-DOF param carrier** and never recursed past it. G is a free on-segment/extension carrier on BD; the rhombus's free shape DOF is its angle, carried by the `rotated` object D = **G's parent** — unreachable because the walk halted at G. So the recruit surfaced only G (+ the placement gauge A,B, which can't change the shape) and the joint solver could only push G onto D. **Fix:** in `drivable` mode, record a free on-segment carrier AND keep walking past it to its `pointParents` (the shape DOFs behind its segment). Effectively scoped to on-segment/extension points (`pointParents` of an on-circle carrier is `[]`; the circle DOFs are surfaced separately), and only the failure path uses `drivable`, so eager figures are untouched; the joint solver's regulariser keeps a surfaced-but-unneeded parent at seed. **Proof-before-commit:** a temporary patch made all seeds solve — `GE⊥AB` exact (cos=0), G at t≈1.27 (strictly beyond D), |GD|≈1.2 — then the **full suite (1322) ran green WITH the patch**, confirming zero blast-radius before finalising. General fix for the class (any constraint that must flex an upstream shape DOF reached through a parametric point), not a special-case. Locked by scenario `perpendicular-from-midpoint-flexes-rhombus` (the operator's exact sequence). **1323 tests green, build clean.** _Recurring lesson reinforced: the drivable walker "stops at a free carrier" semantics under-recruits whenever a needed shape DOF sits behind a parametric point — sibling of ADR-095 (no `line-circle`/`circle-circle` traversal) and ADR-103 (circle centre not surfaced)._

- **2026-06-24 — Theorem-vocabulary gap closed: named shapes + regular polygons + DOF-cue fix ([ADR-110](06-decisions.md#adr-110)/[111](06-decisions.md#adr-111)/[112](06-decisions.md#adr-112)).** Operator: "is there no support for דלתון?" → audit of the bagrut theorem list ([07-theorem-reference.md](07-theorem-reference.md)) vs. the construct vocabulary. Found genuinely-missing nameable shapes (kite·דלתון th.37–38, isosceles/equilateral triangle, isosceles trapezoid, triangle midsegment·קטע אמצעים) and the one true engine gap (no >4-vertex polygon). Operator directive: "I want to have them," with the rule **the user must never get an error** (LLM may cover a missing construct, but only once the proxy is deployed → common shapes want a deterministic path). **Decided with the operator** (after explaining the DOF math — free quad 4 / cyclic quad 3 / square 0): build regular polygon deterministically; include the DOF correctness fix. **Built:** (1) ADR-110 — a `shapeMacro` parser factory; each named shape decomposes to existing canonical commands (quad/triangle/trapezoid + equal-side/midpoint constraints), **zero new engine constructs** (the constraint solver does the work; a passing sweep `['triangle ABC','AB=AC']` already proved it). (2) ADR-111 — a generic `{type:'polygon';ids:Id[]}` command + `polyEdges` + `case 'polygon'` (the polygon object/renderer/convexity were ALREADY n-ary), and a `regularPolygon` rule placing n vertices equally-spaced on a hidden free-radius circle (corners pinned/rigid); fires only on explicit `regular`/`משוכלל`/N-gon (a bare "pentagon" → LLM, honouring ADR-052); n=3→equilateral, n=4→square, n≥5→generic. (3) ADR-112 — `rawMovableDof` now gives a **pinned** parametric vertex 0 DOF (an `isPinnedParam` mirroring the pinned-free-point case), correcting a pre-existing over-report on ALL inscribed shapes → the ladder reads exactly (square/regular polygon now read 0 and fire "✓ fully determined"). **One regression caught + fixed:** the macro intercepted `symbolic-2alpha`'s `"משולש שווה שוקיים ABC … חוסם במעגל"` and `'stop'`ped it — `incircle`'s `triCircumscribes` needs the label right after משולש, so "שווה שוקיים" makes the parse defer to the `circumcircle` rule; gave the triangle macros the same circumcircle-deferral guard `triShape` already has. Catalog entries (auto-feed the LLM prompt) + LLM decomposition examples (incl. the irregular-polygon segment-loop net) + i18n `kite` corrected `עפיפון`→`דלתון`. New `engine/__tests__/named-shapes.test.ts` (13) + scenarios `kite-named-shape`/`regular-hexagon` + 22 parser-coverage pairs. **1322 tests green, build clean.** Phase 6 (theorems) still the gated milestone; this widened the declarative vocabulary the theorems will key off.

- **2026-06-24 — Coordinate-validation campaign BUILT ([ADR-109](06-decisions.md#adr-109)) — clears a long-parked thread.** Operator wanted the deferred "differential coordinate-validation campaign". Discussed scope first and settled the key facts: it's a **dev/CI tool, never production** (production student figures are under-determined → no unique ground truth; the independent oracle can't ship); it works **only on figures determined up to similarity (0 shape DOF)**; and a debug-log audit (replayed 104 successful logged figures through the current engine, computed `freeDofCount`) showed **~11% are 0-shape-DOF** (1 DOF 33%, 2 DOF 28%, …) — so it's a sharp tool for the determined slice, NOT a broad net (the 89% are covered by invariants + stability + verifier). Built `src/validation/`: `coordOracle.ts` (closed-form geometry, imports NOTHING from the engine — that's what makes it differential), `coordCampaign.ts` (seeded recipes that **pin base points** so the frame is fixed → direct coordinate diff, no Procrustes; `compareToConfigs` accepts any one whole valid branch config; `runCoordCampaign` → report), and `__tests__/coordValidation.test.ts` (proves the harness PASSES correct figures, **CATCHES** perturbed/missing coords — the load-bearing test — and gates the 172-figure corpus at zero mismatches). v1 covers midpoint/foot/line∩line/on-segment(t)/by-distances/circle∩circle/distance-driven/equal-driven/square/parallelogram (constructs with an independent closed form + 0 default DOF). **Finding: engine matches the oracle to ~machine epsilon — worst residual 5.6e-15 over a 13,760-figure `COORD_MULT=80` sweep (tol 1e-4)** → the coordinate output, incl. the numeric solver paths, is exact to double precision on every covered construct. Zero engine changes (read-only consumer of `build`/`evaluate`), so zero risk to existing behaviour. **1286 tests green, build clean.** Deferred next slices: a globally-solved/Procrustes-aligned mode (no pinned bases — pure solver, SSS/AAS), set-angle-driven points, circle points anchored via intersections.

- **2026-06-23 — angle ACUTENESS: "זווית קהה" (obtuse) / "זווית חדה" (acute) ([ADR-108](06-decisions.md#adr-108)).** Operator: "we need to support זווית קהה and זווית חדה." `∠C קהה` was not-understood. Added a one-sided `angle-acuteness {vertex,ray1,ray2,obtuse}` constraint modelled like the ADR-039 orderings: a region (one side of 90°), no sign change, 0 DOF removed, reshapes the figure (recruitFreeDofs drives a free DOF) so ∠ is obtuse/acute. Wired through all the order-constraint switches (solve.ts residual/scale/tolerance/describe/refs, evaluate.ts withOrderCons + single-carrier order pref, sample.ts dofRemoved→0, apply.ts set-angle-acuteness). Parser `angleAcuteness` rule handles 3-letter (∠ABC) AND single-vertex (∠C) — the latter resolves arms from a new `ParseContext.neighbors` built by `pointNeighbors(construction)` (segment + polygon-edge adjacency), threaded through App.parseCtx + the test ctxOf helpers. Verified: ∠C קהה → ∠ACB≈101°, ∠C חדה → ≈72°. Locked by `angle-acuteness.test.ts` (7) + scenario `obtuse-acute-angle` + catalog entry + FR-IN-7b. **1282 tests green, build clean.**

- **2026-06-23 — "use the existing point" generalised to the WHOLE placement family ([ADR-107](06-decisions.md#adr-107) Am.2).** Operator: "what other points/segments have a similar issue? fix all of it — when a point exists and is referenced, use the existing point." Audit (throwaway tests per placement type): a redefinition of an existing point reaches `reinterpretAsConstraint`, whose carrier was only a free ANCESTOR (`freeCarrierAncestor`, includeStart=false) — so ANY point whose own free DOF is the natural carrier (on a segment between fixed vertices, on a circle, a free vertex) failed for EVERY placement type (midpoint/foot/arc-midpoint/line∩line/…), not just midpoint. Generalised the prior midpoint-only fix: (1) `reinterpretAsConstraint` skips `point-on-segment` (→ reinterpretAsCollinear) and otherwise drives P's OWN free DOF — non-extension on-segment t, on-circle θ, or non-pinned free-point (x,y) — falling back to a free ancestor for a DERIVED/EXTENSION point; (2) `driveCoincideOn` marks a free-point carrier too. A first cut (any param, no point-on-segment skip) regressed 2 tests (collinear reinterpret + the F-extension-pin-via-ancestor) — the skip + extension-exclusion fixed it. PINNED points (explicit coords) still conflict correctly (can't move); segments are idempotent (no issue). Audited working: midpoint, foot, arc-midpoint, circle∩circle, line∩line redefinitions + a free vertex. Locked by `redefine-existing-point.test.ts` (5 types) + the earlier midpoint/bisector scenarios. **1274 tests green, build clean.**

- **2026-06-23 — "A אמצע CD" onto an existing on-segment point drives it to the midpoint ([ADR-107](06-decisions.md#adr-107) Am.).** Operator: "why did my first attempt fail at A midpoint of DC?" Session lqtx8fn5: A was placed on side CD ("A ו E על הצלעות CD ו BD"), then "A אמצע CD" → weak:error → built-nothing ("'A' is already defined"). Root cause: the midpoint redefinition reached `reinterpretAsConstraint`, but `freeCarrierAncestor(P)` uses includeStart=FALSE — it searches only A's ANCESTORS (C,D = free VERTICES, 2-DOF, not param carriers), never A itself, so no carrier → gave up → conflict. Fix: for a `midpoint` redefinition, use P's OWN free on-segment/on-circle DOF as the carrier (drive A's t to the midpoint). First cut (any redefinition uses own param) regressed 2 tests — it shadowed reinterpretAsCollinear (point-on-segment) and changed the ADR-028 second-placement-pins-an-ancestor carrier; scoped to `midpoint` only → clean. The operator's manual workaround "AD=AC" was the equivalent (|AD|=|AC| + A on CD ⇒ midpoint). Locked by scenario `midpoint-of-existing-on-segment-point`. **1268 tests green, build clean.**

- **2026-06-23 — angle bisector "EG חוצה זוית DEF" onto an EXISTING point is a constraint ([ADR-107](06-decisions.md#adr-107)).** Operator: "do we have treatment for חוצה זוית? why didn't it work?" YES we have it (`bisectorPlacesPoint`), but: they'd placed `G על DF` first, then "EG חוצה זוית DEF" → weak:error → built-nothing. Root cause: the "segment's 1st letter = vertex" branch always CREATED the bisector-foot via line∩line → re-created the existing G → "'G' is already defined". Fix: when the foot point already exists (ctx.points), emit the bisector CONSTRAINT ∠(o1,vertex,G)=∠(G,vertex,o2) (set-angle-ratio k=1) driving the existing G (free on its segment) onto the bisector; new foot still constructed. Same family as ADR-086 (relation onto existing point = constraint, not redefinition). Locked by scenario `bisector-onto-existing-point` (distilled — operator's full figure had a rhombus + ratios around it). **1267 tests green, build clean.**

- **2026-06-23 — verify-then-search auto-resolver ([ADR-106](06-decisions.md#adr-106)).** Operator asked: is there a mechanism that, before drawing, checks the figure meets ALL requirements and if not loops on other options? We HAD the check (givens verifier, ADR-053 → `violations`) but it only REPORTED (amber); auto-loops existed only in pieces (per-step multi-start, `firstSatisfyingSeed` for extension orders only, deferral). Operator chose to build the general gate over SEEDS + BRANCHES. Built in geoStore: `meetsRequirements(facts,seed)` (builds + verifier-clean + extensions + distinct + convex; a PENDING figure passes — nothing to search), `findValidConfig(facts)` (loop current-branches×seeds[0..40], then alternative branch combos[≤16]×seeds[0..6] — which intersection/arc-side/root), `autoResolve()` action (applies the found config or keeps current/amber). Wired into App.submit `resolveAfterCommit()` after each commit, behind a "thinking" state (double-rAF paints first; the search is synchronous + can be slow), firing only when the figure falls short. Locked by `auto-resolve.test.ts` (branch-flip: a wrong circle∩circle branch with |PC|≠3 auto-flips to the branch where |PC|=3; + store action + clean no-op). **1267 tests green, build clean.** LIMIT: bounded search, not an exhaustive solver — a deeply under-determined coupled figure still needs the operator's givens (shows pending/amber). NOTE: scenarios' `run()` uses replay directly (not the store), so autoResolve is an app-layer enhancement tested separately.

- **2026-06-23 — a DRIVEN extension point stays on its extension; "show another" shows a busy state ([ADR-105](06-decisions.md#adr-105)).** Operator (session 3yvigwa7): (1) E on the extension of chord DC, driven by ∠CAE=50, ended up BETWEEN D and C (param 0.16) not beyond C — "E didn't respect it needs to be after D"; (2) "show another configuration" gave no feedback during its (synchronous, slow) search. Root cause of (1): ∠CAE=50 DRIVES E (recruited carrier), and the driven solver searched/placed an on-segment carrier in [0,1] (interior) — single-carrier `drivenRoots` bracket, mixed-carrier grid/binding range — then the unbounded Nelder-Mead pulled E to 0.16; nothing kept it on the extension (t>1) despite `extension:true`. Fix: extension carriers range [1.02,12] in both driven paths AND — the binding one — `setCarrierVals` HARD-CLAMPS a driven extension to t≥1.02, so the optimiser keeps E beyond C and moves the figure's OTHER DOFs (the triangle) to satisfy the angle. E now lands t≈3.24 beyond C with ∠CAE=50 & ∠ABC=60 holding, every seed. (NOTE: I'd reverted this exact extension-range idea on 2026-06-23 earlier because it didn't fix the CD=36 *coupled* case — but it IS the right fix for a driven extension; the clamp in setCarrierVals is the new piece that makes it bind the unbounded optimiser.) Fix of (2): the "another configuration" button paints a busy "thinking…" state via double-rAF before running the blocking search (the search stays synchronous/deterministic). Locked by scenario `driven-extension-point-stays-beyond`. **1264 tests green, build clean.** (Side note: "E נמצאת על המשך המיתר DC" drops E in the deterministic parser → LLM handles it; a `chord-extension` parser rule is a minor future gap, not chased.)

- **2026-06-23 — angle marks drawn more visibly (operator request).** When an angle is stated, the arc was a tiny nick at the vertex (radius 4.2·r ≈ 17px) and the value small (0.85·font) — hard to see WHICH angle it marks. Bumped in `src/render/Figure.tsx` (pure render multipliers, no engine/scene change): angle arc 4.2·r → **6.5·r**, the value sits further into the wedge (offset 2.4·r → **4.2·r**) and a bit larger (**1.05·font** for angles vs 0.85 for lengths), right-angle square 3.2·r → **4·r**. No test pins these numbers; render + full suite green (1263). Visual-only — eyeball in the app.

- **2026-06-23 — ADR-104 amendment: the INPUT GATE must COMMIT a deferrable constraint, not escalate it.** Operator re-ran the full Q4 flow and it STILL failed on `CE⊥AB`. The replay-level deferral (earlier today) was correct but never engaged: `App.submit` dry-runs each step (`dryRunOutcome`) BEFORE committing, and a constraint that can't solve at its position returns `reason:'error'` → the gate escalated it to the LLM (which re-emits/drop) → the ⟂ NEVER entered the fact list, so deferral (which works on the committed list) never saw it. Diagnosed from `logs/debug-log.jsonl` (sessions `75i5duwq`/`xr62u3gt`: `CE⊥AB` → `weak:error` → `built-nothing`). Fix: `hasDeferrableConstraint(commands)` (a `set-*` relation introducing no new point) — when a clean deterministic parse dry-run-errors but carries one, `App.submit` COMMITS it (for deferral) instead of escalating. Shows as a failing step until the pinning givens arrive, then resolves; a genuine contradiction surfaces honestly. LESSON (recurring): a fix verified only via parse→replay is NOT proven — the operator's path is `App.submit` (parse → droppedNewLabels → dryRunOutcome → commit/escalate); locked now by an App.submit-FAITHFUL test `q4-commit-deferred-perpendicular` mirroring that gate. **1262 tests green, build clean.**

- **2026-06-23 — PENDING vs CONTRADICTION: a committed-but-unsatisfied constraint shows a friendly info banner, not the red "over-constrained" (ADR-104).** Operator: the misleading "over-constrained: CE ⟂ AB cannot hold" (it's under-determined-yet, not contradictory) made them give up. Added a `pending` flag to `Derived` + a blue info banner (`figure.pending`, He/En): "this relation is recorded but not determined yet — add the remaining givens (lengths/angles) and it will resolve." The HARD part was distinguishing PENDING (satisfiable once pinned) from a genuine CONTRADICTION (e.g. ∠DAB=37 on a square, structurally 90°). First heuristic "deferrable + figure under-determined" was WRONG (a square has free placement DOFs but ∠DAB is invariant) — it broke phase3's contradiction test. Correct signal, found empirically: does the failed constraint's residual VARY as the free DOFs move? Re-derive it (`applyCommand` on the figure WITHOUT it), sample its residual across a few seeds — SQUARE ∠DAB=37 → 53.0 at every seed (invariant ⇒ contradiction, red); Q4 CE⊥AB → swings 1.0/−0.68/0.02 (flexes ⇒ pending, blue). Implemented as `constraintIsPending`. Locked by `pending-vs-contradiction` (both cases) + the operator's flow. **1263 tests green, build clean.** Operator's conceptual Q answered: more givens make it EASIER, not harder — fewer givens = more free DOFs = the numerical solver has no anchor and can't land in the thin feasible region; the sizes collapse the freedom so it homes in (like "draw a right triangle" vs "…with legs 3 and 4").

- **2026-06-23 — order-independence via DEFERRAL: a constraint that can't solve yet is retried after later givens ([ADR-104](06-decisions.md#adr-104)).** Operator (continuing the Q4 thread): "the solver must handle complex diagrams like this; the diagram should build the same no matter the entry order — if it can't solve one way, the LLM should propose a different order or the engine should try differently; the decision of what point is fixed should not be there (G has a DOF, F derived from G is coupled to it)." Concretely `CE⟂AB` typed BEFORE `CD=36, DE=18` failed, but works sizes-first. FIRST tried a stronger SOLVER (heavy deterministic Halton global multi-start over the full 8-DOF space) — it did NOT converge (thin feasible manifold in 8-D; curse of dimensionality), reverted. The real leverage: the remaining givens make the system DETERMINATE, and the solver handles determinate systems. Fix: `replay` now, after the in-order pass, RETRIES still-failed CONSTRAINT-only facts (no new points) against the now-complete figure to a fixpoint — applying a constraint LAST is exactly the working re-order, so a constraint typed too early auto-defers past the givens that pin it ("the engine tries differently", engine-side + deterministic, no LLM needed). Full Q4 now builds in ALL five orderings tested; the operator's exact flow (CE⟂AB before sizes) builds end-to-end. Gated on `facts.some(deferrable)` so clean figures are untouched; intentional over-constraints still fail honestly; expectViolations scenarios unaffected (a verifier-violation fact has status 'ok', not retried). Locked by scenario `q4-constraints-order-independent`. **1261 tests green, build clean.** LIMIT: deferral gives order-independence for a COMPLETE consistent given set; a genuinely under-determined partial (CE⟂AB with sizes never entered) stays pending until the determining givens arrive (correct — no unique figure yet). The operator's "no artificial fixing" is satisfied: every unstated magnitude is a free DOF the solver may drive; derived points re-evaluate from parents.

- **2026-06-23 — a constraint can drive a circle's CENTRE, not only its radius ([ADR-103](06-decisions.md#adr-103)).** Operator (session `zqvtvh15`, bagrut Q4): `CE⊥AB` → "over-constrained: CE ⟂ AB cannot hold". Long diagnosis (the right method): reproduced via parse→replay; isolated that CE⊥AB works ALONE but fails WITH the prior `∠GEC=∠CHA` angle-ratio; feasibility scan proved a joint solution EXISTS (not genuinely over-constrained); instrumented `recruitFreeDofs` — it produced the RIGHT carriers but the joint solve wouldn't converge; pre-seeding near the feasible point SOLVED it (so: solver reachability, not over-constraint). Drilled into a SIMPLER manifestation, `CD=36` alone, which ALSO failed — and a max-|CD| scan showed the killer: with the circle CENTRES pinned a fixed gap apart, the circle∩circle geometry CAPS |CD| at ~8 however large the radii grow (centres-free reaches 40+). **Root cause: `ancestors`/recruit surfaced a circle's free RADIUS but never its free CENTRE** (and doesn't traverse circle∩circle — the ADR-095 over-recruit guard), so the centres O,P were unreachable. Fix (1 small change in `step.ts ancestors`): surface a circle's free, non-pinned centre as a drivable DOF alongside its radius. `CD=36` now solves (centres spread, |CD|=36) and the FULL Q4 (∠GEC=∠CHA, CD=36, DE=18, CE⊥AB) builds **when the sizes precede the ⟂**. Suite stayed green — the ADR-095 regression that blanket circle∩circle traversal caused did NOT recur (targeted centre-surfacing, regulariser keeps unneeded centres at seed). Locked by `recruit-circle-center.test.ts` (2) + scenario `distance-drives-circle-centres-apart`. **1260 tests green, build clean.** TWO detours tried and REVERTED (didn't help, kept change minimal): an extension on-segment carrier's grid-scan range [0,1]→[0,8] (legit-looking but unneeded once centres recruit), and a coupled-system angular sweep over the on-circle θ. **KNOWN REMAINING LIMITATION:** entering `CE⊥AB` BEFORE the size givens is an under-determined coupled solve (C-angle + centres + radii + G through the G→F→H chain) that still won't converge from the default seed — sizes-first works; a stronger global solver is deferred. (Process note: this is the "validate feasibility → instrument → find the real DOF gap → minimal fix → full-suite" method the operator asked for; the misleading "over-constrained" message for a solver-can't-converge case is itself worth improving later.)

- **2026-06-22 — angle EQUALITY "∠ABC=∠DEF" now parses ([ADR-100](06-decisions.md#adr-100)); the "8 DOF" cue is correct-by-design (not changed).** Operator: "check now why angles cannot be equal. also check dof - it says 8 but i'm not sure this is the case." (1) ANGLES: typing `∠GEC=∠CBA`/`∠GEC=∠CHA` returned not-understood → escalated → LLM not-understood. Pure PARSER gap — the engine already has the relation (`set-angle-ratio` k=1, used by `similarity`); `angle` needs a numeric value and `measureAngle` a symbolic one, so a two-angle equality matched neither. Fix: an `angleEquality` rule (before measureAngle/angle) → set-angle-ratio, Hebrew "זווית"/∠/∢, optional coef "= 2∠DEF", draws both angles' arms (idempotent), fires only when BOTH sides are angles (numeric→`angle`, symbolic→`measureAngle`, bare `AB=CD`→untouched). Locked by `angle-equality.test.ts` (8) + scenario `angle-equality-on-q4` (book part-א ∠EDA=∠CBA, holds). (2) DOF: verified 8 is CORRECT under the deliberate, tested convention (`freeDofCount` = raw movable − constraints, counting the full place/rotate/scale gauge — "a lone square has 4 DOF", phase-sample.test.ts). Q4's 8 = O(2)+P(2)+rO(1)+rP(1)+C(1)+F(1), all genuinely free (ADR-052); the genuine SHAPE freedom (modulo similarity) is 8−4=4. NOTE: because the cue counted the placement gauge, the "✓ fully determined" badge (App.tsx, fires at freeDofCount===0) was unreachable for any figure without absolute-coordinate PINNING — a rigid SSS triangle showed 3, not 0. Operator chose (AskUserQuestion) to switch the cue to SHAPE-DOF → [ADR-101](06-decisions.md#adr-101) (next entry). **1257 tests green, build clean (before the DOF change).**

- **2026-06-22 — the DOF cue now reports SHAPE degrees of freedom (modulo similarity) ([ADR-101](06-decisions.md#adr-101), refines ADR-018 Stage 3).** Follow-up to the "8 DOF?" question: operator chose "switch to shape-DOF". `freeDofCount` now subtracts the FREE similarity gauge — `translation(2, gone once a point is pinned) + rotation(1, ≥2 points, gone with ≥2 pins) + scale(1, until a numeric distance / fixed-length radius / ≥2 pins fixes it)` — so **shape DOF = raw − constraints − gauge**. No double-count: a length constraint that pins scale is already in the constraint subtraction, so the gauge omits scale then. A figure rigid UP TO SIMILARITY now reads 0 → "✓ fully determined" finally fires (was unreachable). Worked values: lone square 0 (was 4), triangle 2 (was 6), SSS triangle 0 (was 3), single circle 0, parallelogram 2 (was 6), Q4 **4** (was 8). `freeDofs` (the samplable LIST) is unchanged, so "show another" still varies size/placement even when the SHAPE is determined (intended — "determined" = the shape, not the page placement; meaningful when no size stated, ADR-052). Only `phase-sample.test.ts` pinned freeDofCount values (3 assertions updated). **1257 tests green, build clean.**

- **2026-06-22 — inscribing in an EXISTING named circle asserts membership, never re-creates it ([ADR-099](06-decisions.md#adr-099)).** Same manual session (`99j7krj3`, full bagrut Q4) as ADR-098: after the two extensions, `מרובע EBAD חסום במעגל O` weak-errored → LLM built-nothing (twice). Root cause: the `inscribedPolygon` rule always emitted `circumcircle(circle-O, E,B,A)` to BUILD the circumscribing circle, but circle O ALREADY existed → redefined centre O (`'O' is already defined`) → whole step dropped. E,B,A,D were already ON O (A,B=O∩P, D,E=line∩O). Fix: when the named circle exists, assert membership per vertex (`point-on-circle`, idempotent for an on-circle point per ADR-093, converts a free one) + draw the polygon; the create-a-circle paths (circumcircle through 3 existing points, or fresh circle for new vertices) are untouched. The book's part-א theorem ∠EDA=∠CBA holds in the resulting figure (verified numerically, exact across configs) — confirming the operator's "issue with the angles" was a CONSEQUENCE of the wrong-side bug (ADR-098), not a separate angle defect: with both fixed, the figure is geometrically correct. (Open question for the operator: did they also want to TYPE angle-equality `∠GEC=∠CBA`? The parser returns not-understood for a direct `∠1=∠2` — the engine HAS the constraint via `set-angle-ratio k=1` (used by `similarity`), but no parser rule for the bare two-angle-equality phrasing. Deferred pending clarification.) Locked by scenario `inscribe-existing-points-in-existing-circle`. **1248 tests green, build clean.**

- **2026-06-22 — a free point's placement is SAMPLED to satisfy a directional "המשך" extension, never DRIVEN across a degeneracy ([ADR-098](06-decisions.md#adr-098)).** Operator (session `n19qmb3t`): two circles meet at A,B; `נקודה C על מעגל P` (C FREE on circle P); `המשך CA חותך מעגל O בנקודה D`; `המשך CB חותך מעגל O בנקודה E` → "point C is not positioned in a place that can satisfy the input". Reproduced via `parse→replay`: at the sampled C (top of P) C is OUTSIDE circle O, so on line CB the far crossing IS B — the only other crossing falls BETWEEN C and B, so E was on the near side (`המשך` "beyond B" violated) yet the figure showed GREEN. THREE converging gaps: (1) `extend-onto-circle`'s shared-endpoint branch records no directional intent (unlike the neither-on-circle branch); (2) C's free θ was sampled blind to it; (3) the verifier re-derived neither membership nor order. A scan proved a valid placement EXISTS (~8% of seeds) — and that the existing `two-circles-mutual-tangent-secants` scenario had the SAME latent wrong-side bug at seed 0 (its check asserted collinearity, never order). Operator chose (AskUserQuestion) SAMPLE/GATE over DRIVE: adding `collinear-order` to the construction was rejected because `evaluate` hard-fails on every constraint → would trigger `recruitFreeDofs` to drive C across the E=B tangent degeneracy (the collapse the deterministic branch avoids). Fix: verifier re-derives `extend-onto-circle` membership + a HARD directional-order check (wrong-side → amber); `firstSatisfyingSeed`/`resample` gate on a clean extension margin (`extensionsClear`, beyond the endpoint by ≥5% of figure span, so a near-tangent secant is rejected); `execute` auto-advances the seed to the first satisfying config; the scenario harness `run()` mirrors it (returns 0 for non-extension figures). Locked by `free-point-on-circle-both-extensions-reach-far-side` (replay) + `free-on-circle-extensions-auto-advance` (store) + new order assertions on `two-circles-mutual-tangent-secants`. **1247 tests green, build clean.** KNOWN GAP (deferred): a figure DETERMINED into such a config (no free DOF to sample) would need a different mechanism — sampling only works because this figure is under-determined.

- **2026-06-22 (work PC) — a constraint on a secant crossing can drive UPSTREAM free DOFs ([ADR-095](06-decisions.md#adr-095)).** Operator: "what is the problem with the angle" — "∠CAE=45" on a tangent/secant figure → "over-constrained: ∠CAE=45 cannot hold". Validated it's NOT genuinely over-determined: a scan showed ∠CAE ranges ~2–143° over the free DOFs (apex B, radius) → 45° IS reachable. Root cause (found by instrumenting recruitFreeDofs → cand=[]): the ancestor walker `ancestors`/`pointParents` had NO case for `line-circle`/`circle-circle` points (default → []), so it dead-ended and recruit never reached B. Fix: in `ancestors` drivable mode, traverse a secant crossing (`line-circle`) through its line to the apex. CRITICAL: traversing `circle-circle` TOO (first attempt) over-recruited (Thales-aux chain) and broke the ADR-093 collinear step — caught by the full suite, narrowed to secant-only. Angle now solves to 45° with |AK|=3, |ED|=7, CD∥EA intact. Locked by `recruit-through-secant.test.ts`. **1242 tests green, build clean.** PROCESS: validated via scan (reachable?) → instrument (why not recruited?) → fix → full-suite (regressions?) — the right method after the "test it yourself" feedback. KNOWN BROADER GAP: a constraint on a circle∩circle crossing still can't drive upstream (over-recruit risk) — deferred; the tangent/secant family's "derived-point drivability" could use a unified, careful pass.

- **2026-06-22 (work PC) — two tangents from E when a touch point already exists ([ADR-094](06-decisions.md#adr-094)) + an App.submit-faithful test harness (operator feedback).** Operator: "still doesn't build — why can't you test this yourself before telling me it's fixed." Fair — I'd been testing via `parse → replay`, which proves the ENGINE builds the commands but NOT that the APP commits them (App.submit's `dryRunOutcome` error → escalate to LLM → builds nothing). Built a harness mirroring App.submit (parseCtx → droppedNewLabels gate → dryRunOutcome → store.execute) and use it to verify the operator's VERBATIM sequence commits. **Use this harness pattern to verify before claiming fixed.** The bug it caught: "מנקודה E יוצאים משיקים למעגל בנקודות A ו D" with A an existing diameter endpoint → `tangentsFromExternal`'s Thales circle∩circle RE-CREATES A → "'A' is already defined". Fix: when either touch point exists, fall back to the constraint form (point-on-circle idempotent + set-perpendicular(O,P,E,P) per touch, the two-tangent generalisation of ADR-081/093); all-new keeps Thales. Verified: OA⟂AE, OD⟂DE, A≠D, both on circle, commits through the harness. Locked by `two-tangents-one-touch-already-exists`. **1240 tests green, build clean.** (Recurring theme this session: the diameter/tangent family has many "a point/circle already exists" edge cases — ADR-081/082/090/091/092/093/094 — each a real construct, not a new one.)

- **2026-06-22 (work PC) — a circle's through-point counts as ON the circle ([ADR-093](06-decisions.md#adr-093)).** Operator: B mid AC → "AB קוטר" (circle O = diameter AB, `circle-through(O,A)` so A defines O's radius) → tangent from E at D → C on ext ED → "EA משיק למעגל בנקודה A" → "unresolved dependencies" (cycle). NOT a new construct (tangent-at-point, ADR-081). Root cause: the tangent emits `point-on-circle A`, but `pointOnCircle` didn't recognise a `circle-through` point as on the circle, so apply case (c2) CONVERTED A to on-circle → A→circle-O (on-circle) and circle-O→A (radius through-point) = cycle. Fix: `pointOnCircle` returns true for the through-point (it's on the circle by construction) → `point-on-circle A` is the idempotent no-op (case a), only the tangency constraint (OA⟂EA) is added. General + correct everywhere pointOnCircle is used; case (c)/(c2) for OTHER points unaffected (through-point caught earlier). Locked by `tangent-at-a-diameter-endpoint-no-cycle` scenario. **1239 tests green, build clean.**

- **2026-06-22 (work PC) — a GIVEN diameter defines the circle ([ADR-092](06-decisions.md#adr-092)).** Continuing ADR-090/091: after "B אמצע צלע AC" (A,B exist), "AB קוטר במעגל O" still ERRORED "'B' is already defined" (routed to `diameter`/add → B antipode), and bare "AB קוטר" was not-handled — the define-marker discriminator didn't cover the plain "in circle O"/bare phrasing. Real discriminator: whether there's an existing circle to ATTACH to, not just phrasing. circleOnDiameter now also fires for a GIVEN diameter — both endpoints exist AND the referenced circle is missing (named-but-absent, or no circle at all) — and drops the circle-keyword requirement in that case (bare "AB קוטר" works). Guards preserve the add-path: named-and-existing circle returns early; cyclic "AD קוטר במעגל ABCD" (ctx.circles non-empty) → `diameter`; "diameter DE in circle O" with NEW endpoints → `diameter`. Locked by `given-diameter-defines-circle` scenario + extended parser test. **1237 tests green, build clean.** (Same recurring lesson: a standard construct that escalates to the LLM and builds nothing is a parser gap, not the LLM's job.)

- **2026-06-22 (work PC) — midpoint creates its endpoints + circle defined by centre/radius with a diameter ([ADR-091](06-decisions.md#adr-091)).** Two standard constructs that wrongly escalated to the LLM (which built nothing): (1) "B אמצע הקטע AC" on an empty figure → "unresolved dependencies for: B" because `midpoint` didn't create A,C (unlike `segment`). Fix: when an endpoint is NEW, midpoint prepends an idempotent segment (creates + draws AC); both-exist case unchanged. (2) "AB קוטר במעגל שמרכזו O ורדיוסו R" → routed to `diameter` (the "במעגל"=in) → "'B' is already defined" (tried to make existing B an antipode), though the circle was being DEFINED by centre/radius. Fix: circleOnDiameter (ADR-090) define-markers now include the centre/radius spec (שמרכזו/ורדיוסו/centered/radius); label extraction strips the centre/radius clause so only the diameter endpoints feed labelRun. Plain "diameter X in circle O" still routes to `diameter`. KEY POINT for the operator: the LLM WAS reached in both cases (log src:llm) but built nothing — a STRUCTURAL gap can't be rescued by the LLM (it emits the same command and hits the same wall); core constructs must be parser-handled, not LLM-dependent. Locked by 2 scenarios + extended `circle-on-diameter.test.ts`. **1235 tests green, build clean.** (NOTE: also worth a future look — why the live LLM built nothing on these in the operator's session; possibly proxy/config, but moot now that the parser owns them.)

- **2026-06-22 (work PC) — a circle DEFINED BY its diameter ([ADR-090](06-decisions.md#adr-090)).** Operator tried 5+ ways to make a circle with AB as diameter, all failed: define phrasings ("AB קוטר של מעגל", "מעגל שבו AB קוטר", "circle with diameter AB") were not-handled; "AB קוטר של מעגל O" misrouted to the `diameter` rule (which adds a diameter to an EXISTING circle via on-circle+antipode) and ERRORED because A,B already existed (triangle vertices). No construct for the INVERSE (circle defined by its diameter). Fix: `circleOnDiameter` rule for the DEFINE phrasings → segment AB + midpoint(centre) + circle-through(centre, A): centre = midpoint of AB, radius |AB|/2, A,B the endpoints; segment creates A,B if new (midpoint needs them) + draws the diameter. Discriminator is the PHRASING (positive define marker: of/with/whose-diameter), NOT circle-existence — so "diameter X IN circle O" / "במעגל" stays with `diameter` (and the cyclic "AD קוטר במעגל ABCD"). Runs before `diameter`/`circle`. Locked by `circle-on-diameter.test.ts` + `circle-defined-by-its-diameter` scenario. **1235 tests green, build clean.**

- **2026-06-22 (work PC) — typo escalation gate ([ADR-089](06-decisions.md#adr-089), the N1 backlog item).** Operator typed "מנוקדה D יוצא משיק למעגל בנקודה B" (typo of מנקודה — ק/ו swapped). The parser matched the tangent on the correct tail ("בנקודה B"), drew the tangent, but DROPPED the "from D" apex, and COMMITTED the partial figure instead of escalating. Fix: a `droppedNewLabels(utterance, commands, existingPoints)` gate in `App.submit` — a successful parse that leaves a NEW input label (not already in the figure) unreferenced by the commands is treated as weak → escalate to the LLM (typos are the LLM's job, ADR-023; grammar stays strict, NO fuzzy keyword matching). "New + unconsumed" avoids false positives: an EXISTING label a command doesn't re-name is context, not dropped (validated zero false positives over 12 real per-step sequences). Helper exported from `@/parser`, used by App.submit, unit-tested in `dropped-labels.test.ts` (the operator's exact typo flags D; correct spelling + existing-label + id-embedded cases flag nothing). The end-to-end LLM recovery runs in the app (mocked in tests per the no-autonomous-API rule), so the regression test covers the ESCALATION DECISION, not the LLM output. **1232 tests green, build clean.**

- **2026-06-22 (work PC) — two operator feature requests: set radius by value ([ADR-087](06-decisions.md#adr-087)) + hide a circle ([ADR-088](06-decisions.md#adr-088)).** (1) "רדיוס מעגל P הוא 4" used to build-nothing; the workaround "PF=4" invented a point + drew the radius segment. New `set-radius {circle,value}` command + `setRadius` parser rule: sizes an EXISTING circle by value, NO segment, no point. Resolves per RadiusSpec — a through-radius incircle gets a distance(centre, foot, value) constraint that flexes the figure; free/length radius is set directly. Fires only for an existing circle (creation still → `circle`); runs before `circle` and the shape rules (which 'stop' on רדיוס). Witness-via-circleMembers was tried first and FAILED (the incircle's foot is a `foot` point, not an on-circle member → no witness), which is why a real engine command was needed. (2) Circles couldn't be hidden (segments/points could). Added `hiddenCircles` display set + `toggleCircleHidden` (UI-only, not undoable, rename/merge-rewritten, clear-reset) + made circles clickable → the on-canvas menu → hide/show (operator chose click→menu over a text command). Engine untouched (distinct from the construction-level `Circle.hidden` for cyclic quads). Both proven engine/store-first, locked by `set-circle-radius-by-value-no-segment` scenario + `hidden.test.ts` circle block. **1228 tests green, build clean.** (Catalog/commands-panel entry for set-radius NOT added yet — optional polish.)

- **2026-06-22 (work PC) — "C on the extension of DA" preserves the ORDER for an existing point ([ADR-086](06-decisions.md#adr-086)).** Triangle ABC inscribed, tangent from D at B, then "C נמצאת על המשך DA" — wanted D→A→C (C beyond A), but C landed BETWEEN D and A (param 0.18, the near secant intersection). Root: `pointOnExtension` always emitted `point-on-segment … extension` (t=1.3), but C already existed as an on-circle vertex, so the apply kept it on the circle as a bare collinearity at the near root, discarding the order. Fix: when the named point EXISTS in ctx.points, emit an ORDERED `set-line [a,b,id]` (= [D,A,C]) instead — drive the existing point, on whatever carrier it has, beyond the far end in order. C now lands beyond A (param 1.22), still on the circle. New points still get point-on-segment. Proven engine-first (set-line gives D<A<C) before the parser change. Locked by `existing-vertex-on-extension-keeps-order` scenario. **1223 tests green, build clean.** Generalises the ADR-050/054 collinear-order family to "X on the extension of YZ" when X pre-exists.

- **2026-06-22 (work PC) — scalene default + lone on-line marker sign ([ADR-085](06-decisions.md#adr-085)).** Two operator reports on the tangent-from-D figure, both the SAME ADR-052 "fixed default" flaw: (1) the one-shot "המשיק למעגל בנקודה B והמשך CA נפגשים בנקודה D" ERRORED "lines tan-B and line-CA are parallel"; (2) D always on the same side of B. Root cause of (1): the inscribed triangle defaulted to ISOSCELES — golden-angle spacing gives 3 points two equal gaps → B at the arc-midpoint of AC → AB=BC AND tangent@B ∥ CA exactly (measured: seed 0 AB=BC=9.32, angle 0.00°), so the deterministic line∩line had no solution. NOT a regression from today (golden spread is old), but ADR-081 now routes the tangent through the path that surfaces it. Operator (AskUserQuestion) chose the GENERAL root fix: a bounded alternating skew in `nextTheta` (`+(n%2)*0.35`) → generic scalene default. (2): the sampler preserved an on-line marker's sign (to keep a ±pair straddling); now a LONE marker flips sign (a pair still keeps signs). Fallout was exactly 2 intended coordinate snapshots (`stability-snapshot.test.ts`, updated `-u`) + the ADR-036 marker test (updated to assert both-sides). Locked by `scalene-default.test.ts` + `inscribed-triangle-scalene-tangent-meets-CA` scenario. **1220 tests green, build clean.** NOTE for later: the SCALENE_SKEW=0.35 default touches EVERY inscribed figure's seed-0 look — watch for any corpus figure that now looks slightly off (none seen; sampler still jitters for seed>0).

- **2026-06-22 (work PC) — a tangent's named external point now appears immediately, then is driven by a later fact ([ADR-084](06-decisions.md#adr-084)).** Triangle ABC inscribed, "מנקודה D יוצא משיק למעגל בנקודה B" (from D, tangent at B) drew the tangent but DROPPED D (operator: "still didnt create point D and just created a tangent line"), and the defining "המשך CA נפגש עם המשיק בנקודה D" was not-handled. The engine was never the obstacle — the one-sentence form already builds D as a line∩line. Operator chose (AskUserQuestion) "D appears at step 2". Fix: (1) `tangentLine` creates the named external apex as a free `point-on-line` marker on the tangent (appears + slides); (2) a tight `extensionMeetsExistingPoint` rule reads "extension of CA meets the tangent at [existing] D" as `set-line [C,A,D]` — D already on the tangent, so the only new info is "D on CA extension", which drives its on-line DOF to the crossing (order C→A→D). **No parse-context plumbing needed** (because D is already on the tangent, step 3 never names the tangent). Proven at the engine level FIRST (a point-on-line marker driven by set-line lands on tangent∩CA), then the parser rules. Locked by `tangent-from-external-D-then-pinned-by-extension` scenario. **1220 tests green, build clean.**

- **2026-06-22 (work PC) — "one circle → no name needed" ([ADR-083](06-decisions.md#adr-083)).** Operator principle: with exactly one circle in the diagram, a definite "the circle" / "המעגל" should resolve to it without naming. Surfaced finishing the full bagrut Q4 — the secant "BD חותך את המעגל בנקודה A" (unnamed) was `not-handled` and escalated to the LLM. Root cause: `lineMeetsCircle`/`extendOntoCircle` required a NAMED circle in two places (the centre via `circleCenter`, and the crossing anchor `(?:circle|מעגל)\s+[A-Za-z]\d*` needing a name letter — which "המעגל בנקודה A" lacks). Fix: `resolveMentionedCircle` (resolve the single circle when the utterance mentions a circle at ALL — named or definite — but NOT for a circle-less line∩line, so no false grab) + `crossingAfterCircle` (name-optional anchor). Applied only to those two rules (the chord/tangent/two-crossing/external rules already fell back to the single circle). Full Q4 now completes deterministically; a plain "AC חותך את BD בנקודה E" with one circle present stays a line∩line. Locked by `unnamed-circle.test.ts` (positive + NEGATIVE) and the `unnamed-circle-secant-full-q4` end-to-end scenario. **1219 tests green, build clean.** (Note: `lineCutsCircleTwice` already uses `resolveCenter` — even MORE permissive — and is fine since "at C and D" = two crossings is inherently circle-shaped; left as-is.)

- **2026-06-22 (work PC) — "KB tangent to the circle" (no explicit touch point) finally fixed at root ([ADR-082](06-decisions.md#adr-082)).** Operator: this tangent was reported broken "3rd or 4th time" despite ADR-075/081. Diagnosed empirically from `logs/debug-log.jsonl` (sessions `xstllu0i`/`mmfbpvaz`): the failing utterance is **"KB משיק למעגל"** — tangent to the circle with **no "at K"** — returning `not-understood`. Every prior fix handled the *explicit-touch* phrasing ("KB tangent **at K**"); the natural form omits the touch because it's geometrically forced (K is already on the circle). Root cause was a **parser gate** (`tangentLine`: `if (!center || !atM) return null`) — the touch point was only ever read from an explicit "at X" clause; `tangentFromExternal` also bailed (both endpoints exist → no unique external apex). **The engine was never the problem** — fed the command it builds a true tangent (K on circle, OK⟂KB, KB∥CD, verifier clean), proven before touching code. Fix: when there's no "at" clause, **infer T from the segment endpoint that is a member of this circle** (`ctx.circleMembers`, exactly one — both → a chord, not a tangent); the existing ADR-081 branch then fires. Purely additive (explicit-"at" path byte-identical; no on-circle endpoint → still null, external tangent unchanged). Locked by the operator's exact 4-utterance sequence as a real end-to-end scenario (`scenarios.test.ts` → `segment-tangent-no-explicit-touch-point`). **1214 tests green, build clean.** Lesson reinforced: a green unit test on a *phrasing variant* is not proof the *feature* works — the operator's verbatim utterance through the real pipeline is the bar. (Housekeeping noticed, not yet done: many Dropbox conflict copies in `logs/` + one stale conflict copy of `phase5c.corpus.test.ts` in `src/engine/__tests__/` — safe to delete.)

- **2026-06-21 — OPERATOR IDEAS BACKLOG (captured for triage, NOT yet investigated or built).** The operator dictated a set of concerns + feature ideas. Items that already had a planned home were **folded into it** (no duplicate tracking) and are listed under "Already planned" below; only the genuinely-NEW items are tracked here. None diagnosed yet — **triage next session** (Phase 6 still gated).

  **NEW items (no existing home).**
  - **N1 — LLM escalation calibration ("error but image looks OK").** The LLM fires ONLY on a deterministic-parser `not-handled` (right architecture, [ADR-023](06-decisions.md#adr-023)). But the operator often sees "an error yet the image looks fine" — so the error/escalation SIGNAL may be miscalibrated: either (i) a build that visually succeeded still shows an error/amber, or (ii) a genuinely-wrong build never escalates because the parser "handled" it into something subtly wrong (parser-handled ⇒ never escalates). **Question:** is `not-handled`-only the right gate, vs also escalating (or amber-ing more honestly) on a verifier-`violations` / low-confidence parse? Capture concrete "error but fine" cases from `logs/debug-log.jsonl` first. (Relates to [ADR-053](06-decisions.md#adr-053) verifier + [ADR-055](06-decisions.md#adr-055) dry-run, but the *trigger-calibration* question is new.) **Sub-note:** also clarify+document the LLM's real role (freeform→canonical-commands decompose; typo-tolerance is a side effect, NOT the purpose — the operator assumed "it's for typos").
  - **N2 — seed quality.** "Is the seed part working well enough?" — revisit `sample.ts`: are initial placements good enough that clean configurations are reachable? ([ADR-078](06-decisions.md#adr-078) just fixed one seeder pathology — chord/secant jitter width, ~100× clean-config-rate lift; there may be sibling cases.)
  - **N3 — synonym handling (צלע / קטע / ישר …).** The parser should treat near-synonyms uniformly. NOTE this is NOT blanket — it collides with [ADR-077](06-decisions.md#adr-077) (a *segment* "AC" means the segment; `הישר` = infinite line; `המשך` = extension). So: accept synonyms WITHIN each meaning, but keep the segment/line/extension distinction. Define the synonym sets explicitly.
  - **N4 — clearer "working" / "searching" indicators.** A visible busy state while the engine builds, and a distinct one for "show another configuration" while it searches.
  - **N5 — clearer error messages.** A pass on error copy (He/En) — make failures actionable, not opaque.
  - **N6 — ★ auto-replay / step-through (operator: probably the most useful for HIS testing).** When a figure fails at step 4–5, the operator must retype every earlier step by hand to re-test a fix. Want: a control that **feeds the recorded steps one at a time** (press → next step entered + processed), advancing to any point, then hand off to manual entry. `logs/debug-log.jsonl` `figure` snapshots already hold the exact ordered `facts[].cmd` per session — the data source; build a **dev-only step-through panel** over it. **High testing-leverage, low risk.** Likely the best first pick.

  **Already planned → folded in (no separate item).**
  - **GUI decomposes a complex sentence? + image→steps** → **[ADR-073](06-decisions.md#adr-073) / [FR-IN-10](02-requirements.md)** (whole-problem decompose). Added there as operator-raised nuances: (a) surface/verify the produced ordered command list in the GUI; (b) an **image-of-the-problem** input is a second front-end to the same decompose path.
  - **False "point is fixed" feedback / DOF correctness** → the parked **ADR-052 DOF-conformance sweep** (a value counted by `rawMovableDof` but absent from `freeDofs` = a default masquerading as fixed) + the deferred DOF-cue inflation under [ADR-065](06-decisions.md#adr-065). Audit where the "fixed" feedback originates and whether it's truthful.
  - **"No more DOF" colour-coding** → **[FR-ALT-4](02-requirements.md)** — the `✓ fully determined` cue ALREADY EXISTS (ADR-018 Stage 3); the FR now also asks for a clear visual/colour emphasis at 0. (Partly done — needs only the visual emphasis.)

- **2026-06-21 — "the diameter from F cuts side AC at E" — E lands ON the segment (figure flexes), and "קוטר … cuts …" now parses ([ADR-077](06-decisions.md#adr-077)), 1201 green, build clean.** Same figure as ADR-075/076. Operator session `59tzde4c`: after the figure, `קוטר המעגל היוצא מנקודה F חותך את הצלע AC בנקודה E` (1) **escalated to the LLM → built nothing** (`lineLineIntersection` `stop`s on "קוטר"; no diameter-cuts-a-side rule), and (2) the operator's manual line∩line workaround put **E on the *continuation* of AC** (measured s≈1.148, just past C), not the segment — "I wanted E on AC, not its continuation; this would require F to move."
