Root cause: `line-line-intersection` crosses two INFINITE lines; nothing keeps the crossing within the named segment, and `dir1/dir2` only give *beyond*-style order, never the *between* order [A,E,C] that "the **side** AC" implies. **Fix:** new `diameterCutsSegment` rule (before `lineLineIntersection`) — the diameter through on-circle F is line F–O, so emit `line-line-intersection(E,F,O,A,C,dir1)`; and because the target is the **side**, also emit `set-line [A,E,C]` (E between A and C), a soft order constraint that drives a free DOF so the figure reshapes (free DOFs were `[A,C,B,H]`; F moves via the triangle) to bring E onto the segment. Draw chord F–E. "side ⇒ on the segment, line ⇒ anywhere on the line" carried by the operand keyword (הצלע/side vs הישר/line). **Verified:** parses deterministically, E lands at s≈0.879 on segment AC, collinear with F,O, violations empty. Locked: `scenarios.test.ts` `diameter-from-point-cuts-side-onto-segment` (operator's intended 5-step sequence) + `diameter-cuts-segment.test.ts` (He/En, side-vs-line, bare-diameter untouched); catalog entry added. **Amendment (session `50w3vlt3`, 1203 green): a BARE "AC" defaults to the segment.** The first cut only constrained E to the side when "הצלע"/"side" was explicit; the operator naturally wrote `קוטר המעגל מנקודה F חותך את AC בנקודה E` (bare "AC") → E went back to the extension ("still not working"). Operator stated the principle: **a segment reference like "AC" means the segment between A and C, not the continuation — UNLESS the user says `המשך` (the extension).** Fix: constrain E to the segment BY DEFAULT, with two keyword opt-outs — "המשך AC"/"extension" → E beyond the segment (dir2, order [A,C,E]); "הישר AC"/"line" → E free on the infinite line. Verified on the exact bare-"AC" wording (E at s≈0.879). **Scope/follow-up (noted, not done):** this segment-default principle is general but is implemented only in `diameterCutsSegment`; extending it to the general cut rules (`lineLineIntersection` cut-form, perpendicular/tangent cuts — used by scenarios like "EC חותך את AD בנקודה G") is a separate, carefully-regressed change.

- **2026-06-21 — "F, G, H on AB, AC, CB" (N points pairwise on N segments) now parses deterministically ([ADR-076](06-decisions.md#adr-076)), 1196 green, build clean.** Follow-up to ADR-075 on the same figure. Operator session `svjp9x5e`: right-triangle ABC, then `נקודות F, G, H נמצאות על הישרים AB, AC, CB` → **escalated to the LLM and built nothing** ("worked on a previous version"). Not a regression from ADR-075 (which only touched `tangentLine`); a genuine grammar gap. The deterministic parser had `pointOnSegment` (one point) and `pointsOnSegment` (two points on ONE segment) but **no rule for a LIST of points mapped pairwise to a LIST of segments** — so this figure-setup phrasing always escalated, and the live LLM was wording-sensitive (the earlier `הצלעות`/"sides" wording happened to yield three `point-on-segment`; `הישרים`/"lines" built nothing). **Fix:** new `pointsOnSegments` rule — split at the first "on"/"על", take UPPERCASE point labels before it and uppercase endpoint labels after, require exactly 2·N labels for N≥2 points, chunk into ordered pairs, emit `point-on-segment(P_i,a_i,b_i)`. Uppercase-only extraction (geometry convention) means lowercase words ("points/on/sides/lines") aren't read as labels, so He/En and "sides"/"lines" parse identically; a triangle side/line AB = segment AB. The 2·N guard makes it mutually exclusive with the two-on-one and singular rules; defers circle phrasings. Added to the catalog (coverage map). **Verified:** all five wordings parse to the three commands; the full ADR-075 figure now builds end-to-end through the deterministic parser (no LLM step). Locked: `points-on-segments.test.ts` (5 phrasings + non-steal guards) + the upgraded `existing-line-tangent-adapts-the-circle` scenario (its F,G,H step is now the real utterance, not an LLM mock). Lesson (per ADR-066): extending the deterministic vocabulary is more robust than leaning on the LLM — and it also extends what the LLM can compose.

- **2026-06-21 — "AB tangent at F" on an EXISTING line now flexes the circle instead of crashing ([ADR-075](06-decisions.md#adr-075)), 1187 green, build clean.** Operator session `ze5qda8y` (debug log): right-triangle ABC, F/G/H on sides AB/AC/CB, quad GCHF inscribed (circumcircle of G,C,H + F concyclic), then `AB משיק למעגל בנקודה F` → **errored** `unresolved dependencies for: A,B,F,G,H,O,tan-F,circle-O` (LLM then built nothing) — so the circle never adapted. Two-layer root cause: (1) the `tangentLine` parser rule treated the EXISTING segment AB as a *new drawn tangent* and re-created the triangle's vertices A,B as `point-on-line` markers on `tan-F`, closing a dependency cycle `A → tan-F → circle-O → O=circumcentre(G,C,H) → G(on AC) → A` (topological `evaluate` can't order it → keep-prior); (2) there was no path reading "existing line tangent to circle" as a circle-flexing CONSTRAINT — a `tangent` is only ever a line *derived from* the circle. **Fix:** when the line's naming labels (A,B) AND the touch point (F) all pre-exist, `tangentLine` now emits a tangency **constraint** `set-perpendicular(O,F,A,B)` (radius ⟂ the existing line, with F already on the circle via its concyclic incidence) and nothing else — no new object ⇒ no cycle; `driveOrCheck` flexes a free DOF (the t of G/H along the sides moving the circumcircle, and/or the triangle shape) so OF⟂AB holds. The new-label drawn-tangent path ("line CD tangent…") is unchanged, and a pre-existing label is now skipped when adding markers so the cycle can't recur from mixed naming. **Verified** end-to-end: lastError null, all steps ok, givens verifier clean (`violations:[]`), cos∠(OF,AB)≈−3.5e-8, |OF|=|OG|=|OH|=|OC| to 1e-7 (F on circle). **Scope (narrow, stated):** assumes T is already on the circle (the marked-touch-point case); a touch point not yet on the circle is a follow-up — `circleMembers` reports only *structural* membership (so a concyclic F isn't detectable there) and there's no clean single constraint to put an already-defined on-segment point onto a circle. Locked: `scenarios.test.ts` `existing-line-tangent-adapts-the-circle` (exact 4-step sequence) + `tangent-points.test.ts` (rule emits set-perpendicular / no point-on-line; drawn-tangent path unchanged). Diagnosed from the debug log; geometry validated via the Opus-oracle path (no live API).

- **2026-06-21 — HARDENING Pillars 4a/4b/4c (the gated tail), 1183 green, build clean.** Operator chose "Both, R7 last": low-risk robustness items first, then the deep R7 rework. **4a — extension-`t` sampling** ([ADR-074](06-decisions.md#adr-074) Am.1): an `extension:true` point's distance is unstated, so "show another configuration" now varies it (`isSamplableExtension`, t∈[1.15,2.2], in `freeDofs`) — closes the ADR-052 smell (counted by `rawMovableDof`, never sampled); still not eagerly driven; seed 0 holds t=1.3 so ADR-064/bagrut scenarios are unaffected. **4b — generated stability property** (`stability-property.test.ts`): the decidable slice — a purely ADDITIVE fact (midpoint/segment/free point/foot, no constraint) must leave every prior point byte-identical (<1e-9), a base×additive matrix (20). The full constraint-adding generated stability (needs a `movedAncestors` diagnostic) stays deferred — already guarded by the fixed-seed snapshot + campaign determinism. **4c — R7, done evidence-first (NOT a speculative rewrite):** a binding-fragility probe found TWO real false over-constraints — `triangle`+3 stated sides (equilateral/scalene) and `triangle`+2 angles+side (AAS) — both constructible yet rejected (greedy claims 2 vertices, the 3rd constraint finds none, the lone free vertex unreachable). **Fix ([ADR-074](06-decisions.md#adr-074) Am.2):** generalised the failure-path "free the blocker" from extension-only to `recruitableFreeDof` (any unclaimed movable carrier incl. a free vertex; still excludes pinned/stated-ratio per ADR-064) — `|AB|=5` re-binds to vertex A, freeing B for `|BC|=5`. Failure-path-only ⇒ stability snapshot green with **no `-u`**; genuine contradictions (square side 5 + diagonal 10) still rejected. Locked in `sweep-verify.test.ts`. **The full R7(3) "retire greedy + real per-DOF stay-put penalty" is deliberately NOT done** — the probe found no over-recruit DRIFT case (the penalty's purpose), so per the audit + CLAUDE.md rule 4 it stays a future dedicated hot-path session, NOT blocking "fully working + tested". **Pillar 5 (production proxy deploy + error UX) is the remaining pillar — it needs operator/ops involvement.**

- **2026-06-21 — HARDENING Pillar 1: the givens verifier is now COMPREHENSIVE (ADR-053 extended), 1110 green, build clean.** Per the operator's "full working + tested system, no rush, root causes" directive, started the hardening program (Phase 6 gated). Pillar 1 = make "green = VERIFIED" cover EVERY stated relation, not just on-circle/tangency. `checkGivens` ([src/engine/verify.ts](../src/engine/verify.ts)) now re-derives each metric/incidence relation a command asserts — distance, angle, equal, ratio, length-radius, angle-ratio, parallel, perpendicular, collinear, concyclic, + set-line collinearity — as a plain `Constraint` and checks it with the engine's own `isSatisfied` tolerance (reusing `residual`/`describeConstraint`/`constraintRefs`, NOT a parallel reimplementation). Soft one-sided ORDER constraints (angle-order/length-order/set-line ordering) deliberately excluded (config selectors, not hard givens — a marginal gap mustn't read as "wrong"). **Two locks:** (1) `verify.test.ts` +5 — proves each new check FIRES (a tampered distance/angle/⟂/∥/equal is flagged, a satisfied one isn't — so it's not a silent no-op); (2) the scenarios runner now asserts `fig.violations` empty for EVERY scenario (opt-out `expectViolations`), so a green-but-givens-violating figure fails the suite. **Discovery result: all 56 scenarios verify clean** — no silent-wrong figures in the existing corpus (the comprehensive verifier confirmed they genuinely match their givens, and they're now self-verifying regression). Engine `evaluate` already re-checks pushed constraints each step, so the verifier's standing value is (a) catching silently-DROPPED relations, (b) the corpus self-verification gate, (c) command-intent vs built-constraint mismatches.

  **Pillar 2 DONE (same session), 1135 green:** the corpus circle figures (Q5–Q7 + ~10 others, `phase5c.corpus.test.ts`) now self-verify too (`checkGivens`-clean wired into the `reproduce` helper), and a new `sweep-verify.test.ts` build+verifies a 25-figure spanning set across the constraint vocabulary (distance/angle/equal/ratio/∥/⟂/angle-ratio on real shapes, He+En). **Discovery: zero silent-wrong figures** anywhere (corpus + 56 scenarios + 25 sweep all satisfy their givens) — the existing figures are genuinely correct, now locked as standing self-verifying regression. The work-list of broken figures is EMPTY, which is the good outcome; deeper generative fragility-hunting is Pillar 3. **Next: Pillar 3** (property/negative-space tests, ADR-047 Tier B/C — generative over-constraint rejection + fast-check stability/determinism), then Pillar 4 (root-cause fragilities), Pillar 5 (proxy deploy).

- **2026-06-21 — R7 remainder CLOSED for the bagrut ∠ADB case: it was BINDING, not convergence ([ADR-074](06-decisions.md#adr-074)), 1105 green, build clean.** Operator picked the deep R7 solver remainder (the deferred [ADR-045](06-decisions.md#adr-045) step-3 item). **Reproduced first (throwaway test, deleted):** the real tangent-secant figure (circle free R, external apex A, secant AO→C,D, `G על המשך DB`, `AG⊥AD`, `∠ADB=<n>`) sat RIGIDLY at the seed 19.86° and reported `over-constrained` for every target. **Key finding that redirected the whole fix:** two experiments through the real parse→replay path — (a) drop AG⊥AD → ∠ADB=30 reaches 30.00° exactly; (b) introduce G via AG⊥AD+collinear instead of the extension command → 30.00° exactly — proved the coupled apex+radius solve **converges fine**. The 2026-06-21 deep-dive's "joint solve doesn't converge" conclusion (which led to two reverted solver-seeding patches) was the WRONG layer. A binding-state dump (gated `GEO_BIND_DEBUG`, then removed) confirmed the real cause: greedy `driveOrCheck` gives `AG⊥AD` the free apex `A`, and `G על המשך DB` freezes G at the default extension t=1.3 — so `∠ADB` finds no DOF. **The ADR-064 figure uses the IDENTICAL `pointOnExtension` construct** (D frozen — correctly, so `∠BOC=2α` drives the shape) → proved no per-point flag can distinguish "drive G" from "don't drag D"; the binding must be joint. **Fix (ADR-074), surgical + failure-path-only (so ADR-064 eager path is byte-identical, snapshot green with no `-u`):** (1) a `pointOnExtension` point carries a new `extension:true` flag — NOT `free` (never eagerly driven), only RECRUITABLE; (2) `recruitFreeDofs` gains case (D) "FREE THE BLOCKER": when a just-failed constraint K needs a DOF claimed by an earlier K1, and K1 references an `extension:true` on-segment Y not yet driving, re-point K1→Y and release the DOF to K (AG⊥AD drives G, freeing A for ∠ADB) → joint solve reaches the angle. **Verified:** ∠ADB=25/30/40 all exact, AG⊥AD + D-B-G collinearity hold; ADR-064 D stays t≈1.300; full suite 1105 green + build clean. Locked: scenario `bagrut-q4-numeric-angle-drives-the-figure`. **Still deferred (the genuinely larger R7 rework):** retiring the greedy apply-time pick into one up-front joint decision + a real per-DOF stay-put penalty (the existing λ=1e-3 sufficed here); and sampling the extension t on "show another configuration" (full ADR-052 — recruitable now, not yet sampled). Headline milestone still Phase 6 (theorems).

- **2026-06-21 — DESIGN (no code): the student never orders the construction — the LLM reorders + validates ([ADR-073](06-decisions.md#adr-073), FR-IN-10).** Operator showed bagrut **Q4** (quad BKCD, KB∥CD, CD a chord, KB tangent at K, BD∩circle=A, BC∩AK=E) and asked how a student — who won't plan ahead and types the problem "more or less as is" — should break it into commands. Diagnosed the real friction: **reading order ≠ construction order** (the organising **circle** is mentioned *second*, after a free quad places B,K,C,D, and is **implicit** — no centre/radius given), plus **coupled givens** (tangent at K + KB∥CD jointly force K to the arc-midpoint). A left-to-right topological pass can't absorb that order. **Operator's decision:** "if the user enters data in an order that's hard to construct, the LLM should reorder the steps so the result passes the validations — the user should not care about this part." Captured as **ADR-073**: the LLM produces an **ordered command list that the engine accepts AND the givens verifier ([ADR-053](06-decisions.md#adr-053)) confirms** — reordering into construction order + **inserting implicit objects** — in a **validate-and-repair loop** with the engine as oracle (propose → `parse→replay`+`checkGivens` → on a `not-handled`/`error`/`violations` feed the **specific failure record** back → bounded ≈2 retries → else honest message, never a silent/violating figure). **Guardrail:** reorder/insert ONLY — **never drop or weaken a stated given** to force green (same cardinal sin as a fixed default, [ADR-052](06-decisions.md#adr-052)); real contradictions surface as over-constraint. Specced the **failure-feedback contract** (`ValidationFeedback` = attempt/commands/outcome + `notHandled[]` | `error{atIndex,message}` | `violations[]`) so a retry is actionable. Consequence: a **whole-problem "paste" path** (decompose+reorder+strip question text; numbers = optional constraints) complements incremental entry. **Distinct from [ADR-049](06-decisions.md#adr-049)** (that rescues the *solver* on a hard-but-valid figure; this rescues *input ordering/decomposition*) — they compose. Synced: ADR-073 + FR-IN-10 + plan status line. **Next when built:** Opus-oracle test of the Q4 canonical order (circle→C,D+chord→K→tangent+∥ pin→B free on tangent→A=BD∩circle→E=BC∩AK) through the real parse→replay→verify path (no live API); two near-term enablers flagged — an **implicit/circumscribed circle** and a **tangent-at-P∥chord ⇒ arc-midpoint** recognizer. Proposed, not built; live path waits on the production proxy deploy.

- **2026-06-21 — edit a point's letter ON THE CANVAS: click to rename / hide ([ADR-072](06-decisions.md#adr-072), FR-RN-10), 1099 green, build clean.** Operator request: change an auto-assigned letter (engine gave `C`, want `E`) and REMOVE a letter the diagram shouldn't show (an intersection that's lettered but unmarked). Chosen UX: **click the point on the canvas** → small popover menu; "remove" hides **label + dot**, reversibly (a faint clickable ghost; click again to restore). Built as a pure **display layer** (zero engine blast radius): a UI-only store field `hidden: Id[]` + `toggleHidden` (sibling to `seed`/`showMeasures` — NOT undoable; un-hide by clicking the ghost), `rename`/`merge` rewrite it, `clear` resets it. Rename reuses the existing store `rename` ([ADR-035](06-decisions.md#adr-035), still a fact-list rewrite). `Figure.tsx`: a transparent hit-circle per point opens the menu (rename input + hide/show); a hidden point renders a dashed ghost ring; `scene.ts` is untouched so geometry/fit/label-placement don't reflow. i18n `pointMenu.*` (He/En). Tests: `hidden.test.ts` (toggle, rename/merge rewrite, clear) + a `phase2.test.tsx` ghost render. **Note:** the typed `rename C to E` already worked — the new part is the click affordance + hide. Limits: hide doesn't persist across reload / isn't undoable (by design). **Then (same session, operator: "when you press on a segment, show/hide or change to dashed"): extended to SEGMENTS** ([ADR-072](06-decisions.md#adr-072) amendment) — a UI-only `segStyle: Record<segId,{hidden?,dashed?}>` + `toggleSegHidden`/`toggleSegDashed` (independent flags), `rename`/`merge` rewrite the seg-id keys (`renameSegKey` re-derives `seg-XY` from the renamed re-sorted endpoints), the on-canvas menu generalised to `point|segment` (a wide transparent hit-line opens it), dashed renders via `stroke-dasharray` and hidden as a faint ghost line; `scene.ts` untouched (no reflow). i18n `segMenu.*`. 1104 green, build clean.

- **2026-06-21 — R7 step 3 SHIPPED (the high-value slice): a constraint can SHARE an over-subscribed claimed DOF ([ADR-045](06-decisions.md#adr-045) amendment), 1093 green, build clean.** After scoping R7, the operator manual-tested and the binding bug **recurred on a real figure** (session `qm0gbjhr`): parallelogram ABCD, F/E on extensions, G=EC∩AD, H=FD∩BC, `HF=4`, `GE=5`, then `מרובע ABHD בר חסימה במעגל` (concyclic) AND `BH=FH` — **both FALSELY "over-constrained"** though the figure had 6 DOF and each builds fine alone. Diagnosed (binding-state dump): `HF=4`/`GE=5` had CLAIMED every free vertex the new constraints could reach, and the ancestor walker treats a claimed free-point as a dead-end → returns empty → no carrier → false over-constraint. Crucially `HF=4` had **4 carriers (A,B,C,F) = over-subscribed = slack**. **Fix (minimal, principled R7 step 3):** the one `ancestors` walker gains an `includeSolving` flag; `recruitFreeDofs` case (C) — when a new constraint finds no FREE DOF — re-points one OVER-SUBSCRIBED claimed DOF (constraint with ≥2 carriers) to it, so it joins the existing joint solve (`resolveMixedCarriers` already solves the marked set jointly) while every prior constraint keeps a carrier. `driveOrCheck` untouched (stays the apply-time tie-break, per the audit). No slack reachable ⇒ genuinely over-constrained ⇒ fails honestly (verified: `HF=4` then `HF=9` still fails+keeps-prior; the full 4-constraint stack solves with `violations: []` from the ADR-053 verifier — a real green, not false). **Safe by construction:** fires ONLY on the already-failed path, so the stability snapshot is green with **no `-u`** (zero happy-path drift). Locked as scenarios `r7-concyclic-after-competing-distances` + `r7-equal-after-competing-distances`. **Still deferred (harder remainder):** retiring the greedy split into one up-front joint decision + per-DOF stay-put penalty, and the **coupled free-vertex+radius convergence** (the bagrut `∠ADB=<number>` case — binding correct, joint solve doesn't converge, tangled with ADR-064). This slice is the high-value/low-risk part; the rest stays a future gated session.

- **2026-06-21 — R7 deep-dive (operator: "start work on R7") → the reported bagrut failure is 3-layered, not a binding problem; Phase 1 = a symbolic radius is now FREE (ADR-052), 1091 green, build clean.** The operator chose to defer Phase 6 (theorems) and tighten the engine backbone, picking R7 ("decide constraint→DOF binding once") from the [2026-06-17 audit](13-design-audit-2026-06-17.md). **Investigation (no code changed first):** reproduced the deferred ADR-069 case — the bagrut-Q4 figure with `∠ADB = 30` (a NUMBER, not symbolic α) → "over-constrained, cannot hold", stuck at 19.86°. Proved 30° IS geometrically reachable (∠ADB = atan√((d−r)/(d+r)) ∈ (0°,45°), so d=2r gives 30°) → a solver/binding failure, not a real over-constraint. Isolation variants nailed the mechanism: the ISOLATED figure (circle+tangent+secant+∠ADB) solves the whole range exactly; the failure is **order-dependent** (∠ADB-then-AG⊥AD ✅; AG⊥AD-then-∠ADB ❌) — the greedy `driveOrCheck` claims the free apex A for whichever constraint comes first. **But** dumping the objects showed the deeper truth: **(layer 1)** `circle-O` was `radius.via=length` — "מעגל שרדיוסו R" pinned the radius (the ∠ADB DOF) — an ADR-052 violation; **(layer 2, non-issue)** once the radius is free the recruit reaches it fine via `circlesOfPoint`; **(layer 3, the hard core)** even with a free radius + correct joint binding (A drives AG⊥AD, radius drives ∠ADB, pooled by `recruitFreeDofs`), the coupled free-vertex+radius **joint solve doesn't converge** (the binding-aware Gauss-Seidel sweep in `resolveMixedCarriers` only sweeps BOUNDED carriers, never re-solves a free vertex against its own constraint). So binding is NOT the bottleneck for the reported case — free-radius policy + solver convergence are. Operator decisions: symbolic radius → **Free (ADR-052)**; sequence → **ADR-052 conformance first, then the solver**. **Phase 1 shipped:** `freeRadius = !r.numeric` in both circle rules + DROP the `set-var R` so R stays unvalued and "AB = √2R" couples to the free radius via [ADR-071](06-decisions.md#adr-071) (vs freezing it). Blast radius: exactly 3 `reserved radius symbol R/r` tests, updated to the new scale-free behavior (|AC| = 1.6·radius, not the artifact 8). Audit result: the symbolic radius was the ONLY remaining pinned-should-be-free magnitude (points/shapes conform; the reverse DOF-cue inflation is deferred under [ADR-065](06-decisions.md#adr-065)). [ADR-052](06-decisions.md#adr-052) living-audit note updated. **Phase 2 attempted + reverted (kept clean; Phase 1 stands alone):** dug into the coupled solve and proved it is NOT a simple convergence tweak. Tried (a) extending the binding-aware Gauss-Seidel sweep to free vertices via a per-constraint local 2-D descent — **diverged** (cost 7.9→103; a 2-DOF vertex under ONE constraint has degenerate minima); (b) adding the free vertex's coords as coordinate-descent axes on the JOINT cost — **stuck at a coordinate-wise local min** (the solution is off the symmetric seed in a diagonal direction). Then the GEOMETRY settled it: with G **pinned at t=1.3** ("G על המשך DB" defaults t=1.3, no `free` flag — another latent ADR-052 default), ∠ADB=30 (⇒ d=2r) and AG⟂AD (⇒ 0.3dr+1.3r²=d²) are **genuinely incompatible** (1.9r²=4r²) — so the "over-constrained" message is actually CORRECT for the current wording. Freeing G (so AG⟂AD slides it) makes a solution exist *in principle*, but (i) the coupled [free apex A / sliding G / free radius] solve still didn't converge through the current solver, and (ii) **naively freeing extension points re-broke ADR-064 in a prior session** ([ADR-069](06-decisions.md#adr-069)). So the bagrut `∠ADB=<number>` case is the genuine, multiply-deferred **R7 binding tangle**: which DOF a constraint drives (G's extension slide vs the shape) must be decided jointly, without pinning an extension point a later constraint should determine, AND without dragging an extension point the student fixed (ADR-064). **★ NEXT — this needs the real "decide binding once jointly" R7 rework** (audit R7 step 3 / [ADR-045](06-decisions.md#adr-045)), as its own focused, snapshot-gated session — NOT a solver-seeding patch (proven not to work). Note: the textbook uses the SYMBOLIC `∠ADB=α` form, which already builds clean ([ADR-069](06-decisions.md#adr-069)); the numeric form is the stress case. Phase 1 (free symbolic radius) is committed and independent of all this.

- **2026-06-20 — a concyclic constraint FLEXES the figure (no fixed-size assumption) ([ADR-070](06-decisions.md#adr-070)), 1089 green.** Operator's figure: rectangle ABCD, E on AD, F=CE∩DB, "EABF בר חסימה במעגל". First ERRORED "unresolved dependencies for: E,F,O,circle-O" (a cycle: hidden circumcircle through E ⇒ O depends on E; F depends on E; constraint drives E). Then silently failed — EABF concyclic needs CE⟂BD, impossible for the SHORT default rectangle. Operator (rightly) pushed back: "why must the rectangle be short? why can't the engine change it?" — that's ADR-052 (the rectangle's size is a free DOF, flex it). Two fixes: (1) extend the ADR-062 coupled-promotion to a SELF-coupling (a solved-on-segment whose constraint references a point that transitively depends on it — `dependsOn` scrapes object point-fields) → route E to the numeric solver, breaking the cycle; (2) keep `concyclic` as a CHECK in driveOrCheck's on-segment branch, so when E alone can't satisfy it, applyStep's recruit-more-DOFs fallback grows a free SHAPE DOF (the rectangle's height, reached via F→C=perp-offset) and joint-solves. Result: the rectangle reshapes (~2.7×5.1), E stays on AD, ABCD stays a rectangle, EABF genuinely concyclic. Regression `concyclic-flexes-the-rectangle`. (One slice of the joint-DOF solve flagged in ADR-069; doesn't yet cover the indirect-angle case.)

- **2026-06-20 — bagrut Q4 (tangent-secant-perpendicular) reproduces; external apex is a free DOF ([ADR-069](06-decisions.md#adr-069)), 1088 green.** Operator showed the actual textbook figure (circle R, external A, tangent AB at B, AD through O cutting at C/D, AG⟂AD, D-B-G collinear, ∠ADB=α): "it's supposed to be solvable." It IS — and the engine reproduces it with the book's OWN given, the SYMBOLIC `∠ADB=α` (a label, not a number): err=null, D-B-G collinear, AG⟂AD, C/D on the secant through O — matches the drawing. The operator's earlier failures were from stacking NUMERIC givens (`AG=8` AND `AC=0.5DC`) onto a shape-determined figure (too little residual freedom). Two findings: (1) `tangentFromExternal` PINNED the external apex A, but A's distance is unstated → should be FREE (ADR-052); fixed (single-tangent apex now `free`, matching the two-tangent rule; no test regressions; a single length given like `AG=8` now flexes A). (2) Setting `∠ADB` to a NUMBER still over-constrains — the angle depends on A only indirectly (through B and D), and the solver can't flex A through that chain. The real fix (joint-solve / drive whichever DOF satisfies each constraint) is deferred; an attempt to also free the EXTENSION point G was reverted (re-broke ADR-064 — a relation that can't be satisfied by sliding the extension point must drive the shape; both need the same carrier-fallback). Regression `bagrut-q4-tangent-secant-perpendicular`.

- **2026-06-20 — no phantom circle from a centre reference; a named line can cut the circle twice ([ADR-068](06-decisions.md#adr-068)), 1087 green.** Tangent-secant figure. (1) "ישר AD עובר דרך מרכז המעגל" built a PHANTOM circle P — the `circle` rule's `centred` trigger matched "מרכז" even in "מרכז המעגל" (a REFERENCE to the existing circle's centre, not a definition). Fix: `centred` is a circle definition only when a centre is actually NAMED; all real circle definitions unchanged. (2) "AO חותך את המעגל בנקודות C ו-D" (a named line cutting the circle at TWO points) had no rule (lineMeetsCircle = one crossing; secantFromExternal needs "from a point"). Fix: `lineCutsCircleTwice` → line-through + a line-circle-intersection per branch + the secant; resolves the circle by name or the single context circle. Builds C,D on the circle, collinear with A–O (a secant through the centre). Catalog entry + regressions `line-through-center-and-secant.test.ts` (5). Diagnosed via Opus-oracle (no API call).
- **2026-06-20 — a perpendicular operand is never silently read as a plain line ([ADR-067](06-decisions.md#adr-067)), 1082 green.** Operator: "המשך DB והאנך לישר AD נפגשים בנקודה G" produced a wrong/degenerate figure (then "DG" errored). Root cause: the generic line∩line rule grabbed the 4 labels (D,B,A,D) and **silently dropped "אנך"** — reading "the perpendicular to AD" as "line AD"; since AD and DB share endpoint D, the crossing collapsed onto D so G couldn't build. Deeper: "the perpendicular to AD" gives NO through-point — the figure only works with the ⟂ at A. Fixes: (1) `lineLineIntersection` defers when a ⟂/∥ modifier (אנך/מאונך/מקביל/⊥/⟂/∥/perpendicular/parallel) is present — never mangle the modifier into a plain line; (2) the `perpendicular … cuts … at` form (ADR-061) now accepts a "המשך"/extension cut target, so the explicit "האנך ל-AD בנקודה A חותך את המשך DB בנקודה G" builds G = (⟂ to AD at A) ∩ line DB (verified). The ambiguous one-liner now escalates (the LLM can infer the through-point and emit the cut form, which parses). Regression `perpendicular-cuts-extension.test.ts` (4). Diagnosed via the Opus-oracle path (no API call — per the no-autonomous-api-calls rule).
- **2026-06-20 — the LLM composes the tangential triangle (ADR-066 amendment), 1078 green.** Operator asked: the long sentence "דרך כל קודקוד של משולש ABC מעבירים משיק למעגל; המשיקים נפגשים ב-D E F" won't parse deterministically, but can the LLM break it down? YES — and it's the intended design. The LLM's vocabulary IS the supported catalog, which now includes `twoTangentsMeet`, so it decomposes the "each vertex" quantifier into THREE "tangent at X and tangent at Y meet at Z" lines → builds the tangential triangle DEF (verified end-to-end: all 6 tangent segments ⟂ their radius). Added a prompt example (He+En) so Haiku reliably does the expand-and-pair. Key lesson recorded in the ADR: extending the deterministic vocabulary automatically extends what the LLM can compose — the layers compound. Regression `tangential-triangle-via-llm-decomposition`.
- **2026-06-20 — triangle-circumscribes-circle (incircle) + two-tangents-meet ([ADR-066](06-decisions.md#adr-066)), 1077 green.** Two operator gaps. (1) "משולש DEF חוסם את המעגל" (triangle circumscribes the circle) was misparsed to a CIRCUMCIRCLE (circle through D,E,F) — backwards; it's the same figure as the incircle (circle tangent to the 3 sides). Fix: the `incircle` rule also matches the triangle-first phrasing, ORDERED (triangle-labels … חוסם/circumscribes … circle) so a circle-first "מעגל חוסם משולש" stays a circumcircle. Limit: builds a fresh incircle of DEF; tying to a named existing circle (tangential triangle) is a follow-up. (2) "המשיק מנקודה A והמשיק מנקודה C נפגשים בנקודה D" (two tangents meeting) had no rule (only tangent∩segment) → LLM → built-nothing. Fix: `twoTangentsMeet` emits the tangent at each on-circle point + a `line-intersection` (D = pole of chord AC); tolerates the מנקדה typo. Catalog entries + regressions `triangle-circumscribes-circle-is-incircle`, `two-tangents-meet-at-a-point`.
- **2026-06-20 — "show another configuration" reports when there's no different drawing ([ADR-065](06-decisions.md#adr-065)), 1075 green.** Operator: "5 DOF but every 'show another' gives the same figure — need an on-screen indication whether something is/isn't happening." Diagnosed: their figure's SHAPE is determined (isosceles in circle + ∠BAC=2∠CAD fixes the apex angle); ∠CAD=21.7°/∠BAC=43.4° are identical at every seed — only similarity (size/rotation) varies. `resample` advanced to the first VALID seed without checking it looked DIFFERENT, so the button felt dead. Fix: `resample` now compares candidates via a similarity-invariant **shape fingerprint** (pairwise distances normalised by their mean) and accepts only a genuinely-different drawing; returns `true`/`false`. App shows a transient note on `false`: "No other configuration — the shape is determined (only size/position can change)". The ∠BAC=2α constraint DID apply on first entry (ratio 2.0 at every seed) — the "didn't adjust" perception was the subtle change + no feedback, now addressed. **Reverted a scope-creep detour:** I tried to also stop the DOF *count* inflating (exclude a stated-position param from `rawMovableDof`/`recruitFreeDofs`) but it over-constrained on-circle figures that legitimately recruit such freedom — and "extend BC to D" gives a FREE extension point, not a stated one, so dragging it is fine. DOF-count honesty deferred. Regression `dof-radius.test.ts` (resample returns true on a free triangle, false on the shape-determined figure).
- **2026-06-20 — the REAL α/2α bug: only a FREE on-segment point is driveable ([ADR-064](06-decisions.md#adr-064)), 1073 green.** Operator clarified they used the α GLYPH (not spelled "alpha"), so ADR-063 wasn't their bug. Reproduced their figure (isosceles AB=AC in circumcircle O, D on extension of BC at t=1.3, ∠CAD=α) + a central angle ∠BOC=2α → ERROR "cannot place D on segment BC so that ∠BOC=2·∠CAD". Root cause: `driveOrCheck` step (1) drove D (the first on-segment ref) to satisfy the relation, but D is a GIVEN the student positioned (an extension point t=1.3), not a DOF — and a central angle can't be met by sliding D. Fix: step (1) drives only `free === true` on-segment points (the parser's mark for "no stated ratio"); a stated-ratio/extension point is left put, so the relation drives the triangle's free shape and D stays at 1.3 (∠BOC=2∠CAD holds). One store test (ADR-043 branchable driven on-segment) used a non-parser representation (explicit t, no free) → realigned to the free form (omit t); behaviour unchanged. The driveability test is now "is this a free DOF?" not "is this a parametric kind?". Regression `scenarios.test.ts` `symbolic-2alpha-drives-shape-not-the-fixed-point`. (ADR-063 spelled-out-Greek fix stays — a genuine separate improvement.)
- **2026-06-20 — spelled-out Greek names normalise to symbols ([ADR-063](06-decisions.md#adr-063)), 1073 green.** Operator: "I enter alpha for one angle then try 2alpha to another and the result is wrong." Root cause: a symbolic var is a single Greek letter (`[α-ω]`), so spelled-out "alpha" missed the symbolic-measure rule — `∠BAC=alpha` → not-handled, and `∠ABC=2alpha` was then caught by the numeric `angle` rule which read "2" as **2 degrees** and dropped "alpha" → a 2° sliver, not 2α (the ADR-024/026 numeric-prefix half-parse). Fix: `normalizeGreek` at the `parse()` entry maps alpha→α … omega→ω before any rule, so the single-letter machinery works unchanged. Guards: lowercase-only keys (uppercase point pairs "MU"/"XI" safe) and "not-a-letter" lookarounds rather than `\b` so a DIGIT prefix counts ("2alpha"→"2α") but "alphabet" doesn't; "pi" omitted (collides with point PI). Covers every rule at once (incl. `alpha<beta`→`α<β`). The simple α/2α symbolic case ALWAYS worked — the bug was strictly the spelled-out form. Regression: `corner-circle-and-ratio.test.ts` (Greek block) + `scenarios.test.ts` `spelled-out-alpha-then-2alpha`.
- **2026-06-20 — coupled chained equalities (AL=LK=KC) joint-solve ([ADR-062](06-decisions.md#adr-062)), 1068 green.** Operator: "support a case such as AL=LK=KC — multiple equalities." The parser already chained it (AL=LK + LK=KC), but the figure ERRORED "unresolved dependencies for: L, K". Root cause: `driveOrCheck` upgrades a free on-segment point pinned by an equality to closed-form `on-segment-solved`; with a chain, AL=LK drives L and LK=KC drives K, but each references the OTHER → L needs K, K needs L, a cycle the topological evaluator can't break, and `resolveDriven` only routes plain `on-segment` (not `on-segment-solved`) to the numeric joint solver. Fix: `resolveDriven` detects coupled solved-on-segment points (constraint references another solved point), promotes that group to numeric `on-segment` carriers (seeded at `t0`), and re-enters → they joint-solve via `resolveMixedCarriers`. Closed-form path untouched for the uncoupled common case. Now `AL=LK=KC` trisects AC, `AC=CD=DB` trisects AB — n-section falls out of the general solver. Regression `scenarios.test.ts` `chained-equality-trisects-segment`.
- **2026-06-20 — a perpendicular that "cuts AB at E" creates the crossing ([ADR-061](06-decisions.md#adr-061)), 1067 green.** Operator tried several ways to create a perpendicular segment EK and "it just drew a line." "האנך ל-AC בנקודה K חותך את AB בנקודה E" parsed to a SINGLE `perpendicular-line` — the rule built the line through K but dropped the "cuts AB at E" clause, so E was never placed. Fix: `perpendicularLine` now detects a cut clause (cut verb + segment + result point; the cut verb anchors so the 2nd "בנקודה" is the result, not the through-point), builds the perpendicular as scaffolding (visible:false — the student wants the segment, not a long line), crosses it with AB via `line-through`+`line-intersection` to place E, and draws segment K–E. Mirrors `tangentLineIntersection`. The inverse phrasing "from E on AB drop a perpendicular to K" still escalates to the LLM (follow-up). Regression `scenarios.test.ts` `perpendicular-cuts-segment-at-new-point`.
- **2026-06-20 — two points on one segment ([ADR-060](06-decisions.md#adr-060)), 1066 green.** Operator: "why can't I add 2 points on a segment? tried 2 ways, both failed." Way 1 "L ו-K נקודות על AC" → LLM → built-nothing; way 2 one-at-a-time → the SECOND "K על AC" errored "L and K would be at the same point". Root cause: a free `point-on-segment` always seeded t=0.5, so the 2nd collided with the 1st (ADR-017 guard). Fix: `freeSegT` seeds a new free point in the middle of the largest open gap among the segment's existing points (0.5 → 0.25 → 0.75 → …), so any number spread; plus a `pointsOnSegment` parser rule for the combined "L and K are points on AC" / "L ו-K נקודות על AC". **JS-regex gotcha (recorded in the ADR):** the Hebrew "points" word matched `נקוד\w*` but `\w` is **ASCII-only** in JS → it stopped after "נקוד" and broke the match; Hebrew fragments need `[א-ת]*`. Flagged for a wider audit (other rules may have the same latent bug). Regression `scenarios.test.ts` `two-points-on-one-segment`.
- **2026-06-20 — a "show circle centres" view toggle ([ADR-059](06-decisions.md#adr-059)), 1065 green.** Operator: "with two circles, unless we have the centres like O and P it's impossible to know how to address each circle — we should be able to show/hide the centres." FR-RN-8 hides an unused auto-centre (keeps a single figure tidy) but backfires with two unnamed circles (both centres hidden → can't tell O from P). Fix: a **view-level toggle** (default off, ⊙ button beside rotate/flip in the figure toolbar) that reveals every circle's centre with its label — `buildScene(…, { showCenters })` skips the unused-auto-centre suppression. Pure display preference: no engine/fact/undo state, lives in the renderer's local `view`. Auto-centres already carry labels (O, P via `freeLabel`), so flipping it on names the circles on the canvas. Global toggle (not per-circle) — the 80% solution for "tell them apart"; per-circle is a noted follow-up. Regression in `lines-circles.test.tsx`.
- **2026-06-20 — an extension-meet draws both lines to the meeting point ([ADR-054](06-decisions.md#adr-054) A2), 1064 green.** Operator: "המשך CA ו-BD נפגשים בנקודה G — should also draw the lines up to G." The crossing G was placed, but `lineLineIntersection` drew the bare operand segments (CA, BD), which stop at the inner points and don't reach G (operator drew CG/BG by hand). Fix: when an extension word (המשך/extension) is present, draw each line base→G (C→G, B→G) — the inner points A, D then lie *on* those segments so the extension shows — and emit the `line-line-intersection` (which DEFINES G) **before** the segments (else a segment to a not-yet-defined G creates a stray free point → "'G' is already defined" conflict, which I hit first and fixed by reordering). A plain diagonals crossing without an extension word ("M = intersection of AC and BD", M between) is untouched. Regression `scenarios.test.ts` `extension-meet-draws-lines-to-G`.
- **2026-06-20 — three manual-test answers: corner circle needs no named circle (ADR-057 A2), √ in a ratio (ADR-058), 1063 green.** Operator asked three questions about the corner-circle session. **Q1 ("AB וAD משיקים למעגל failed; a space fixed it; shouldn't the LLM be called?"):** the space was a **red herring** — all spacings fail when no circle is named, because the rule required `resolveCenter` to find one. The LLM **is** called on `not-handled` (it built the figure; the "failure" was its latency). Fix ([ADR-057](06-decisions.md#adr-057) A2): `cornerTangentCircle` **auto-names** a fresh centre when none is given (incircle pattern), so it builds offline either way. **Q2 ("how did it know E and K — did it remember K?"):** **no** cross-run memory — E and K are the rule's fixed default tangency-point labels (prefer E→F/G, K→M/N). **Q3 ("EB/AE=√2/2 didn't work — does √ need parens?"):** **no** — it silently misparsed to `AE=√2` (a wrong length, dropping `EB/` and `/2`). Two root causes ([ADR-058](06-decisions.md#adr-058)): `segmentRatio`'s RHS took only numeric fractions, and when it fell through, the measure-VALUE rules grabbed the `AE=√2` **fragment** (the ADR-024/026 class). Fix: `segmentRatio` now reads a `√` on the numerator/denominator (`√2/2`, `√2`, `1/√2`), and a shared `SEG_RATIO_LHS` guard bars every length rule from fragment-matching a ratio — so an unreadable RHS like `(√2)/2` fails honestly to the LLM instead of misparsing. Regression: `corner-circle-and-ratio.test.ts` (11).
- **2026-06-20 — corner circle, cont. ([ADR-057](06-decisions.md#adr-057) A1): putting an EXISTING vertex on it GROWS it, 1052 green.** Operator then added "C נמצאת על המעגל" (vertex C on the corner circle) → built nothing ("why isn't C adjusted to be on the circle?"). C is a derived rectangle vertex (can't slide onto the circle), so `point-on-circle` apply hit its give-up branch (case c). But the corner circle has a **free size DOF** (the centre's slide along the bisector). Fix: in case (c), when the circle's radius is set by a point T on it (`circle-through` — which the corner circle is), "P on circle" ⟺ `|centre·P| = |centre·T|` is pushed as an `equal`; `driveOrCheck` routes it to the centre's free on-line offset and grows the circle until P lands on it — tangency preserved (centre stays on its bisector). No new mechanism. Plus a parser broadening: `pointOnCircle` resolves a **definite/unnamed** circle ("על המעגל" / "is on the circle") via the single circle in context, so it parses deterministically (the operator's phrasings "C על המעגל", "נקודה C נמצאת על המעגל", "point C is on the circle" all parse now; 0 or 2+ unnamed circles still defer). Regression `scenarios.test.ts` `corner-tangent-circle-grows-to-vertex`.
- **2026-06-20 — new construct: a circle tangent to two GIVEN sides (inscribed-corner circle), [ADR-057](06-decisions.md#adr-057), 1051 green, build clean.** Operator entered "מלבן ABCD" then "AB ו-AD משיקים למעגל O" — it escalated to the LLM and **built nothing**, and the operator asked whether the LLM should decompose such a sentence. **Diagnosis (from the debug log, session d0utx8bw):** not an LLM failure — a **missing primitive**. Every "tangent" the engine knew was *circle → tangent* (tangent line to a given circle / tangents from a point / two circles tangent). The operator's sentence is the **inverse** — the *sides* are given and the *circle* must be constrained tangent to them — and no command expressed "tangent to a given line", so the LLM (which may only emit canonical commands) correctly built nothing. **Fix — composed from existing primitives, no engine change (like the incircle):** new parser rule `cornerTangentCircle` (runs before the tangent/line rules, since the משיק keyword makes `lineLineIntersection` 'stop'). For two sides sharing a vertex it emits a `bisector` of the corner, a **free `point-on-line`** centre on it (the size is a sampled free DOF — [ADR-052](06-decisions.md#adr-052); "show another configuration" grows/shrinks it), two `foot` tangency points (named or auto), and a `circle-through` the first foot ⇒ r = distance to that side; the other side is tangent automatically (centre equidistant on the bisector). No new `Command`/`RadiusSpec`/object kind, so engine/evaluator/renderer/sampler/undo carry it for free; tangency is **structural** (can't drift → no verifier check needed). **Scope (v1):** sides must share a vertex (a corner); parallel/opposite sides fall through. **Refinement deferred:** bound the offset DOF so a large resample can't push a tangency point past a short side's end ([ADR-048](06-decisions.md#adr-048) applied to this DOF). Catalog entry added (both locales parse — guard green); regression `scenarios.test.ts` `corner-tangent-circle` (the operator's exact 2-step sequence, asserting |OE|=|OK|, feet on the sides, OE⟂AB & OK⟂AD).
- **2026-06-20 — manual-testing fixes: diameter no longer false-escalates, + a symmetric constraint keeps the quad convex (ADR-056), 1050 green, build clean.** Continued the manual-testing marathon. **(1) `קוטר` "not recognized" — a false LLM escalation.** `AB קוטר` on a cyclic quad parsed fine, but `dryRunOutcome` ([ADR-055](06-decisions.md#adr-055)) read the step as "produced nothing" and bounced it to the LLM (which then failed). Root cause: a *diameter* on an existing inscribed quad adds **no new object** — it RESHAPES (B → antipode of A). The "did it build something?" check only counted new objects/constraints/labels, so a pure reshape looked empty. Fix: `dryRunOutcome` now also counts a step that **moved any position** (`after.positions` differs from `before` by >1e-6, or the count changed) as produced. Locked in `dry-run.test.ts` (the diameter-on-cyclic-quad case). **(2) "OD⊥AC messed the shape up — it was good up to that point" ([ADR-056](06-decisions.md#adr-056)).** Cyclic quad ABCD in O, AB diameter, E on ext(AD) with CE⊥AE, then `OD⊥AC` — applied with no error yet **crossed the quad**. Root cause: `OD⊥AC` has two roots (the two arc-midpoints of AC, half a circle apart); D sat at ≈330° (already near-perpendicular, residual ≈0) but the 1-DOF driven solve (`resolveDriven`) returned `roots[0]` = the FAR root ≈148°, which lands between A(30°) and B(210°) and re-orders the vertices into a self-intersecting quad. The solve had **no stability preference**. Fix: when **no order constraint** rides the carrier, order the roots by **nearness to the carrier's current parameter** (circular for on-circle θ, linear for on-segment t) so branch 0 is the smallest move — D nudges to ≈328°, quad stays convex, OD⊥AC still holds; "show another configuration" still reaches the far root. This is [ADR-028](06-decisions.md#adr-028) stability applied to driven-root choice (previously honoured only by accident of root order); the order-constraint path (directional cuts) is untouched. Regression: `scenarios.test.ts` `perp-constraint-keeps-quad-convex` (the operator's exact 4-step sequence, asserting `convexQuad` + OD⊥AC holds). **Resume — the two deep Wave-3 reworks remain parked** (R4 Stage 2 `DerivedVertex` fold, R7(3) binding unification); headline milestone Phase 6 (theorems).

- **2026-06-19 — debug-log analytics → grammar wins + Wave 3 R8 step 1, 1029 green, build clean.** Operator asked "how many calls really make it through the engine vs go to the LLM." The irreducible (genuine freeform + typos) is ~15%. **Grammar wins:** (1) a **bare-segment catch-all** — `AB` / `line AB` / `ישר AB` → `segment` (anchored to the WHOLE input so it never shadows `AB=6`, `AB⊥CD`, `line ABE`, runs last before `freePoint`); (2) `⟂` (U+27C2) accepted for perpendicular alongside `⊥` (U+22A5). Locked as `bare-segment.test.ts` (5) + a `collinear.test.ts` assertion updated (`line AB` is now a segment, not not-handled). These alone would have turned ~42 of the 111 LLM calls into free offline parses. **Wave 3 R8 step 1 (audit §8):** extracted the line∩circle `avoid` one-off into a shared, exported **`otherCrossing(sols, placed, avoid, branch)`** (geometric "keep the crossing that isn't the known point"; **property test `other-crossing.test.ts` written FIRST**, per the audit). line∩circle routes through it (behaviour-preserving — stability snapshot unchanged); **circle∩circle gained an `avoid`** so `twoCirclesMeet`'s B is "the crossing that isn't A" (geometric, not branch-order — the R8 robustness goal), with `step.ts`'s not-cyclable special case widened to both kinds. **Remaining R8:** the signed-side geometric KEY so `branch` selects "the + side"; then circle∩circle's `cyclableBranch=false` workaround retires. Audit tracker (§8) updated. **Then continued through Wave 3 (operator: "complete this phase"), 1033 green, build clean:** **R7(1)** — three ancestor walkers → one `ancestors(objects, start, mode, includeStart)` (`'param'` ⊂ `'drivable'`; singular now skips a solving carrier). **R8 step 2** — signed-side `bySide` geometric key (line∩circle `branch` selects a stable side along the line dir; property test first); **R8 DONE**. **R9(b)** — `circleMembers` accumulates across LLM steps (`accrueMembers`), so "arc BC" resolves to a circle an earlier LLM step made; regression in `llm.test.ts`; **R9 DONE**. **R7(2)** — `reinterpretDiameter` + `reinterpretAsConstraint` share `driveCoincideOn` (antipode pin kept). **Stopped before R4 Stage 2 + R7(3) — deliberately.** Both are the audit's "deliberate, unhurried / gate it" deep reworks: R4 Stage 2 (fold 4 vertex kinds → `DerivedVertex`) is ~44 sites across 6 engine files with delicate per-kind solver seed/scale and ZERO user-facing benefit; R7(3) (unify apply-pick + recruit + stay-put penalty) is the hot solver path. Rushing either right before manual testing risks a subtle regression the fixed-seed snapshot could miss. **Resume — the two deep Wave-3 reworks (each its own focused, snapshot-gated session):** R4 Stage 2 (`DerivedVertex` fold), R7(3) (binding unification). Optional tail: R6(b) construct-vs-constrain parser fold (family by family), Test Tier C (`movedAncestors` + fast-check). Headline milestone still Phase 6 (theorems).

  **Then — manual test (session 0mjr1ots): cut-form line∩line + English-step UX, 1038 green.** Operator typed `המשך BD חותך את המשך OC בנקודה A` (A = line BD ∩ line OC). It **escalated to the LLM** (the parser only knew "BD **and** OC intersect at A", not "BD **cuts** OC at A" — the verb BETWEEN the segments), and the LLM rewrote it **in English**, lossily, as "point A on the extension of BD and on the extension of OC"; the parser then matched only the first clause (`point-on-segment` at t=1.3) and **dropped the OC half** → wrong point. **Two fixes:** (1) added the **cut-form** to `lineLineIntersection` (seg1 · cut-verb · seg2 · point → line∩line; `חות[כך]`/cuts/crosses/meets, "extension"/`המשך` ignored) — so it parses deterministically, stays Hebrew, places A right; (2) **UX** — an LLM-handled input now shows as ONE step row labelled by the **student's original utterance** (all built commands share one group, like a deterministic multi-command parse), never the LLM's English canonical lines; editing the row re-runs the original wording (which now parses directly). Fix:** a `המשך`/extension operand now sets `dir1`/`dir2` on `line-line-intersection` (detected per operand by which side of the cut verb the word falls on); `evaluate` checks the crossing is BEYOND the named 2nd point (proj > 1) and, if not, returns "the extensions of XY and ZW point away from each other — they don't meet" (operator chose **(a)**: clear message, figure unchanged — over **(b)** adapt). Locked: `phase4.test.ts` (directional + one-sided + non-directional). **Then operator showed the textbook (bagrut Q5) — it DOES work, "where is the mistake?" — and the earlier "impossible by symmetry" diagnosis was WRONG.** It's configuration-dependent: rendered the figure to an SVG + swept D's distance and found tangents-from-D meet beyond D/C only when D is CLOSE (D=1.2R → clean A=(10.7,−7.1) like the textbook; D=2.4R → wrong side; a parallel singularity at ≈1.4R between the basins). The construct's default put D at 2.4R (wrong basin). **Tried (b) adapt-the-figure** (emit `collinear-order` → `recruitFreeDofs` drives D): it DOES reach a beyond-D/beyond-C config, but seeded from the far D and blocked by the singularity it lands an ugly near-parallel solution (A flung to (44,−37)). **Shipped instead an actionable check:** wrong-side crossing → "…cross on the wrong side here — move the external point closer to the circle so the extensions reach A" (honest about the current config, no false impossibility); positioning D close makes it land cleanly. **Operator pushed back: "a free DOF should be SOLVED by the engine, not by the user moving D."** Right. **Shipped fix (two parts):** (1) the directional operand emits a `collinear-order` so `recruitFreeDofs` **drives the free apex** until the extensions reach A (no manual move); (2) `tangentsFromExternal` now seeds the apex **CLOSE** (x:6 ≈ 1.2R, like a textbook sketch) instead of far (x:12 ≈ 2.4R). Verified end-to-end through the REAL parse→replay path: the operator's exact 3 utterances (`מעגל O` / `מנקודה D שני משיקים למעגל בנקודות B ו C` — parses deterministically now / `המשך BD חותך את המשך OC בנקודה A`) build CLEAN: lastError null, A=(10.7,−7.1) beyond D and C — the textbook figure. Scenarios: `directional-cut-works-when-apex-close` (clean, no driving) + `directional-cut-drives-free-apex-from-far` (a forced far seed is still driven to a valid A). The default change broke no existing tangent/secant test (they assert relations, not absolute positions). 1041 green, build clean. **Honest limits (follow-ups):** driving from a FAR seed lands a valid-but-UGLY near-parallel A (the close default avoids it for the real figure) — a compactness-preferring driven solve is the fix; and the absolute `x:6` seed assumes the default r5 origin circle — a radius-relative apex seed is the other follow-up.

For that figure the constructible radius range is ~[4.3, 6) (below: the directional cut over-constrains; ≥6: D falls inside the circle, no tangents). **Fix:** `setRadius` now dry-runs `replay` with the candidate and commits ONLY if it still builds (`lastError===null`) — a breaking value is rejected, so the slider stops at the boundary. **Found+fixed a latent bug doing this:** `replay`'s `lastError` was BUILD-ONLY, so a radius override that made the FINAL `evaluate` fail left `lastError` null with the figure silently gone — `replay` now surfaces the override-evaluate failure too. Locked: scenario `circumcircle-of-triangle-cuts-chord` (the operator's exact 5 steps → D placed, on CE, ≠ C) + `circumcircle-cuts.test.ts` (compound both langs, standalone Hebrew circumcircle, plain triangle/right-triangle untouched). Now ∠CAB=90 drives the FREE apex A, the radius stays free + playable, and **dialing R re-solves A to keep ∠CAB=90** (verified R=4..6.5 all keep the angle 90 with D following). - **2026-06-18 (home PC) — directional `המשך` semantics shipped ([ADR-054](06-decisions.md#adr-054)), 1011 green, build clean.** Picked up the ★ NEXT STEP resume task. `המשך XY` is now **directional**: the new point lands **beyond the 2nd letter** (`המשך AC`→past C). New `extend-onto-circle` command — D is an on-circle point driven onto line AC by `collinear` + a one-sided `collinear-order [A,C,D]` pinning it past the 2nd letter; parser rule `extendOntoCircle` (triggered by המשך/extension/extended + a NAMED circle) runs before the order-agnostic `lineMeetsCircle` (which keeps plain "line AC meets circle P"). **The crux finding held:** with default radii a line often has NO extension crossing, so honouring `המשך` genuinely requires *resizing* the circle. Two regimes, reusing existing machinery: (a) a root exists at the current radius → a small **order-awareness added to `resolveDriven`'s 1-DOF path** sorts the collinear roots by order residual and picks the FAR crossing, leaving the radius untouched (no collapse); (b) no root → the order check fails, `recruitFreeDofs` grabs the circle's FREE radius (via `circlesOfPoint`) and the mixed solver (`withOrderCons`) GROWS it until an extension root appears. **Decision the user made:** *full-strict* per the plan — so the bagrut scenario's `המשך AC`/`המשך BD` were reworded to `המשך CA`/`המשך DB` (its E/F are past A/B) and the phrasing-matrix test was split (directional→`extend-onto-circle`, plain→`line-circle-intersection`). **Rejected an early design** (marking D *and* the radius as carriers up front): with a root available the under-determined radius drifted to the clamp floor (a degenerate dot — the ADR-052 "default masquerading as movable" smell); recruiting the radius only on failure fixes it. **Coverage:** `extend-onto-circle.test.ts` (2: far-crossing-without-resize + radius-grows-to-reach), `circle-meet-phrasings.test.ts` (split matrix + exact-command, both locales), reworded bagrut scenario green via the real parse→replay path. **Env repair:** `@types/node` was missing from this machine's junctioned `node_modules` (the 4 `llm.test.ts` tsc errors) — restored with `npm install @types/node` (NOT `npm ci`, which clobbers the junction); `npm run build` now exits 0. **Known limit (in ADR-054):** the 1-DOF order-aware pick needs `drivenRoots` to return BOTH crossings; a pathologically axis-aligned line (crossing at θ=0/π, a touch-zero of `|sin∠|`) can return one and fall back to recruit+resize (still valid, resizes when it needn't) — a general `drivenRoots` touch-zero fix is deferred.
