Diagnosed from the geometry (a throwaway repro printing cos(CA,OA)=0): C was defined as "tangent to circle O at A meets circle P", so **line CA IS the tangent to circle O at A** — it touches O only at A, so there is NO second crossing F. The figure is impossible as typed (an operator mislabel vs the original bagrut, where the secant runs through a point NOT on the tangent). **Two root-cause fixes:** (1) `extend-onto-circle` now detects when a named endpoint is already on the TARGET circle and routes to a **deterministic `line∩circle` avoiding the shared endpoint** (the addCollinear/ADR-050-Am.2 second-intersection pattern) instead of the fragile driven-θ solve — which is what collapsed F onto A; (2) the `line-circle` resolver, when `avoid` is set and NO fresh crossing remains (tangent / chord), returns a **clear "line is tangent … no second crossing to extend onto"** message and keeps the prior figure, instead of falling through to return the avoided point (the opaque coincidence crash). Locked as scenario `extend-onto-tangent-line-is-rejected-clearly` (the operator's exact 4 steps → graceful rejection, F not placed, message names the tangent). **NOTE for the operator:** to place F on circle O via a real secant, extend a line that is genuinely a secant of O (through B, the other O∩P point, or through D which is on O) — not line CA, which is tangent to O.

  **Then — "a silent fail with no indication" → second-try-LLM + honest problem ([ADR-055](06-decisions.md#adr-055)), 1019 green.** Operator: a typo `שנקודה` (for `בנקודה`) made a tangent-meets-circle phrasing fall through to `circles-tangent`, which silently repositioned the circles and **drew no tangent line** — no error shown. Root cause is the parser-first hole: a greedy rule MATCHES malformed input, produces a confident wrong/empty parse, so it never escalates to the LLM that would've understood the typo. **Two fixes:** (1) `circlesTangent` no longer fires when a cuts/meets keyword (`INTERSECT_KW`) is present — mutual tangency is a STATE, never a crossing event; (2) a new `dryRunOutcome` (store) replays a deterministic parse BEFORE committing — if it **errored** or **changed nothing** (no new object/constraint/label; bare `x=4` exempt) the step is given a **second try through the LLM**, and if neither builds anything the input layer shows an explicit "I understood that but it didn't build anything" message (`input.producedNothing`). **Operator's chosen policy (AskUserQuestion):** trigger on *error* or *no-change* only — a givens *violation* keeps the ADR-053 amber cue (the figure is still shown; not a silent fail) and does NOT force an LLM retry. Locked as `dry-run.test.ts` (5) + the `circles-tangent` guard in `circle-meet-phrasings.test.ts`. The givens verifier (ADR-053) is the net for "built the WRONG figure"; this is the net for "built NOTHING".

  **Still to do (the operator's call):** in-browser VISUAL confirmation of a directional figure (the plan's "green-but-wrong" caution — automated end-to-end + verifier are green, but the eyeball check is David's). **Resume:** fold `collinear-order` into the givens verifier (next slice); then Phase 6 (theorems).

- **2026-06-18 — Bagrut two-circle figure + givens verifier + UX (layout, DOF sliders), 1004 green.** A long figure-reproduction + trust session.
  - **Shipped & pushed (`23c68a7`):** (1) two parser constructs — `tangentMeetsOtherCircle` ("the tangent to circle O at A meets circle P at D", before `circlesTangent` so it's not misread as mutual tangency) and `lineMeetsCircle` ("[extension of] AC חותך מעגל P בנקודה E"); (2) **`פוגש`/`פגש`** added to the shared `INTERSECT_KW` (the missing synonym that made the operator's phrasing fall through to a misparse — the canonical "missing keyword in the shared set" root cause); (3) **apply-time fix** — re-defining an existing point as on-circle no longer silently no-ops in `addObj` (it becomes the second-intersection `line-circle`, the `addCollinear` pattern); (4) **the givens verifier ([ADR-053](06-decisions.md#adr-053))** — `src/engine/verify.ts` `checkGivens` re-derives the relations the COMMANDS assert and checks them against the final coordinates; `evaluate` now also returns the resolved `circles` map; `Derived.violations` + an amber "the figure may not match what you described" cue. **"Green" now means VERIFIED, not just no-error.** Process rules (phrasing-matrix coverage + drive-the-real-app) in [08-testing-strategy.md](08-testing-strategy.md).
  - **UNCOMMITTED until this push — UX (operator manual-test feedback):** (a) **scroll restored** — removed leftover `html,body,#root{height:100vh;overflow:hidden}` (old JSXGraph era) in `index.css` → `min-height:100vh`, so the page scrolls when the step list is long; (b) **responsive canvas** — the figure was a fixed 560px box; now fills the space beside the sidebar + viewport height via a `ResizeObserver` (`canvasWrap` in `App.tsx`), sidebar 340→400; (c) **playable DOF — radius sliders (first slice)** — a "כוונון האיור / Adjust the figure" panel with a slider per free-circle radius. Store: `radiusOverrides` + `setRadius` (UI-only, not undoable, cleared by resample — a viewing scratchpad per ADR-048); `replay(facts, seed, radiusOverrides)` applies them + returns `radiusDofs`; locked as `dof-radius.test.ts`.
  - **Verifier scope (honest):** first slice covers on-circle membership + a tangent's tangency point. **Next slices:** constraint residuals (distance/angle/∥/⟂) + collinearity folded into the same cue.
  - **DOF-slider scope:** radii only. **Next:** sliders for on-circle / on-segment positions + free points (all already enumerated by `freeDofs`).
  - **★ NEXT STEP — directional `המשך` semantics (the resume task).** Operator: "`המשך הצלע AC ... D` means D is AFTER C (order A→C→D)." **Confirmed convention:** `המשך` is directional, **beyond the 2nd letter** (`המשך AC`→past C, `המשך CA`→past A), and the order must **adapt the figure** (drive free DOFs so the extension actually reaches), not just pick a root. **Current bug:** `lineMeetsCircle` is order-agnostic — verified the bagrut figure puts E at t=0.36 (BETWEEN A and C) and F at t=−0.235 (beyond B), ignoring order. **Key geometric finding:** with the default radii, line AC meets circle P only WITHIN [A,C] (A at t=0, E at t=0.36) — **there is NO extension intersection on either side**, so honoring `המשך` genuinely REQUIRES resizing circle P (validates the operator's "adapt the figure" choice). **Plan (additive, low-risk to existing figures):** a new `extend-onto-circle` command — D = a driven ON-CIRCLE θ DOF + `collinear(A,C,D)` + `collinear-order [A,C,D]` (the existing one-sided order residual, solve.ts:141, pins D past the 2nd letter); the joint solver slides θ to the extension root and the **free radius adapts** (via solver/sampler) so an extension root exists. Parser: route `המשך XY …` directionally to it (keep the unordered `lineMeetsCircle` for non-`המשך`). **Caveats to handle when building:** it CHANGES how the bagrut figure draws — under strict semantics that figure's E is past A and F past B, so those steps become `המשך CA …` / `המשך DB …`, and with default radii there's no extension root so the figure must adapt (won't be valid at seed 0 until the radii settle/resample). It touches the **delicate constraint solver** (θ driven against an order constraint + radius adaptation) — build with a test asserting D lands PAST the named point AND on the circle, then VERIFY in the running app (do NOT rush — this is exactly the "green-but-wrong" risk class the verifier was built for). Reuse, don't reinvent: `set-line` (apply.ts:777) already drives on-circle θ for `collinear-order`; the only reason not to route through it directly is `addCollinear`'s line-circle conversion (order-agnostic) — the new command sidesteps that by keeping D a driven on-circle point.

- **2026-06-17 — "No fixed assumptions" elevated to a GUIDING PRINCIPLE ([ADR-052](06-decisions.md#adr-052)), 989 green.** Operator reiterated the core pedagogy: a student enters only what the question SHOWS; the tool must assume NO size/angle/position/proportion unless stated. A default is fine as a *starting* value but must be a free DOF — changeable by "show another configuration" or forced by a later constraint — NEVER fixed (a fixed default silently asserts a given the question never gave — the same cardinal sin as drawing a figure that violates the givens). Wrote ADR-052 unifying ADR-018 (free points), ADR-033 (shape DOFs), ADR-051 (free radius). **Two violations found by audit + fixed this turn:** (1) a SINGLE circle with no stated radius was fixed at 5 → now `freeRadius` whenever no numeric/symbolic radius is stated (parser sets it; `parseRadius.numeric/symbolic` distinguishes), so ALL unstated-radius circles flex, not just two-circles; (2) a point "G on AB" (no ratio) was pinned at the midpoint t=0.5 → added a `free` flag to `OnSegmentPoint` (mirrors on-circle's `free`), set when `cmd.t===undefined`, sampled in `sample.ts` (`isFreeOnSegment`, t∈[0.15,0.85]) + counted in `freeDofs`; a STATED ratio or extension t>1 stays fixed. Both: seed 0 keeps the default so existing figures look identical (0 test fallout), only resampled views vary. **Conformance test for the future:** a value counted by `rawMovableDof` but NOT in `freeDofs` (sampled) is a smell — a default masquerading as fixed. Locked as `free-defaults.test.ts`. **Test gotcha hit:** `const st = useGeoStore.getState()` captures a SNAPSHOT — `st.seed` stays stale after `st.resample()`; read `useGeoStore.getState().seed` fresh each iteration. **Audit still open:** sweep for any other counted-but-unsampled DOF / rule baking a fixed angle/length.

- **2026-06-17 — Circle radius can be a free solver DOF ([ADR-051](06-decisions.md#adr-051)), 986 green.** Operator: "the radii need to be DOFs — there is no chance the fixed values will hold for any question." `twoCirclesMeet` froze radii at 5 / 3.6 (fixed 0.72 ratio), which can't adapt to a problem's givens. Now a circle with NO stated size gets `radius:{via:'free', value}` — a driveable SHAPE SCALAR (the circle analogue of perp-offset dist) flowing through the existing carrier machinery: `carrierOf` → shape/dof1, `carrierSpec`/`setCarrierVals` read/write it, and `freeDrivableAncestors` surfaces a circle's radius when the walk hits a point ON it (`circlesOfPoint`) so a constraint recruits it — e.g. `|OC|=9` (C on circle O) sizes circle O to radius 9. A STATED radius ("circle O radius 5") stays `via:'length'` fixed. **Renderer gotcha:** the driven radius is baked into `positions` (points sit at the solved size) but NOT the stored seed, so `scene.ts` recovers it as |centre − a point on the circle|, drawing the circle to match its points. **Deliberately NOT sampled:** jittering radii on "show another configuration" made near-tangent secants (regressed the secant-web scenario), so radius variety is left to constraints; centres still resample. **Scope:** only `twoCirclesMeet` emits free radii (bounded blast radius — every other circle untouched). Files: types/carriers/evaluate(carrierSpec,setCarrierVals,resolveCircle)/sample/step(freeDrivableAncestors,circlesOfPoint)/apply/parse/render. Locked as `radius-dof.test.ts` (5). Resume/follow-ups: extend free radii to bare "circle O"; R-var binding still reads the seed (corner case); a guarded radius-resample could re-add size variety.

- **2026-06-17 — Ordered multi-point line "line ABE" ([ADR-050](06-decisions.md#adr-050) Amendment 3), 981 green.** Operator asked if "line ABE" could mean "A, through B, then E" — the typed ORDER pinning the side. Built: `line ABE`/`ישר ABE`/`line ABEF` → a `set-line` command = collinearity (each later point onto the line through the first two, via the shared `addCollinear` helper so the Am.2 line∩circle conversion still applies) + a one-sided `collinear-order` constraint (sum of backwards-overlaps of the points' projections; mirrors angle/length-order — rides the optimizer, joins `withOrderCons`, 0 DOF in the sampler). To fix the side it drives the free PARAMETRIC carriers AMONG the named points directly (an on-circle/on-segment anchor slides until ordered), NOT via `recruitFreeDofs` — which would reach a fixed intersection point's parent circle CENTRES and distort the circles (the key gotcha). Parser: uppercase-labels-only after the keyword (so "through" isn't read as labels), ≥3 labels ("line AB" falls through). Result: operator's `line DBE` now puts E beyond B (B between D and E) deterministically — no cycling. Locked as parser+engine tests (`collinear.test.ts` ordered-line block) + catalog entry. **Limit:** a brand-new point named only in `line ABE` that must MOVE must be introduced on a host (circle/segment) first — `point E` is pinned. **Three amendments this session all build on each other:** Am.1 (binding seed + keep-accepted) for generic driven collinear; Am.2 (line∩circle+avoid) for the second-intersection pattern; Am.3 (ordered line) for explicit sequencing.

- **2026-06-17 — Collinearity "second intersection" fix ([ADR-050](06-decisions.md#adr-050) Amendment 2), 979 green.** Manual testing (operator session `uln4fwow`) showed the driven-collinear model is WRONG for a common circle figure: "E on line DB" where E is on circle O and B is ALSO on circle O is really *the second intersection of line DB with O* (the line meets O at exactly B and E). Driving E's angle by collinearity let the solve land on the degenerate E=B or the wrong side, seed-dependently (operator saw both; fixed it only by cycling). **Fix:** in the `set-collinear` handler, when the driven point is on circle K and a line-point is structurally also on K (`pointOnCircle`, a per-point `circleMembers`), model it as `line-circle(line, K, avoid = shared point)` — `avoid` picks the crossing farthest from the shared point, so it's the OTHER crossing, never degenerate, seed-independent. Covers both halves (C on line AD with A∈O; E on line DB with B∈O); composes with the parser rule AND the redefine reinterpret (both call `applyCommand(set-collinear)`). The point becomes a 0-DOF derived crossing tracking the remaining free point. Generic collinearity (driven point not on a circle, or no co-circular line point) still uses the Amendment-1 driven solver. Locked as `collinear.test.ts` (second-intersection block) + scenario `second-intersection-avoids-shared-point`. **Lesson:** for "point on a circle, on a line through another point of that circle", reach for line∩circle+avoid, not a numeric collinearity DOF solve — the residual is zero at BOTH crossings so the solver can't prefer the right one.

- **2026-06-17 — Collinearity chain fix ([ADR-050](06-decisions.md#adr-050) Amendment 1), 976 green.** Manual testing of the new collinear constraint found a chain that wouldn't solve (operator session `kd24do14`): two circles meet at A,B; C on O, D on P; "line AD through C" (drives D) then "line DB through E" (drives E) — a *triangular* system that failed with a false `over-constrained: A,D,C collinear cannot hold`. **Two driven-solver defects, both fixed:** (1) the joint solver minimised the SUM of all residuals over all carriers, so the shared carrier D was pulled toward BOTH constraints and satisfied neither (the R7 binding issue) → added a **binding-aware seed** (Gauss-Seidel: each bounded carrier solved against the constraint IT drives, others held; `argMin` skips the degenerate D=A root via the collinear NaN); (2) `multiStartSolve` **discarded a solution it had already found** — the grid/binding seeds reached cost≈0 and passed the accept gate, but the final polish wandered off into a degenerate same-cost basin (the second collinear root) and the function returned the SEED → now it tracks the best ACCEPTED candidate across restarts+polish and returns it if the polished point is rejected (general — protects every driven solve). Gated by the stability snapshot (unchanged) + full suite; chain locked as `collinear.test.ts` + scenario `two-collinear-chain-solves`. Debugging method that worked: a throwaway repro test + a `GEO_DEBUG` env print of seed/grid/bind costs and accept flags inside `resolveMixedCarriers` — showed the solver FOUND an accepted config (costBind 5e-26, accept=true) but returned the seed, pinpointing `multiStartSolve` as the culprit, not the seeding.

- **2026-06-17 — Collinearity constraint built ([ADR-050](06-decisions.md#adr-050)), 974 green.** Session start was a sync-hygiene detour: on this PC `git status` showed 8 "modified" files, but `.git` had advanced to `615ec55` (= GitHub) at 7:26pm while the working files were stale 2:02pm snapshots — Dropbox synced the tiny `.git` ref ahead of the larger working files (NOT in-progress lag: unchanged after 50 min; 96 `comp11229's conflicted copy` files were piled in `logs/`). Reconciled with `git restore .` (the discarded content was all older committed states, fully in history) + cleared the conflicted copies (~57MB). **New operational reality to expect across machines: the working tree can lag an advanced `.git` — `git restore .` from the GitHub-backed HEAD is the fix; confirm Dropbox is "Up to date" before editing.** **Then the feature** (operator bug from `logs/debug-log.jsonl` session `de88adm7`): two circles meet at A,B; C on one, E on the other; "line CE passes through A" was the goal (secant through an intersection point). It had no expressible form — `ישר CE עובר בנקודה A` was silently DROPPED (LLM → "A on line CE", no rule) and `E על המשך הצלע AC` hit `'E' is already defined`. Root cause: no way to constrain an *already-placed* point onto a line through two others. Added a `collinear` constraint (modelled on `parallel`: sin∠ residual, scale-free, sign-changing → on-segment closed-form / on-circle numeric; routed via the existing `driveOrCheck`; the trivial collapse onto A excluded by the degeneracy gate so it finds the OTHER crossing). Two entry points: a parser rule (`collinearConstraint`, He+En: "E on line AC" / "line CE passes through A" / "A,B,C collinear", line-named forms also draw the segment) + `step.ts` `reinterpretAsCollinear` (a redefining "P on segment" of an existing free carrier → collinear, the on-line analogue of the ADR-028 coincidence reinterpret). Catalog entry exposes it to the LLM. Coverage: `src/engine/__tests__/collinear.test.ts` (12) + end-to-end scenario `line-through-intersection-collinear` (the operator's exact words). 961 → 974 green, no regressions; `tsc` clean (the 4 pre-existing `node:fs`/@types/node errors in `llm.test.ts` are a junction artifact on this PC only — present on clean HEAD too). **Resume:** rest of Wave 3 (R7 binding, R8 geometric branch keys, R4 Stage 2, R9(b)); Phase 6 (theorems) is the headline milestone.

- **2026-06-17 — R5 Pass 2 SHIPPED (`a4a2b91` + `a169744`, 961 green): the coupled-param solver block merged into `resolveMixedCarriers` (3 driven solvers → 2).** The audit's risky half of R5, done behind the stability harness. **Step 1 (`a4a2b91`):** closed a harness gap — the snapshot had NO coupled-block figure (every figure routed through 1-DOF/free/mixed), so added two that drive ≥2 on-circle θ's jointly via chord constraints (`|AB|=7`; `|AB|=6 & |BC|=6`), both hand-verified valid. **Step 2 (`a169744`):** deleted the coupled block; the router now sends ≥2 pure-param carriers through `resolveMixedCarriers`, which gained — **gated on bounded parametric carriers so pure shape/on-line figures are untouched** — the two capabilities unique to the old block: **NEAR-FIRST** local accept (keeps a near/convex config) and the **full-range GRID-SCAN** coordinate-descent seeding (finds the far branch cardinal restarts miss — the audit's must-port item). The absolute residual + inline soft-barrier are replaced by the existing **relative residual** + the **coincide-exempt non-degeneracy gate** in `solutionAccepted` (coincide exemption ported, so "C = midpoint of OB"-style intended merges aren't rejected). **Behavior-preserving:** the stability snapshot (incl. the 2 new coupled figures) passes WITHOUT `-u`; tsc clean; 961 green. **Adversarial verification (subagent) caught a real defect** before landing: `resolveMixedCarriers`'s `span` is free-point-only, so a PURE-param figure passed `span=1` and the degeneracy threshold `1e-3·span` was ~200× too small on an r5 circle — **fixed** so `solutionAccepted` scales the threshold to the referenced points' ACTUAL extent (floored by span), matching the deleted block. **Documented trade-offs (narrow-fix rationale):** the old SMOOTH in-search degeneracy steering is now replaced by relative-residual + more restarts + the reject gate (a pathological scale-free-constraint collapse basin is theoretically reachable but no corpus figure exhibits it — re-add a steering term if one appears); the old grid-scan returned unconditionally, the merged path returns the seed (honest over-constraint) when the accept gate fails. **R5 status:** the consolidation goal is substantially met (coupled-block duplicate killed; one scaffold `multiStartSolve` + one accept gate `solutionAccepted` + one relative residual + one degeneracy/coincide policy). `resolveFreeDriven` remains a thin free-only wrapper (works in real-coord space vs the mixed solver's normalised space) sharing the same scaffold/gate — **fully collapsing it into `resolveMixedCarriers` is an OPTIONAL follow-up** (would move free figures real→normalised; verify against the snapshot). **Resume — rest of Wave 3:** R7 binding (keep `replaceCyclicForDiameter`'s antipode pin), R8 geometric branch keys, R4 Stage 2, R9(b). Stability snapshot gates all.

- **2026-06-17 — R5 Pass 1 SHIPPED (`da35c96`, 959 green) + the test-runner-hang env fix.** Started Wave 3 / R5. **First, an environment detour:** `npx vitest` hung indefinitely on this machine; root-caused (after two wrong guesses — Defender, then Dropbox placeholders, both disproven) to **Dropbox cloud-filter latency on ESM loads** (same file: 2000ms in the Dropbox tree vs 1ms outside). Fixed by junctioning `node_modules` out of Dropbox to `C:\dev\geo_builder\node_modules` — see the new Operational note for the full mechanism + the `npm ci` clobber caveat + the target-must-be-named-`node_modules` gotcha. Tests are fast again (full suite ~11s). **Then R5 Pass 1 (the safe half of the solver consolidation):** `resolveFreeDriven` and `resolveMixedCarriers` each carried a byte-identical "regularised basin search from seed + cardinal restarts → polish with shrinking simplex → accept-or-keep-seed" body. Folded into one **`multiStartSolve()`** + one **`solutionAccepted()`** gate (evaluable + every constraint satisfied + not degenerate). **Pure refactor — same Nelder–Mead calls/args/residual conventions → identical floats**, proven by the driven-solver **stability snapshot passing WITHOUT `-u`**. tsc clean, 959 green. **Resume — R5 Pass 2 (the RISKY half, its own focused pass):** merge the coupled block (`evaluate.ts` ~131-223, the >1 param-carrier path) into `resolveMixedCarriers`. ⚠️ Order: (1) FIRST close the stability-harness gap — it has NO coupled-block figure (every snapshot figure routes through 1-DOF / free / mixed, not the coupled path), so add a multi-param far-branch figure (cyclic-quad / "C=midpoint of OB drives E while |ED|=7"); (2) PORT the coupled block's full-range grid-scan seeding (`argMin` coordinate-descent over bracketed ranges) into the mixed path — its unique far-branch capability; (3) fold the inline soft-barrier degeneracy onto a steering term; (4) switch the coupled block's ABSOLUTE residual to the RELATIVE convention; (5) preserve NEAR-FIRST local accept; THEN route it through `multiStartSolve`. Adversarially verify; snapshot gates it.

- **2026-06-17 — LLM-assisted solve for figures the engine can't crack alone ([ADR-049](06-decisions.md#adr-049), Proposed).** Operator idea: when the engine can't solve a diagram, send constraints to the LLM, get coordinates, draw it, and a feedback loop "teaches the engine." Discussed + recorded as a Proposed ADR with the naive form rejected and the sound form chosen. **Naive (rejected):** LLM returns coordinates → LLMs are worst at numerical constraint-satisfaction (plausible-but-wrong numbers), and it risks the cardinal sin — drawing a figure that doesn't satisfy the givens; also the engine is deterministic code, not a learned model (no runtime "teaching"). **Sound (decided):** LLM owns *structure* (a re-decomposition into primitives the engine solves exactly) or a *seed* (rough layout / branch / which DOF to drive), the engine owns *arithmetic + verification* — every LLM output passes the `isSatisfied`/residual gate before anything draws (reject → keep-prior). Contradictions are flagged, never "solved." The "learning" is **offline + supervised**: auto-harvest each LLM/seed success-where-engine-failed into a corpus/regression fixture + a "missing construct / weak basin" queue (the existing debug-log→scenario rule, automated). **Sequencing:** depends on the ADR-045 solver/seeding consolidation + the production proxy deploy (parked); not before. Highest-value variant = LLM-as-seed (most non-contradiction failures are basin/far-branch failures, per the audit). No code.

Kept deliberately apart from the synthetic NL-construction flow (construction must stay coordinate-free). FR-IN-9 updated to point at the phase. Still deferred, sequenced after the rework → Phase 6 → Phase 8 and the other parked threads; gate TBD. Noted honestly as a scope-widening **product decision** past the v1 non-goal "not a general dynamic-geometry system," not just a phase. No code.

- **2026-06-17 — Wave 2 (partial) SHIPPED + pushed: stability harness, R4 Stage 1, R9(a), R3 Piece 2. 941 → 951 green, build clean.** Continued the audit rework. Each increment gated by the green suite (and, for the centerpiece, an adversarial-verification subagent):
  - **Stability harness (`44f29f3`):** `src/engine/__tests__/stability-snapshot.test.ts` — a golden `toMatchSnapshot` of the CHOSEN configuration of 9 figures spanning every carrier/solver path. The campaign invariants accept any valid drawing; this catches a refactor converging a driven figure to a DIFFERENT valid config. **This is the gate for all Wave 2/3 solver/carrier work** — if it goes red, a config moved; review before `-u`.
  - **R4 Stage 1 (`0af6c6d`):** `commandConflict`'s `isShape` literal → a structural `reusesBase` derived from apply's own output (`cmd.type !== 'free-point' && produced.some(free-point)`). Provably equivalent (placeBase is the only base-free-point creator besides the `free-point` move) but can't drift — kills the circumcircle-false-conflict class for ALL future reuse-or-create commands. +guard test in phase1.
  - **R9 part (a) (`253dd6b`):** LLM `absorb` registers a `circumcircle` centre (was only circle/circle-through). **Part (b)** — accumulating `circleMembers` across LLM steps via a running construction — DEFERRED (needs the engine's circleMembers as single source; its own pass).
  - **R3 Piece 2 (`62c7b22`) — the centerpiece:** new `src/engine/carriers.ts` `carrierOf(o)` = an EXHAUSTIVE switch over the point-kind union (no default → a new kind is a COMPILE error) returning `{family:'free'|'param'|'line'|'shape', dof}`. Routed the drift-prone classification sites through it (rawMovableDof, freeDofs shape disjunct, freeShape, freeDrivableAncestors free1/shape, markDriven, evaluate shapeCarriers). **Adversarially verified EQUIVALENT** at every site; stability snapshot unchanged.
  - **KEY LEARNING — R3 Piece 2 is more nuanced than the audit's one-paragraph framing.** A FULL `carrierOf` (the audit's vision: filters + carrierSpec/setCarrierVals + sampler all routed) does NOT cleanly work: (1) the `resolveDriven` typed filters (`freeCarriers`/`paramCarriers`/`onLineCarriers`) need `Extract<>` type guards — routing them through a `family ===` check would lie to the type system; (2) the SAMPLER set ≠ the carrier set (on-segment is a param carrier but is NEVER sampled; on-circle only when its `free` flag is set), so `freeDofs` can't be `carrierOf`-only; (3) the seed/scale (carrierSpec/setCarrierVals) and jitter magnitudes are figure-tuned VALUE math that must stay per-kind. So Piece 2 was scoped to the CLASSIFICATION (the high-drift kind-LISTS) — the shape-scalar triple (5 sites), the param pair, the DOF count — which is the real "no silent drift" win, compile-checked. The value-math + typed filters stay (noted follow-up; they're cohesive + exhaustive-ish already).
  - **Tier B over-constraint coverage SHIPPED (`87de166`, 955 green):** `src/engine/__tests__/over-constraint.test.ts` — figures from PINNED points (no DOF to absorb the constraint) so a wrong distance/angle/equal/ratio is provably unsatisfiable; asserts evaluate REJECTS it + keeps the prior figure, paired with the consistent version (so rejection isn't spurious). The large-span threshold probe was SKIPPED on purpose — the operator resolved figures stay small (ADR-045), so a small fixed degeneracy threshold is fine; nothing to relativize.
  - **R6 part (a) SHIPPED (`ab6079c`, 959 green) — via ANCHORING, not the full fold.** The documented half-parse class (a greedy rule grabbing a numeric/var PREFIX and dropping the rest) is now structurally closed: the two genuinely-greedy `=`-RHS rules — `distanceConstraint` (numeric) and `measureLength` (var) — ANCHOR their RHS to end-of-input (`\s*$`), so they must consume the whole RHS; a leftover radical/exponent/unit escalates (not-handled → LLM) instead of a wrong partial parse. +4 regression tests (phase4: √ coverage, power-read-whole, clean `AB = 6`, `AB = 5∛x` escalates). **Decision (recorded):** the full fold of all 7 heterogeneous `=`-RHS rules into one `parseLengthRHS` is DEFERRED — `measureLength`'s affine/fraction/text build logic + the heterogeneous LHS (`ratioConstraint`'s leading coef) make it high-risk surgery at the parser epicenter for marginal benefit once anchoring closes the class; the known suffixes (√/²/π) are already order-handled. Revisit only if a real misparse shows up.
  - **R5(3) SHIPPED (`791e4e3`, 959 green) — WAVE 2 COMPLETE.** `resolveFreeDriven` now uses the RELATIVE residual (÷ constraintScale), matching `resolveMixedCarriers` — one residual convention, un-gameable by shrinking (ADR-033 Am.1), and it balances disparate-magnitude constraints (a |DE|=3/|EF|=300 SSS no longer starves the small one). **Adversarially verified SAFE** (zero set unchanged, scale floored for collapse/NaN → fail-safe; accept/over-constraint gate independent of the cost; λ tie-breaker dropped by the polish phase). The stability harness moved ONE figure (parallelogram + |AB|=|AC|) to an equivalent valid config (C shifted ~1.2° on the |AC|=6 circle) — reviewed + accepted via `-u`. Degeneracy policy was already unified (free+mixed both `degenerateSpread`). **Known low/fail-safe residual risk:** a `resolveFreeDriven` figure mixing a scale-free (angle) + large-length constraint with a far-side basin could shift reachability; worst case = honest over-constraint, never a wrong figure. A Tier-B coordinate-span probe would pin it.
  - **Resume — Wave 3 + leftovers, each its OWN focused pass:** **R5 scaffold/merge** — extract the shared Nelder-Mead scaffold across the 3 solver bodies + the capability-preserving merge of the coupled block into `resolveMixedCarriers` (MUST port the coupled block's full-range grid-scan SEEDING first, or far-branch parametric figures regress — ADR-045); also unify the coupled block's soft-barrier degeneracy onto `degenerateSpread`. **R7 binding** — decide constraint→DOF binding once (retire greedy `driveOrCheck` + after-failure `recruitFreeDofs`); keep `replaceCyclicForDiameter`'s antipode pin or migrate it to a first-class persistent relation FIRST (deleting it breaks bagrut-4d / cyclic-quad — adversarially confirmed in the audit). **R8** geometric branch keys. **R4 Stage 2** (`DerivedVertex` fold). **R9(b)** accumulate circleMembers across LLM steps. **R6 full fold** (only if a real misparse appears). All ADR statuses + the audit doc current. The stability snapshot harness gates all of it.

- **2026-06-17 — Wave 1 of the rework SHIPPED (938 → 941 green, tsc + vite build clean, on `rebuild-foundation`; not yet committed).** Operator said "go" on the audit's low-risk Wave 1. Landed, each gated by the green suite:
  - **R3 Piece 1 (ADR-043):** `GeoPointKind = GeoPoint['kind']`; `POINT_KINDS` derived from `POINT_KIND_FLAGS: Record<GeoPointKind, true>` so a forgotten kind is a **compile error** (the `Record` pattern beat a `satisfies`+`AssertNever` list, which strict TS flagged as an unused type — TS6196). Two inline `solve?: {constraint;branch}` shapes folded into the named `SolveDirective`.
  - **R1 (ADR-044) — refined mechanism:** instead of threading cached world-coord maps through `EvalOk`, the engine now **exports `resolveLine`/`resolveCircle`** and the renderer **calls them** (deleting `scene.ts`'s `lineGeometry` + the tangent-inner radius re-derivation). Same single-source-of-truth, but `buildScene`'s signature is unchanged (zero test churn) and orientation is automatic (pure fn of the already-oriented positions). Fixes the live `tangent-inner` + `via:'through'`-outer render bug.
  - **R5 cleanup (1) (ADR-045):** one exported `isSatisfied(con,get)` in `solve.ts` replaces the 4 byte-identical gates in `evaluate.ts`. Scaffold extraction / solver merge / binding / branch-identity stay Wave 2/3 behind the stability harness.
  - **R2 — the feature (ADR-043):** on-segment-solved is now genuinely cyclable AND survives replay — `point-on-segment` command carries `branch?`, threaded onto `OnSegmentPoint.solveBranch?` (named apart from `branch` so it doesn't pollute the `'branch' in o` set) and read in `driveOrCheck`; store `cycleAlt` recognises `point-on-segment`; one engine-owned `firstCyclableBranch(c)` replaces the drifted `BRANCHABLE_KINDS` literals in `App.tsx`/`scenarios.test.ts`. +regression in `phase3.test.ts` (2-root |CG|=4 figure cycles + survives replay).
  - **Tier A (ADR-047):** determinism + **build()/replay() equivalence** over the 343-figure campaign corpus (`campaign.test.ts`) — the latter was previously untested and passed clean on first run. Distinctness skipped (evaluate already guarantees it).
  - **Learnings:** (1) `Record<K, true>` is the cleanest compile-checked-membership pattern under strict TS (no unused-type-alias error). (2) R1's "share the resolver fn" beat the literal ADR (thread maps + re-orient) on risk/churn — recorded as an ADR-044 refinement. (3) when adding an optional field to a union member, watch the `'X' in o` narrowing — name it apart if it would join a set that means something specific (`branch` vs `solveBranch`). **Resume: Wave 2** (carriers.ts / SHAPES `reusesBase` registry / parser `=`-RHS collapse / one-residual+degeneracy / Tier-B) — and per the operator's "high effort for risky waves," each Wave 2/3 risky diff gets adversarial verification + a `maxDelta` stability harness before landing.

- **2026-06-17 — Drag + position-preview design decided (ADR-048; FR-RN-5 refined, FR-RN-9 added). Phase 8, not built.** From the post-audit planning discussion. **Drag = soft reseed, NOT a pin:** dragging a point sets its DOF to the drop spot and re-evaluates, but the point stays free (future constraints can still drive it, "show another" still samples it, can re-drag); contrast typed `A=(x,y)` which pins (ADR-011). On-object points project onto their host; derived points aren't draggable; unsolvable-at-drop → snap back (keep-prior, no new error path). **"Where can it go" preview (FR-RN-9, "Could"):** select a point → highlight ~10 well-separated valid candidate positions = the same residual-freedom machinery as "show another configuration" (FR-ALT-2) scoped to one point and shown all-at-once (parametric → host-curve samples / discrete solveParam roots; free → ADR-018 resample of that point; derived → none). Easy case (free / unconstrained-parametric) reachable first; constrained case ("keep all givens") needs the consolidated joint solver (ADR-045). Operator principle behind both: *everything movable should be able to move*. Recorded in [ADR-048](06-decisions.md#adr-048) + [02-requirements.md](02-requirements.md) FR-RN-5/FR-RN-9.

- **2026-06-17 — DESIGN & DEVELOPMENT AUDIT (no code changes; report + Proposed ADRs).** Operator: "I have been debugging this engine case by case and patching it — I want a full review and to consider re-work and generalizations." Ran a multi-agent audit (8 subsystem deep-reads → 4 critic lenses → 13 theses → adversarial verification of each → synthesis). **Verdict: the constructive dependency-graph engine is SOUND — keep it; do not rewrite.** The patching feeling is real but narrow: it concentrates in **four seams** where one concept is hand-enumerated across 5–8 sites with no compiler link, so a forgotten copy is a *silent* bug, not a type error — (1) "which kinds carry a DOF" (~7–8 sites incl. an un-checked `POINT_KINDS` Set vs the `GeoPoint` union); (2) "which commands reuse-or-create base points" (`isShape` & 3 sibling lists vs the real authority `placeBase` — already drifted: the circumcircle false-conflict); (3) the DOF re-solver fractured into 3 near-duplicate numeric bodies + a 6-way router with 3 residual conventions; (4) the parser re-implements construct-vs-constrain in ~8 rules and the `=`-RHS family survives on hand-tuned ordering only. **The clean layers to pull everything toward (do NOT touch):** `solve.ts` residual interface, `evaluateCore`, `geometry.ts`, the replay store, `fitTemplate`, label scorer. Recommendations R1–R9 + a test-strategy shift (per-figure scenarios → invariant/negative-space/differential properties over the existing campaign + `_invariants.ts`). **Adversarial verification mattered** — it caught that a naive solver-merge loses a far-branch capability (port the grid-scan seeding first), the diameter `replaceCyclicForDiameter` antipode-pin can't be deleted, `on-segment-solved` isn't replay-persistable, and one "parser reads coordinates" claim was wrong. **Deliverables:** [13-design-audit-2026-06-17.md](13-design-audit-2026-06-17.md) (full report, file:line evidence) + **Proposed** [ADR-043…047](06-decisions.md#adr-043) (carrier/reuse/branchable consolidation; engine owns resolved geometry; one solver + binding; parser `=`-RHS + reinterpret fold; test strategy). **Operator decision: Phase 6 (theorems) is DEFERRED — "I want a full working diagramming tool before Phase 6."** So the rework waves (1: R1/R3-pt1/R2/R5-cleanups/Tier-A tests; 2: carriers.ts/SHAPES registry/`=`-RHS/Tier-B; 3: binding/branch-keys/Tier-C) are the path to "working tool," not a detour. ADRs are Proposed pending discussion — none built yet. **Open Qs for operator:** on-segment-solved cycling (feature?); anti-collapse thresholds at large spans; scenario-growth CI gate; confirm the "reuse circleCircleIntersect/solveParam ordering" hard rule.

- **2026-06-17 — a FREE point on an arc (ADR-042): "F על קשת BC" is now a real 1-DOF point, not the fixed midpoint (938 green).** Follow-up to the parse fix: the operator needs F to be **free** (slidable / drivable), not pinned to the arc midpoint. **Model:** `OnCirclePoint` gains `between?: [Id,Id]`; when set, `theta` is a FRACTION in [−1,1] of the (minor) half-arc from the midpoint (not an absolute angle), so the parser needn't compute an angle and F can never slide onto the far arc. evaluate places F at `baseAngle + clamp(theta,−1,1)·0.92·halfArc` (0 = midpoint, the canonical seed-0 default); the sampler varies a `between` point's theta uniformly in [−1,1] (within the arc) instead of the full-circle spin. Parser splits the arc rule: "on/על" → `point-on-circle` with `between` (free); "midpoint/אמצע" → `arc-midpoint` (fixed). Tests `free-arc-point.test.ts` (canonical=midpoint; resampled=varied, always on circle P AND within arc BC) + updated scenario. **Learning:** to make "a point on X" a genuine FREE DOF when the parser can't compute its coordinate (needs positions), parametrize the DOF as a *normalized fraction* the engine maps onto the geometry at eval time — default 0 gives the canonical placement, and the sampler/solver vary the fraction within bounds, so it's both correct-by-default and free. **Limit:** a CONSTRAINT-driven (vs free) on-arc point isn't yet arc-bounded by the solver (the generic on-circle carrier still assumes an absolute θ); only the minor arc is selectable.

- **2026-06-17 — "F על קשת BC" (a point ON an arc, not its midpoint) now parses instead of dropping (936 green).** Operator typed `F על קשת BC` → escalated → **dropped** ("error"); a retry produced `F על קשת BC במעגל O` which fell to plain `point-on-circle` and put F generically on the WRONG circle O (near the E–D side — "got F on ED"). Diagnosed from `logs/debug-log.jsonl` (seq 17–19). **Root cause:** the `arcMidpoint` rule's regex required the word midpoint/אמצע, so "F **on** arc BC" matched no rule at all; the LLM retry then matched the generic on-circle rule, which reads the literal "circle O" and ignores the arc. **Fix:** broaden the arc rule's connector to also accept on/על, so "point on arc BC" is handled (deterministically, no LLM) and — via the `circleMembers` resolution from the prior fix — lands on the circle holding both B and C (P). Regression `point-on-arc-no-midpoint-word`. **Honest limit flagged:** "F on arc BC" currently places F at the arc's MIDPOINT (a determined point), not a freely-sliding point along the arc — a free on-arc DOF (point-on-circle with a `between:[B,C]` range + sampler support) is the proper follow-up. **Learning:** "on arc"/"on the circle" and "midpoint of arc" are different intents that must BOTH parse; gating the arc rule on the midpoint word silently dropped the more common "a point on the arc" and handed it to the LLM, which then picked the wrong circle.

- **2026-06-17 — "arc BC" now resolves to the circle that actually contains both B and C (935 green).** Operator, with two circles drawn (O through A,B,E,D; P through A,B,C), typed `F על הקשצ BC` (typo for הקשת) → escalated to the LLM → canonical `F אמצע הקשת BC במעגל O` → arc-midpoint of BC on **circle O**. But C is NOT on O (only on P), so F landed in a meaningless spot ("placement of F is wrong"). Diagnosed from `logs/debug-log.jsonl` (seq 35–36). **Root cause:** `resolveCenter` only auto-picks a circle when exactly ONE exists; with two, an unnamed arc deferred → the LLM guessed (wrong) O, and the parser had no point→circle membership to catch it. **Fix:** new `circleMembers(construction)` engine helper (centre letter → points structurally on that circle: on-circle, circumcentre's a/b/c, antipode/arc/line∩circle/circle∩circle outputs), threaded into `ParseContext.circleMembers` (App's `parseCtx` + the LLM re-parse). The `arcMidpoint` rule now resolves to the circle holding BOTH endpoints — overriding a wrongly-named circle even on the LLM canonical re-parse. Regression `arc-resolves-to-circle-holding-both-endpoints`. **Learning:** when a phrase names points that pin down which circle/line it means ("arc BC", "chord BC"), resolve by MEMBERSHIP, not by "the only circle" or a named token the LLM may get wrong — the parser needs structural point→circle membership in its context, and the same context must reach the LLM re-parse so a mis-named circle is corrected, not trusted.

- **2026-06-17 — a second inscribed/circumscribed circle auto-names a FRESH centre instead of colliding on O (934 green).** Operator, on the same triangle figure with a cyclic quad already drawn (`circle-O`, centre O), typed `משולש ABC חסום במעגל` → `"'O' is already defined"`. Diagnosed from `logs/debug-log.jsonl` (seq 12–13). **Root cause:** the inscribed/circumcircle centre auto-picker (`inscribedPolygon`, the `circumcircle` rule, `incircle`) only dodged the **vertex** letters, not points already in the figure — so the second circle re-picked 'O' and `commandConflict` rejected redefining the existing circumcentre. **Fix:** every auto-named centre/auxiliary point now passes `ctx.points` into `freeLabel`'s avoid-set, so a second circle gets a fresh centre (P). Regression `second-inscribed-circle-fresh-centre`. **Learning:** any parser rule that auto-names an auxiliary point (circumcentre, incenter, tangency point) must dodge **the whole figure's existing labels**, not just the labels in the current utterance — otherwise the Nth construct of a kind collides with the (N−1)th. The `freeLabel(used, …)` helper makes this a one-arg fix; the trap is passing only the local ids as `used`.

- **2026-06-17 — `concyclic` constraint (ADR-041): a cyclic/inscribed quad whose 4 vertices already exist now shares one circle instead of detaching them (933 green).** Operator built `משולש CED`, `A על CD`, `B על CE`, then a cyclic quad ABDE two ways — `מרובע ABDE חסום במעגל` (→ LLM → `מרובע ABDE בר חסימה`) and `מעגל ABED` (→ LLM → `circle through A B E D`) — both wrong. Diagnosed from `logs/debug-log.jsonl` (seq 15, 18). **Two root causes:** (1) `inscribedPolygon` had an existing-points path only for a *triangle* (3 points = unique circumcircle); a *quad* fell through to the fresh-circle path and **re-placed A,B,D,E as new on-circle points** — detaching A from CD, B from CE. (2) The `circumcircle` rule read only the first **3** of 4 labels, silently dropping D. The real gap: 4 arbitrary points aren't concyclic — it's a genuine *constraint*, which the engine couldn't express on existing points. **Decision (operator chose "proper concyclic constraint" over a stopgap):** new `ConcyclicConstraint` + `set-concyclic` command, wired through the GENERIC machinery — residual = signed length-unit deviation of points beyond the first three from the circumcircle of those three (0 ⇔ concyclic, sign-changes so an on-segment carrier solves in closed form); `driveOrCheck` picks the DOF (A's slide on CD here, or a free vertex via resolveDriven, or a pure check → honest over-constraint). Parser: all-vertices-exist → circumcircle of 3 (drawn for חסום, hidden for בר-חסימה) + `set-concyclic` over all + edges; `circumcircle` rule handles 4 existing labels; `circumcircle` command gains `hidden`. Regressions `cyclic-quad-existing-vertices`, `circle-through-four-existing-points` + 2 engine tests (free-vertex carrier). **Learning:** "make these existing points concyclic" is a constraint, not a construction — model it as one residual through `driveOrCheck` and the engine reuses every existing carrier path (on-segment, on-line, on-circle, shape-scalar, free-vertex) and the over-constraint check for free. No new point kind, evaluator rule, or solver — exactly the ADR-014 generic-constraint pattern.

- **2026-06-17 — circumscribed circle of a triangle with pre-existing vertices no longer false-conflicts (929 green).** Operator built `משולש CDE`, `A על CD`, `B על CE`, `משולש ABC`, then `מעגל חוסם את משולש ABC` (→ LLM → canonical `מעגל חוסם את ABC`) — and it errored `"'A' is already defined — it can't be redefined as something different"`, dropping the circle. Diagnosed from `logs/debug-log.jsonl` (seq 53–54). **Root cause:** `commandConflict` (step.ts) detects redefinition by running `applyCommand` against an EMPTY construction and comparing the produced objects to the prior figure; commands that legitimately *reuse-or-create* their base points (shapes, `segment`, `circle`, `circle-through`) are whitelisted in an `isShape` set so their base free-points don't trip the gate. **`circumcircle` was missing from that set** — its three points (A,B,C) are reuse-or-create exactly like a triangle's, but with A,B already existing (as on-segment points) the gate saw fresh free-points A,B,C and flagged a false conflict. (apply's `placeBase` already reuses existing points correctly, so the *only* defect was the conflict gate.) **Fix:** add `cmd.type === 'circumcircle'` to `isShape`. Regression scenario `circumcircle-of-existing-points` (asserts all steps OK + O equidistant from A,B,C). **Learning:** any new command that reuses-or-creates named base points must be added to `commandConflict`'s `isShape` allow-list, or it will false-conflict the moment those points pre-exist — the apply-side reuse logic and the conflict-gate allow-list are two places that must agree.

- **2026-06-17 — secant's second crossing is "the OTHER crossing" (ADR-040), fixing a resample-instability class (928 green).** Operator, building a book figure (two circles meet at A,B; C on the right circle; secant AC cuts the LEFT circle at D; secant CB cuts it at E): "when I try other views, the constraints are not kept and the shape never looks like the book." Diagnosed from `logs/debug-log.jsonl` (`lastError: "B and E would be at the same point"`). **Two compounding root causes:** (1) the LLM decomposed "AC cuts the left circle at D" as "from A a line cuts circle O at **C and D**", and the parser's existing-point secant branch **re-placed the existing C onto circle O** — pinning C to BOTH circles, so the solver dragged it to the intersection (seed 0/1) or dropped it (seed 2+). (2) The new crossing D/E was a `line-circle` with a FIXED `branch` index, but `lineCircleIntersect` orders roots by line parameter and that order **flips with the line's direction** — so under resampling the branch intermittently landed on the known on-circle point (A/B), collapsing D/E onto it. **Fix (ADR-040):** `LineCirclePoint` gains `avoid?: Id`; `evaluate` then drops any root coinciding (within `LEN_EPS`) with an already-placed point and returns the genuinely new crossing (tie-broken farthest from `avoid`) — "the OTHER crossing" by non-coincidence, stable as the line turns. `branchCount`→1 for an avoid-pinned point (not offered to "show another"). Parser: an existing named crossing is a direction point, never re-placed on the circle; the new crossing carries `avoid`. Now C stays on circle P only, D/E are real second crossings on circle O, valid on canonical + 8 resamples. Regression `two-circles-secant-web` + ADR-040. **Learning:** any "second intersection of a line through a known on-circle point" must be defined as *the crossing that isn't an existing point*, never a fixed branch index — a fixed index is unstable under the figure's own resampling. **Honest limit flagged to operator:** the LLM's "cuts at C and D" labelling (an off-circle direction point named as a crossing) is decomposition-quality, not a parser defect; matching the book exactly is up-to-placement, not pixel-identical.

- **2026-06-17 — LLM fallback now re-parses its canonical steps WITH the figure context (927 green).** Operator, continuing the two-circle figure: `C נקודה על המעגל הימני` (→ LLM → `C על מעגל P`, OK), then `AC חותך את המעגל השמאלי בנקודה D` — "the next command failed" (nothing drew). Diagnosed from `logs/debug-log.jsonl`: the step escalated, the LLM produced the canonical line `מנקודה A ישר חותך את המעגל O בנקודות C ו-D`, and the parser **dropped** it (`built: []`). **Root cause:** `llmParse` re-parsed each canonical line with bare `parse(step)` — **no figure context**. `secantFromExternal`'s "another secant from an EXISTING external point E" branch only fires when `ctx.points.includes(E)`; with no context, E=A looked new, so it fell to the "first secant" branch (which requires an "outside/מחוץ" cue not present) → `null` → dropped. This was a GENERAL bug: every context-dependent rule was blind when reached via the LLM. Fix (`llm.ts`): `llmParse(utterance, context, figureCtx?)` threads the same structured `{points, circles}` the deterministic parse used into each step's re-parse, and **accumulates** ids introduced by earlier LLM steps (`absorb`) so a later step can reference them (the LLM often emits "circle O" then "point on circle O"). App passes `parseCtx()`. Tests: 2 focused `llm.test.ts` cases (context makes the secant build not drop; cross-step accumulation) + e2e scenario `two-circles-then-secant-from-A`. **Learning:** the LLM fallback's re-parse must run with the SAME figure context as the first-pass deterministic parse — otherwise context-dependent rules silently drop and the escalation looks like "it just failed."

- **2026-06-17 — two intersecting circles: no chord, distinct radii, "show another" no longer errors (924 green).** Operator, on the FR-EN-19 two-circle figure (`שני מעגלים נחתכים`), reported three things: (1) the chord AB shouldn't be drawn, (2) both circles look fixed/same-size, (3) "show another configuration" *sometimes* errors about point B. **Root cause of (3):** A and B are the SAME `circle-circle` crossing at branches 0 and 1 — BOTH already on screen — so the "another configuration" button (which picks the first branchable point and cycles it) flipped A's branch 0→1 onto B's, making A≡B and failing the second crossing; it alternated error/no-error per press. Fixes: `twoCirclesMeet` (parse.ts) now (1) omits the common-chord segment and (2) gives the two circles **distinct default radii** (5 and 5·0.72≈3.6) so they read as two different circles, not a symmetric lens. New `cyclableBranch(c, id)` in step.ts (exported via `@/engine`) reports whether a branchable point has an *unshown* branch — for the two-circle figure both branches are already drawn (a `sameBranchSource` sibling occupies the other), so it returns false; App.tsx's `branchId` now gates on it, so the button **only resamples the circle centres** (which already vary as free points) and never collides A onto B. Regression scenario `two-circles-show-another` (12 presses, asserts evaluate-OK + A,B distinct + branchId undefined) + updated `two-circles-meet-at-A-and-B` (no segment, distinct radii). **Learning:** "show another configuration" must not cycle a branch whose complement is already materialised — for a construct that draws BOTH solutions up front, variation comes from resampling, not branch-stepping.

- **2026-06-16 — SESSION CLOSE (long corpus/manual-testing marathon: 876 → 923 green, all pushed).** A full day of the operator building real bagrut circle/tangent/secant figures step-by-step and reporting each thing that broke; every fix diagnosed from `logs/debug-log.jsonl`, fixed at root, regression-tested, committed+pushed individually (commits `988aa58`…`9c6e358`). The per-fix entries are below; the **durable learnings** that should shape future work:
  - **Construct-vs-constrain is a duality with TWO pre-existence arrangements.** A phrasing ("CD ⟂ AB at F", "CD ⊥-bisector of AB") means *construct* or *constrain* depending on which points already exist — AND there are two arrangements (the NAME pre-exists vs the through/cut point pre-exists). A single fix/test covers only one; **always cover both** (the `perpendicular-cuts-at-existing-point` vs `existing-segment-perpendicular-cuts-at-new-point` pair).
  - **A loose pre-drawn segment can't be cleanly *constrained* into a positioned construction** (the segments don't visibly meet); the robust path is to *reposition* via the construct (an `on-line` marker may replace a loose free point — ADR-036 A4). Constraint-retrofit onto free points is fragile/over-constrains.
  - **The LLM mis-decomposes multi-step circle constructs** — a secant → a circular reference (`E on OA` before A exists); "two circles meet at A,B" → a single point G; a tangent-from-E → a `circle-through` that redefines the circle. **Parser-first is the fix:** build a deterministic rule for each common construct (secant/tangents/two-secants/two-circles-meet) rather than leaning on the LLM.
  - **A generic "stop on keyword" guard can abort a parse before a more specific rule runs** (the `חותך`/"cuts" intersection guard killed the perpendicular construction). Order specific rules before the generic ones.
  - **Symbolic vars need binding to bite:** the radius `R` now auto-binds to the circle's radius; a lone symbolic measure is a free *label*, two sharing a var form a ratio.
  - **Sampler/DOF rules:** "show another configuration" must resample free DOFs *and* cycle branches (not branch-only); on-circle jitter scales with count (a 2-point chord reshapes freely, a polygon stays spread); the DOF count is `raw − constraints` (a `solve`-marked free point is NOT determined).
  - **Scaffolding is hidden, meaningful points are shown:** auxiliary helpers use a `~`-prefix id (scene skips them); a circle's centre shows only when **used or named** (FR-RN-8).
  - **Label-capture regexes need a leading `\b`** or the "point"/prefix word leaks a letter (`point A` → "t").
  - **New constructs added this session:** secant from an external point (FR-EN-17), two tangents + a single tangent + multiple secants from a point (FR-EN-18), two circles intersecting at A,B (FR-EN-19), unnamed-circle-with-hidden-centre (FR-RN-8). All bilingual, cataloged, covered, with regression scenarios. **Resume:** Phase 6 (theorems) remains the headline; the parked threads (area constraint, production proxy deploy, coord-validation campaign) are unchanged.

- **2026-06-16 — a circle's centre shows only when used or named (FR-RN-8); + fixed "point A on circle O" mis-parse. 920 green.** Operator design question: "sometimes we want to see the circle's centre, sometimes not." Chose (AskUserQuestion) **"show if used or named."** Implemented: a new `autoCenter?` flag on the `circle`/`circle-through` command + `Circle` object. The parser/apply set it on AUTO centres — circumcentre (`circumcircle`, always), incentre (`incircle`, unless named), a defaulted "two circles" centre (`twoCirclesMeet`, the unnamed ones), and an unnamed inscribed-polygon centre. A NAMED centre (`circle O`) leaves it unset. `scene.ts` collects auto-centre ids and **skips drawing one that has no incident segment** (the `incident` map already tracks segments per point — a radius/central-angle arm makes the centre "used"). Verified: `circle O` → O shown; `circle O`+∠AOB → O shown (arms); `two circles meet at A,B` → centres hidden; `circle through A B C` → centre hidden; incircle → incentre hidden, tangency foot shown; drawing a radius later → centre appears. **Pre-existing bug surfaced & fixed:** `point A on circle O` parsed to `id:T` — the `pointOnCircle`/`midpoint` label captures lacked a leading `\b`, so they grabbed the "t" of "poin**t**"; added `\b` (and a coverage line). Render test (centre show/hide/appear-on-use) + 2 coverage lines. FR-RN-8. **Follow-up (operator, same session):** "when I write מעגל it still shows O — only show O if I say מעגל O." Cause: a bare/unnamed circle wasn't parsed deterministically (it required a named centre), so it escalated and the LLM invented "circle centered at O" (a *named* circle → shown). Fixed: the `circle` rule now handles an UNNAMED circle (`מעגל` / `circle` / `circle radius 5`) → auto-assigns a centre with `autoCenter` (hidden); a standalone named `circle O` / `מעגל O` is created with the centre shown; `A on circle O` (another label remains) and "draw a circle somewhere" (words remain) are NOT mistaken for a circle definition (a leftover check stripping the circle word + named centre + filler). `circle` rule now takes `ctx` (freeLabel for the auto centre). +3 coverage lines.

- **2026-06-16 — "two circles intersect at A and B" now creates both circles + both intersections (FR-EN-19, 915 green).** Operator: two circles "supposed to meet at A and B but don't." No deterministic rule existed, so it escalated and the LLM made a SINGLE point G (not A *and* B). (The circles themselves DO overlap at default centres — O=(0,0), P=(4,0) — so that wasn't the issue; the missing pair of points was.) Built `twoCirclesMeet` (before `circleCircleIntersection`): trigger = plural circles (`circles`/`שני מעגל`/`מעגלים`) + an intersect keyword + the two points (the pair after "at"/"בנקודות", so named centres "O and P" aren't misread as the points). Creates circle O + circle P (default radius) + A = branch 0, B = branch 1 + the common chord AB. Centres default to O, P (or the named ones). Verified He + En + named ("circles O and P meet at A and B"): A,B distinct and equidistant from BOTH centres (on both circles). The single-point `G is the intersection of circle O and circle P` (existing circles) and `circles … tangent` are NOT stolen. Catalog + coverage + scenario `two-circles-meet-at-A-and-B`.

- **2026-06-16 — "OC = 0.5R" now sizes the chord against the radius (two bugs: unbound R + a chord/midpoint half-parse). 912 green.** Operator: declared the circle radius R ("nothing happened"), then `OC=0.5R` didn't reshape; only `OD=R` (D on the circle) made it adjust. Two root causes, both fixed: **(1) R was unbound.** The reserved radius symbol `R` (ADR-034) only got a value when the circle was *declared* `radius R`; a numeric-radius circle left `R` free, so `OC=0.5R` was a label (and the standalone "the radius is R" went to the LLM and was dropped). `OD=R` only worked because it formed a *ratio* `|OC|=0.5|OD|` with D on the circle. Fix: `buildSymTab` now **auto-binds `R` to the circle's radius** when a measure uses it and no value was given (ADR-034 Amendment 1) — so `OC=0.5R` → `|OC|=2.5` directly, driving A,B via the derived-midpoint recruit. **(2) chord/midpoint half-parse.** `נקודה C היא אמצע מיתר AB` ("C is the midpoint of CHORD AB") was grabbed by the `chord` rule (made A,B + segment, **dropped midpoint C**) because `chord` ran before `midpoint`. Fix: moved `midpoint` before `chord`/`diameter` (still after `arcMidpoint`, which owns "אמצע הקשת"). Verified end-to-end: circle r5 → secant → `C אמצע מיתר AB` (midpoint) → `OC=0.5R` ⇒ |OC|=2.5. Scenario `oc-half-radius-sizes-the-chord` + 2 coverage lines. *(Note: a standalone "the radius is R" declaration still isn't parsed — unnecessary now that R auto-binds.)*

- **2026-06-16 — on-circle resample variety scales with count, so a secant's chord AB reshapes freely (908 green).** Operator: "I cannot get AB to look like the book." Verified the figure is mathematically correct/equivalent and resampling already varied AB — but only across a NARROW band (arc gap ~96–152°), because the on-circle sampler used a shared spin + a fixed ±30° per-vertex jitter (designed to stop inscribed POLYGONS collapsing to a sliver). For a secant's two free on-circle ends, that meant AB mostly rotated rather than reshaping. Operator chose (AskUserQuestion) "improve resample variety." Fix (`sample.ts`): the per-vertex jitter now **scales with the count of free on-circle points** — `≤2` (a chord/secant) gets a WIDE ±153° (AB now spans ~6°–177° arc: short to near-diameter chords, any orientation; a seed that makes the two coincide is just skipped by `resample`), `≥3` (an inscribed polygon) keeps the safe ±30° (verified an inscribed triangle's min vertex gap stays ~66°). So book-like configs are easy to reach by pressing "show another configuration." +1 sample test. **Context that frames this:** matching a textbook layout EXACTLY isn't a goal — the standard is "a valid drawing up to free placement/rotation/scale/reflection" (the givens don't pin A,B); direct positioning/drag of free points is Phase 8, not built.

- **2026-06-16 — the tangent-from-external auxiliary midpoint is now a HIDDEN helper (was a stray, un-removable point). 908 green.** Operator: a point N "somehow" appeared and "when I removed the step that created it, N stayed and I had no way to removing it." Diagnosis: N was the **auxiliary Thales-circle centre** (midpoint of O-E) the tangent-from-external construction materialised — a visible, labelled point the student never asked for. `removeGroup` actually DOES remove it (verified) — but it lives *inside* the tangent step (no row of its own), so you can't remove just N without losing the tangent, and it's unclear which step owns it. Fix: name the aux midpoint with a **`~` prefix** (`~tanmid-<O><E>`) — `scene.ts` already skips `~`-prefixed ids (the ADR-028 hidden-helper convention), so it stays in the construction (the hidden circle needs it) but is **never drawn/labelled**. Both tangent-from-external rules (one & two tangents) updated; the aux circle id is now `tanaux-…`. Verified: drawn points are just O,A,B,E,D — no stray aux — and the tangents are still valid (D on circle, ED⟂OD). Regression in `single-tangent-from-external-point` (asserts a `~` helper exists and no unexpected single-letter point is drawn). FR-EN-18 limit note updated.

- **2026-06-16 — "show another configuration" was branch-cycling EXCLUSIVELY when a branch existed (free DOFs ignored). 908 green.** Operator (comparing to the book): a secant+tangent-from-E figure showed **5 DOF** but "show another configuration" only swapped between **2 options** and **AB was fixed**. Root cause: the App button was `branchId ? cycleAlt(branchId) : resample()` — since D (circle∩circle) is branchable, `branchId` was truthy, so it ALWAYS cycled D's 2 branches and NEVER resampled the free DOFs (A,B free on-circle). Fix: the button now calls **both** — `resample()` (no-op when no free DOFs) **and** `cycleAlt(branchId)` if a branch exists — so each press varies the continuous free DOFs *and* steps the discrete branch. Verified A,B vary across seeds for this figure (resample was always capable; the button just never invoked it). Regression `secant-tangent-resample` (a figure with BOTH a branchable point and free DOFs must vary the free DOFs). FR-ALT-2 updated. *(Note: the `circle∩circle` object's kind is `'circle-circle'`, not the command name `circle-circle-intersection`.)* This also answers the operator's "doesn't match the book" — both are valid drawings; with resample now reachable they can cycle A,B to a book-like arrangement.

- **2026-06-16 — single tangent from an external point (completes the external-point/tangent family, FR-EN-18, 907 green).** Operator: "the part with the משיק isn't working" — after a secant from E they typed `ED משיק למעגל` (LLM dropped it) and `מנקודה E יוצא משיק למעגל` (LLM turned it into a `circle-through` that **redefined circle-O** → error). A SINGLE tangent from an external point was unsupported (only the new two-tangent rule + tangent-AT-a-point). Built `tangentFromExternal` (between the two-tangent rule and `tangentLine`): the apex is the EXISTING external point (explicit "from E"/"מנקודה E", or the existing letter of a named pair "ED"; a new "from E" is placed external), the touch point is named (D) or auto; one branch of the same Thales-circle construction. **Label extraction:** uppercase tokens (`/[A-Z]\d*/`) = point labels (works for He+En since keywords are lowercase/Hebrew); "בנקודה/at X" marks the TOUCH (not the apex), so tangent-AT-a-point ("tangent to circle O at A") still routes to `tangentLine`. Verified: `ED משיק למעגל` after a secant → D on circle, ED⟂OD; tangent-at unaffected. Catalog + coverage (single tangent, both locales), scenario `single-tangent-from-external-point`. *(Cosmetic: the construction shows the midpoint of O-E as an auxiliary point M — points have no hidden flag.)*

- **2026-06-16 — external-point family extended: TWO tangents from a point + MULTIPLE secants from one point (FR-EN-18, 904 green).** Operator asked which of three external-point constructs we have: (#2) one secant — ✅ (FR-EN-17, just built); (#1) two tangents from an external point — ❌; (#3) two secants from the same point — ❌. Built both. **#1 two tangents:** the touch points satisfy tangent⟂radius (Thales: ∠OAE=90°), so A,B = `circle O ∩ circle-on-diameter-OE`. Built from existing primitives — midpoint M of OE, a **hidden** auxiliary `circle-through(M,O)` (added a `hidden?` flag to `circle-through` + apply), the two circle∩circle touch points, and the two tangent segments EA,EB. Verified: A,B on circle, E outside, EA⟂OA & EB⟂OB, |EA|=|EB|. A new external E defaults to a standard outside position (limit: single circle near origin). **#3 two secants from one E:** a constraint-based 2nd secant moved E *inside* (the joint solver drove E's parents). Fix: the 2nd secant (E already in `ctx.points`) reuses E with **no constraint** — `line-through(E,C)` + `D = line-circle branch 0` (branch 1 coincides with C); E stays external. The `secantFromExternal` rule now branches on whether E exists. **Gotcha (shared):** `circleCenter` reads the English article in "the circle **a** line" as a centre — both rules accept a named centre only if uppercase, else the single context circle. Catalog + coverage (both locales) for the tangents; regression scenarios `two-tangents-from-external-point` + `two-secants-from-same-point`; FR-EN-18.

- **2026-06-16 — new construct: a SECANT from a point outside the circle (FR-EN-17, 900 green).** Operator: "a new construction to test … perhaps I wrote the steps too complicated but it should still work" — `נתון מעגל O שרדיוסו R`, then `מנקודה E מחוץ למעגל מעבירים ישר שחותך את המעגל בנקודות A ו- B` ("from external point E a line cuts the circle at A and B"). The parser had no such construct → it escalated, and the LLM decomposed it into `נקודה E על המשך OA` — **referencing A before A exists** (`unresolved dependencies for E`), a circular definition. Built a deterministic `secantFromExternal` rule (before the generic intersection rules, which would `'stop'` on the "חותך"/cuts keyword): trigger = outside/מחוץ + a cut keyword (חותך/cuts/secant/meets) + a circle; extract the external point E (after from/מנקודה), the two intersections A,B (the `X and/ו Y` pair). Emits A,B `point-on-circle` (a chord) + the chord segment + `point-on-segment E` on the extension (a=B,b=A,t=1.3 → E beyond A, **outside**) + the external part EA. Expressed entirely with existing primitives. **Gotcha fixed:** `circleCenter` read the English article in "the circle **a** line" as a centre "a"; the rule now accepts a named centre only if it's **uppercase** (a real label), else the single context circle. Verified He + En + the "secant" wording: A,B on circle, |OE|>R (E outside), E-A-B collinear. Catalog + coverage entries (both locales), regression scenario `secant-from-external-point`, FR-EN-17.

- **2026-06-16 — a loose "segment CD" then "CD ⟂ AB at F" now becomes a CLEAN perpendicular cross (engine change, ADR-036 Amendment 4, 897 green).** Operator: "still this scenario isn't working" — `segment AB`, `segment CD`, `CD אנך ל AB בנקודה F`. The prior day's constraint-only fix (set-perpendicular + intersection) made CD ⟂ AB but the **segments didn't visually cross** — F (the line∩line) landed at param ~2.5 on AB and ~−1 on CD (off both segments). Probed **five** constraint formulations (collinear via `CF∥CD`, midpoint, `CF⟂AB`+`DF⟂AB`, …) — all left F off-segment or over-constrained. Root cause: retrofitting a *loose free* segment into a *positioned* perpendicular is not something the constraint solver does (it pins the angle, not the position). **The clean model is the CONSTRUCT path** (perpendicular through F with C,D as markers straddling F) — verified to give a perfect centred cross when C,D are NEW; the only blocker was that pre-existing free C,D couldn't be repositioned (`addObj` won't replace; redefinition rejected). **Operator chose (AskUserQuestion) the engine fix.** Built: (1) `perpendicularLine` always constructs — creates the cut-point F as `point-on-segment` on AB if new, the `perpendicular-line` through F, and `lineMarkers` for the named endpoints; (2) an **`on-line` marker repositions a loose free point** — `commandConflict` exempts `on-line` over a free-point (like `on-circle`), and `apply`'s `point-on-line` **replaces** a free point with the marker (structural points untouched). Result: `segment CD` then `CD ⟂ AB at F` → C,D move onto the perpendicular, F centred on AB — clean cross, every arrangement (name new/old × F new/old). Removed the ⟂ constrain-branch from the day before (parallel keeps its `set-parallel` branch — no cut-point). `existing-segment-perpendicular-cuts-at-new-point` strengthened to assert the clean cross (E on AB AND between C,D). **Lesson:** a loose-segment-then-declare-it construction wants reposition-via-construct, not constraint-retrofit.

- **2026-06-16 — perpendicular naming, the MIRROR configuration: existing CD ⟂ AB cutting at a NEW E (896 green).** Operator: "check this last case that has an error. it was tested before so why wasn't it caught" — `segment AB`, `segment CD`, then `CD אנך ל AB וחותך אותו בנקודה E`. **Why the earlier `perpendicular-cuts-at-existing-point` scenario missed it:** that one tested the *opposite* arrangement — the NAME (ED) was new and the cut-point (C) already existed (so the through-point anchored the construction). Here the NAME (CD) **already exists** and the cut-point (E) is **new** — so the construct path anchored the perpendicular on the not-yet-made E (`unresolved dependencies for perp-E-AB`) and re-created C,D as markers (`'C'/'D' is already defined`). **Fix:** `perpendicularLine`/`parallelLine` are now context-aware (take `ctx`): if the named segment's points already exist, it's a **CONSTRAINT** — `set-perpendicular` (or `set-parallel`) on the existing segment, and (for ⟂) the cut-point is created as the `line-line-intersection` of the two segments (only if it's new; parallels make no cut-point). Else the existing CONSTRUCT path runs (through-point anchors a new drawn line + markers). Lesson: a construct-vs-constrain duality has **two** "which points pre-exist" arrangements — cover both. New regression `existing-segment-perpendicular-cuts-at-new-point` (the mirror), alongside the prior `perpendicular-cuts-at-existing-point`.

- **2026-06-16 — named perpendicular bisector "CD אנך אמצעי ל-AB" (construct OR constrain). 895 green.** Operator: "i added something we didnt deal with אנך אמצעי". The `perpBisector` rule read the first 2 labels after stripping keywords, so `CD אנך אמצעי ל AB` grabbed **CD** (the line's name) and bisected *that*, **dropping AB**. Two fixes: (1) the **bisected segment is the one after the connector** (`of`/`ל`/`to AB`), never the leading name; (2) a **leading name** (`CD …`) labels the bisector line. Then context-aware (`ctx.points`): if the named segment **already exists** (operator had drawn `segment CD` first) it's a **CONSTRAINT** — emit `|CA|=|CB|` and `|DA|=|DB|` (two `set-equal`) so the existing line CD is driven to the ⊥-bisector of AB, **without re-creating C/D** (which had errored `'D' is already defined`); if C,D **don't** exist it **CONSTRUCTS** — midpoint M of AB + the drawn ⟂ line + C,D markers straddling M. Plain forms (`אנך אמצעי ל-AB`, `perpendicular bisector of AB`) unchanged (bisect AB, auto-named M, no markers). Regression scenario `named-perp-bisector-of-existing-segment` (the constrain path) + index. `perpBisector` now takes `ctx`.

- **2026-06-16 — the "degrees of freedom" cue was reading 0 when the figure was plainly free (root: a wrong DOF model). Fixed to `raw − constraints` (895 green).** Operator report: built `segment AB` + `segment CD` + `CD⊥AB` + `F = AB∩CD`; the cue said **"✓ fully determined"** though the figure obviously has freedom. Diagnosed from `logs/debug-log.jsonl` (session `pq8dei7m`) + a probe: the `set-perpendicular` constraint marks **all four** referenced free vertices (A,B,C,D) with a `solve` directive (the joint-solver design, ADR-030/033), and my first Stage-2/3 cut counted **any** `solve`-marked free point as 0 DOF → 8 raw − (4 vertices × 2) ⇒ 0. **Conceptual error:** a `solve`-marked free *vertex* is NOT determined — it just participates in the joint solve; one scalar constraint removes **one** DOF regardless of how many vertices it couples. **Fix (sample.ts):** `freeDofCount` = Σ `rawMovableDof` (free point 2, parametric/shape scalar 1, derived/pinned 0) − Σ `dofRemoved(con)` (equality 1, `coincide` 2, order/inequality 0 — ADR-039), gathering constraints from BOTH `construction.constraints` (checked) and every object's `solve.constraint` (driven — a driven constraint lives ONLY there), deduped by identity. Verified: the two-segments-⟂ figure = **7**; lone square = 4; pinned square = 0; parallelogram + `|AB|=|AC|` = 5 (6−1); lone triangle = 6. **Also corrected the sampler:** `applySeed`/`freeDofs` no longer exclude a `solve`-marked free *vertex* (only fully-consumed *parametric*/shape-scalar carriers stay excluded — perturbing those is a no-op). So such a figure is **resamplable** again (perturb the free vertices, re-solve the constraint → a different valid drawing), and the "show another configuration" button reappears. Rewrote the Stage-2 sample test (6→**5**, not 6→4) + added the reported `set-perpendicular`-over-four-vertices case. Docs: ADR-018 "Correction (2026-06-16)", FR-ALT-4, this entry. **895 green, build clean.** (Note: still uses the placement-inclusive convention — a lone square is 4, a 3-side triangle is 3 rigid-motion DOF — same as the earlier decision; a "shape-determined regardless of placement" mode remains an option if wanted.)

- **2026-06-16 — a NAMED perpendicular ("DE אנך ל-AB בנקודה C") now keeps its endpoints, deterministically (893 green). + a future-capability note.** Operator testing report (from `logs/debug-log.jsonl`, session `5f5wkzin`): built `ישר AB` → `נקודה C על AB` → `DE אנך לAB בנקודה C`. The third step **escalated to the LLM**, which rewrote it to an UNNAMED `line through C perpendicular to AB` — **dropping D and E**, so the perpendicular drew but its named endpoints never existed. Root fix in the **parser** (parser-first, so the LLM isn't needed and can't drop the name): (1) the perpendicular/parallel-line rules accept the through-point via **"at P" / "בנקודה P"** (new shared `THROUGH_PT`), not only "through/דרך" — so "⟂ AB at C" reads C as the foot; (2) `lineNameLabels` also reads a **leading** line name (`DE ⟂ …` / `DE אנך …`, anchored on the relation keyword), not only a name after the word "line". So `DE אנך לAB בנקודה C` → `perpendicular-line through C ⟂ AB` + D, E as `on-line` markers straddling C (referenceable, ADR-036). The bare ⟂/∥ **constraint** ("AB ⟂ CD") and the **foot** ("perpendicular from C to AB") are untouched — both lack a through-point, so the line rule still returns null for them. Regression: the operator's exact 3-step sequence as scenario `named-perpendicular-through-point` (step 1 recorded as its logged LLM command). +parser changes, +1 scenario, indexed in docs/test-scenarios.md. **893 green, build clean, pushed.**
  - **Follow-up (same family, "cuts" variant): `ישר ED אנך לAB וחותך אותו בנקודה C` (894 green).** This escalated too, and the LLM modelled the foot as `C על ED` — **redefining C** (already on AB) → "'C' is already defined" over-constraint. Root cause: the `חותך`/"cuts" keyword is in `INTERSECT_KW`, so the generic `lineLineIntersection` rule (which runs before `perpendicularLine`) recognised it but couldn't read it → returned **`'stop'`**, aborting the whole parse to the LLM. Fix: **moved `perpendicularLine` + `parallelLine` before `lineLineIntersection`** in the RULES order, so a perpendicular/parallel line that "cuts" another at a named point is claimed as a line-through-the-point construction (reusing the existing C) before the intersection rule can stop on it. They only fire on a perpendicular/parallel keyword + an explicit through-point, so a plain `F = AC ∩ BD` still falls through to `lineLineIntersection`. Regression scenario `perpendicular-cuts-at-existing-point`.
  - **Operator comment recorded as a FUTURE capability (FR-IN-9, Later):** a **coordinate data-entry mode for analytic geometry**. Students don't normally type coordinates when building a synthetic figure (the engine uses them internally, and a lone `A = (x,y)` already parses) — but a deliberate opt-in coordinate/grid mode would be useful for *analytical* geometry. Deferred; not a first-class mode today.

- **2026-06-16 — "show another configuration" must surface only a CLEAN CONVEX quad (two fixes + a confirmed decision).** Operator testing the deferred-backlog batch built `מרובע ABCD` + `AC=10` + `DB=10` and cycled configurations. Two reports, two fixes (commits `478b4be`, `966f32e`):
  - **Self-crossing quad (`478b4be`).** Resampling landed on a tangled (bowtie) ABCD.
