Root cause: `resample()` accepted any seed that merely *evaluates*, and a crossed quad evaluates fine (no coincident points). Added a polygon guard the sampler must pass; the operator's exact sequence became scenario `quad-diagonals-resample` (resample-aware — it presses "show another configuration" across seeds, which the seed-0 scenario runner can't reach).
  - **Concave "dart" quad (`966f32e`).** After the crossing fix it still landed on a concave dart (B caved inward). Strengthened the guard from *simple* (`polygonsSimple`) to *convex* (`polygonsConvex`, exported from geoStore — every turn around the `ids`-cycle has the same orientation, rejecting BOTH crossed and concave). ~35% of seeds are convex for that figure (max gap ~7), well inside resample's 16-seed window.
  - **Confirmed decision → general PRINCIPLE (AskUserQuestion + operator follow-up):** **every polygon is CONVEX by default; a concave shape appears only if the student explicitly asks for it (`קעור` / "concave").** The operator's words: *"unless I say something about קעור, the shape is convex."* Rationale: bagrut/textbook figures are conventionally convex; a dart reads as a glitch. So `polygonsConvex` is intentional, **not** an overreach — do not relax it to allow concave darts by default. Also clarified: `מרובע` ≠ square (`ריבוע`); equal diagonals don't force a square, so "no square" was expected. 892 green throughout. **Coverage of the principle today:** default templates (seed 0) convex ✓; the sampler (`resample`, seed > 0) convex ✓ (this fix). **Deferred gaps (build when a corpus problem needs them, not speculatively):** (1) *constraint-driven* reshaping isn't yet convexity-guarded — a constraint could pull a vertex concave and the solver wouldn't prevent it (would need a convexity preference/penalty in `resolveFreeDriven`/`resolveMixedCarriers`); (2) there is **no `קעור` keyword** yet — no way to opt INTO concave, and no construct behind it. Captured as **FR-EN-16**.

- **2026-06-15 (home PC) — cleared the deferred-backlog (the five "smaller deferred directions" the plan had parked). 876 → 891 green, build clean, all pushed.** Operator returned from a full office day and asked for "another batch"; via AskUserQuestion they chose **clear the deferred backlog** over starting Phase 6. Knocked out all five plan items in order, one commit each:
  - **(a) Auto-draw segments for distance/equal/angle givens (FR-IN-7 extension, `988aa58`).** `AB = 6` now draws `AB`, `AB = CD` draws both compared segments, `∠ABC = 37` draws its two arms (`BA`,`BC`) — the same "draw its segments" convenience that ⟂/∥ already had. Idempotent (no dup on an existing corner); the chained `AB = AC = 3x` inherits it via its recursive re-parse. Symbolic measures (`AB = 3x`, `∠ABC = 2α`) deliberately NOT auto-drawn (they flow through the lowering pipeline) — a noted, intentional consistency boundary. Updated the parser tests that pinned exact command arrays/counts (phase4 angle block → 3 cmds; symbolic distance/equal/chain/radius-symbol). +2 focused tests.
  - **(b) Explicit merge command (FR-HS-8, `082b9b0`).** The fold rename refuses (ADR-035 forbids a silent merge). `merge F into E` / `מזג F ל-E` (`parseMerge`, distinct keywords merge/fold/combine/unify, מזג/אחד) drops F's own single-point definition (E survives), rewrites every F→E across facts+utterances, and drops collapsed facts (`segment EF`→`EE`, etc. via `collapsedDegenerate`). Refuses a **shape vertex** (no standalone def to tear out → `source-in-shape`) and a missing target (merging into a NEW letter is a rename → `no-target`). Undoable. `merge.test.ts` (7).
  - **(c) Free on-line marker's offset is now sampled by "show another configuration" (ADR-036 Amendment 2, `18ba417`).** Closed the A1 limit: `applySeed`/`freeDofs` treat an `on-line` marker with `solve===undefined` as a free DOF; the sampler scales its signed offset 0.4×–2.4× (sign kept, so a tangent's C/D keep straddling T). +1 test.
  - **(d) Named-line markers widened beyond the tangent (ADR-036 Amendment 3, `ee1e581`).** Extracted the tangent's marker logic into shared `lineMarkers()` + `lineNameLabels()` (the 1–2 labels right after the line word, anchored so it can't misfire on a keyword). `perpendicularLine`/`parallelLine` now mark a named far end: `line PQ through P ⟂ AB` creates `Q` on the line at +offset from `P`. The bisector-named-by-a-point case already had its own path (`line-intersection`) — unchanged. +3 tests.
  - **(e) ADR-018 Stages 2 & 3 (FR-ALT-4, `8f452e0`).** **Stage 2:** `applySeed`/`freeDofs`/the new `freeDofCount` now also skip a constraint-DRIVEN free point (`FreePoint.solve`, ADR-030), not just the parametric carriers — so pinning a vertex removes it from sampling/counting (verified: a parallelogram's `|AB|=|AC|` drops the DOF count 6→4). **Stage 3:** `freeDofCount(construction)` (free point = 2, free parametric/shape DOF = 1, driven/pinned = 0) drives a live cue beside "show another configuration" — *"Degrees of freedom: N"* (blue) while underdetermined, *"✓ fully determined"* (green) at 0. The convergence story made literal. Shared `freeDofWeight` backs both functions. +4 tests. **ADR-018 is now fully built (all 3 stages).**
  - Docs kept in sync each commit: FR-IN-7 widened, new FR-HS-8 + FR-ALT-4, ADR-018 status → Stages 2&3 built, ADR-036 Amendments 2&3. A **hands-on verification checklist** for this batch (what to type, what to see, per change) is in **[manual-verification-2026-06-15.md](manual-verification-2026-06-15.md)** — every utterance in it was scratch-verified to parse+build. Also swept two stray Dropbox/editor `.tmp` files (`PROJECT-MEMORY.md.tmp…`, `Figure.tsx.tmp…`). **Next milestone is unchanged: Phase 6 (theorems)** — the backlog is now empty except the bigger parked items (area constraint; production proxy deploy; the differential coordinate-validation campaign).

- **2026-06-15 — inequalities between named measures ("α<β") actively reshape the figure (ADR-039, FR-IN-8). 870 → 876 green.** Operator report (from `logs/debug-log.jsonl`): on the Q5 median figure they typed `α<β` (∠BAP=α, ∠ABP=β) and it errored ("not supported"); the system figure had ∠BAP **>** ∠ABP (opposite the book), and "show another configuration" 8× never fixed it. Operator's call (AskUserQuestion): **active reshape** (not selection-only or label-only). They also clarified the `AE⊥BD` they'd added is legit (a later sub-question), not the cause. **Diagnosis via a throwaway probe** (build the exact figure, sample 277 seeds): α<β IS feasible but holds in only **~3.6 % of seeds** (max gap β−α ≈ 21.7°) — that's why resampling missed it; the default config violates by ~26°. **Built:** a new **order/inequality constraint** (`angle-order`/`length-order`) with a **one-sided residual** `max(0, smaller−larger+MARGIN)` — pushed as a *check* (a region, not a point, satisfies it, so no bracketing carrier). Two thresholds **decouple visible-target from acceptance**: solver aims for MARGIN (8° / 12 %), accepts any strict gap ≥ MIN_GAP (1° / 2 %) — so a tight-geometry figure isn't falsely flagged a contradiction. Parser `measureOrder` (`α<β`/`x>y`/`≤`/`≥`, bare single-letter vars) → `measure-order` symbolic command → lowered (symbol table → which measure each var names) to `set-angle-order`/`set-length-order`. **The key fix** (`withOrderCons`, evaluate.ts): an inequality never owns a carrier, so when its DOFs are already driving another constraint (here |BP|=3|PD| had recruited A,B,C,E) the joint solver — which only minimised *carrier* constraints — ignored it → "over-constrained". Now every solver path (mixed/free/coupled) **also folds the construction's order constraints into the minimised set**, so they solve jointly with the already-moving DOFs (surgical — only figures with an order constraint are touched). Added a `<` toolbar button + catalog entry. Result: `α<β` reshapes deterministically to ∠BAP≈41° < ∠ABP≈49° (every seed) with the givens intact. **Files:** types/solve/lower/apply/evaluate/parse/catalog + App.tsx (button) + geoStore (type). **Tests:** the operator's exact 10-step sequence as scenario `alpha-less-than-beta-reshapes` (NON-NEGOTIABLE rule), +5 unit/lowering tests. **876 green, tsc + build clean.** Open thread the operator raised: a later given **"area of triangle = 0.25k²"** — an *area* constraint is a new capability (no engine support yet); deferred, flagged for a future session.

- **2026-06-15 (work PC) — long corpus-driven testing session: new constructs + a string of figure fixes, capped by robust coupled-constraint solving. 836 → 864 green.** The operator built real bagrut figures step-by-step (the cyclic-quad + tangent-chord family) and reported each thing that broke; I diagnosed from the new **debug log** (`logs/debug-log.jsonl`, see operational note) and fixed in order. Everything committed + pushed (`4978f2f`…`7f1af3a`). What landed:
  - **Cyclic quadrilateral `מרובע ABCD בר חסימה` (FR-EN-14, `4978f2f`).** A concyclic convex quad whose circle is **NOT drawn** — a new optional **`hidden`** flag on the `Circle` object (constrains its on-circle points; `scene.ts` skips it, and it doesn't pad the fit). Reuses `inscribedPolygon`; a generic-convex ordered angle set (`CYCLIC_QUAD_ANGLES`) so vertices stay in cyclic order. Contrast `חסום במעגל` (inscribed), which draws the circle.
  - **Semicircle + quarter circle (FR-EN-15, `4a7f4be`).** A first-class **`Arc`** geo-object (CCW from→to around a centre) drawn as an SVG elliptical-arc `<path>` (`SceneArc`; scene computes largeArc/sweep for the Y-flip; fit includes the arc's swept extent). Semicircle = 180° arc on a diameter (diameter drawn); quarter = 90° arc + two radii; both carried by a hidden circle so only the arc + straight edges render. Sweep is **0** for a world-CCW arc (screen Y is down) — got this backwards once, fixed.
  - **Debug session log (`aaa81ca`)** — see the operational note above; it made every report reconstructible.
  - **Diameter on a cyclic polygon reshapes it convexly (`f7914b5`), persistently (`c566e97`).** "AD diameter" on a cyclic quad re-places ALL vertices (anchor A, D antipodal, redistribute B,C across each arc) instead of shoving one vertex onto a neighbour; D becomes the **antipode** of A so the diameter SURVIVES a later constraint (e.g. `∠BDA=24`); guarded by `constraintRefs` so figures whose vertices are already driven (ADR-028) keep the coupled path. Also: an **on-segment-solved point prefers the root nearest its `t0`**, so a foot on an *extension* (F on the continuation of CB) stays on the extension, not segment CB.
  - **Align-segment-horizontal now PERSISTS (FR-RN-6, `7d3a2cc`).** Stores the *segment* (not a frozen angle) and recomputes the rotation each render (`alignRotation` in `transform.ts`), so the chosen segment stays horizontal as the figure reshapes; manual rotate adds as an offset; reset clears it.
  - **Parser robustness:** bare `ABCD חסום במעגל` / `ABCD בר חסימה` (no shape word) infer quad/triangle from the label count → inscribed-vs-cyclic stays deterministic, drawn-vs-hidden, no LLM (`2ab3362`); the inscribed *general* quad uses the convex ordered angles too (was crossed via golden-angle spread, `ef19d85`); **`חותך`/`cuts`** added to `INTERSECT_KW` so "tangent at C **cuts** the extension of AB at E" creates E (was clobbering A,B by naming the tangent, `7a1b1c9`).
  - **★ "AC bisects ∠ECD" as a constraint + robust JOINT solving (`7f1af3a`) — the session's hardest win.** Parser: when the angle's vertex is the segment's *second* letter and all points exist, it's the constraint ∠ECA=∠ACD (`set-angle-ratio`, k=1), not a point placement. Engine: the coupled `resolveDriven` path (≥2 on-circle/on-segment carriers) used a purely GLOBAL search that drove one cyclic vertex onto another (collapse) or into a crossed-quad branch. Fixed with (a) a **degeneracy barrier** that penalises collapsing two referenced points — **exempting pairs a `coincide` constraint intends to merge** (ADR-028's `~` targets) — and (b) **near-first / global-fallback**: a local solve from the seed, accepted only if it satisfies every constraint non-degenerately (keeps the convex order), else the global search runs (so ADR-028's far coupled cases still solve). Result: the tangent-chord figure builds with `AB=CB` AND `AC bisects ∠ECD` both holding, convex. This generalises — coupled multi-constraint figures are much more robust now.
  - Two small UX/correctness notes from the same session: subscripted labels (`O1`→`O₁`) verified working end-to-end (parser tokens + renderer `<tspan>`, ids stay ASCII); the circle-radius-as-DOF work (ADR-037 A2, `0bb3d12`) and the severe **Dropbox `.git` corruption + recovery** are documented in their own entries below.

- **2026-06-15 — Phase 6 (theorems) design settled; ADR-038 + a new Phase 9 (Reveal) written. No code.** Think-out-loud design session with the operator on the theorem feature. Settled, and recorded across docs: (1) **structural-first detection** — a theorem is raised by *what the student typed* (construct + dependency parents), not by coordinate measurements, so trace-to-trigger is exact and free (ADR-038). (2) **Delta-driven surfacing into a growing feed beside the canvas** — each entry pinned to the fact that raised it, deduped, dropped with its fact (operator picked "separate growing feed" over pinning under each fact row). (3) **Confidence as ordinal tiers** *certain/possible/recall* driving order + colour (green-first), **not** a fabricated percentage — keeps the catalog's exact-and-citable authority; the operator wanted the orderable/green-100% feel, which tiers give without false precision. (4) **"Nothing without a reason" is aspirational, not literal** — construction primitives/definitions legitimately surface nothing; the trigger is often fact+context, not one-fact-one-theorem. (4b — operator's closing refinement) **the analysis is whole-figure each step, not just the last fact**: a hypothesis can span several facts and only be completed by a later one, and an already-shown theorem's **confidence can change** (possible→certain) as more givens accumulate — so each step re-runs matchers over the entire enabled figure and surfaces the *diff* (new + tier-changed), updating entries in place. (5) The operator's **"unmask the shape"** idea (show all equal segments/angles/measures) is the home for the **deferred geometric/coordinate analysis** — but **opt-in only** (no unsolicited clues), and the operator chose to make it a **separate later phase**, not part of Phase 6. Wrote: revised **FR-TH-1..6** + new **FR-RV-1..3** ([02](02-requirements.md)); **ADR-038** ([06](06-decisions.md)); Phase 6 rewrite + new **Phase 9 — Reveal** ([09](09-implementation-plan.md)); charter §3/§4 notes ([10](10-pedagogy.md)). The Pedagogy §4 trigger map is the matcher spec for Phase 6. **Docs only — no tests/build run (still 836 green from the prior session).**

- **2026-06-15 (work PC) — circle radius is now an honest DOF (ADR-037 Amendment 2); the queued radius-DOF task is DONE.** Replaced Amendment 1's hidden ½-shrink. Scope **Light** (operator's pick via AskUserQuestion): the constraint/residual system is **position-only** (radii aren't positions), and internal-equal tangency is **underdetermined** (any inner radius ∈ (0, r₁) is valid), so the full "radius-aware residual carrier" path was *not* needed. Instead: a new `RadiusSpec` variant **`{ via:'tangent-inner', outer }`** → `r = r(outer) − |centre − centre(outer)|` (largest circle inside `outer`, tangent to it). For internal tangency of equal circles, the **2nd circle gets this derived radius** and its **centre stays a free point** (seeded at gap r₁/2 ⇒ default inner radius r₁/2, identical to before). Result: the radius **flexes** with the centre, **"show another configuration" varies it** while tangency holds *structurally* (no baked distance constraint), and the drawn radius is **honest** (computed, not the phantom typed value). The touch point `M` is a new 0-DOF **`radial-toward`** point (on the outer circle, toward the inner centre) so it tracks the centre under resample. Threaded the resolved-circles map into `resolveCircle`; `scene.ts` mirrors the derivation. **External** + **non-equal internal** tangency unchanged (typed radii kept — a radius only flexes when the fixed figure is impossible). 5 files (types/evaluate/apply/step/scene), +4 tests. **836 green, tsc + build clean.** Note this also un-stuck a Dropbox/.git desync on arrival: the "named median segment" work had already been committed+pushed from the home PC, but the *source* synced as uncommitted local edits with a stale branch ref — `git rebase origin/...` dropped the duplicate cleanly (patch already upstream). **Limit:** the inner radius isn't independently pinnable by a `radius = k` on this construct (derived from the gap); a general radius-sized-by-relation still wants the full carrier path — defer until a corpus figure needs it.

- **2026-06-15 (work PC) — subscripted point labels (`O1`→`O₁`) DONE; built the SETTLED light design.** ASCII id + presentation-only subscript, exactly as agreed: **engine/store unchanged** (ids are opaque strings, so `O1`/`O2` are just nodes; `circle-O1` forms naturally). **Parser** — widened the point atom from `[A-Za-z]` to a token **`[A-Za-z]\d*`** (a letter + optional digit subscript) at ~123 sites via a scripted global replace; `labelRun` rewritten token-aware for both the contiguous (`O1O2`) and spaced (`O1 O2`) forms. `ABCD` still splits to four points (letters carry no digits). The **two angle-bisector label runs stay single-letter** (they index chars / use `.length`) — a documented rare limit (no subscripts in a bisector angle-name). **Renderer** — `subscriptLabel()` in Figure.tsx splits trailing digits into a smaller, lowered `<tspan>` so `O1` *displays* `O₁`; id stays `O1`. +6 tests (5 parser tokenization incl. the `ABCD` regression guard, 1 render). **842 green, tsc + build clean.** Three commits, all pushed. **Gotcha caught:** the token-aware `labelRun` rewrite first DROPPED the per-token `\b` boundaries, so "triangle ABC" leaked the "e" → "median from A" regressed; restoring `\b(${PT})\b` fixed it (the only regression, caught by the existing coverage matrix).

- **⚠️ 2026-06-15 — Dropbox CORRUPTED `.git` badly mid-session (worse than the prior "phantom files" warning). LESSON: never `git stash` (or any quick ref-rewriting op) on this repo.** Trigger: I ran `git stash push/pop` on `parse.ts` to compare a baseline — the pop silently lost the change AND Dropbox then sprayed `… (comp11229's conflicted copy 2026-06-15)` files across the tree **including inside `.git`** (conflicted-copy `refs/heads/…`, `index`, `logs/…`). Symptoms: `git fetch` failed (`did not send all necessary objects`, `unable to update .git/info/refs: No such file or directory`); **local HEAD had REWOUND** to an older commit (19f5bff) — my pushed commits 0bb3d12/a98484e were gone from the local ref but **safe on GitHub and still present as local objects**, with the correct ref hiding in the conflicted-copy ref file. The newest source edits were preserved as `… conflicted copy` files while the *main* files were reverted to old content. **Recovery that worked (non-destructive):** (1) `find .git -iname "*conflicted copy*" -delete` (the bogus refs broke every git op); (2) verify the good commit with `git cat-file -t`/`git show <sha>:path`; (3) restore reverted source from the conflicted-copy files + `git checkout <good-sha> -- <paths>`; (4) `git reset <good-sha>` (MIXED, not `--hard` — `--hard` was correctly blocked by the harness as it'd discard the uncommitted parser work); (5) `find src docs -iname "*conflicted copy*" -delete`. **`git reset --hard` is unavailable here (harness blocks discarding uncommitted work) — plan recoveries around MIXED reset + targeted `checkout <sha> -- path`.** **Commit + push early and often** so GitHub always holds the truth; the repo really should live OUTSIDE Dropbox (re-clone elsewhere is the long-term fix the memory keeps flagging).

- **2026-06-14 (home PC, cont.) — two circles tangent to EACH OTHER (ADR-037, new construct).** Operator's two-circles figure (`מעגל O` + `מעגל P` + `מעגל O ומעגל P משיקים זה לזה בנקודה M`) drew overlapping circles + a stray tangent line — the `משיק` was grabbed by the tangent-LINE rule, and we had no notion of *mutual* tangency (only `circle-circle-intersection` = crossing). Added a `circles-tangent` command (`circle1/circle2/at/external`) + a parser rule (two named circles + tangent keyword ⇒ mutual tangency; runs before `tangentLine`). Expressed via existing primitives: `applyCommand` reads the circles' (length) radii, places `M` as an on-segment point on the centre line at fraction `r1/d` (the touch point), and adds a `distance(c1,c2)=d` constraint the generic `driveOrCheck` uses to **move a free centre** to the touching distance. So the relation reshapes the figure — circles separate until they kiss at M. **Both directions, explicit + bilingual:** **external** is the default (`r1+r2`, side by side — also "externally"/"חיצונית"/"מבחוץ"/"from outside"), **internal** on "internally"/"from inside"/"inside"/"פנימית"/"מבפנים" (one inside the other, `|r1−r2|`; r=8 outer / r=3 inner → centres 5 apart, M on both). **Then (ADR-037 Amendment 1) — internal-equal no longer fails:** the operator noted a radius is a flexible DOF, so internal tangency of two equal circles **shrinks the 2nd circle to ½** (an interim default) instead of rejecting; the bigger circle is always the outer. +7 tests, 2 catalog entries (both locales). **832 green, build clean.** Limits: the ½ shrink is a hardcoded interim (step row still shows the typed radius) → the **radius-as-first-class-DOF** task is queued above; a radius-through-a-point circle still can't be sized for tangency (the one remaining honest rejection).

- **2026-06-14 (home PC, cont.) — labels nudge off lines (global clearance pass).** The largest-empty-wedge seed (`scene.labelDir`) only knows the segments *meeting at* a vertex, so a label could still land on a line passing nearby (B sat on the circle arc). Added `chooseLabelDirs` (Figure.tsx): in screen space it scores each label's candidate box against EVERY segment, drawn line, and circle (+ other vertices), sweeping 24 directions from the seed and picking the clearest, with a turn-penalty so it only rotates away when the seed is actually blocked. Computed at a reference scale in the scene `useMemo` → **zoom-independent** (labels don't jump when zooming) and carried into the PNG export. Pairs with the white halo as the second layer of readability. +3 unit tests (stays-when-clear, rotates-off-a-line, rotates-off-a-circle). 825 green, build clean.

- **2026-06-14 (home PC, cont.) — white halo behind labels (readability).** Operator: a vertex letter (B) landing on a line was hard to read. Added a white outline painted UNDER each glyph (`paint-order: stroke`, `stroke=#fff`) on both the point labels and the blue measure labels, so a label stays legible over any line without needing perfect placement; carries into the PNG export too. Render-only; 30 render tests green.

- **2026-06-14 (home PC, cont.) — a ⟂/∥ relation now DRAWS its two segments (FR-IN-7).** Operator: "when I say AD ⟂ TD I expect AD to be drawn — currently I have to ask explicitly." The `set-perpendicular`/`set-parallel` constraints were invisible (constrained but drew nothing). Both parser rules now emit `segment` for each named segment alongside the constraint (idempotent — a no-op if already drawn), so `AB ⊥ CD` / `AB ∥ CD` put both lines on the canvas. Updated the two tests that asserted the constraint was `commands[0]` (now it's last, after the two segments). 822 green. (Distance/equal/angle still don't auto-draw — could extend if wanted.)

- **2026-06-14 (home PC, cont.) — a tangent named by two points creates them (ADR-036), + `parseRename` connectors.** Two operator bugs from manual testing. (1) **`החלף את F עם E` fell through** — `parseRename` didn't know the `עם`/`with`/`replace` connectors, so it escalated to the LLM (which gave up); also a reminder that rename **refuses a merge** onto an existing point (the operator had both a 45% `E` and the diagonal-intersection `F`). Added `עם`/`with`/`by`/`replace`/`swap`. (2) **`הישר CD משיק למעגל בנקודה T` drew the tangent but created no `C`/`D`** → a later `AC⊥TC` failed "references an unknown point". The `tangentLine` rule ignored the `CD` name; the engine had no point-on-a-line concept. Added an **`on-line`** point kind (`anchor + offset·dir` of a resolved `Line`) + a `point-on-line` command; the tangent rule now, when the line is named by two labels, emits the tangent **plus** two markers at `±5` from `T`. End-to-end: C/D land on the tangent (⟂ radius, straddling T) and are referenceable. **Then (ADR-036 Amendment 1) the operator clarified they must be DRIVABLE** ("there just to create the initial segment but later adjust to the constraints"): `on-line` got the standard `solve?` directive, its offset is now a 1-DOF the solver drives (unbounded → routed through the `resolveMixedCarriers` seed/scale optimizer, not the bracketed range-solve). In `driveOrCheck` the on-line slider is tried right after on-segment, **before** on-circle/free-vertex — so `AC⊥TC` (C on-line, T on circle) moves the marker **C** to the ⟂ foot, leaving the tangency **T** put. +12 tests (6 tangent-points incl. the drivable case, +connectors). **821 green, build clean.** Limits: the ±5 default is a fixed *starting* offset (cosmetic — tangent drawn clipped); only the tangent rule emits markers so far (bisector/perp/parallel naming-by-points not yet widened); "show another configuration" doesn't yet sample a free marker's offset.

- **2026-06-14 (home PC) — named median `AD תיכון`, smaller dots, image export, rename command + letter-placement guide.** First home session after committing the work-PC day (commit `7a1566b`, pushed). Four operator items from manual testing:
  - **Named median (`AD תיכון` / `AD תיכון לצלע BC`).** The student names the foot `D`; previously only `תיכון מ-A במשולש ABC` (auto-named `M`) parsed deterministically, so `AD תיכון` escalated to the LLM which named it `M` and dropped both `D` and the side. The `median` rule now reads the named segment (apex-first, foot-second), an explicit `לצלע BC` / `to (side) BC`, or — with no side — the opposite side from a named triangle or single-triangle figure context; ambiguous bare forms escalate (never half-parse). Catalog entry + coverage/context tests (+10).
  - **Textbook-small vertex dots** (`Figure.tsx`): a separate `pointR` (2px, was 4px) so corners read as a small dot beside the letter, not a big disc; marks/crossings keep the old `r`.
  - **Export image** (`Figure.tsx`): **⤓ save PNG** + **⧉ copy-to-clipboard** buttons (top-right by Reset view), rasterised 2× on white via `svgToPng` (clone → serialise → `<img>` data-URL → canvas → blob); `encodeURIComponent` carries Hebrew labels. Realises FR-HS-5.
  - **Rename command (ADR-035, FR-HS-7).** `rename E to G` / `שנה שם E ל-G` / `החלף E ב-G` relabels a point across the whole fact list, geometry identical, one undo. Kept **out of the engine**: a `parseRename` boundary the App intercepts before `parse()`; a store `rename(from,to)` that exact-substitutes the single-letter id (safe because point ids are single uppercase letters, line/circle ids are multi-char; the measure `expr` is skipped). Refuses to merge onto a taken letter (`target-taken`/`no-source`/`same` → localized note). Plus the **both** half of the operator's choice: the naming-order + orientation + rename guide in [12-letter-placement.md](12-letter-placement.md). +9 tests. **814 green, build clean.** Also cleaned 7 stray Dropbox `.tmp` files + an accidental `.env.example` deletion (restored).

- **2026-06-14 (work PC) — square-root lengths + LLM steps in the student's language (extends ADR-031).** Two operator requests. (1) **Steps shown in Hebrew:** deterministic Hebrew input already labelled rows with the student's utterance (Hebrew); only **LLM-escalated** steps showed English (App labels them with the LLM's canonical `g.step`). Fixed in the prompt (`llmShared.ts`): "output each step in the SAME language the student wrote in" + a Hebrew→Hebrew example. `parse()` is bilingual and the catalog's Hebrew forms are test-guaranteed to re-parse, so Hebrew canonical steps both build and display in Hebrew. (2) **Square roots (`AD = 12√x`):** root cause was a **half-parse** — no √ support, so `distanceConstraint` grabbed `AD = 12` and silently dropped `√x`; that bogus `=12` on an already-determined segment produced the "⚠ over-constrained: |…|=12 cannot hold" the operator saw (so "no square root" and the over-constraint error were the SAME bug). Added roots & powers via the existing symbolic-measure pipeline (ADR-031, Amendment 1): `MeasureExpr`'s symbolic variant gains a **`pow?`** exponent (½ = √, 2 = x², n = xⁿ). Two parser rules — `measureSqrt` (`√`/`\sqrt{…}`/`sqrt(…)`) and `measurePower` (`x²`/`x^n`) — run *before* the numeric rules so the radical/exponent is never dropped (the half-parse that caused the bug). A **variable** base is symbolic (`coef·base^pow`, resolves on value: `x=4 ⇒ 12√4 = 24`; `x=3 ⇒ 2·3² = 18`); a **numeric** base is concrete (`12√2 ≈ 16.97`, `5² = 25`). `lower.ts` links two **same-exponent** measures as a ratio (the `varⁿ` cancels) — mixed exponents stay free labels (honest limit). Also wired the **`∠`** trigger + trailing **`°`** into the angle rules (`∠ABC = 37°`). UI: the insert toolbar is now **two labeled rows** — Greek (α β γ δ θ) and Symbols (√ x² xⁿ ∠ ° ⊥ ∥; ⊥/∥ already parsed). Catalog entries `AD = 12√x` and `AB = x²`. +14 symbolic/glyph tests this thread, +3 spelling tests earlier. 749 green, tsc clean. Limits: one variable per measure, no nested/multi-term radicals, no exponents on angles.
- **2026-06-14 (work PC) — segment-length ratio `AE/ED = 2/3` (was a half-parse leaving a point unplaced).** Operator: "why didn't it locate E well." `AE/ED=2/3` (|AE|/|ED| = ⅔) half-parsed to `set-distance ED=2` — the numeric rule grabbed the "ED=2" in the middle, dropping `AE/` and `/3`, so the ratio never drove E (it sat at the wrong spot). Added a `segmentRatio` rule (before the numeric rules): `AB/CD = m/n` → `set-ratio(a,b,c,d, k=m/n)`. Now `point E on AD` + `AE/ED=2/3` places E at t=0.4 (40% from A).
- **2026-06-14 (work PC) — angle MARKS on the figure (right-angle square / angle arc), only when STATED (FR-RN-7).** Operator: draw the angle/right-angle symbol when the student gives the fact — but NOT for a merely computed 90°. Built a fact-sourced `angleMarks` in `replay` (geoStore): `angle ABC = α/37` → an **arc**; `angle ABC = 90`, `AB ⟂ CD` (square at the shared vertex), a `right-triangle` (square at the last id) → a **right-angle square**. A computed 90° (a rectangle's corners, a plain rhombus) gets **no mark** — the source is the FACTS, never the geometry. Threaded through `buildScene` (new `SceneAngleMark`, resolves the ids to positions) and drawn in `Figure.tsx` (a 2-segment square polyline, or an arc sampled the short/interior way; blue like the measures, gated on the "show measures" toggle, follows orientation). Marks for a *failed* fact are dropped (points must exist). +4 store tests. 786 green.
- **2026-06-14 (work PC) — affine LENGTH relation `CE = k + 2` now reshapes the figure (was a free label); + compound "such that".** Two issues on the same rhombus figure. (1) **`CE=k+2` didn't change the shape** between `k+2`/`k+4`. With `AD=k`, `CE=k+2` means |CE|=|AD|+2 — a real constraint that pins the SIZE (an absolute difference), but affine measures were treated as free labels. Fixed by giving the `ratio` constraint / `set-ratio` an optional **`add`** (`|ab| = k·|cd| + add`); `lower.ts` now links an affine measure to its linear representative as `set-ratio(…, k=coef/repCoef, add=const)` (still free-label for a √/² const = nonlinear). Verified `CE=k+c` sizes the rhombus so |CE|−|AD|=c (c=2→|AD|=5, c=8→|AD|=13.4 — genuinely different figures). residual/scale/describe all carry `add`. (2) **compound `point F on extension of AD such that CF⊥DF`** half-parsed (read "AD⟂CF", never created F) — each half parses fine alone, so added a `compoundSuchThat` rule (runs first): split on `such that`/`כך ש`, parse each side in order, concat. Now F is created then `CF⟂DF` (correct segments) applied. +5 tests. 782 green. **The whole figure (with `CE=k+c` and the compound F step) builds correctly and responds to the constant.**
- **2026-06-14 (work PC) — perpendicular FOOT on an extension (`CF ⟂ DF`, F past D) no longer collapses onto D.** Operator's figure: `point F on extension of AD` + `CF ⟂ DF` → "D and F would be at the same point". Three causes, all fixed: (1) `unit((0,0))` returns `(0,0)`, so the ⟂ residual `u·v` is a spurious **0** at F=D → the root finder snapped F to D. Fixed: the parallel/perpendicular residual returns **NaN** when a segment is degenerate (solveParam already skips NaN). (2) The residual *sign-flips* across F=D (DF reverses), bracketing a fake root there; and an on-segment solve only searched **[0,1]**, never reaching the real foot at t≈1.5 (beyond D). Fixed: `SolvedOnSegmentPoint` now carries `t0` (the initial param); an EXTENSION point (t0∉[0,1]) searches a wide range, and `solvedOnSegmentCandidates` **discards any root that lands the point on another referenced point** (the degenerate F=D). Now F lands at the ⟂ foot (∠CFD=90°, on line AD past D). **The operator's full rhombus figure (CD=k, E on AD with AE/ED=2/3, ∠ADC=α, CE=k+2, F on AD-extension, CF⟂DF, DF=k−5/2) now builds ALL GREEN** with correct labels. 779 green.
- **2026-06-14 (work PC) — affine measures `k+2`, `k−5/2` (RESOLVED the half-parse above).** `MeasureExpr`'s symbolic variant gains an optional **`const`** (the affine term); `measureLength` reads an optional trailing `± c` (c a number or fraction) after the var, kept verbatim in `text` (`"k + 2"`, `"k − 5/2"`). Lowering: resolves to `coef·value^pow + const` when the var is known (`k=4 ⇒ CE=6`); while unresolved an affine measure is a **free label** (not a ratio — an affine relation isn't a ratio, and an affine binding can't anchor one). `CE=k+2` now shows "k + 2" and no longer half-parses to `CE=k`. +4 tests.

Since ADR-033 made those scalars real DOFs, `applySeed` (sample.ts) now perturbs free (`solve===undefined`) `rotated`/`perp-offset`/`scaled-offset` scalars too (rhombus ∠ ∈ [40°,140°] so it reaches OBTUSE; rect/right-tri offset 0.55–1.85×; trapezoid top-ratio 0.3–0.85), and `freeDofs` counts them. A **constrained** scalar (driven by an angle/length given) is left alone. So a bare/`∠ADC=α`-only rhombus can now be cycled to an obtuse-at-A shape via the button — no forced angle needed. +2 sample tests. 774 green.

The stored text is correct (the step row shows `12√2`); only the SVG `<text>` rendered wrong. Fixed: the `SceneMeasure` `<text>` in `Figure.tsx` now forces `direction:ltr; unicode-bidi:bidi-override` (measures are always math — 12√2, 7k/5, 2α — never Hebrew). Render-only; 30 render tests green.
- **2026-06-14 (work PC) — faithful measure display (12√2, 7k/5) + fractional coefficients.** Operator reproducing a real bagrut figure hit three things: (1) `12√2` showed as "16.971" — fixed by adding an optional **`text`** to `MeasureExpr` (both variants) that holds the faithful original ("12√2", "2π", "5²"); `measureLabelText` shows `text` while unresolved, the computed number once the var has a value. (2) `7k/5` **half-parsed to 7k** (dropped `/5`) → it imposed `AC = 7·AD` and over-constrained. Added a **fractional coefficient**: `measureLength` reads an optional trailing `/d` (`7k/5` ⇒ coef 7/5, `text:"7k/5"`); two fractional measures sharing a var resolve right (`k=5 ⇒ 7, 24`). This fixed both the parse AND the spurious `AC` over-constraint (the fraction makes it a satisfiable ratio the solver sizes via the free legs). 761 green. (3) `BD=12√2` is **still** an honest over-constraint — see the open item below. Fractions/√/π/powers all now show faithfully and the only red step in the operator's figure is the BD one.
- **Recruit free DOFs through derived/line-intersection deps (built, FR-EN-11).** Operator chose "deep-solve it" for `BD=12√2`. Extended the recruit so a constraint whose refs are all *derived* (e.g. |BD| with B a ⟂-offset, D a `line-intersection` of two bisectors) can reach the figure's free DOFs: new `freeDrivableAncestors` (step.ts) walks through derived points AND a line-intersection's **lines** (`lineSpecPoints`) back to free **vertices** (not just on-seg/on-circle carriers); `recruitFreeDofs` gained a (B) path that, on an evaluate failure, marks ONE distinct free ancestor per un-driven new constraint so the joint `resolveFreeDriven` sizes the figure. Safe/additive (only fires after a failure; the satisfied+degeneracy guard still rejects genuinely-impossible constraints). 761 green.
- **2026-06-14 (work PC) — RESOLVED: shapes now carry their true DOF (ADR-033, FR-EN-13).** Implemented Approach 1 from the audit below. The hard-coded scalars (`perp-offset.dist`, `rotated.angleDeg`, `scaled-offset.k`) gained an optional `solve` directive and are now **solver-driveable**; a new `resolveMixedCarriers` (evaluate.ts) jointly drives a heterogeneous DOF vector (free-vertex coords + parametric t/θ + shape scalars), per-coordinate-normalised, with a degeneracy penalty so a length can't be "satisfied" by collapsing the figure. `driveOrCheck` drives a referenced scalar (rectangle height via BC); the recruit reaches a scalar behind a derived vertex (rhombus angle behind C, rectangle height behind D). Templates: square dropped `rigid` (resizes; impossible constraints still rejected by the guards), rectangle's D became a `parallelogram-vertex` (single height DOF). **All 5 shapes verified** sizing/shaping; defaults byte-identical; the old "square is rigid" tests updated to assert resize. **Then closed the coupled case too (ADR-033 Amendment 1):** the operator's full figure (`BD=12√2` + `AC=7k/5`, both through the incenter D) now solves to the **real triangle** (CA=5.6, CB=19.2, k=4 — the actual bagrut answer), `AC/AD=1.4000` exact, GREEN. Two per-constraint-scale-relative changes did it: length tolerances are `max(1e-6, 2e-4·constraintScale)` (the constraint's OWN length, not the figure span a far point could inflate), and `resolveMixedCarriers` minimises **relative** residuals (`residual ÷ constraintScale`) — un-gameable by shrinking (a wrong ratio stays ~1% relative however small), so the solver finds the genuine spread-out solution, not a degenerate fake (let the degeneracy penalty be dropped). 770 green (9 shape-DOF incl. the full coupled figure). Impossible constraints (square side=diagonal, `AB=5;AB=7`) still reject.
- **AUDIT (2026-06-14) — [RESOLVED by ADR-033 above] locked-dimension flaw is SYSTEMIC across templates, not just right-triangle.** Operator: "before fixing, check what other similar assumptions were made (like square)." Audited every `applyCommand` shape + verified empirically (each rejects its natural sizing constraint): **square** (`placeBase rigid` → `AB=10` rejected — can't resize at all), **rectangle** (`perp-offset dist:4` → height locked, `BC=10` rejected), **rhombus** (`rotated angleDeg:60` → angle locked, `∠ABC=80` rejected), **trapezoid** (`scaled-offset k:0.6` → top-base ratio locked, `DC=2` rejected), **right-triangle** (`perp-offset dist:5` → leg locked, `CB=12` rejected). FINE (fully flexible, all sized OK): triangle, quadrilateral, parallelogram — their derived vertices maintain only the *defining property* (`parallelogram-vertex D=A+C−B`) without pinning a free dimension. Root cause = a derived vertex hard-codes a scalar (perp-offset `dist`, rotated `angleDeg`, scaled-offset `k`) or the rigid flag, so the shape carries one fewer DOF than the real shape. **Proposed fix (Approach 1, recommended): driveable shape-parameter DOFs** — make those scalars solver-driveable (defaults unchanged until a constraint needs them; structural property kept — a rectangle stays rectangular), generalising `resolveDriven`/`recruit` to a unified DOF vector (free-point coords + parametric `t`/`θ` + shape scalars); plus drop the square's `rigid` flag (its A,B drive; shape-violating constraints still rejected by the satisfied+degeneracy guards). Approach 2 (rebuild from free points + soft perpendicular/equal constraints) = more convergence risk, properties go soft. Awaiting go-ahead on Approach 1 + scope (all 5 at once).
- **[RESOLVED by ADR-033] OPEN — the right-triangle template is a 1-DOF family; `BD=12√2`+`AC=7k/5` need 2.** With the recruit above, `BD=12√2` *alone* can now reach the legs — BUT it still can't be satisfied because **`right-triangle` hard-codes one leg**: B = `perp-offset(C, ⟂CA, dist:5)`, so |CB|=5 is FIXED and the triangle can only vary |CA| (1 shape DOF). Analytically BD maxes ~3.8 with CB=5, so 12√2≈16.97 is genuinely unreachable as built. Tried making the offset **relative** (|CB| ∝ |CA|): BD then reached 16.97 exactly (✓) but that **locks the aspect ratio** → regresses "right triangle, ∠A=30" (the non-right angles become fixed) AND the operator's own next given `AC=7k/5` (which needs CB/CA≈24/7, not the default) → reverted. **Real fix:** the right triangle needs BOTH legs independently free (a 2-DOF family). Lowest-risk route identified: make `perp-offset`'s **`dist` a driveable scalar DOF** (keeps the 90° structural, default dist=5 unchanged so the corpus is untouched; only a length given that needs it drives it) — i.e. add a perp-offset carrier kind to `resolveDriven` + `freeDrivableAncestors`. A focused next task (corpus revalidation needed); not rushed at session end. Display/fractions already make the figure faithful; this is the last red step in the operator's figure.
- **2026-06-14 (work PC) — triangle congruence (≅) & similarity (~) reshape the figure (ADR-032, FR-EN-12).** Operator wants `ABC ≅ DEF` / `ABC ~ DEF` as givens, and chose (via AskUserQuestion) **reshape-to-enforce** over annotate-only. A probe surfaced the real blocker: two independent triangles were placed at the *same* default template coords → ADR-017 coincidence guard rejected the second ("A and D would be at the same point"). Fixed in `placeBase` (apply.ts): a shape sharing **no** point with the figure (0 anchors) is offset to the right of all existing geometry (max-x + gap); composed shapes (≥1 shared point) are untouched, so stability/composition tests are unaffected. Then expressed each relation as **existing** constraints (no new engine primitive): congruence = **SSS** (3 `set-equal` driving the second triangle's sides to the first's), similarity = **AA** (2 `set-angle-ratio` k=1 driving its angles) — both ride the ADR-030/031 coupled solver. Parser `congruence`/`similarity` rules (before the shape rules) read `≅`/`congruent`/`חופפים` and `~`/`similar`/`דומה`, pull the first two label-triples (handles both "ABC ≅ DEF" and the Hebrew "המשולשים ABC ו-DEF חופפים" order), and via `ParseContext.points` emit a `triangle` only for whichever isn't drawn yet. `≅ ~ ∼ ∽ חופ דומ` added to SHAPE_LEFTOVER. UI: **≅** and **~** buttons. Verified end-to-end (sides equal / angles equal). 759 green, tsc clean. **Pre-req now in place** (place-apart) for any future two-figure work. Limits: triangles only; second-named always adapts; tick/arc marks not yet drawn (reshapes but doesn't *mark* the equal parts).

Generalised the symbolic measure `sqrt?` → **`pow?`** exponent (√ = ½, x² = 2, xⁿ = n); `measureSqrt`/`measurePower`/`measurePi` run before the numeric rules so a radical/exponent/π is never half-dropped. Added **π** as a numeric constant (`2π` ≈ 6.28; glyph only — "pi" collides with segment PI), and wired the **`∠`** trigger + trailing **`°`** into the angle rules. Toolbar is now **two labeled rows**: Greek (α β γ δ θ) and Symbols (√ x² xⁿ π ∠ ° ⊥ ∥ ≅ ~). `⊥`/`∥` already parsed. Decision per operator: fractions/subscripts not needed; `2AB` already means 2·AB so no × symbol. Catalog + tests for each. (Rolled into the same green run as ADR-032.)
- **2026-06-14 (work PC) — Hebrew spelling variant: `זוית` (defective) vs `זווית` (plene).** Operator typed `משולש ABC ישר זוית` and the right-angle part was silently dropped (drew a plain triangle). Cause: the parser matched only the plene spelling `זווית` (two vavs) at ~8 sites; the defective `זוית` (one vav, equally standard) matched nothing, so rightTriangle didn't fire AND the SHAPE_LEFTOVER guard didn't catch the stray word to escalate → a half-parse to a bare triangle. Fix: every angle-word site now accepts both via `זוו?ית` (optional 2nd vav) — rightTriangle (trigger+cleanup), inscribed kind-detect+cleanup, the plain `angle` rule, bisectorIntersection/bisectorSegmentIntersection/bisectorLine/bisectorPlacesPoint, and SHAPE_LEFTOVER. - **2026-06-14 (work PC) — fresh-machine gotcha: `@anthropic-ai/sdk` not installed → LLM fallback 502.** Switched from home PC; freeform input silently failed. The proxy returned 502 (not 503), i.e. key present but the dynamic `import('@anthropic-ai/sdk')` threw "Cannot find package" — the weekend bumped the lockfile but this PC's `node_modules` predated it. `npm install` (added 6 pkgs) fixed it; verified `/api/parse` returns 200 with canonical steps. Tests never caught it (SDK is mocked). Reinforces the standing rule: **run `npm install` first thing on a machine switch.**

- **2026-06-14 — Symbolic measures + on-figure measure labels (ADR-031, plan-driven).** Bagrut givens are relational ("AB = 3x", "DF = x", "∠ABC = 2α"). Added a variable layer on the compiler pipeline: parser emits store-level `SymbolicCommand`s (`measure-length`/`measure-angle`/`set-var`; lowercase latin = length var, Greek = angle var, points stay uppercase), and the store's `replay` (a middle-end) lowers them via a **symbol table** (`src/engine/lower.ts`) into existing engine constraints — first binding of a variable is the free *representative*, the rest link as `set-ratio` / the new `angle-ratio` constraint; a value (`x = 4`) resolves all to absolute. Numeric measures keep their `set-distance`/`set-angle` output and are labelled from constraints (FR-RN-2). Renderer gained a `SceneMeasure` (length at segment midpoint, angle at vertex), upright, blue, gated on a `showMeasures` store flag (on by default; "הצג מידות" toggle); App got α/β/γ/δ/θ insert buttons. Engine: `AngleRatioConstraint` + `set-angle-ratio` through solve/driveOrCheck; `driveOrCheck` free-vertex pick now falls back to any free vertex so two angles of a triangle solve jointly. 3-phase build, **732 tests green** (15 new). Limits: one-kind variables, no multi-term expressions, heuristic joint solve.

- **2026-06-13 — Free-vertex constraint driving (ADR-030).** Reported bug: `מקבילית ABCD` + diagonal `AC` + `|AB|=|AC|` was rejected "over-constrained" though a parallelogram with side = diagonal exists. Cause: the ADR-028 driven-DOF machinery only drove 1-DOF parametric carriers (on-segment/on-circle); a shape's **free vertices** (2 DOF) were never driven, so the constraint fell to a failing check. Fix: `FreePoint.solve`, `driveOrCheck` drives a free non-pinned/non-rigid vertex, and `resolveFreeDriven` (regularised Nelder–Mead → nearest config, then a pure-residual polish, plus a degeneracy guard so an unsatisfiable constraint stays an honest over-constraint instead of collapsing a segment). A **square** is tagged `rigid` (committed sides+angles) so its over-constraint checks still fire; parallelogram/quad/triangle/rect/rhombus stay drivable. +2 regression tests; FR-EN-11 already covered "drive a free point". 715 tests green.

- **2026-06-13 — Requirements backfill + sync rule.** Noticed `02-requirements.md` had not been updated alongside this session's user-visible changes. Backfilled two FR lines: **FR-RN-6** (figure-orientation controls — rotate 90°/180°, flip H/V, free rotate, align-named-segment-to-horizontal, reset; view-only, labels stay upright) and **FR-EN-11** (a constraint on already-placed objects *reshapes* the figure — drives/recruits a DOF — rather than only checking; composes; rejects per FR-EN-8; ADR-028). Added a standing **requirements-stay-in-sync rule** to the top of this file. Also removed stale `09-implementation-plan.md.tmp.*` debris from python doc-edits.

- **2026-06-10 — Phases 2→5 (partial), one long session.** Built on the Phase-1 engine through to a usable, breadth-y app. End state: **98 tests green, build clean**, all on `rebuild-foundation`.
  - **Phase 2** — SVG renderer (`src/render/`): pure `transform` + `scene` + declarative `Figure` (pan/zoom/reset, highlight). Renderer is a pure, swappable consumer of engine output; tested headlessly via `react-dom/server`.
  - **Phase 3** — store/app shell (`src/store/geoStore.ts`, Zustand + zundo). **Fact list is the source of truth; the figure is derived by `replay`.** Facts are select/deselect/delete-able (ADR-010), dependents auto-drop reversibly. Hebrew/RTL, undo/redo, clear.
  - **Phase 4** — grammar parser (`src/parser/parse.ts`): deterministic, offline, bilingual `utterance → command[]`; keyword-order-independent; `not-handled` is the Phase-7 LLM boundary. **The text box is live.**
  - **Phase 5a** — quad, parallelogram, segment, line∩line; **corpus Q1 reproduced** from typed He/En utterances.
  - **Polygon family** — triangle, rectangle, rhombus, trapezoid; **in-app command reference** (`parser/catalog.ts` → "What can I type?" panel, open by default).
  - **Phase 5d (first slice)** — constraint-driven DOF: `angle BEA = 90` with E on AC now *solves* E to the perpendicular foot (ADR-012).
  - **Decisions made this session:** ADR-009 (redefinition is a conflict) → amended by **ADR-011** (re-placing a free point is a *move*); **ADR-010** (facts select/deselect/delete, replay model); **ADR-012** (constraints solve a free DOF — first slice done); **ADR-013** (shapes build on existing points / composition). Plus the recorded rationale for keeping Phase 7 (LLM) after Phase 5.
  - **Notable bugs fixed from real use:** duplicate fact rows on re-issue (idempotent `execute`); `square ADFG` on a parallelogram rejected (→ ADR-013); `ABCD ריבוע` order rejected (order-independent grammar); `angle BEA=90` rejected (→ 5d slice).
  - **Resume here (next):** Phase 5b proper — a **line** object to enable **parallel-line** ("BC ∥ AD") and **perpendicular + foot**, then **angle bisector** and **right-triangle** → reproduce **Q2–Q4**; then 5c (circles → Q5–Q7). Also pending: the rest of 5d (on-segment as a ray, free-point/length/parallel drivers).

- **2026-06-11 — Design-review fixes (pre-5b hardening).** An architecture review flagged structural issues; all fixed before starting 5b. End state: **118 tests green, build clean.**
  - **ADR-014** — constraint-driven solving refactored from a bespoke point kind per (carrier × constraint) into one generic mechanism: `solve.ts` (constraint refs / residual / description) + `solveParam` (generic deterministic 1-D root finder) + a generic `on-segment-solved` carrier kind that embeds the driving constraint. New constraints = new residual cases, not new point kinds.
  - **DOF selection fixed** — `set-angle` now drives the first referenced point (vertex → ray1 → ray2) that has a free DOF, not only the vertex. "∠GBA = 37°" with G on AD (vertex determined, freedom in the ray point) now solves; previously it was wrongly rejected.
  - **Vision example corrected** — the docs' flagship "∠GAB = 37°" was geometrically impossible (G on AD ⇒ ∠GAB = 90° for every t); docs now use the satisfiable "∠GBA = 37°", and the impossible variant became a 5d test of the honest "cannot place" rejection.
  - **Parser misparse defense** — the dangerous failure is the silent half-parse, not the miss: added lines-first intersection phrasing ("האלכסונים AC ו-BD נחתכים בנקודה E"; note נחתך final-ך ≠ נחתכ in inflected forms), lowercase filler-word stripping in `labelRun` ("connect A to B" no longer reads T,O), word-bounded segment labels in point-on-segment ("F on the extension of AD" escalates instead of reading "th"), and a stop-on-unreadable guard (a recognised intersection keyword with an unreadable sentence aborts the parse rather than letting `segment` half-parse it). Negative corpus in `phase4.test.ts` pins all of it.
  - **GitHub backup remote added** — private repo, `git push` now backs up history beyond Dropbox.
  - Review watch-items recorded for later phases: 5c must handle both circle dependency directions (inscribed vs circumscribed phrasings); Phase-6 theorem detection should derive "definite" structurally or by jiggle-testing free DOFs, never from one drawing's coordinates.

- **2026-06-11 (cont.) — renderer polish + in-place fact editing (manual-testing session).**
  - **Lines only, no fill** — polygons are no longer filled or stroked (every shape edge is already a `segment`); the canvas is just its lines. Selection now shows as accented edges + vertices, not a fill.
  - **Label placement** — each vertex label sits in the *largest empty angular wedge* around it (`scene.ts` `outwardDir`/`labelDir`, pure + unit-tested), so labels land on the outer side and never on a line. Handles shared vertices, single edges, and collinear (midpoint → perpendicular).
  - **ADR-015 — edit a fact in place** — new store `update(id, cmd, utterance)` + an inline ✎ editor on each fact row (re-parse the phrasing, write back at the same position). "Change E to 40%" now works; previously re-typing was rejected as a redefinition conflict and delete+re-add broke replay order. The replay model (ADR-010) made this a few lines, no engine change; undoable; dependents follow or auto-drop.
  - **ADR-016 — snap to intersection** — segment crossings are a *suggestion*, never auto-created (auto-create would reintroduce figure-recognition, clutter O(N²), and be unstable under drag/alternatives). The renderer detects interior crossings of declared segments (`render/intersections.ts`, pure) and draws a faint hollow dot; clicking it creates a real named `line-line-intersection` point (host picks the next free letter). Stays constructive; behind an optional `onPickIntersection` prop so the renderer stays side-effect-free.
  - 142 tests green, build clean.

- **2026-06-11 (cont.) — composition degeneracy fix (found in manual testing).** Building `triangle ABC` then `parallelogram ABDF` in the running app drew a collapsed (collinear) parallelogram. Root cause: a composed shape (ADR-013) reused existing base corners but gave its *new free vertices* absolute template defaults, so a free non-base vertex (parallelogram/quad/trapezoid/triangle) landed on the reused edge's line. Fixed by fitting the shape template to its anchors via a similarity transform (`fitTemplate`/`placeBase` in apply.ts; `pos` threaded into `applyCommand`); see the ADR-013 amendment. **Why tests missed it:** shape tests only built standalone-from-empty, and the property-checked shapes (square/rectangle/rhombus) derive their non-base vertices from the base edge so they can't degenerate — the vulnerable (free non-base vertex) × (composed) cross had zero coverage. New `phase5-composition.test.ts` (15 tests) builds every shape standalone and on an edge, plus single-vertex sharing, derived-anchor fit, allocation (new-vs-reused point accounting), idempotency, and stability — all asserted from (x,y). 133 tests green, build clean.

- **2026-06-11 (cont.) — composition order-independence (ADR-013 amendment 2, found in manual testing).** `trapezoid ABCD` then `ריבוע RTCD` (a square on side DC, new corners named first) was rejected "C is already defined" — the existing C,D fell on the square's *derived* slots, and a shape can reuse existing points only at *free base* slots. Fix: `normalizeShapeComposition` (apply.ts) cyclically rotates the vertex tuple so existing points land on free slots before the conflict check + build (a rotation = same polygon, different start vertex). Diagonal pairs / all-vertices-declared still conflict (no rotation frees them). 147 tests green, build clean.

- **2026-06-11 (cont.) — no two nodes on the same point (ADR-017, found in manual testing).** A 3rd shape on edge CD (`מקבילית ABCD`, `ריבוע CDFG`, `מקבילית CDTY`) placed T,Y exactly on A,B — the fit rebuilt the first parallelogram on itself. Two layers: (1) `evaluate` now fails any figure with two coincident distinct points (`coincide:true`, keeps prior, explains) — a general invariant; (2) `applyStep` auto-retries a colliding composed shape with `mirrorComposition` (reflect its new free vertices across the reused edge → other side). Default side preferred; flip only on collision; if no free vertex to flip (2nd square on an edge) it errors honestly. 149 tests green, build clean.

- **2026-06-11 (cont.) — textbook-clean defaults + empty-side composition (ADR-017 amendment).** Operator wants shapes to look like textbook figures (upright, base horizontal). Required two things together: (1) a `flip` flag on the derived corner kinds (`derived`/`perp-offset`/`rotated`) so square/rectangle/rhombus can mirror to either side of their base edge without relabelling — `mirrorComposition` now reflects free vertices *and* toggles `flip`, mirroring the whole shape; (2) `chooseComposition` in `applyStep` evaluates both default and mirror and picks the side *away* from existing geometry (not only on collision). This decoupled composition from template tuning, so the standalone templates were normalised to base-on-x-axis / built-upward (parallelogram/trapezoid were base-at-top; quad had a tilted base). Net: a square on a parallelogram's edge flips to the empty side; standalone shapes are upright. NOTE the renderer auto-centres/scales, so world origin doesn't fix screen position — orientation + proportions are what matter. 150 tests green, build clean.

### Session wrap — 2026-06-11 (end)

One long session: a **code review** (Fable) of the build, then **manual stress-testing** that surfaced a string of real bugs — mostly in **shape composition**, which is now clearly the richest/least-specified corner of the engine. End state: **150 tests green** (from 98 at the 06-10 wrap), build clean, all committed on `rebuild-foundation`, pushed to GitHub `dcodish/geo_builder`. **No new corpus coverage** — this was all hardening + UX, so the corpus track is still at **Q1 (5a)**.

What landed, in order (each its own commit + ADR where noted): ADR-014 generic constraint solving + DOF-selection fix; parser misparse defense; ADR-013 amend-1 (similarity-fit composition, fixes degenerate parallelogram-on-edge); ADR-015 edit-a-fact-in-place (✎ on each row); ADR-016 snap-to-intersection; renderer lines-only + largest-empty-wedge label placement; categorized **Commands** coverage-map panel; ADR-013 amend-2 (vertex-order-independent composition); ADR-017 (coincidence invariant + mirror); empty-side composition + `flip` flag + textbook-upright templates. Captured-but-not-built: **ADR-018** (alternatives = sampling residual freedom).

**The five composition bugs (pattern worth remembering):** (1) free non-base vertex kept absolute default → degenerate collapse; (2) shared edge had to be named first; (3) third shape rebuilt on top of an existing one (coincident nodes); (4) composed shape overlapped instead of taking the empty side; (5) standalone shapes drawn base-at-top / tilted. All fixed. **5b will add lines/perpendiculars that attach to existing geometry the same way — expect similar edge cases.**

**Resume here next session:**

1. **Decide the track** — corpus **Phase 5b** (line object → parallel, perpendicular+foot, bisector, ray/extension, right-triangle → Q2–Q4), or cross-cutting **ADR-018 Stage 1** (pinned-vs-free points + seeded DOF sampler for "show another configuration"), which pairs with 5d. Operator leaned toward more **stress-testing** first.
2. **Read first:** this file + [09-implementation-plan.md](09-implementation-plan.md) Status line + [06-decisions.md](06-decisions.md) ADR-013…018 + `../CLAUDE.md`.
3. **Sanity check:** `npm install` (fresh machine), `npx vitest run` (expect 150 green), `npm run dev`.

- **2026-06-11 (home PC) — Phase 5b complete (corpus Q2–Q4).** First session from the home machine (caught up cleanly from GitHub; noted the Dropbox `.git` corruption above). Built the **`Line`** object and reproduced **Q2, Q3, Q4** — the corpus track moves from Q1 to Q4.
  - **ADR-019 — Lines are constructive scaffolding.** A first-class `Line` (`types.ts`) with a `LineSpec` (`through` / `bisector` / `perpendicular` / `parallel`) that the evaluator resolves to an `(anchor, dir)` pair (`resolveLine`) **in the same topological sweep as points** (a line needs its points; a `line-intersection` point needs its lines — they interleave to a fixed point). Lines carry no coordinates and are **never rendered** (`scene.ts` ignores `kind:'line'`) — only the segments the student names are drawn, so a bisector adds a crossing point with no stray auxiliary dot.
  - **New derived points:** `line-intersection` (two `Line` ids — used only when a line is *derived*, e.g. bisector∩bisector; through∩through still uses the 5a 4-point `line-line-intersection`), `foot` (⟂ foot onto a line), `midpoint`. New line kind = one `resolveLine` case; new derived point = one `tryEval` case — no new top-level types.
  - **`right-triangle ABC`** — right angle at the **last** id: A,C free legs + B derived `perp-offset` ⟂ to CA at C, so ∠C stays 90° as A or C moves (like other shapes fixing a default size). Point-on-extension = on-segment with `t > 1`.
  - **Parser widened in lock-step:** `right triangle`, the **bisector-meet** compound ("E is where the bisectors of BAC and BCA meet" / "E חיתוך חוצי הזוויות BAC ו-BCA"), `foot`, `midpoint`, `extension`. **Deferred on purpose** (→ Phase 5d, recorded in the misparse-defense test): a *single* bisector fact and the `parallel`/`perpendicular` **constraints** ("BC ∥ AD") — those shape an underdetermined figure, they're not one-shot constructions.
  - **Tests:** `src/engine/__tests__/phase5b.test.ts` (11) — unit (bisector-meet = incenter/equidistant; foot ⟂+collinear; midpoint; right-angle preserved under a leg move) + Q2/Q3/Q4 reproduced from typed He/En utterances. Catalog flipped (right-triangle, midpoint, extension, foot, bisector-meet) + a **guard test** that every supported catalog example parses both locales. **159 green, build clean.**
  - **Resume next:** **Phase 5c** — circles → Q5–Q7. (Done in the same session — see below.)

- **2026-06-11 (home PC, cont.) — Phase 5c complete (corpus Q5–Q7). The full v1 corpus (Q1–Q7) is now reproduced.** Also fixed two things found while the operator played with the running app: a bare **`segment AB`** with no prior points silently drew nothing (it referenced undefined endpoints) → it now **creates its endpoints** like a shape's base (reuses them if they exist; a colliding auto-placed endpoint is rejected by the ADR-017 guard); and a **BiDi/RTL bug** in the commands panel — `dir="auto"` keys off the first strong char, so a Hebrew example starting with a Latin label ("C במרחק…") got an LTR base and reflowed into garbage → replaced with a content-aware `textDir()` (any Hebrew letter ⇒ RTL) on the commands panel, chips, fact rows, edit field, and main input.
  - **ADR-020 — Circles are first-class drawn objects.** A `Circle` (`types.ts`) with a centre id + `RadiusSpec` (`length` | `through` a point). Resolved in the **same interleaved fixed-point sweep** as lines+points (circle needs its centre; tangent line needs its circle; on-circle/line∩circle point needs its circle). **Circles ARE drawn** (unfilled outline, before segments; `scenePositions` adds the circle extent to the fit so it isn't clipped) — unlike lines (ADR-019, scaffolding).
  - **New circle-dependent points** (each one `tryEval` case): `on-circle` (1 DOF angle θ; a lone point auto-spreads by the **golden angle** so inscribed vertices never coincide), `antipode` (diameter far end, 2·centre−of), `arc-midpoint` (normalized sum of the two radius dirs; `branch` flips arc), `line-circle` (0/1/2 solutions, `branch`). The **tangent** is one more `LineSpec` (`{via:'tangent',circle,at}` → ⟂ radius). An inscribed polygon is **not** a new primitive: `circle` + N `on-circle` points + the existing `triangle`/`quadrilateral` reused on them (composition, ADR-013). `circle`/`segment` create their centre/endpoints if missing.
  - **Renderer** now draws circles (`scene.ts` `SceneCircle` + `Figure.tsx` `<circle fill=none>`, radius = world r × `transform.scale`). **Store** `cycleAlt` generalised to cycle `arc-midpoint`/`line-circle` branches too.
  - **Parser** widened: circle (centre+radius/through), inscribed triangle/quad, chord, diameter, arc-midpoint, point-on-circle, and three intersection compounds — tangent∩segment, bisector∩segment (one bisector meeting a side), parallel-line∩circle. Circle id convention is `circle-<centre letter>` so "circle O" is referenceable by name; Hebrew radius word needed `רדיוס\S*` (not `\w*`, which doesn't match Hebrew). **Still deferred** (5d): the parallel/⟂ *constraints*, a single bisector fact, a 3-point (circumscribed) circle.
  - **Tests:** `phase5c.test.ts` (8 unit) + `phase5c.corpus.test.ts` (6 — Q5/Q6/Q7 in He+En) + a circle render test in phase2. **173 green, build clean.** Note: corpus figures are valid up to **free configuration** (e.g. Q6's two chords cross at a generic point, not the algebra's "C = midpoint of OB" — solving stays a non-goal).
  - **Resume next:** no new constructs remain for v1 — **finish 5d** (constraint phrasings left out of grammar) + **ADR-018 Stage 1** (pinned-vs-free + DOF sampler), then **Phase 6 (theorems)**.

- **2026-06-11 (home PC, cont.) — Phase 5d constraints + ADR-018 Stage 1 (sampler).** 186 tests green, build clean.
  - **ADR-021 — the constraint family is one generic mechanism.** Added `distance` (|AB|=k), `equal` (|AB|=|CD|), `parallel` (AB∥CD), `perpendicular` (AB⟂CD) — each is just refs/residual/describe/tolerance cases in `solve.ts` + a generic `driveOrCheck` in apply.ts (drives an on-segment carrier if one is referenced, else an over-constraint check). `evaluate`'s constraint loop is now generic (was angle-specific). Parser: `AB = 6` / `AB = CD` / `AB ∥ CD` / `AB ⟂ CD` (He/En). **Fixed a real `solveParam` bug**: a root landing exactly on a grid sample (symmetric config, residual exactly 0 at t=0.5) was missed — now exact-zero samples count as roots.
  - **ADR-018 Stage 1 (built) — seeded sampling of residual freedom.** `FreePoint.pinned` (explicit `point A at (x,y)` = pinned; shape/segment/circle-defaulted vertices = free). `applySeed(construction, seed)` (`sample.ts`, deterministic mulberry32) perturbs only non-pinned free points (rotation of the free cluster + per-point jitter); shapes stay valid (derived points recompute). Store has a `seed` (not undoable); `replay(facts, seed)`; `resample()` finds the next non-degenerate seed; the "show another configuration" button resamples when no discrete branch is selected. `freeDofs()` lists the figure's remaining freedom. Deferred: Stage 2/3 (constraints auto-prune DOFs; a DOF-remaining cue); a single-bisector / free-point (2-DOF) constraint carrier.
  - **Next:** a large differential coordinate-validation campaign (LLM-generated fully-determined diagrams → predict coords → build command-by-command → assert each node's coordinates against an independent reference; fix engine bugs; rerun until all pass).

- **2026-06-12 — Visible construction lines + two-circle intersection (ADR-022).** Fixes the "we don't have משיק" gap. A `Line` gains a `visible` flag: scaffolding lines (inside compounds) stay invisible; a *drawn* tangent / bisector / perpendicular / parallel sets `visible:true` and the renderer draws it as a **dashed line clipped to the viewport** (`scene.ts` `lineGeometry` + `Figure.tsx`). New standalone parser rules: `bisector of angle ABC` (≠ "AD bisects ∠BAC" which places a point — still deferred), `tangent to circle O at A`, `line through P perpendicular/parallel to AB`. New **`circle-circle`** point + `circle-circle-intersection` command + parser "G is the intersection of circle O and circle P" (0/1/2 branches, cyclable). Tests: `render/__tests__/lines-circles.test.tsx` + a `circle-circle` campaign family (357 cases). 550 green. **Deferred:** implicit circle (`משיק ב-A` without naming the circle — ambiguous once two circles exist); single-bisector point placement; right-angle/tick/arc marks.

- **2026-06-12 (cont.) — auto-create circle points (tangent / arc-midpoint).** A construct that references a point which must lie on a circle now **creates it on the circle if missing** (like `segment`/`circle` auto-create their endpoints/centre): `tangent to circle O at A` with no A places A on the circle (golden-angle `nextTheta`) and draws the tangent; same for `arc-midpoint`'s endpoints. `commandConflict` got an `on-circle` create-or-reuse exception so an explicit `A on circle O` added *after* the tangent doesn't conflict (it reuses A). Fixes the user-reported "tangent failed: unresolved dependencies for tan-A" and "adding A afterward didn't help" (the fact list replays in order, so a forward-referenced point can't retroactively satisfy an earlier fact — auto-create is the fix). Tests in `lines-circles.test.tsx`. 552 green; 42k stress sweep clean. **Still deferred:** auto-create for non-circle constructs (foot/midpoint/bisector references) where a sensible default position is less obvious; implicit-circle reference.

- **2026-06-12 (cont.) — Phase 7: LLM fallback for freeform input (ADR-023).** The deterministic parser was too rigid for students to "program"; now `not-handled` escalates to Claude. **Design:** the LLM doesn't emit raw command JSON — it **normalises** freeform He/En into the app's *canonical command phrasings* (the supported `COMMAND_CATALOG`, embedded in the system prompt), and the client **re-parses each line with the tested `parse()`** → only valid commands reach the engine. Model `claude-haiku-4-5`, forced tool use (`emit_steps` → `{steps:string[]}`), low max_tokens. **Key custody:** server-side only — a Vite dev plugin (`server/llmProxy.ts`) serves `POST /api/parse`, reads `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` from `.env.local`/shell via `loadEnv` (never a `VITE_` var, never the browser); SDK dynamically imported; per-IP rate limit + size cap. Pure core `src/parser/llmShared.ts` (no SDK/engine import) shared by proxy + tests; client `src/parser/llm.ts` only fetches. App `submit` is now async (parser-first → LLM on miss → re-parse → execute; "thinking…" state, figure context = existing point/circle ids). Tests `llm.test.ts` (LLM mocked, no live calls) incl. a **guard that no shipped src/ file imports the SDK or reads the key** — caught + removed two vestiges of the abandoned browser-key approach (`src/env.d.ts` VITE_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, old `.env.example`). 561 green, client bundle key-free. **To use:** copy `.env.example` → `.env.local`, set the key, restart `npm run dev`. **Deferred:** production proxy (serverless wrapper of the same handler).

- **2026-06-13 — Parser anti-half-parse guard + coverage matrix (ADR-024).** Operator hit a real bug: `משולש ABC חסום במעגל` drew a bare triangle — the triangle rule matched `משולש ABC` and silently dropped `חסום במעגל` (no circle centre named). Fixed two ways: (1) `inscribedPolygon` auto-creates a circle when the centre isn't named (triangle/quad); (2) a general `SHAPE_LEFTOVER` guard — after a shape rule takes its keyword+labels, if a geometry-significant token remains (circle, `=`/`⟂`/`∥`, special line, `segment`/`point`, inscribed, angle; He+En, plural-tolerant, word-bounded), it returns `'stop'` → escalates to the LLM instead of half-parsing. A *right* triangle inscribed defers out of inscribedPolygon so it escalates (engine can't build it). Now `square/rect/rhombus/trapezoid inscribed`, `square ABCD with AB=6`, `triangle ABC with angle BAC=37`, `square ABCD and segment AC`, `rectangle ABCD with diagonals` all escalate — none draw a wrong figure. `parser-coverage.test.ts` = 86-case matrix (parses-to-X vs escalates, both locales) pinning the boundary. **Half-parse > miss is the rule: a miss escalates, a half-parse draws a wrong figure.** 649 tests green.

- **2026-06-13 (cont.) — inscribed cyclic polygons (square/rectangle/rhombus/isosceles trapezoid).** Operator: "a טרפז שווה שוקיים can be inscribed." Right — an isosceles trapezoid is cyclic. The engine couldn't inscribe a trapezoid only because the `trapezoid` command derives one corner off the circle. Fix: `inscribedPolygon` now builds the cyclic quads **directly** — 4 `on-circle` points at fixed angles (`INSCRIBED_ANGLES`: square `[45,135,225,315]`, rectangle `[40,140,220,320]` (diagonals=diameters), rhombus=square, isosceles trapezoid `[215,325,60,120]` (AB∥DC, equal legs)) + a `quadrilateral` for the edges; the shape comes from the angles. Triangle/general-quad still spread evenly. Verified geometrically (vertices on circle; trapezoid: one pair parallel, isosceles legs). Also added a leftover-guard to inscribedPolygon so "inscribed … with AB=6" escalates (doesn't drop the constraint). So `square/rectangle/rhombus/trapezoid ABCD חסום במעגל` now **draw**; only `right triangle inscribed` (hypotenuse=diameter) still escalates. 651 tests green.

- **2026-06-13 — built the constructs ADR-024 was escalating (ADR-025).** Operator: "fix this right triangle issue and any other such issues." Several escalating cases were *unbuilt*, not unbuildable. Added, engine+parser in lock-step: **right triangle inscribed** (Thales — 180/0/90 on the circle, right angle at C); **circumcircle** `circle through A B C` / `מעגל חוסם את ABC` (new `circumcenter` 0-DOF point kind + `circumcircle` command, auto-creates the 3 pts); **special lines** as compositions of existing primitives (no new engine kind) — `median` (opposite midpoint + segment), `height`/`altitude` and bare-foot `perpendicular from A to BC` (foot + segment), `perpendicular bisector` (midpoint + visible ⟂ line), `AD bisects ∠BAC` (bisector ∩ opposite side → places D; the angle-bisector foot, fully determined, so no ADR-018 1-DOF needed). **Ordering gotcha:** the Hebrew special-line rules must run *before* the shape rules — `במשולש` embeds `משולש`, which `triangle` would grab and `stop` on. `triangle ABC with a height/median from A` now parses directly (triangle + the line). Catalog entries flipped to `supported` (so the help panel AND the LLM prompt learn them). Still escalating: genuine compounds (shape + constraint). 662 tests green. See [[geo-builder-llm-transparency]] precursor.

- **2026-06-13 — pedagogy charter added: [docs/10-pedagogy.md](10-pedagogy.md).** Operator asked for a single doc capturing every pedagogical intention for the tool. Captures: the thesis (entering givens one-by-one turns *copying the figure* into *reading the givens* — Vision G4/G5); how each mechanic teaches (incremental entry, adapt-on-fact, alternatives, stability, over-constraint, theorem surfacing); theorem surfacing as the central feature (citable by official bagrut #, P=use vs C=recognise/justify, definite-vs-possible, surface-don't-solve); and — the actionable part — a **construction→theorem trigger map** (the Phase-6 payload): when the student builds X, raise theorem #N. The operator's example is in it: `right triangle inscribed in a circle` → **Thm 103** (inscribed angle on a diameter = 90°) + **104** (converse). Grow that table as constructs/theorems land. Linked from docs/README + repo CLAUDE.md. This is where future pedagogy decisions get recorded.

- **2026-06-13 — step grouping: one submission = one row.** Operator saw `טרפז ABCD חסום במעגל שרדיוסו 8` render as ~6 identical step rows. Cause (not a bug): one utterance expands to many commands (inscribed shape = circle + 4 on-circle + quad), and each command was its own `Fact` row sharing the same label. Fix: tag every command from one submission with a shared `group` id (`Fact.group`, `groupKey(f)=f.group??f.id`); the fact list renders **one row per group**. Added store actions `setGroupEnabled`/`removeGroup`/`replaceGroup` (whole-step toggle/delete/edit, one undo entry; replaceGroup splices in place — a group is a contiguous run). App: `submit` assigns one group per utterance, the LLM path one group per canonical step (so each LLM step is still its own row); select/highlight/edit operate on the group (highlight = union of the group's introduced ids). The engine/replay model is unchanged (still 1 cmd/fact) — this is display + batch ops only. 666 tests green.

- **2026-06-13 — re-stating a circle resizes it (override), not a conflict.** Operator built an inscribed trapezoid (auto circle-O r5), then `רדיוס המעגל הוא 8` → LLM emitted `circle centered at O radius 8` → `commandConflict` refused to redefine circle-O (r5→r8) → step showed ✗. Not a crash (graceful keep-prior), but the intent (resize the only circle) is valid. Fix: engine `upsertCircle` REPLACES an existing circle's radius/centre on re-issue; `commandConflict` now allows circle-over-circle (a resize, ADR-011 analog). So a later `circle O radius 8` overrides the radius and the inscribed vertices re-evaluate outward. Store polish: re-stating a **standalone** circle collapses in place (no stacking); resizing a circle that belongs to a bigger step (inscribed shape) appends a visible override step instead (keeps the group's label). Why tests missed it: we tested that redefinition CONFLICTS but never the legitimate resize flow (the capability didn't exist), and the LLM path is mocked (no live call exercising `רדיוס...→circle O radius 8→conflict`). Added store tests. Still deferred: implicit-circle reference so the *unnamed* `רדיוס המעגל הוא 8` parses deterministically (today the LLM bridges it via context). 668 tests green.

- **2026-06-13 — proportion constraint `|AB| = k·|CD|` (ADR-026).** Operator asked where proportions like `AB = 2·AD` are handled — answer was *nowhere*, and `AB = 2 AD` actually HALF-PARSED to `set-distance AB = 2` (dropped the `AD`, wrong figure — the misparse ADR-024 guards on shapes but not constraint rules). Added a `ratio` constraint via the generic ADR-014 mechanism (residual `dist(a,b) − k·dist(c,d)`, refs, `set-ratio` cmd → same `driveOrCheck`): no new point kind/solver — it drives a 1-DOF carrier (`AE = 2 EB` → E at the 2:1 point) or is an over-constraint check. Parser `ratioConstraint` (`m·AB = n·CD`, k=n/m; forms `2 AD`/`2*AD`/`2·AD`/`2AD`; Hebrew `AB פי 2 מ-AD`) runs BEFORE equal/distance so `AB = CD`→equal, `AB = 6`→distance still hold. Catalog gains `AB = 2 AD`. 676 tests green. Deferred: textual coefficients (`twice`), left to the LLM.
